# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

## [Unreleased]

### Changed

- **R20 dependency hygiene closes reproducibility and audit blind spots (#1999).** Package path repositories now match internal runtime/dev requirements in both directions under CP007, blocking stale local resolution metadata; PHPUnit suite paths are self-audited against every package `*Test.php`, including 14 tests that were previously outside all suites; blocking admin jobs use `npm ci`; scheduled Composer/npm vulnerability audits run weekly; Dependabot admin updates rebuild dist in a read-only job and publish only validated artifacts from a separate write-capable job, so freshness can recover on the dependency PR without bridging untrusted lifecycle code into the token boundary; and Bimaaji truthfully declares its live `graph:dump` and `bimaaji:install` CLI integration as an optional Composer suggestion without adding a forbidden runtime layer edge.
- **R20 layer-map and analysis coverage collapse (#1999).** `check-package-layers` is the sole package-layer map and exports it to the require-dev audit; stale per-manifest layer metadata and per-package PHPStan paths are removed. Static analysis now covers the package root automatically, spec drift maps every package with an enduring spec (including SSR), and lefthook is the only documented hook path.
- **R20 makes CI/release parity fail closed (#1999).** The blocking `ci/unit-tests` context now runs the Architecture suite, `ci/verify-gates` runs the PL008 meta-test, and tag-triggered split fan-out requires a completed successful `ci.yml` run for the exact tagged SHA. All setup-php and GitHub-release action uses are pinned to verified full commit SHAs, and splitsh-lite v1.0.1 is SHA-256 checked before extraction.
- **R20 server-side release governance (#1999).** The main-branch ruleset now requires every unconditional blocking CI context, and an idempotent repository operator applies or audits forward-only release-tag protection across every split mirror when organization-level rulesets are unavailable.
- **R19 dead public surfaces (#1992).** The first-party MCP admin routes are attached to their existing read models; the consumerless search read surface is explicitly parked as `@internal` until an access-checked first-party endpoint adopts it; and the unused migration plugin registry, `SearchIndexJob`, duplicate embedding pipeline, zero-implementation pipeline step contract, and undrained AI pipeline dispatcher are deleted without shims.
- **Media CAS/versioning is explicitly parked as internal (#1992, #1742; R19).** Version-namespace classes and their API/admin readers are no longer advertised as `@api`. Reactivation requires a boundary test proving real upload bytes survive the production request boundary and are durably persisted to CAS before version rows are exposed. The #1951 early-return guard remains unchanged; this does not finish or activate CAS.

### Fixed

- **Release synchronization preserves Composer suggestion descriptions (#1999).** Internal version updates now touch only `require` and `require-dev`; optional `waaseyaa/*` integrations under `suggest` remain honest human-readable descriptions on the exact release commit.
- **R20 package and repository gates now fail on the hidden forms they claim to cover (#1999).** Package-layer scanning maps `attachment`, self-checks layer/namespace parity, detects inline and quoted upward FQCNs in every layer, and fails on any same-layer cycle outside the five-pair reviewed baseline. The secret scan covers repository source rather than only `defaults/`, and four stale `agent-output` JSON adapters now reject their retired flag explicitly instead of loading absent formatter classes.
- **Spec-drift acknowledgements now match the blocking CI contract (#1999, R20 WP5).** The detector reads revision-attached commit messages only, matching the repository's commit-trailer rule instead of advertising an unwired `PR_BODY` escape hatch, and accepts `spec-reviewed:` paths wrapped in Markdown backticks. Fixture-driven Architecture coverage pins both behaviours.
- **Production runtime reachability now matches the advertised notification, SEO, and routing contracts (#1992, R19).** `sendAsync()` delivers directly when the default synchronous queue is active instead of executing an intentionally worker-only job; the SEO provider attaches its registered Twig extension during boot; and `HttpKernel` upcasts entity-typed route parameters before dispatch, returning 404 for a missing entity. The duplicate class-based notification migration was deleted in favor of the timestamped package migration.
- **R19 production invariants and install boot (#1992).** Relationship saves now reach `RelationshipPreSaveListener` through the production lifecycle dispatcher, `config:import` rejects structurally invalid workflow assignments before active-store writes, and `ConfigManagerInterface` is bound for the supported `install` command outside the `db:init` path.

### Removed

- **Deleted the producerless Telescope and codified-context stack (#1992, R19).** The auto-discovered middleware wrote to an in-process or raw-PDO store with no production reader; the CLI resolved no store and called methods outside its contract; and no provider ever attached the codified-context store to `HttpKernel`. The recorders, raw-PDO stores, phantom CLI commands, API/router ports, admin pages, and associated schemas/tests are removed with no compatibility shim. The deletion also exposed and removes the test-only `EntityAuditLogger::prune()` and `NorthCloudCache::clear()` methods instead of silently baselining them. The `waaseyaa/telescope` package name remains reserved as an empty split-package shell.

- **Deleted five test-only or vestigial content/CLI subsystems (#1992, R19).** Genealogy's shadow field catalogue and stale vendored API copy, Bimaaji's unvalidated Task DSL, menu's callerless tree builder, path's superseded manager/processor trio, and the orphaned parallel ingest connector/normalization pipeline are removed without shims. Canonical genealogy registration, menu entities, path resolution/uniqueness, and `IngestionEnvelopeNormalizer` remain production paths.

### Security

- **R20 field guards reduce credential and hidden-work regressions (#1999).** The admin-dist checkout no longer persists its write token in the runner's Git config, and the documented repository `bin/git` entrypoint mechanically rejects every `git stash` form while preserving normal Git command arguments. A tooling regression gate pins both controls; the existing dangling `da4d26758` stash remains untouched.

## [0.1.0-alpha.261] - 2026-07-14

### Added

- **Fixed-bundle migrations now compose split source and id-map partitions without site adapter classes (#1981, G-032).** The migration package adds the format-neutral `FilteredSource` decorator, `PartitionedLookupProcessor` for mixed reference lists, and ordered `MigrationIdMap::lookupDestinationAcross()` for non-process consumers such as media URL resolvers. A fresh-install boundary test applies the real package migrations and runs category/tag plus image/document definitions through `MigrationRunner`, `EntityDestination`, the real access gate, and least-privilege per-bundle system accounts.

### Changed

- **Repository agent rules now pin the mandatory PR, TDD, changelog, spec-drift, worker-safe git, local-gate, release-fan-out, and auto-merge conventions.** `AGENTS.md` remains a terse entry point to the authoritative `CLAUDE.md` and workflow/spec documents while ensuring future agent sessions inherit the process guardrails up front.

### Fixed

- **Blocking CI now exercises the production-cutover fresh-install path (#1985).** The required `ci/unit-tests` check creates a clean database through the real installer, requires `schema:check` to pass, persists an import-derived bundle field, renders that field from a separate HTTP process, and traverses a freshly-created relationship through SSR. This closes the upgraded-fixture coverage gap that let fresh schema and boot-time read regressions ship in alpha.260.
- **Fresh HTTP processes now hydrate import-derived bundle fields (#1982, G-034).** The migration provider replays derived field declarations only for bundle config entities already persisted by a completed import, restoring the process-local field registry before repository reads without moving full content-model registration back into first-install boot. Clean installs still register at import time, and repeated boots leave existing schema and rows unchanged.
- **Fresh installs now support relationship traversal during SSR rendering (#1984).** Relationship queries resolve endpoint, status, type, and timeline fields from the canonical `_data` payload on fresh sql-blob tables while continuing to use historical dedicated columns when present, so upgraded installs are unaffected and SQL-level timeline filtering is preserved.
- **Fresh installs no longer report immediate `media_version` schema drift (#1980, G-031).** An additive migration supplies the common content-entity columns omitted by the parked CAS-era table without removing its typed columns or enabling the subsystem tracked by #1742. Schema diagnostics now correctly recognize SQLite's parameterized `VARCHAR(n)` declarations as TEXT-affinity columns. Existing rows and CAS columns are preserved on upgraded installs.
- **WordPress-style trailing-slash aliases resolve through the canonical SSR path (#1983).** Path-alias persistence and lookup now share one normalizer: non-root trailing slashes collapse to the slashless stored key while `/` remains unchanged. Canonical, hierarchical, dated, query-string, and language-prefix paths retain one entity target without introducing redirect behavior.
- **Agent cancellation and approval now use exact-status compare-and-swap updates (#1975, R18 M5).** Controller actions no longer save a stale hydrated run row that can regress a worker's terminal status or overwrite transcript and cost fields. Cancellation requires the status observed by the request; approve/deny additionally require the pending call id, and a lost race returns 409 without audit or broadcast side effects.
- **Production agent runs now dispatch the canonical observability lifecycle (#1975, R18 M4; closes #1743).** The `AiAgentServiceProvider` and agent `MessagingServiceProvider` factories inject the kernel's real dispatcher into `AgentExecutor` and `RunAgentHandler`, so the already-live `AgentRun*` producers reach `AgentRunTelemetryListener` outside manually wired tests.
- **Outbound MCP servers are now bootstrapped in production (#1975, R18 M6).** The complete, fail-closed `McpServiceProvider` existed but was absent from `waaseyaa/ai-agent`'s package-provider manifest, leaving remote tool discovery unreachable on normal kernel boot. Package discovery now loads it; hosts without MCP configuration or a tool registry remain inert, and individual remote-server failures still degrade without aborting boot.
- **Persistent workers no longer retain stale state reads or abandoned AI-run telemetry (#1971, R17).** `SqlState` now reads its authoritative database on every `get()`/`getMultiple()` call instead of serving a per-instance cache that could outlive the request, while `AgentRunTelemetryListener` replaces its one-active-run aggregation slot when the next Messenger job starts so a missing terminal event cannot grow memory for the worker lifetime. Two-request/one-process regressions cover both single and multiple state reads and interrupted telemetry aggregation.

### Removed

- **Deleted the producerless legacy AI trace listener chain (#1975, R18 M4).** `LlmCallListener`, `ToolCallListener`, their internal `TokenAccountant` / `ModelPricing` branch, and the never-constructed `LlmCallCompleted` / `ToolCallStarted` / `ToolCallCompleted` events no longer advertise an inert automatic trace path. Historical trace storage, the explicit recorder API, and the public `CostRecord` value object remain available; canonical run pricing continues through `ModelPriceTable`.

### Security

- **Production agent runs now execute only tools explicitly allowlisted by their agent definition (#1975, R18 M2+M3).** `RunAgentHandler` advertises the allowlisted registry descriptors instead of hardcoding an empty tool list, while `AgentExecutor` independently rejects any model-emitted or direct tool invocation absent from that allowlist and rechecks the tool capability before execution. The tool loop and its security boundary land together so enabling tools can never expose the global catalogue to every agent.
- **Persistent HTTP workers no longer carry Inertia shared props or the active language into the next request (#1971, R17).** `InertiaMiddleware` clears request-shared props in `finally` on every response path, preventing account-shaped props from surviving in process statics, and SSR routing resets the shared `LanguageManager` before applying the current request's prefix. Two-request/one-process regressions pin both boundaries; companion coverage verifies that the dual static Twig environments are already replaced on every production-order provider boot and require no runtime change.

## [0.1.0-alpha.260] - 2026-07-13

### Security

- **JSON:API write-side field allowlist closes the pointer-column write hole (#1920, CW-v1 option-1 PR-4).** `.superpowers/sdd/final-review-findings.md` finding #1 [CRITICAL]: `JsonApiController::store()`/`update()` applied every submitted `data.attributes` key with only per-field ACCESS as the gate — no allowlist restricted writes to declared, non-bookkeeping fields, unlike the read-path query allowlist (`validateQueryFields()`). Neither `revision_id` nor `published_revision_id` (real base columns added by WP-2) carries a field definition or a shipped field-access policy, so an account holding only plain entity `update` permission — **no workflow/publish permission at all** — could move the published pointer (or forge the current-revision id) directly through a PATCH body attribute, bypassing `WorkflowPointerMoveGuard` and every transition permission entirely (finding #2 [IMPORTANT]: the same hole generalizes to `revision_id` on any revisionable, workflow-bound type). This is a real **behavior change for clients**: a JSON:API create that submits an undeclared attribute, or `revision_id`/`published_revision_id`/`uuid`/`langcode`/`default_langcode`, now **422s** (`code: FIELD_NOT_WRITABLE`, `meta.refused_keys`) instead of silently applying; an **update** that submits one of the identity/bookkeeping keys with a value that **differs** from the entity's current stored value 422s the same way — a pure echo of the current value (what a read-modify-write client naturally sends back) is accepted and dropped before it is applied (see the echo-tolerant rejection paragraph below; a genuinely undeclared field is never echo-tolerant on either path). New shared `Waaseyaa\Entity\Write\EntityWritePayloadGuard` (`packages/entity/src/Write/EntityWritePayloadGuard.php`, `@api`), modeled on ai-tools' `EntityKeyGuard` but keyed by payload KEY presence and checked against the bundle-scoped declared-field set (`EntityTypeManagerInterface::resolveFieldDefinitions()` — the exact source the read-path allowlist already uses) rather than only entity keys: a payload key is refused when it is an identity/bookkeeping column (entity-key kinds `uuid`/`revision`/`langcode`/`default_langcode`, unioned with the literal floor `revision_id`/`published_revision_id`/`uuid`/`langcode`/`default_langcode` — the literal floor is what closes the hole, since `published_revision_id` carries no entity-key kind on any shipped type) regardless of declaration, or is neither a declared field nor a writable entity key (`label`/`bundle` are deliberately never refused). `status`/`workflow_state` are ordinary declared fields on `node`, so they pass this guard untouched — their write stays gated by field-level access (`NodeAccessPolicy::PUBLISH_GATED_FIELDS`, unchanged) — this guard does not double-gate them. Applied to `JsonApiController::store()`/`update()` (reject-not-strip, before `create()`/any `set()`/`save()`, unconditional — not gated on an access handler/account being wired, a structural validation not an access decision), `GenericAdminSurfaceHost` create/update (inherited for free — it fully delegates to `JsonApiController`, no separate change needed), and GraphQL's `EntityResolver::resolveCreate()`/`resolveUpdate()` (defense-in-depth: the generated GraphQL input type already bounds the surface). `FieldAutoSaveController` was verified already declared-field-allowlisted (its per-field endpoint 404s an unregistered key at step 5) and is unchanged; a collateral finding during verification: ai-tools' `EntityKeyGuard` did **not** already cover `published_revision_id` (empirically confirmed red — no entity-key kind names it, only a literal-floor addition closes it) — its `LITERAL_FLOOR` gains `revision_id`/`published_revision_id` alongside this PR, `EntityCreateTool`/`EntityUpdateTool` otherwise unchanged. `docs/specs/content-workflow.md`'s "Write-side field allowlist / pointer-column write hole" deferral bullet is marked CLOSED; the other option-1 deferred items (read-side pointer awareness, first-publish edge rule on review-required workflows) remain open follow-ups. Pinned end-to-end (real SQLite, real `NodeServiceProvider`+`WorkflowServiceProvider` wiring, real `NodeAccessPolicy`) by `packages/api/tests/Integration/WriteAllowlistPointerBypassFlowTest.php`, reproducing finding #1's exact scenario with a raw-SQL proof that the base row's pointer columns are byte-unmoved by the refused PATCH. See `docs/specs/api-layer.md` "Write-side field allowlist (CW-v1 option-1 PR-4)".

- **Echo-tolerant rejection fixes an admin-UI-wide round-trip BLOCKER in the write-side allowlist above (#1920, CW-v1 option-1 PR-4 rework).** A fresh-context review of the guard found its hard, unconditional refusal of `revision_id`/`published_revision_id`/`langcode` was itself a BLOCKER on `update()`: `ResourceSerializer` emits those as ordinary READ attributes (FR-008 — `revision_id` is documented load-bearing on reads specifically so optimistic-locking clients can read it), and the admin SPA's `SchemaForm.vue` submits the **full loaded attribute object** back on every save (`formData.value = { ...entityResult.value.attributes }` then `update(props.entityType, props.entityId, formData.value)`) — so the hard reject 422s **every** node edit through the admin UI, and CI was green only because no test round-tripped a serialized revisionable entity end-to-end. Fix (Drupal JSON:API parity): `EntityWritePayloadGuard::evaluateForUpdate()` — a new method used ONLY by `update()` call sites (`store()`/GraphQL `resolveCreate()` are unchanged, hard-refuse, no stored value to echo against) — refuses an identity/bookkeeping key ONLY when its submitted value **differs** (type-lenient: `(string)`-normalized comparison; `null`/absent both count as "stored null") from the target entity's current stored value (`EntityInterface::toArray()`); a pure echo passes but is reported separately (`Waaseyaa\Entity\Write\EntityWritePayloadGuardResult::$echoedKeys`, alongside the unchanged `$refusedKeys`) so the caller strips it before BOTH the field-access loop and the apply loop — belt: even an allowed echo must never reach `$entity->set()`. The undeclared/unknown-field branch is untouched and stays hard-refused unconditionally either way — echo tolerance applies ONLY to the identity/bookkeeping set. Wired into `JsonApiController::update()` (strip immediately after the guard call) and GraphQL's `EntityResolver::resolveUpdate()` (new private `assertWritableForUpdate()`, strip before the apply loop); `GenericAdminSurfaceHost` inherits the fix for free via delegation. New round-trip pin tests (the coverage gap the review found): `WriteAllowlistPointerBypassFlowTest::full_attribute_round_trip_patch_persists_the_changed_title_and_the_pointer_stays_self_consistent()` (GET → PATCH the full attribute set back with one field changed → 200, real Node + workflow wiring) and `::echo_equal_published_revision_id_is_accepted_and_the_pointer_is_not_rewritten_by_an_ordinary_edit()` (a never-published node, where a null echo alongside a genuine content edit that legitimately cuts a new revision proves the PUBLISHED pointer stays put — still null — while the TIP revision id correctly advances), plus a `GenericAdminSurfaceHostWriteAllowlistFlowTest` admin-surface-host round trip and guard-level unit coverage (`EntityWritePayloadGuardTest`'s echo-tolerance matrix, including a translatable-shape `langcode` case). A rebase-time finding (this PR landed after `#1920 PR-2`'s "default-revision discipline" engine wiring merged): once a node carries a published pointer, an AUTHORIZED same-state edit of already-published content legitimately re-publishes it (same-state republish) — so an accepted echo PATCH of a published node's `published_revision_id` correctly ADVANCES the pointer together with the newly-cut revision rather than leaving it byte-unmoved; the round-trip pin test asserts pointer SELF-CONSISTENCY (`published_revision_id === revision_id` post-save) accordingly, not byte-immutability, and the dedicated "value provably not rewritten by the save" pin uses a never-published node instead, where no independent engine mechanism can mask whether the guard's own strip-before-apply did its job. See `docs/specs/api-layer.md` "Echo-tolerant rejection on update() (PR-4 rework)" → "Interaction with PR-2's same-state republish".

- **`store()`'s per-bundle create-access fix (same review) tightens behavior for installs that granted only the type-level create permission.** While adding the guard above, `store()`'s pre-existing bundle-resolution bug was also fixed: it used to read the literal attribute key `'bundle'` (almost never an entity type's real bundle key — node's is `'type'`), so it silently fell back to `checkCreateAccess($entityTypeId, $entityTypeId, ...)` — e.g. `checkCreateAccess('node', 'node', ...)`, which checks the type-level `create node content` permission — for any real client that never happened to send a bogus literal `'bundle'` attribute. The fix resolves the bundle from the entity type's OWN bundle key (`$guardBundle`, already computed for the guard above) and reuses it for the create-access check, so `checkCreateAccess('node', 'article', ...)` now correctly checks the BUNDLE-level `create article content` permission. **This is a real behavior change**: an install/account that held only the type-level permission (`create node content`) and relied on the bug for bundle creates to work now gets **403**. Pinned (deny path) by `WriteAllowlistPointerBypassFlowTest::account_with_only_the_type_level_create_permission_cannot_create_an_article()`.

### Removed

- **Retired workflow dry-run/guards machinery deleted outright — CW-v1 WP-5 WP1 (#1920).** The CW-v1 engine (WP-1 through WP-4) fully replaced the prototype's read-only dry-run/guards surface, leaving it dead. This is a straight deletion — no compat shim, no feature flag, no gated route left registered. Removed: `WorkflowDryRunController` (`packages/api/src/Workflow/`, `POST /api/workflow-definitions/dry-run`), `WorkflowGuardsController` (`packages/api/src/Controller/`) and `WorkflowGuardsApiRouter` (`packages/api/src/Http/Router/`, `GET /api/workflow-definitions/{workflow_id}/guards`), their `ApiServiceProvider` route registrations and router wiring, the `WorkflowDryRunController` dispatch branch in `WorkflowDefinitionsApiRouter`, `packages/workflows/src/AuthoringRoleMatrix.php` and its `WorkflowServiceProvider` singleton binding, `tests/Integration/PhaseWorkflowGuards/` (`WorkflowGuardsEndpointsTest`, `WorkflowGuardsBindingIntegrationTest`), and all unit test coverage for the deleted classes. Admin SPA: deleted `TransitionDryRunForm.vue`, `WorkflowGuardsTable.vue`, `useWorkflowGuards.ts`, their usage on `/workflows/{id}`, `useWorkflowDefinitions`'s `dryRun()` method, and the orphaned `workflow_guards_*`/`dry_run_*` i18n keys (en + fr); admin dist rebuilt. `EditorialTransitionAccessResolver` and `EditorialWorkflowPreset` are explicitly KEPT (the latter is live CW-v1 data; the former still backs the not-yet-deleted `EditorialWorkflowService`, deferred to a later WP-5 slice along with `DomainValidationListener`, `ContentModerator`, `ContentModerationState`). Docs truth-up: `docs/specs/api-layer.md`, `docs/specs/content-workflow.md`, `docs/specs/infrastructure.md`, `docs/specs/admin-spa.md`, `docs/specs/jsonapi.md`, `packages/workflows/README.md`.

- **Spec Kitty retired from the repository (housekeeping, per the 2026-07-06 workflow decision).** Deleted the Spec Kitty machinery trees — `.kittify/` (runtime/config/charter-interview state), `.agents/skills/` (the spec-kitty/spk skill pack), `kitty-ops/` (dispatch op logs) — plus the dead `tools/orchestrate-missions.py` driver and the spec-kitty session hooks in `.claude/settings.json`. The project charter was relocated verbatim from `.kittify/charter/charter.md` to `docs/governance/charter.md` with a relocation preamble (directives remain binding; amendments now flow through anchor-issue-traceable PRs). Live guidance (`CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `README.md`, `docs/specs/workflow.md`, `.github/pull_request_template.md`, skeleton/skill docs) rewritten around the anchor-issue + design-first workflow (`Closes #N` / `Part of #N` traceability). Historical artifacts are preserved read-only: `kitty-specs/` mission archives, `docs/audits/`, `docs/history/`, and CHANGELOG entries are untouched.

### Added

- **Surface pointer-awareness: every EDIT/PREVIEW/WORKFLOW surface targets the working copy (#1920, CW-v1 option-1 PR-3, design §4).** #1920 PR-2 made `find()` itself pointer-aware BY CONSTRUCTION for every disciplined entity — the base row IS the published revision, so public read surfaces (JSON:API item/collection GET, GraphQL reads, MCP reads, listing, SSR public render) needed no further change and are untouched by this PR. What remained was the write/edit side: an editor loading, patching, or checking the workflow position of a bound entity mid-forward-draft was still seeing the PUBLISHED pointer's content and state, not the tip they were actually working on. This PR closes that. **Client-visible behavior changes:** (1) **`JsonApiController::update()`'s PATCH TARGET is now the working copy**, `$repository->loadWorkingCopy((string) $entity->id()) ?? $entity` — identical to `find()` for every undisciplined entity (pinned by regression test), but for a disciplined entity mid-draft a PATCH now lands on and returns the TIP, not the served/published row. The write-allowlist's echo-tolerant comparison (`EntityWritePayloadGuard::evaluateForUpdate()`, #1920 PR-4) now compares a submitted identity/bookkeeping value (e.g. `revision_id`) against the WORKING COPY's own stored value, not the gate entity's — a client that read the working copy and echoes its own `revision_id` back is no longer spuriously refused. Entity/field-access gates are unaffected — they still evaluate the `find()`-loaded gate entity (type/bundle-scoped, not revision-scoped). (2) **New item GET query param `?workingCopy=1`**: after the existing view gate passes (canonical 404 unchanged for a missing/view-denied id — not an existence oracle), serves `loadWorkingCopy()` to an account with entity UPDATE access; **403** (`JsonApiError::forbidden`) when denied. Equals the plain GET byte-for-byte when no draft is in flight (pinned by test). `JsonApiRouter::handle()` now threads the request's query string into `show()` too (previously only `index()` received it — a pre-existing gap where sparse fieldsets silently never reached a single-resource HTTP GET, fixed as a side effect of this wiring). (3) **`WorkflowTransitionController`**: the R8 view-access gate is UNCHANGED (byte-identical 404 on a `find()`-denied entity), but once it passes, `meta.workflow_state`, the offered transitions (`GET .../workflow/transitions`), and the transition target (`POST .../workflow/transition`) now resolve from `loadWorkingCopy()` — a bound entity's TIP state drives what the endpoint reports and offers, not the served/published pointer's (which, under discipline, always reads `'published'` regardless of an in-flight draft). Passing the working copy explicitly also means the controller's own resolve trivially agrees with `TransitionService`'s internal one, so an ordinary request never hits this endpoint's own `RevisionConflictException` → 409 mapping (a genuine concurrent-transition race still can). (4) **`GenericAdminSurfaceHost::get()`** serves the working copy, unconditionally, to any account with entity UPDATE access (documented as "unconditional for editors" rather than a query-param opt-in: the admin SPA's single edit-surface GET backs both its view and edit client-side sub-modes with no per-request signal to gate a param on) — **no SPA/TypeScript code change**, the transport contract is unchanged and the server decides transparently; `handleUpdate()` needed no code change either, since it already delegates entirely to `JsonApiController::update()`. (5) **SSR preview** (`?preview=1`) now renders the working copy, but ONLY when a SECOND `EditorialVisibilityResolver::canRender()` call — re-evaluated against the working copy, not the served entity — actually authorizes it; gating on the primary (served-entity) `canRender()` result alone would leak the draft to anonymous `?preview=1` requests, since that check short-circuits to Allowed the instant the served entity reports `'published'`, which it always does under discipline regardless of an in-flight draft. The public (non-preview) render path is byte-untouched (pinned: an anonymous render of a mid-draft node equals the pre-draft response). (6) **`FieldAutoSaveController`** and GraphQL **`EntityResolver::resolveUpdate()`** gain the identical working-copy save-target pattern (same echo-comparison-basis fix as (1) for GraphQL). New HTTP-level draft-window byte-stability oracle (`packages/api/tests/Integration/WorkingCopyPointerAwarenessFlowTest.php`, real Node + real `NodeServiceProvider`/`WorkflowServiceProvider` wiring, real `JsonApiController`): publish → anonymous GET → forward-draft edit via a validated `TransitionService` save → anonymous GET byte-identical → `?workingCopy=1` as the editor serves the draft → PATCH as the editor lands on the tip (`loadRevision()` proves it; the published row is raw-SQL-unchanged) → promote → anonymous GET now serves the new content; companion coverage in `WorkflowTransitionControllerWorkingCopyTest`, `SsrPreviewWorkingCopyTest`, `FieldAutoSaveControllerWorkingCopyTest`, `EntityResolverWorkingCopyTest`, and `GenericAdminSurfaceHostWorkingCopyTest`. `docs/specs/content-workflow.md`'s "Read-side pointer awareness" deferral bullet is marked fully CLOSED (engine half: PR-2; surface half: this PR); see `docs/specs/api-layer.md` "GET single"/"PATCH — update" (updated) and "Workflow Transition Endpoints" (tip-state semantics), and `docs/specs/admin-spa.md` (`GenericAdminSurfaceHost::get()` note).
- **`revise` (forward-draft entry edge) restored to the shipped `editorial` workflow (#1920, CW-v1 option-1 forward-draft rebuild PR-6, design §7).** `DefaultWorkflows::EDITORIAL` regains `revise: { label: 'Create new draft', from: [published], to: draft, permission: 'use editorial transition revise' }` (Drupal editorial's "Create New Draft"), pulled from the shipped workflow during the WP-2 rework descope (findings #1/#2/#4/#5/#11: no read path was pointer-aware, so a forward draft's unreviewed tip content leaked publicly while `status`/pointer said "published") and sanctioned again now that option-1 PRs 1–3 make the read side pointer-aware BY CONSTRUCTION (the base row holds the published revision — `find()`/JSON:API/SSR/search-index readers serve it without per-reader patching, nothing left to leak). **`DefaultWorkflowsTest`'s structural pin test — which used to FORBID any shipped transition entering a `default_revision: false` state from a `published: true` state — is INVERTED in this same PR, deliberately, not deleted and not worked around:** it now asserts `revise` exists with exactly this shape, so a future regression that silently drops the edge is caught the same way the old pin caught a silent reintroduction. Upgraded installs receive the edge automatically at next boot via the existing additive top-up (`WorkflowServiceProvider::seedDefaultEditorialWorkflow()`, machine-name keyed, the `restore_to_published` top-up's precedent — `DefaultWorkflowSeedTopUpTest` gains a fixture pinning a pre-PR-6 six-transition persisted `editorial` and proves the top-up adds only `revise`, leaving the six existing transitions untouched). The permission string `use editorial transition revise` is auto-derived by `Workflow::permissionFor()` — no `PermissionProvider`/catalog registration exists for any of the other six shipped transition permissions either, so none is needed here; **operators must grant it to roles explicitly** (it is not implied by any other permission). Not touched: `NodeAccessPolicy::PUBLISH_GATED_FIELDS` (gates `status`/`workflow_state` field writes, orthogonal to a workflow transition edge). New `packages/workflows/tests/Integration/ShippedEditorialForwardDraftFlowTest.php` proves the mechanics end-to-end on the REAL boot-seeded `editorial` workflow entity (not a test-local stand-in, unlike the pre-existing `ForwardDraftFlowTest` engine spine): create → publish → `revise` (base row byte-stable across every column; `find()` — the anonymous-visible read path — keeps serving the published title/state throughout the draft window) → `submit_for_review` → `publish` (promotion) → `archive` → `restore` (archived → draft, itself a forward-draft-style entry from a default-revision state) → `publish` again, closing the archived-recovery round trip (the underlying archived → published pointer move is authorized via the `restore_to_published` permission, not `publish`'s). `docs/specs/content-workflow.md`'s "Deferred: no `revise` edge ships on `editorial`" paragraph, the config-schema YAML example, the WP table, and all three deferred-follow-up themes are updated to CLOSED.
- **Default-revision discipline: workflow engine wiring — the storage substrate goes live (#1920, CW-v1 option-1 forward-draft rebuild PR-2).** PR-1 shipped the discipline flags dormant (no production caller set them); this PR wires them. `WorkflowStateGuard::onPreSave()` now sets `RevisionableEntityTrait::setDefaultRevisionDiscipline()` as an unconditional boolean (never set-on-true-only) on every guarded save, and its `guardUpdate()`'s original-state basis moves from the base row to `EntityRepositoryInterface::loadWorkingCopy()` (mechanically safe on any entity — undisciplined ones degenerate to `find()`), fixing a spurious `published → draft` re-validation on every disciplined forward-draft edit. **Named behavior change (invariant 5):** a disciplined, revision-creating, SAME-state save into a `default_revision: true` state (a plain content PATCH on already-published content, e.g.) now requires the same any-of authorization `WorkflowPointerMoveGuard`'s same-state branch already used for pointer moves — because under discipline, changing what serves IS publishing. The rule is UNCONDITIONAL over revision-creating AND in-place saves (fix wave, adversarial review): gating only revision-creating saves let a `new_revision: false` bundle's in-place save of the published tip reach the served base row with no workflow permission checked (the storage `writeBase` rule) — the unconditional gate correctly also covers `TransitionService`'s own status-flip save and sanctioned in-place published edits, which pass because the acting account holds the fired transition's permission (a transition INTO the target state). On authorization of a REVISION-CREATING save the guard arms a new `Waaseyaa\Workflows\Republish\RepublishMarker` (a `\WeakMap` keyed on the entity object, arm-at-PRE_SAVE / consume-at-POST_SAVE so the promote branch's own first save is never misclassified as same-state; the guard also clears any stale arm unconditionally at the start of every guarded save, so a PRE_SAVE-aborted save can never leave an arm a later save consumes); a new `Waaseyaa\Workflows\Listener\WorkflowRepublishListener` (POST_SAVE) consumes the marker and republishes the just-saved tip through `setPublishedRevision()` — self-skipping when the base row's live `published_revision_id` already equals the saved revision (a spurious arm is possible in the guard-first PRE_SAVE listener order, and promoting the already-served revision would re-fire pointer events/audit/reindex for a no-op). **Doctrine inversion, named:** "raw saves never enact pointer moves" becomes "raw saves never enact STATE-CHANGING pointer moves; an authorized same-state edit of a default-revision state re-publishes through the choke point." `WorkflowPointerMoveGuard::onBeforePointerMove()` now sets the event's discipline flag (`publish`: always when bound; `rollback`: only when a live published pointer already exists) and denies `revert` (`setCurrentRevision()`) outright on a bound entity with a live published pointer — a direct base-row repoint has no coherent meaning once the base row belongs to the published pointer; callers are directed to `rollback` (into the working copy) or the transition path. `rollback()` under discipline therefore becomes pure draft-restore: the restored content lands in a new tip revision only, the base row (still serving whatever the published pointer points at) stays byte-unchanged — a conscious inversion of the pre-option-1 "base row carries the restored content" assertion. `TransitionService::transition()` now resolves `fromState` from the working copy (not the passed object, which may be a stale `find()`-loaded snapshot under discipline) and enforces a deterministic content rule: the passed entity's content rides the transition only when its revision id matches the working copy's, otherwise it throws `RevisionConflictException` rather than silently discarding or promoting stale content. Two-axis (revisionable + translatable) entity types can no longer be bound to a workflow at all — `WorkflowBindingResolver::resolve()` hard-throws (mirroring its existing non-revisionable throw); a new pure-function `Waaseyaa\Workflows\Validation\WorkflowAssignmentsValidator` exposes the same two checks for a future config-import surface to call (none exists yet — a documented gap, not a regression: `ConfigImporter` has no domain-validation hook for this settings-shaped config item today). `SearchIndexSubscriber` and `EntityEmbeddingListener` now re-source from `repository->find()` (the served base row) on POST_SAVE, `RevisionPointerMovedEvent`, and the legacy `REVISION_REVERTED`, instead of indexing the in-memory saved/reverted entity — this closes the previously-documented de-index bug (editing published content into a forward draft used to delete its AI-vector embedding) WITHOUT the previously-sketched `WorkflowVisibility` precedence flip, which is retired (`WorkflowVisibility`'s existing precedence and its pinned test stay untouched). `TransitionDeniedException`/`RevisionConflictException` are newly mapped (403/422/409) on `FieldAutoSaveController`, GraphQL `resolveCreate()`/`resolveUpdate()` (as a client-safe `GraphQL\Error\UserError` — an unmapped denial was previously masked by webonyx/graphql-php as a generic "Internal server error"), and `WorkflowTransitionController`'s POST (`RevisionConflictException` → 409); `GenericAdminSurfaceHost` needed no code change — it already delegated entirely to `JsonApiController`, which mapped both before this PR (pinned by a new regression test). The rewritten `packages/workflows/tests/Integration/ForwardDraftFlowTest.php` is the option-1 integration spine: create → publish → forward-draft edit (`find()` byte-stable — the full raw base row, not spot-checked — while `loadWorkingCopy()` serves the draft) → submit for review (still byte-stable) → publish the draft (promotion) → an authorized same-state raw edit (auto-republish) → the same shape unauthorized (denied, nothing committed) → archive → rollback under discipline (revision-only, base row byte-unchanged) → revert denied — with a full audit-trail assertion. `docs/specs/content-workflow.md` gains "Default-revision discipline (CW-v1 option-1, #1920 PR-2 — as-built)"; the "Read-side pointer awareness" deferral finding is split into its ENGINE half (closed here) and its SURFACE half (PR-3); "Visibility (read side)" is rewritten around the retired precedence-flip plan.
- **Default-revision discipline storage substrate — dormant (#1920, CW-v1 option-1 forward-draft rebuild PR-1).** The WP-2 rework deferred forward drafts because no read path was pointer-aware: a forward draft's unreviewed tip content was served by `find()`-based readers while `status`/pointer still said "published" (`docs/specs/content-workflow.md` "Deferred: forward drafts on the shipped workflow"). Option 1 fixes this by construction — the base row holds the published (default) revision, drafts live only in revision rows — but a naive "published pointer present → revision-only saves" storage rule would break every install pointered-but-unbound (Playbook H steps 1–4 without binding). This PR ships the storage mechanics only, honored by two new transient, workflow-layer-set flags, **wired to nothing yet** (the workflows engine sets them in the next PR): `RevisionableEntityTrait::setDefaultRevisionDiscipline()`/`isDefaultRevisionDisciplined()` (per-save entity flag, mirrors the existing `$newRevision`) and `BeforeRevisionPointerMoveEvent::applyDefaultRevisionSemantics()`/`defaultRevisionSemantics()` (per-operation event flag). `EntityRepository::doSave()` honors the entity flag: a disciplined revision-creating save writes the revision row only (base row, `revision_id` advance, and bundle-subtable upsert all skipped); a disciplined in-place save reaches the base row only when the revision being updated equals the base row's live `published_revision_id`. `setPublishedRevision()` becomes a complete promotion primitive under the event flag — base row overwritten from the target revision's full values, both pointers moved to the target, one transaction — instead of today's targeted single-column pointer UPDATE (unflagged: byte-identical, `EntityRepositoryPublishedRevisionTest` untouched). `rollback()` goes revision-only under the event flag (no base write/repoint). Pruning/deletion immortality (`pruneRevisions()`, `RevisionableStorageDriver::deleteRevision()`) is extended to also exclude the LATEST revision, alongside the existing current+published pointer exclusions — needed because discipline decouples the base `revision_id` pointer from the tip. New `EntityRepositoryInterface::loadWorkingCopy(string $id): ?EntityInterface` returns the tip revision when it has diverged from the base pointer, otherwise `find()` — every in-repo implementor of the interface (including test doubles) gained the method in the same change. A stated `SaveContext::withExpectedRevisionId()` on a disciplined save throws `\LogicException` (new optimistic-locking rejection-matrix row): the guarded claim is a base-pointer UPDATE, structurally meaningless when the base pointer must not move. Undisciplined behavior — including for pointered-but-unbound entities — is byte-identical to before this PR; `docs/specs/revision-system-unified.md` gains §7 "Default-revision discipline (CW-v1 option-1)" documenting all of the above mechanically.
- **Workflow transition API endpoints, admin SPA transition UI, and the membership write surface (#1920, CW-v1 WP-4).** The content-workflow engine gets its user-facing surface. (1) **API**: `GET /api/{type}/{id}/workflow/transitions` (what `_account` may fire from the entity's current state — exactly `TransitionService::getAvailableTransitions()`, the one sanctioned UI read side, permission- AND group-filtered — plus `meta.workflow_state`) and `POST /api/{type}/{id}/workflow/transition` (body `{"transition": "publish"}`), registered per entity type with `requireAuthentication()`, served by new `Waaseyaa\Api\Controller\WorkflowTransitionController` via `WorkflowTransitionApiRouter`, and registered only when `resolveOptional(TransitionService::class)` resolves — an install without `waaseyaa/workflows` wired gets neither routes nor router and 404s naturally instead of crashing boot. Entity view access is enforced **in the controller** under the R8 oracle standard: a view-denied entity returns the same canonical 404 document, byte-identical, as a missing id (one private factory serves both branches, pinned by a `json_encode`-equality test; fails closed 404 when no `EntityAccessHandler` is wired) — a deliberate deviation from the spec's original `_gate` sketch, since a route-option gate's 403 would be an existence oracle. `TransitionDeniedException` keeps the WP-2 HTTP contract on the new endpoint: `permission` → 403, every other reason (`illegal_edge`, `unknown_transition`, `unbound`, `group_constraint`) → 422, `code: WORKFLOW_TRANSITION_DENIED` + `meta.reason`. The WP-3 ambient-vs-explicit account caveat is satisfied structurally on this surface (`SessionMiddleware` unconditionally syncs the ambient `AccountContextInterface` to `_account` on every HTTP request), pinned by a real-SQLite end-to-end test on the real `WorkflowServiceProvider`; the multi-edge denied-promotion residue caveat applies verbatim through the endpoint (a POST can 422 from the pointer guard after its revision-creating save committed). (2) **Admin SPA**: new `useWorkflowTransitions` composable and `<WorkflowTransitionControls>` (`components/workflow/TransitionControls.vue`) on the entity view surface — one button per available transition, inline JSON:API `errors[0].detail` on denial, entity re-fetch on success, and a GET 404 (missing or unviewable) rendering no buttons rather than an error, consistent with the R8 contract; `workflow_state` renders as a status-pill badge in `SchemaList` listings (schema column, or a synthetic trailing column when entities carry the attribute the column set omits); en+fr i18n; prebuilt `packages/admin-surface/dist` rebuilt in the same PR (D6 freshness gate). (3) **Membership write surface** (the WP-3 "hand-crafted relationship rows" follow-up, scoped into WP-4 so department routing is operable end-to-end — the group constraint is fail-closed, so a transition UI without membership tooling would ship a dead surface): `GroupMembershipService::addMember()/removeMember()/assignContent()/unassignContent()`, idempotent and soft-revoke-aware (add/assign reactivate a `status = 0` row or no-op on a live one, never duplicating; remove/unassign soft-revoke and never delete; add/assign throw `\InvalidArgumentException` on a nonexistent target group, revokes tolerate one), plus operator CLI `groups:create` (ensures the `group_type` bundle config entity exists first), `groups:member-add`, `groups:member-remove`, `groups:content-assign`, `groups:content-unassign` (new `Waaseyaa\CLI\Provider\GroupsCliServiceProvider`; `cli` gains the downward `waaseyaa/groups` composer edge). Still out of scope, unchanged: the `group` JSON:API access PARK (#1871), admin SPA membership management, temporal-window evaluation, and wiring `RelationshipPreSaveListener` into production boot.
- **Group (department) transition constraints on the content-workflow engine (#1920, CW-v1 WP-3).** A workflow transition may now carry `group_constraint: content_groups`, validated at import against `WorkflowTransition::GROUP_CONSTRAINT_CONTENT_GROUPS` (the only v1 kind — any other value is a `WorkflowValidator` config violation). Membership and content-department assignment are plain `relationship` rows, not a bespoke join table: new `Waaseyaa\Groups\GroupRelationshipTypes` (`group_membership`, directed `user`/{uid} → `group`/{gid}; `group_content`, directed `{entityType}`/{id} → `group`/{gid}) and `Waaseyaa\Groups\Membership\GroupMembershipService` (`waaseyaa/groups`, mirroring `GenealogyFamilyService::memberPersonIds()`, system-context `->accessCheck(false)` lookups, scalar uid/entity-id arguments so `groups` does not need to require `access`) expose `groupIdsForUser()`/`groupIdsForContent()`/`isMemberOfAny()`, each filtered to live (`status = 1`) relationship rows only — a soft-revoked membership or content-group row never counts, mirroring `RelationshipTraversalService`'s own status filtering (temporal windows are out of v1 scope). The constraint is enforced at all four sites that can change or reveal a workflow state, in the same fixed order everywhere — permission checked before the group gate, never the reverse, so an account holding neither is denied with reason `permission`, not `group_constraint`, even when the account is itself a department member: `TransitionService::transition()` (new `TransitionDeniedException::REASON_GROUP_CONSTRAINT`), `TransitionService::getAvailableTransitions()` (filters group-denied transitions so UIs never offer a button the write side would refuse), `WorkflowStateGuard::guardUpdate()` (raw-`PATCH` parity, checked only when an acting account context exists — a null context stays edge-legality-only, unchanged from WP-2), and both branches of `WorkflowPointerMoveGuard` (the different-state branch checks the edge's own constraint; the same-state any-of rule over transitions into a state now requires permission AND satisfied constraint per candidate, with constraint-less candidates still counting on permission alone, and a same-state total-failure denial keeping `REASON_PERMISSION` by design). Fail-closed throughout (design invariant 5): content with no recorded `group_content` row can never satisfy any constraint, and an unrecognised constraint kind denies rather than degrading to unconstrained. All four sites take an optional `?GroupConstraintChecker`, following the same syntactic convention as `TransitionService`'s existing optional `dispatcher`/`auditWriter` — but null now fails **closed**: a transition with a non-null `group_constraint` is denied when the checker is null (unconstrained transitions are unaffected); production wiring (`WorkflowServiceProvider`) always injects a real one via `resolveOptional()`, so this only changes the failure mode of a wiring regression, from silently un-gating to loudly denying. `workflows` (L3) gains two new downward composer edges, `waaseyaa/groups` and `waaseyaa/relationship` (both L2). New `packages/workflows/tests/Integration/DepartmentRoutingFlowTest.php` proves the full client story — department routing — end-to-end on the REAL `Node` entity type through the real `NodeServiceProvider`/`RelationshipServiceProvider`/`GroupsServiceProvider`/`WorkflowServiceProvider` wiring: a dept_a author creates and submits a node for review; a dept_b-only decisionmaker holding the `publish` permission is denied `group_constraint` with persisted state proven unchanged; `getAvailableTransitions` excludes `publish` for that decisionmaker and includes it for a dept_a decisionmaker; a dept_a member lacking the `publish` permission is denied `permission` (pinning gate-order precedence end-to-end); the dept_a decisionmaker then publishes, and a full audit-trail assertion covers all four `TransitionService` calls. The test-local `editorial_dept` workflow deliberately keeps `draft` in `publish`'s `from[]` (the shipped `editorial` shape) — `docs/specs/content-workflow.md`'s new "Group constraints (WP-3)" section documents this as a **first-publish shape requirement**: a group-routed workflow whose `publish` is reachable only via an intermediate state (no `draft → published` edge) wedges on first publish regardless of group constraints, a known WP-2 gap the option-1 forward-draft follow-up owns, not WP-3.
- **Bundle-aware `create` subject through `EntityAccessGate`, so per-bundle create permissions now work on the import path (#1946).** #1940's G-025/G-014 series left a documented gap: `EntityAccessGate::allows('create', string $entityTypeId, ...)` hardcoded bundle `''` when calling `EntityAccessHandler::checkCreateAccess()` — the adapter's own `@todo` — so a permission like `'create article content'` (`NodeAccessPolicy::createAccess()`) or `'create terms in tags'` (`TermAccessPolicy`) could never match through the gate `EntityDestination` consults, and `MigrationSystemAccount`'s docblock explicitly warned callers off per-bundle permissions as import-account grants. `EntityAccessGate::allows()` now also accepts an array subject shaped `['entity_type' => string, 'bundle' => string]` for the `'create'` ability, forwarding straight into `checkCreateAccess($entityType, $bundle, $user)`; the pre-existing bundle-less string-subject path (bundle `''`) is unchanged for backward compatibility, and a malformed array subject falls through to the existing warn+deny path. `EntityDestination` gains a private `buildCreateSubject()` that reads the destination entity's bundle-key value (populated from `DestinationRecord::$bundle`, applied earlier in `applyValuesToEntity()`) and passes the array subject when the destination entity type declares a bundle key and the value is non-empty, falling back to the original string subject otherwise. New `packages/migration/tests/Integration/EntityDestinationBundleAwareCreateAccessTest.php` proves the whole real stack (`EntityAccessGate` + `EntityAccessHandler` + `NodeAccessPolicy`, no fixture policies, real `EntityDestination` write): a `MigrationSystemAccount` holding only `['create article content']` (no `administer content`/`administer nodes`) is granted create for `node`/`article` and denied for `node`/`page`. `MigrationSystemAccount`'s docblock and `docs/specs/access-control.md`/`docs/specs/migration-platform.md` §7.5 are rewritten to document both subject forms — the entity-type/group-wide admin trio (`administer content`/`administer taxonomy`/`administer media`) remains the correct grant for bundle-less migration definitions.
- **`ContentModelRegistrar` is blessed and wired as the one supported path for import-derived per-bundle content models; invoked at import time, not kernel boot (#1940, G-026).** `ContentModelRegistrar`/`ContentModel`/`ContentTypeModel`/`DerivesContentModelInterface` shipped marked "UNWIRED / EXPERIMENTAL (audit C-5)" — no service provider bound the registrar and no kernel discovery dispatched the interface. The Sheguiandah pass-1 build (read-only reference) worked around this by constructing `ContentModelRegistrar` by hand inside `SfnWordPressMigrationProvider::boot()`, which runs during `AbstractKernel::bootProviders()`'s schema-sync phase — before that provider's own destination tables were guaranteed to exist — so registration silently no-op'd on the first boot after `db:init`. New `Waaseyaa\Foundation\ServiceProvider\Capability\AcceptsContentModelProvidersInterface` (mirrors `AcceptsMigrationProvidersInterface`) and `AbstractKernel::injectContentModelProviders()` collect `DerivesContentModelInterface` providers at boot — collection only, no invocation, same as the migration-provider capability bus. `Waaseyaa\Migration\ServiceProvider` binds `ContentModelRegistrar` as a lazy singleton and threads the collected providers into `MigrationRunner`, which invokes `deriveContentModel()` and feeds the result through the registrar in a new `ensureContentModelsRegistered()`, guarded to run exactly once per runner instance (a container singleton, i.e. once per CLI process), immediately before the first migration of the invocation — after full kernel boot, so the destination schema always exists first. Failure semantics changed to match: `ContentModelRegistrar::register()` now raises the new `Waaseyaa\Migration\Exception\ContentModelRegistrationException` on any structural failure (unregistered destination entity type, bundle config entity that cannot be saved, missing field registry, a rejected field) instead of swallowing it behind a log line — the notice/error-and-continue behaviour only existed to survive a boot-time invocation that no longer happens. The only silent paths left are genuine no-ops: an already-registered bundle (idempotency) and a destination entity type with no `bundleEntityType` declared. `docs/specs/migration-platform.md` gains §9.5 documenting the full contract, including the boundary against the static `#[BundleTemplate]`/`#[FieldTemplate]` app-model path (`docs/specs/work-surface.md` §F2, which gains a one-paragraph cross-reference) — both terminate at the same `EntityTypeManager`/`FieldDefinitionRegistry` substrate; this is not a third field-declaration mechanism, just a later-arriving source of truth. New `packages/migration/tests/Integration/ContentModelRegistrationEndToEndTest.php` reproduces the pass-1 sequence against a fresh SQLite database and proves the whole chain — bundle config entity, declared typed field, and a real destination write into that field — succeeds in one import invocation, with no second-boot requirement.

### Fixed

- **First-publish pointer-establishment rule closes the review-required-workflow wedge (#1920, CW-v1 option-1 forward-draft rebuild PR-5, final-review finding #5).** `WorkflowPointerMoveGuard::currentlyEffectiveState()` fell back to the workflow's `initial_state` whenever a `publish` pointer move had no prior published pointer (`fromRevisionId === null` — every first publish), then ran the strict different-state edge rule against that fabricated state. A review-required workflow (`publish` reachable only via an intermediate state, e.g. `draft → review → published` with no `draft → published` edge — precisely the shape CW-v1's department-routing use case is built for) permanently wedged its first publish with `TransitionDeniedException(REASON_ILLEGAL_EDGE)`, thrown from inside `TransitionService::transition()`'s promote branch AFTER the revision-creating save already committed. A `publish` pointer move with `fromRevisionId === null` is now recognized as pointer ESTABLISHMENT, not a state crossing — there is no prior pointer state, so the tip's state (already validated by the save-path guards when it was stamped) is authorized instead via the same any-of rule the guard's same-state branch already used: an authenticated account must hold the permission (and, per WP-3, satisfy the group constraint) of at least one transition INTO the target state; a null account context passes outright (edge-legality only — there is no edge to check). The shared any-of loop is factored into a new private `WorkflowPointerMoveGuard::authorizeAnyOfInto()`, used by both the same-state branch and the new establishment case; the strict different-state rule is byte-identical for every real pointer move (`fromRevisionId !== null`), including the pre-existing "prior revision could not be loaded" fallback to `initial_state` for `rollback`/`revert`. Soundness: a `TransitionService` first publish always passes establishment now (the fired transition's own permission+group were just validated, and the fired edge itself is always a satisfying any-of candidate) — this also dissolves the documented multi-edge "denied-promotion residue" caveat for first publishes specifically (it remains live for forward-draft promotions of an already-pointered entity, the different-state branch, unaffected here). Known benign quirk, pinned by test: an account holding only a transition INTO `draft` (e.g. `reject`) may establish a pointer onto a draft-stamped revision — harmless, since derived `status` stays `0` and nothing is publicly served. The WP-3 "First-publish shape note" constraint (a group-routed workflow must keep an `initial_state → published` edge) is removed from `docs/specs/content-workflow.md` — `DepartmentRoutingFlowTest`'s `editorial_dept` keeps `draft` in `publish`'s `from[]` regardless, matching the shipped `editorial` shape, but that is no longer mandatory. New `packages/workflows/tests/Integration/FirstPublishEstablishmentFlowTest.php` proves a strict `draft → review → published` workflow (no `draft → published` edge) first-publishes end-to-end on the real `Node` entity type, including the WP-3 group-constrained shape (a department member succeeds; a non-member is denied `group_constraint` at `TransitionService`'s own gate, before establishment is ever reached), plus the direct `setPublishedRevision()` denial/null-context/benign-quirk unit and integration coverage.
- **`bin/check-dispatcher-keys` widened to the full three-FQCN served set; a shipped regression this exposed in `MediaServiceProvider::boot()` is re-parked (#1946, follow-up to #1942/G-025).** #1942 widened `ProviderRegistryKernelServices::get()` to serve the event dispatcher under three FQCNs — Symfony contracts, PSR-14, and the Waaseyaa-owned foundation contract (`Waaseyaa\Foundation\Event\EventDispatcherInterface`, guarded by `instanceof`, satisfied by every kernel's `SymfonyEventDispatcherAdapter` binding) — but `bin/check-dispatcher-keys`'s `SERVED_DISPATCHER_FQCN` constant still recognized only the Symfony-contracts FQCN, so the gate kept flagging (or exempting via a now-stale baseline comment) foundation-FQCN resolutions as dead. The constant is now `SERVED_DISPATCHER_FQCNS`, a three-entry served set the scanner checks membership against; `tests/Integration/Phase31/DispatcherKeysGateTest.php`'s foundation-FQCN fixtures are flipped from expect-fail to expect-pass (renamed accordingly), and its "genuinely unserved" fixtures (baseline/no-comment cases) are switched to the Symfony **Component** FQCN, the one dispatcher-interface FQCN the bus still does not serve. Investigating the sole pre-existing baseline entry (`packages/media/src/MediaServiceProvider.php:89`, parked pending #1742's versioned-blob-media cascade-delete finish-or-park decision) under the new served set found it was no longer dead: `MediaServiceProvider::boot()` resolves the foundation FQCN via `resolveOptional()`, which #1942 turned into a live non-null resolution in every production kernel boot, silently activating `MediaCascadeDeleteSubscriber`/`MediaVersionStorageDriver` — a shipped regression, not a deliberate reactivation. `boot()` now re-parks the subscriber wiring with an unconditional early `return;` (the prior wiring is preserved as a comment for #1742's eventual finish-or-park work) so the parking no longer depends on which FQCN the bus does or does not serve; the stale baseline entry is removed since no offender pattern remains.

### Documentation

- **Docs drift: `migrate:up` corrected to the live `migrate` command name (#1966, CW-v1 WP-5 cleanup).** The CLI registers `migrate` (plus `migrate:rollback`/`migrate:status`/`migrate:defaults`) — see `packages/cli/src/Provider/MigrateServiceProvider.php` — but `docs/cookbook/migration-first-cut.md`, `docs/upgrades/waaseyaa-alpha-177-to-178.md`, and a doc comment in `packages/oidc/migrations/2026_04_26_000001_oidc_client_schema.php` still referenced a nonexistent `migrate:up` command. No CLI alias was added; only the docs were corrected. `docs/specs/operations-playbooks.md` was already fixed in #1920 PR-7; historical `CHANGELOG.md` entries, `kitty-specs/` archives, and `docs/history/` remain untouched as they record what was written at the time.

- **Production node→editorial binding un-gated: Playbook H rewritten with the real binding procedure (#1920, CW-v1 option-1 PR-7, design §8 — docs-only, the final PR in the option-1 forward-draft rebuild).** `docs/specs/operations-playbooks.md` Playbook H step 5 ("Do NOT add the `workflows.assignments` binding in production") is replaced with the real procedure, now that PRs 1–6 close every finding the old deferral named: (1) precondition — the option-1 release deployed, steps 1–4 unchanged and still required in order; (2) grant transition permissions to roles, including `revise` (PR-6), and two option-1-specific rules operators must plan for — a same-state edit of already-published content now requires the permission of a transition INTO `published` (`publish`/`restore_to_published`), not merely entity `update` access, because under discipline changing what serves publicly IS publishing (design §3.1); the identical any-of rule applies to in-place edits of archived content via the `archive` permission (design finding A6); (3) add the `workflows.assignments` binding; (4) a post-bind HTTP verification probe — publish → `revise` → edit the draft tip → anonymous GET stays byte-stable (old title, `status=1`) → `?workingCopy=1` as the editor serves the new title → publish → anonymous GET now serves the new content — given as exact `curl` commands against the real, confirmed JSON:API/workflow-transition routes (`packages/api/src/JsonApiRouteProvider.php`, `WorkflowTransitionApiRouter`) and the real `/api/auth/login` route, mirroring `WorkingCopyPointerAwarenessFlowTest`; (5) a rollback procedure, verified against `loadWorkingCopy()`'s actual binding-agnostic implementation rather than assumed — removing the binding stops `WorkflowStateGuard` from ever setting the discipline flag (byte-identical to unbound behavior going forward), but an in-flight draft's revision row is never deleted by un-binding and stays recoverable via `loadWorkingCopy()`/`rollback()` until a subsequent undisciplined save supersedes it as "latest." The "Evaluation-binding caveat" is removed (closed structurally by PR-4's write-side field allowlist); failure mode 1 gains an option-1 clarification (discipline only engages once an entity is both bound AND pointered, so bind-before-backfill stays byte-identical to pre-option-1 until the pointer is established); failure mode 2 gains the two-axis (translatable+revisionable) hard-throw case PR-2 introduced. `docs/specs/content-workflow.md`'s status header flips DESIGN → LIVE, the WP-2 table row gains the PR-7 mention, a stale forward-reference to Playbook H as "a separate follow-up PR" is corrected, and "Deferred: forward drafts on the shipped workflow" is condensed into a short historical record (findings-by-theme and closing-PR/test references kept as evidence, duplicate mechanism detail trimmed since it now lives in "Default-revision discipline" above). Verified before writing "production binding SANCTIONED": the option-1 integration spine — `ForwardDraftFlowTest`, `ShippedEditorialForwardDraftFlowTest`, `FirstPublishEstablishmentFlowTest` (`packages/workflows/tests/Integration/`), and the PR-3 HTTP oracle `WorkingCopyPointerAwarenessFlowTest` (`packages/api/tests/Integration/`) — green on main (`./vendor/bin/phpunit --testsuite Integration -d memory_limit=1G --filter '(ForwardDraftFlowTest|ShippedEditorialForwardDraftFlowTest|WorkingCopyPointerAwarenessFlowTest|FirstPublishEstablishmentFlowTest)'`: 12 tests, 232 assertions, 0 failures).


## [0.1.0-alpha.259] - 2026-07-10

### Added

- **First-class `slug` field on `Waaseyaa\Taxonomy\Term` (#1940, G-019).** The Sheguiandah pass-1 WordPress import had no destination field for WordPress term slugs — `Term` exposed only `name` (the display label) and had nothing URL-safe for a migration to write into, so imported term slugs were silently dropped. `Term` gains a nullable `#[Field(type: 'string', ...)]` `slug` property plus `getSlug()`/`setSlug()` accessors, mirroring the existing `description` field's shape and constructor-default pattern. `taxonomy_term` uses the default sql-blob storage backend, and — like `description`/`weight`/`parent_id`/`status` before it — the new field is NOT given an explicit database column: `SqlSchemaHandler::buildTableSpec()` only emits `id`/`uuid`/`bundle`/`label`/`langcode`/revision-pointer columns plus the generic `_data` JSON blob for this entity type (`docs/specs/bundle-scoped-storage.md` — `taxonomy_term` "continues using flat-table storage with all fields on the base," no per-field columns), so `slug` rides the same `_data` blob every other Term field already does; no schema migration is needed for existing installs.
- **First-class, least-privilege `MigrationSystemAccount` for production migration runs (#1940, G-023).** The Sheguiandah pass-1 WordPress import found no production-safe account existed for batch imports: `EntityDestination`'s nullable `$account` (FR-020) is consulted by the access gate on every create/update/delete, but the only account that ever passed it was `Waaseyaa\User\DevAdminAccount` — explicitly dev-only (constructor throws outside `cli`/`cli-server`/`frankenphp` SAPIs, docblock says MUST NOT be used in production, and `hasPermission()` blanket-returns `true`). New `Waaseyaa\Migration\Account\MigrationSystemAccount` (`packages/migration/src/Account/MigrationSystemAccount.php`) implements `AccountInterface` with `hasPermission()` as a strict membership test against an injected `list<string> $permissions` (default `DEFAULT_PERMISSIONS = ['administer content']` — exactly what `ContentAdminAccessPolicy` requires to grant manage + create on content-group entity types), a stable string sentinel `id()` (`'migration:system'`, never colliding with an integer auto-increment uid, `AnonymousUser`'s `0`, or `DevAdminAccount`'s `PHP_INT_MAX`), and no SAPI guard or login surface — it is a plain value object constructed by import wiring. The pre-existing allow-all `packages/migration/tests/Fixtures/MigrationSystemAccount.php` test double is deleted; all six of its consumers (`EntityDestinationTest`, `EntityDestinationRevisionsTest`, `ReferenceDestinationConformanceTest`, `RollbackTest`, `EndToEndCsvImportTest`, `BundleThreadingEndToEndTest`) now construct the production class instead, proving it serves real import runs. New `packages/migration/tests/Integration/EntityDestinationSystemAccountAccessTest.php` wires a REAL `EntityAccessGate` + `EntityAccessHandler` + `ContentAdminAccessPolicy` (no fixture policy) and asserts a default `MigrationSystemAccount` is granted `create` while `new MigrationSystemAccount([])` is denied with `DestinationWriteException` reason `entity_create_denied`.

### Fixed

- **`MigrationRegistry` builds definitions lazily on first query instead of eagerly at kernel boot (#1940, G-024).** The Sheguiandah pass-1 first production import needed a bespoke lazy-gate shim to work around a kernel-boot crash: `AbstractKernel::boot()` runs `injectMigrationProviders()` → `bootProviders()` → `discoverAccessPolicies()`, in that order, but the migration package's `ServiceProvider::boot()` eagerly resolved `MigrationRegistry`, whose singleton factory called `$registry->boot()`, which invoked every provider's `HasMigrationsInterface::migrations()` — during `bootProviders()`, strictly BEFORE `discoverAccessPolicies()` built the `EntityAccessHandler`. A migration provider whose `migrations()` resolved `GateInterface`/`EntityAccessHandler` off the kernel-services bus to construct an `EntityDestination` plugin therefore crashed boot with `RuntimeException: No binding registered for Waaseyaa\Access\Gate\GateInterface`. `MigrationRegistry` (`packages/migration/src/Discovery/MigrationRegistry.php`) now builds its index lazily: every public query method (`get()`, `has()`, `all()`, `topologicallySorted()`, `graph()`) routes through a new private `ensureBooted()` that runs the existing `boot()` logic exactly once, on first call; `boot()` itself stays public and callers that already call it explicitly are unaffected (a second explicit call still raises `\LogicException`, unchanged). `ServiceProvider::register()`'s `MigrationRegistry` singleton factory no longer calls `boot()`, and `ServiceProvider::boot()` no longer resolves the registry at all — the CLI `import:*` commands already resolve the registry lazily per-command (`ImportServiceProvider`), so the first real query now happens at command execution, after the whole kernel — including access services — is live. Trade-off, accepted deliberately: structural manifest errors (missing dependency, plugin id collision, dependency cycle) no longer fail fast at framework boot; they now surface at the first CLI invocation that queries the registry instead. `docs/specs/migration-platform.md` §9 rewritten for the lazy contract.
- **Split fan-out verification no longer races `main` (#1934).** `verify-monorepo-main-intact` in `.github/workflows/split.yml` previously required live `main` to equal the tagged `github.sha` exactly, so any PR merged to `main` mid-fan-out failed the release even though nothing was wrong (v0.1.0-alpha.257: PR #1933 merged between the tag and the check, tag at `f0cf33c2d`, main at `b4630eee9`). The check now verifies the tagged SHA is still an ancestor of `main` via `gh api .../compare/${EXPECTED_SHA}...main` — `identical`/`ahead` pass (main only moved forward), `behind`/`diverged` fail (main was rewritten/overwritten, the real self-split-collision signal this check exists to catch). All `split.yml` fan-out checkouts (`split`, `verify-tag-parity`, `verify-require-parity`, `publish-github-release`, `publish-packagist`) are now pinned to `ref: ${{ github.sha }}`, as is the framework checkout in `sync-skeleton.yml` and the tag-triggered checkout in `packagist-update.yml`.
- **Bot-token trigger suppression is now solved by chain-dispatch, not a PAT: `RELEASE_PAT` is abandoned (#1934, supersedes the #1937 fallback).** GitHub suppresses recursive workflow triggers on pushes/merges made with the default `GITHUB_TOKEN`, which silently skipped CI on `main` after `.github/workflows/auto-merge.yml` merged a PR and skipped CI on the `admin-dist/update` branch/PR that `.github/workflows/admin-dist.yml` pushes/opens. #1937 pointed both workflows at a `RELEASE_PAT` secret that was never provisioned (and won't be). Instead, both workflows now exploit the documented exemption that `workflow_dispatch` and `repository_dispatch` events *always* create workflow runs even when triggered by `GITHUB_TOKEN`: `auto-merge.yml` polls the labeled PR until GitHub's native auto-merge lands it (up to 2h), then dispatches `ci.yml` on the exact merge commit SHA with the plain default token; `admin-dist.yml` dispatches `ci.yml` on the `admin-dist/update` branch right after creating/updating its dist PR. `ci.yml`'s `workflow_dispatch` trigger gains an optional `sha` input threaded into every job's checkout (`ref: ${{ inputs.sha }}`, empty = tip of the dispatched ref), so the chain-dispatched run proves the exact merge commit even if `main` has since moved. All `secrets.RELEASE_PAT || …` fallbacks are removed; both workflows now hold `actions: write` to perform the dispatch.
- **Kernel-services bus binds a real `GateInterface` so consumers resolving it off the kernel no longer silently deny-all (#1940, G-014).** The Sheguiandah pass-1 first WordPress import run failed 512/512 with `entity_create_denied`: `ProviderRegistryKernelServices::get(GateInterface::class)` had no case at all, so it fell through the sibling-provider-bindings loop and returned `null`; consumers like `packages/listing/src/ServiceProvider.php::resolveGate()` treat a `null` bus resolution as "no host binding" and fall back to a deny-all `new Gate([])`, denying every access check. `ProviderRegistryKernelServices::get()` now resolves `Waaseyaa\Access\Gate\GateInterface` to a shared `EntityAccessGate` built from the kernel's `EntityAccessHandler`, memoized per handler instance. The binding must be lazy: the access handler is constructed by `AbstractKernel::discoverAccessPolicies()` after providers register and obtain the kernel-services bus, so the new case resolves the handler through the same lazy accessor already used for `EntityAccessHandler::class` and degrades to `null` (not a broken gate) when the accessor isn't wired yet — listing's deny-all fallback stays in place as defense in depth for that window and for hosts with no kernel context at all.
- **Kernel-services bus resolves the dispatcher under the PSR-14 and foundation-owned FQCNs, not just the Symfony-contracts one (#1940, G-025).** `Waaseyaa\Migration\Plugin\Destination\EntityDestinationFactory`/`EntityDestination` type-hint `Psr\EventDispatcher\EventDispatcherInterface` per their own docblock; `ProviderRegistryKernelServices::get()` had a case only for `Symfony\Contracts\EventDispatcher\EventDispatcherInterface`, so resolving the PSR-14 FQCN returned `null` and following the factory's own documented pattern threw `RuntimeException("No binding registered ...")` on the Sheguiandah pass-1 migration run. There is also a known latent variant, documented but not previously fixed: provider `boot()` code resolving the foundation contract `Waaseyaa\Foundation\Event\EventDispatcherInterface` got a silent `null` back instead of an exception, which is how the same class of bug shipped live in four providers (relationship's delete guard #1852, field's classification-label subscriber, two ai-observability telemetry providers). `ProviderRegistryKernelServices::get()` now additionally serves `Psr\EventDispatcher\EventDispatcherInterface` (statically guaranteed by the property's declared type, no guard needed) and `Waaseyaa\Foundation\Event\EventDispatcherInterface` (guarded by `instanceof`, since the property's declared type doesn't statically imply it) — all three FQCNs resolve to the exact same dispatcher instance. `bin/check-dispatcher-keys`'s `SERVED_DISPATCHER_FQCN` constant still only recognizes the Symfony-contracts FQCN as served; it is now stale (both newly-served FQCNs are legitimate resolutions post-fix) and is tracked as a follow-up rather than widened in this change.
- **`ValueCaster`'s `datetime_immutable` cast tolerates a `null` domain value without relying on the caller's guard (#1940, G-028).** The Sheguiandah pass-1 WordPress corpus produces records with no modified date at all, and the pass-1 site had to default those values itself before calling `set()`. `ValueCaster::castOut()` already short-circuits `null` for every builtin spec before dispatching to a type-specific caster, so `null` domain values for `datetime_immutable` casts (including the array form `['type' => 'datetime_immutable', 'storage' => 'unix']` used by Node's `created`/`changed` fields) were already returned as `null` through that public entry point. The private `castOutDateTimeImmutable()` helper itself, however, had no independent null guard — it relied entirely on the caller's check, so any future refactor of `castOut()`'s top-level guard (or a caller invoking the helper directly) would resurface the crash `normalizeDomainToDateTimeImmutable()` throws for non-`int`/`string`/`DateTimeInterface` domain values. `castOutDateTimeImmutable()`'s return type widens to `int|string|null` and now returns `null` immediately for a `null` `$domain`, making the helper null-safe on its own terms rather than by convention. New `ValueCasterTest::datetime_immutable_unix_storage_domain_null_does_not_throw` white-box-tests the private helper directly via `ReflectionMethod` (RED before the fix: `CastException` "Cannot cast domain value..."; GREEN after) since the public `castOut()` path was already passing. `docs/specs/entity-system.md` §"Cast kernel" gets a new "Null tolerance" paragraph documenting both the general `castIn`/`castOut` null guard and this helper-level defense-in-depth.
