- **`MigrationDefinition::$bundle` is now threaded into every `DestinationRecord` the runner builds, so imported entities no longer silently land with an empty bundle (#1940, G-015).** `DestinationRecord::$bundle` and `EntityDestination::applyValuesToEntity()` both already consumed a bundle slot, but nothing upstream ever populated it: `MigrationDefinition` had no bundle field at all, and `MigrationRunner::processOne()` built every `DestinationRecord` with only `migrationId`/`sourceId`/`values`. Every node/term/media entity imported by the Sheguiandah pass-1 WordPress migration therefore got an empty bundle, and the pass-1 site had to work around it with bespoke site-side bundle-assigning destination decorators instead of declaring the bundle once on the migration. `MigrationDefinition` now accepts an optional `?string $bundle = null` constructor parameter (validated null-or-non-empty, same as `DestinationRecord::$bundle`), and `MigrationRunner::processOne()` passes `bundle: $definition->bundle` when constructing each `DestinationRecord`. `EntityDestination`, `EntityDestinationFactory`, and `DestinationPluginInterface` are unchanged — the record slot was always the contract, the destination already honored it, only the plumbing to fill it was missing.
- **Adversarial-review follow-ups to the #1940 G-014/G-015/G-023/G-025 series: `EntityDestinationFactory` binding, corrected import-account permission guidance, bus-precedence and rerun-hash disclosures (#1940).** `Waaseyaa\Migration\Plugin\Destination\EntityDestinationFactory`'s own docblock instructed consumers to `$this->resolve(EntityDestinationFactory::class)->create(...)`, but no `ServiceProvider` ever bound it — every real call threw `RuntimeException("No binding registered ...")`. `packages/migration/src/ServiceProvider.php::register()` now binds it as a lazy singleton (resolving `EntityTypeManager`/`GateInterface`/the PSR-14 dispatcher/an optional logger at first-resolve time, after the gate is live — mirroring G-014's laziness), proven by new `packages/migration/tests/Integration/EntityDestinationFactoryBindingTest.php` (fails before access policies are discovered, then resolves and produces a working `EntityDestination` that completes a real write). Separately, `docs/specs/migration-platform.md` §7.5 and `MigrationSystemAccount`'s docblock previously recommended per-bundle create permissions (`create X content`, per-bundle taxonomy/media create) as least-privilege extras for import accounts — dead advice, since `EntityAccessGate::allows('create', ...)` hardcodes bundle `''` (its own `@todo`), so no per-bundle permission can ever match through the import gate. Both now name the working WordPress-shaped trio (`administer content` already covers `node`; `administer taxonomy` and `administer media` are needed for `taxonomy_term`/`media`, which sit outside the `content` group `ContentAdminAccessPolicy` governs) and warn that `MigrationSystemAccount` must never be installed as the kernel's ambient `AccountContext` — `EntityRepository::resolveActor()` casts the ambient account's `id()` to `int`, and `(int) 'migration:system'` is `0`, colliding with `AnonymousUser`'s sentinel. `docs/specs/infrastructure.md`'s kernel-services-bus section now discloses that the hardcoded bus cases (including `GateInterface`) always shadow a sibling provider's own binding of the same abstract inside `ProviderRegistryKernelServices::get()` — a host provider cannot use the bus to make its own `GateInterface` visible to siblings. `docs/specs/migration-platform.md` §7.2 now discloses the G-015 rerun-hash implication: since `computeSourceRecordHash()` folds `bundle` into the hash, adding a bundle to a previously-bundle-less migration definition changes every record's hash, so an in-place re-run over an existing id-map treats every record as changed (update path, new revision per record on a revisionable type) even though the source values didn't change.

## [0.1.0-alpha.258] - 2026-07-10

### Fixed

- **Release recovery: v0.1.0-alpha.258 re-publishes the never-published v0.1.0-alpha.257 (#1934).** The alpha.257 split fan-out failed its `verify-monorepo-main-intact` check because PR #1933 merged to main between the tag push and the verification (tag at `f0cf33c2d`, main at `b4630eee9`) — the check compares live main HEAD against the tagged SHA, so any mid-fan-out merge fails it and blocks the Packagist publish stage. No alpha.257 package was ever published; per the forward-only rule (VERSIONING.md §8) the dead tag stays in place and alpha.258 ships the same content plus this entry. The underlying race (fan-out verification pinned to a moving ref), the fire-once auto-merge bot, and the bot-token downstream-trigger suppression are tracked in #1934.

## [0.1.0-alpha.257] - 2026-07-10

### Added

- **CW-v1 WP-2 tasks 2.1–2.3: `node` opts into revisionable storage; per-bundle `new_revision` knob wired (#1920).** `Node` gains a `revision_id` `ContentEntityKeys` key and a discoverable `workflow_state` field (`FieldStorage::Data` — already persisted per-revision via the generic `_data` blob, so this only makes it visible to `SchemaPresenter`/JSON:API discovery, not a schema change); `NodeServiceProvider` registers the `node` `EntityType` with `revisionable: true, revisionDefault: true` (every ordinary save creates a new revision, Drupal parity). A new shape-guarded, additive migration (`packages/node/migrations/2026_07_06_000001_node_revision_schema.php`) retrofits the `revision_id`/`published_revision_id` pointer columns and the `node_revision` table onto existing installs (schema only — legacy-row `workflow_state` backfill is binding-scoped, task 2.7's CLI). `Node` declares the legacy `RevisionableInterface` so `EntityRepository::shouldCreateRevision()`'s per-entity override branch is live for nodes; new `Listener\NodeRevisionDefaultListener` (`PRE_SAVE`) forwards the bundle's `NodeType::isNewRevision()` onto the node whenever no earlier actor has already decided (an explicit `setNewRevision()` call always wins). `NodeType`'s `new_revision` default flips `false → true` (opt-OUT semantics, design decision 1) — a bundle that never explicitly sets the key must keep revisioning on; the earlier `false` default would have silently disabled it for every standard bundle.
- **CW-v1 WP-2 task 2.4: `BeforeRevisionPointerMoveEvent` — the revision pointer-move bypass choke point (#1920).** WP-1's `WorkflowStateGuard` only validates state changes made through `EntityRepository::save()`; `rollback()`, `setCurrentRevision()`, `setPublishedRevision()`, and the `saveTranslationRevision()`/`saveTranslationRevisions()`/`saveTranslation()` trio move the revision pointer (or write a new revision) WITHOUT going through the save pipeline, so none of them was previously observable to a save-time guard. New `Waaseyaa\EntityStorage\Event\BeforeRevisionPointerMoveEvent` (extends Symfony `Event`, mirrors `WorkflowTransitionEvent`'s shape) is now dispatched by all six methods, by FQCN, before any backend write, carrying `entityTypeId`/`entityId`/`operation` (`'rollback'|'revert'|'publish'|'translation_save'`)/`fromRevisionId`/`toRevisionId`/`actorUid`/the target revision's raw values array. A subscriber denies by throwing `AbortOperationException` — same convention as `BeforeSaveEvent` — leaving storage completely untouched (including rolling back any earlier write already attempted in the same transaction, for the transactional multi-write paths). Task 2.5 wires a workflow pointer-move guard onto this event; this PR only adds the choke point itself.
- **CW-v1 WP-2 task 2.5: `WorkflowPointerMoveGuard` closes the pointer-move bypass; cache/audit ride along (#1920).** `Waaseyaa\Workflows\Listener\WorkflowPointerMoveGuard` subscribes to `BeforeRevisionPointerMoveEvent` (task 2.4) and validates the implied state change exactly like a transition — edge must exist, permission required only when an acting account context exists, null context checks edge-legality only — denying by `TransitionDeniedException` before any write. `translation_save` is a deliberate, unconditional pass-through: v1 `workflow_state` is per revision, not per revision-translation, so a translation write implies no state change. Adjacent gap closed in the same task (deliberate side-scope): pointer moves previously invalidated zero cache tags (`EntityCacheSubscriber` now also subscribes `RevisionPointerMovedEvent` + the legacy `EntityEvents::REVISION_REVERTED`) and `rollback()` was invisible to audit (`PublishPointerAuditListener` only covers `setCurrentRevision()`/`setPublishedRevision()`; new `Waaseyaa\Audit\Listener\RollbackAuditListener` — and new `AuditEventKind::RevisionRollback` — gives it its own audit trail by arming on `BeforeRevisionPointerMoveEvent(operation: 'rollback')`, the pre-write signal only `rollback()` dispatches, and consuming the armed slot on the following `REVISION_REVERTED` for the same entity — every pointer operation's pre-event re-sets the slot, so an aborted rollback or a save-then-publish of the same revision can never fabricate a rollback record).
- **CW-v1 WP-2 task 2.6: forward-draft mechanics land in `TransitionService` — the two-pointer status semantics, in full (#1920).** `TransitionService::transition()`'s Apply step now implements decision 2 completely: every transition-service save forces a new revision regardless of the bundle's `new_revision` default (a bundle opted out of revisioning would otherwise clobber whatever revision happens to be loaded — often the published one); a target `default_revision: true` state saves the new revision, moves the published pointer (`setPublishedRevision()`, which may deny via the task 2.5 guard), THEN flips `status` — pointer-move-before-status-flip, so a denial never leaves `status` lying about a live state the pointer doesn't back; a `default_revision: false` target on an entity with an existing published pointer (a forward draft) leaves the pointer and `status` alone (copied from the published revision), so the live version keeps serving until the draft is promoted. `WorkflowStateGuard::applyState()` mirrors the rule for raw saves: once a published pointer exists, `status` NEVER derives from the state a save is entering — it always derives from the published-pointer revision's own state (falling back to a stored-status copy only for pre-backfill rows whose state is unknown to the workflow) — and any state-changing save on a pointered entity unconditionally forces a new revision, so an opt-out bundle's raw save into `published`/`archived` can never corrupt the live row in place; raw saves consequently never enact pointer moves — only `TransitionService` promotes a `default_revision: true` state. `WorkflowPointerMoveGuard` gains a two-branch reconciliation for its edge check: a SAME-state pointer move (e.g. promoting a forward draft, or a rollback landing on another same-state revision) needs the permission of *any* transition targeting that state; a DIFFERENT-state move needs the real edge's own permission, no exceptions — closing a bypass an earlier draft of this fix briefly reopened (an account holding only `publish` could otherwise resurrect an `archived`-pointer revision with no `archived → published` edge). `DefaultWorkflows::EDITORIAL` gains `restore_to_published` (archived → published, mirroring Drupal's "Restore") so archived-content republishing has a real edge on the shipped workflow. **Amended by the WP-2 rework (below):** the `revise` (published → draft) edge originally described in this bullet was pulled from the shipped workflow before merge — the whole-branch review found no read path is pointer-aware, so `EDITORIAL` ships with `restore_to_published` only; the forward-draft mechanic described above is otherwise unchanged and lives on as dormant engine substrate, reachable via custom workflows and the archived→restore→publish round trip.
- **CW-v1 WP-2 task 2.7: `workflows:backfill-state` CLI + binding-activation runbook (#1920).** New `bin/waaseyaa workflows:backfill-state <entity_type> <workflow_id> [--bundle=] [--dry-run]` (`Waaseyaa\CLI\Handler\WorkflowsBackfillStateHandler`, registered by the new `WorkflowsServiceProvider`, mirroring `revisions:enable`'s registration) stamps a `workflow_state` onto every existing row missing one, deriving the target from each row's own `status` column (`status = 1` → the workflow's published `default_revision: true` state; otherwise → `initial_state`) since that column is the only truthful signal legacy rows carry. Idempotent (rows with a non-empty state are skipped), `--dry-run` performs zero writes, and it reports examined/backfilled/skipped/failed counts — broken down per target state in both real and dry-run output — exiting nonzero on any partial failure (the R16 fail-fast lesson). It also fails fast (nonzero exit, zero writes, dry-run included) on a workflow that defines no published+`default_revision: true` state while published (`status = 1`) rows still need backfilling — a shape `WorkflowValidator` accepts, on which the status-derived routing would otherwise silently stamp published content with `initial_state`; with no such rows it proceeds with an explicit notice (adversarial-panel critical fix). Deliberately binding-agnostic: it does not read `workflows.assignments` and does not require a binding to exist — the whole point is to run BEFORE the binding is added (see the new "Playbook H" in `docs/specs/operations-playbooks.md`), so `WorkflowBindingResolver`/`WorkflowStateGuard` are not yet live for the type/bundle being backfilled. Each save explicitly disables new-revision creation (`setNewRevision(false)`) regardless of `revisionDefault` — a bulk state stamp must not spawn a revision per row. The runbook documents the full activation sequence (`migrate:up` → `revisions:enable node` → `workflows:backfill-state` → only then the binding), both hard-mis-ordering failure modes (binding before backfill silently defaults legacy rows to `draft`; binding a non-revisionable type hard-throws loudly), which rows the backfill writes (base row + current/tip revision row; older revisions untouched — safe via the guard's stored-status fallback), the legacy archived-content collapse (`status = 0` covers both never-published drafts AND deliberately-retired content, and both backfill to `initial_state` — operators who used unpublishing as archiving must pre-stamp retired rows `archived` first; template `json_set` SQL provided, and pre-stamped rows are skipped so it is idempotent-friendly), the pre-WP-2 `NodeType.new_revision` legacy-default ambiguity with concrete check/fix SQL, and the explicit WP-2 revision-pruning stance (`RevisionPruningPolicy`/`EntityRepository::pruneRevisions()` are callable today; no CLI/scheduled pruning ships in WP-2 — unbounded revision growth is the documented default).
- **CW-v1 WP-2 task 2.8 (final): integration spine on real `node` + docs truth-up (#1920).** New `packages/workflows/tests/Integration/ForwardDraftFlowTest.php` — the WP-2 required spine, on the REAL `node`/`node_type` entity types (not a synthetic fixture): create (guard forces `draft`, unpublished) → publish (pointer + `status=1`) → forward-draft edit via a `revise` edge (live content untouched) → submit for review → publish the draft revision (promotion) → archive (pointer moves, `status=0`) → rollback DENIED without permission (persisted state proven byte-for-byte unchanged) → rollback WITH permission (succeeds, produces the `RevisionRollback` audit record via the real `RollbackAuditListener`). New `ImportSaveContextGuardTest.php` verifies — rather than assumes — that `SaveContext::isImport` saves are validated by `WorkflowStateGuard` identically to any other create/update (the guard's subscribed event carries no `SaveContext` at all, so the flag has zero effect; no guard change needed, matching the WP-2 plan's own conclusion). **Known gap, deliberately deferred, not silently dropped:** `WorkflowVisibility`'s read-side precedence (raw `workflow_state` over `status`) predates forward drafts and now mis-reports a still-published node mid-forward-draft-edit as unpublished — concretely confirmed to de-index still-live content via `Waaseyaa\AI\Vector\EntityEmbeddingListener`; a real fix touches a pinned unit test and six consumer packages, so it is tracked as a WP-2 follow-up rather than rushed here (`docs/specs/content-workflow.md` "Visibility (read side)"). Spec-drift resolved: `docs/specs/entity-system.md` (pointer-move cross-reference to the canonical `revision-system-unified.md` §4a), `docs/specs/infrastructure.md` (cache-listener table now reflects the task 2.5 pointer-move/revert subscriptions), and `docs/specs/ocap-audit-log.md` (22 → 23 `AuditEventKind` cases, `RevisionRollback` taxonomy + listener-catalogue + actor-source entries) all updated for real; `tools/drift-detector.sh`'s `PATTERN_TO_SPEC` gains a `packages/node/` → `docs/specs/revision-system-unified.md` entry (previously unmapped). `docs/specs/content-workflow.md`'s WP-1/WP-2 status-table rows flip to `landed`. **Amended by the WP-2 rework (below):** the spine originally ran bound to the boot()-seeded shipped `editorial` workflow via its own `revise` edge; it now runs against a test-local `editorial_forward` workflow (the descoped `EDITORIAL` shape plus a `revise` transition, persisted directly by the test) since the shipped workflow no longer ships that edge — the spine proves the engine mechanics still hold, not that the shipped workflow exposes them. The WP-2 status-table row is likewise further amended: substrate landed, forward drafts on the shipped workflow deferred to the option-1 follow-up.
- **CW-v1 WP-2 rework task 1: `revise` pulled from the shipped `editorial` workflow — forward drafts descoped (#1920).** The final whole-branch review found the forward-draft mechanic unshippable as exposed on `editorial` (reads are not pointer-aware). `DefaultWorkflows::EDITORIAL` (`packages/workflows/src/DefaultWorkflows.php`) no longer defines the `revise` (published → draft) transition; `restore_to_published` (archived → published) ships unchanged. The engine mechanic itself is untouched — `TransitionService`/`WorkflowStateGuard`/`WorkflowPointerMoveGuard` keep full forward-draft support for any workflow that defines a published → draft edge. Engine-coverage tests (`ForwardDraftFlowTest`, `ForwardDraftIntegrationTest`, `GuardWiringTest`, `WorkflowStateGuardTest`) are re-anchored on a test-local `editorial_forward` workflow (the descoped `EDITORIAL` shape plus its own `revise` transition, persisted directly by the tests) instead of the boot-seeded shipped workflow. New `DefaultWorkflowsTest` pins the shipped `EDITORIAL` const to have no transition whose `from` includes `published` and whose `to` is a non-default-revision state — i.e., no shipped forward-draft entry edge. `packages/workflows/README.md` rewritten around the deferral (see `docs/specs/content-workflow.md`, "Deferred: forward drafts on the shipped workflow").
- **CW-v1 WP-2 rework task 2: boot seed additively tops up a persisted `editorial` workflow — upgraded installs gain `restore_to_published` (#1920).** `WorkflowServiceProvider::seedDefaultEditorialWorkflow()` (`packages/workflows/src/WorkflowServiceProvider.php`) previously returned early whenever `editorial` already existed, so any install that first booted before `restore_to_published` shipped (alpha.256 and earlier — dev skeleton, staging, minoo.live) kept the stale shape forever, leaving archived content permanently unrecoverable there. The seed now additively merges: any state/transition present in `DefaultWorkflows::EDITORIAL` but absent BY MACHINE NAME from the persisted entity is added; nothing already present is ever modified or removed (an operator-customized `permission` string survives untouched), and a steady-state install (nothing missing) performs no save at all. Validated with `WorkflowValidator` before saving — log-and-skip on violation (`workflows.default_seed_topup_invalid`), never boot-crash; success logs the added ids (`workflows.default_seed_topup`). Deleting a shipped state/transition from the persisted entity is re-added at the next boot — this is the floor, not a ceiling; operators customize by editing existing entries or binding their own workflow id instead.
- **CW-v1 WP-2 rework task 3: `workflows:backfill-state` establishes the published pointer for published rows (#1920).** Following the pre-rework Playbook H exactly left every legacy published row `status=1, workflow_state='published', published_revision_id=NULL` forever, with no sanctioned code path able to set the pointer afterward — pointer-dependent semantics silently degraded to never-published. `WorkflowsBackfillStateHandler` (`packages/cli/src/Handler/WorkflowsBackfillStateHandler.php`) gains a pointer-establishment phase, gated on the entity type being revisionable AND the workflow resolving a published `default_revision: true` state: for every examined row (newly stamped this run, or already carrying that state from a prior run — the WP-1 tail) whose `loadPublishedRevision()` is still null, it calls `EntityRepository::setPublishedRevision()` to point the published pointer at the row's current revision id — the sanctioned pointer door, so `WorkflowPointerMoveGuard` sees this backfill too. Idempotent (an already-set pointer is left alone); a row with no revision history yet is counted and reported separately rather than failing the command; `--dry-run` reports the would-establish count with zero writes, the real run reports the established count and folds per-row failures into the existing nonzero-exit accounting.
- **CW-v1 WP-2 rework task 4: `TransitionDeniedException` maps to 403/422 JSON:API error documents, never 500 (#1920).** `WorkflowStateGuard` throws `TransitionDeniedException` from PRE_SAVE inside `EntityRepository::save()`; `JsonApiController` (`packages/api/src/JsonApiController.php`) previously caught only `UniqueConstraintViolationException`/`RevisionConflictException`/`EntityValidationException`/`\LogicException`, so a routine editorial mistake (e.g. an illegal `workflow_state` PATCH) surfaced as an uncaught HTTP 500. All three save sites (`create()`, `update()`'s plain save, `saveWithExpectation()`) now catch `TransitionDeniedException` and route through a shared `workflowTransitionDeniedError()` mapper: `REASON_PERMISSION` → 403 Forbidden, every other reason (`illegal_edge`, `unknown_transition`, `unbound`) → 422 Unprocessable Entity, both carrying `code: 'WORKFLOW_TRANSITION_DENIED'` and `meta: ['reason' => …]` (mirrors the `REVISION_CONFLICT` code/meta pattern). `waaseyaa/workflows` is a declared runtime `require` of `waaseyaa/api` (L4 importing L3 is layer-legal), so the catch is a real, always-resolvable dependency rather than an FQCN-string guess.
- **CW-v1 WP-2 rework task 5: the published revision is immortal in `pruneRevisions()`/`deleteRevision()` (#1920).** Both `EntityRepository::pruneRevisions()` and `RevisionableStorageDriver::deleteRevision()` (`packages/entity-storage/src/EntityRepository.php`, `packages/entity-storage/src/Driver/RevisionableStorageDriver.php`) previously immortalized only the current/default revision; the published revision was a deletable pruning/delete candidate, which would silently flip an entity to never-published semantics once published ≠ current became the routine forward-draft state. Both paths now also exclude the published pointer (`published_revision_id`) — `pruneRevisions()` inline in its executor loop (without changing `RevisionPruningPolicy::candidateExcluded()`'s `@api` signature), `deleteRevision()` via a `SELECT *` read that tolerates pre-WP-2 base tables lacking the column entirely (behaves exactly as before: only the current-revision guard applies, no SQL error). `docs/specs/revision-system-unified.md`'s pruning/deletion contract documents the extended FR-038 rule.
- **CW-v1 WP-2 rework task 6: runbook + spec + CHANGELOG truth-up for the substrate descope (#1920).** `docs/specs/operations-playbooks.md` Playbook H is retitled/reframed as the WP-2 SUBSTRATE activation runbook: step 5 no longer instructs binding `node` → `editorial` in production (forward drafts are deferred — non-production/evaluation environments may still bind to exercise the engine); a new Failure mode 3 documents that running `revisions:enable` before (or without) a successful `migrate:up` silently folds the pointer columns into the `_data` blob instead of erroring, with a verification-gate SQL query inserted between steps 2 and 3; step 4's "What gets written" section documents task 3's pointer-establishment phase; the revision-pruning stance paragraph notes task 5's published-revision exclusion. `docs/specs/content-workflow.md`'s WP-2 status-table row, config sample, and `revise` paragraph are rewritten around the descope, the integration-spine paragraph now names the test-local `editorial_forward` workflow, and a new "Deferred: forward drafts on the shipped workflow" subsection lists what the option-1 follow-up owns (read-side pointer awareness, write-side field allowlist, rollback/`setCurrentRevision()` stale snapshot writeback, first-publish edge rule on review-required workflows). CHANGELOG entries for tasks 2.6 and 2.8 amended in place rather than rewritten, per house style.
- **CW-v1 WP-2 rework fix wave: `rollback()`/`setCurrentRevision()` no longer clobber the live published pointer or status (#1920).** The fresh-context final review (`.superpowers/sdd/rework-final-review.md`, finding I-1) found the rework's own task-3 backfill supplied the missing precondition (a populated `published_revision_id`) for a pre-existing containment gap: both `EntityRepository::rollback()` and `EntityRepository::setCurrentRevision()` wrote the target revision's frozen `published_revision_id`/`status` snapshot verbatim onto the base row, silently overwriting the live pointer/status with no guard and no audit trail — reachable via the shipped MCP tools `entity.rollback`/`entity.set_current_revision` with no workflow binding required. Both methods now reuse the base row already read for the pre-write event and, after dispatching `BeforeRevisionPointerMoveEvent`, overwrite `published_revision_id`/`status` in the outgoing row with the live base values (unset when the base row lacks the column, preserving legacy-schema behavior exactly) — restoring CONTENT only, never the pointer, per the invariant that moving the pointer/flipping status is exclusively `TransitionService`'s job. Docs disclosure: `operations-playbooks.md` Playbook H names the invariant near step 4's pointer-establishment text and adds an evaluation-binding caveat for the still-deferred write-side field allowlist (finding I-2); `content-workflow.md`'s Purpose section caveats the forward-draft parity claim, and its "Deferred" subsection moves the rollback/`setCurrentRevision()` bullet from owned-by-follow-up to fixed.

## [0.1.0-alpha.256] - 2026-07-06

### Added

- **CW-v1 WP-1: content-workflow engine core — config schema, `TransitionService`, save-path guard, transition events, permissions, default `editorial` workflow (#1920).** Closes out the second work package of the content-workflow build (`docs/history/plans/2026-07-06-content-workflow-wp0-wp1.md`), following WP-0's interim field gate (#1927). `Workflow`/`WorkflowState`/`WorkflowTransition` gain the config schema the engine needs: states carry `published`/`defaultRevision` flags, transitions carry an explicit-or-derived `permission` string (`use {workflow_id} transition {transition_id}`), and the workflow itself carries an `initial_state` (defaulting to the first declared state). `Validation\WorkflowValidator` rejects malformed definitions (unknown states in `from`/`to`, an `initial_state` that isn't a defined state, zero states). `Binding\WorkflowBindingResolver` maps entity type + bundle to a bound workflow via `workflows.assignments` config (exact `{type}.{bundle}` wins over `{type}.*`), rejecting bindings to non-revisionable types or unknown workflow ids. `Transition\TransitionService` is the one enforcement door: validate (binding, transition, current-state legality, permission) → apply (`workflow_state` + `status` per the target state's `published` flag) → persist through the canonical `EntityRepository` pipeline → announce (`WorkflowEvents::PRE_TRANSITION`/`POST_TRANSITION`, mirroring the live `EntityEvent` lifecycle shape) → record a best-effort audit entry (new `AuditEventKind::WorkflowTransition`); denials throw `Transition\TransitionDeniedException` with a machine-readable reason (`unbound`, `unknown_transition`, `illegal_edge`, `permission`) — never a silent no-op. `Listener\WorkflowStateGuard` makes the raw entity save path (`EntityEvents::PRE_SAVE`, the same event `EntityLifecycleAuditListener` subscribes to) equivalent to the service: a create is forced into the workflow's initial state unless the acting account can reach a different one via a single legal-and-permitted transition (closing the born-published hole for workflow-bound types); an update's `workflow_state` change is validated exactly like a transition, requiring permission whenever an acting `AccountContextInterface` context exists (a null context — CLI/queue/programmatic — checks edge-legality only). `WorkflowServiceProvider::boot()` wires the guard onto the real dispatcher via the Symfony-contracts FQCN (the same pattern `RelationshipServiceProvider::boot()` uses, avoiding the silent-no-op bug that left this package's predecessor listener dead) and seeds the framework-default `editorial` workflow as declarative data (`DefaultWorkflows::EDITORIAL`, never a preset-in-code canonical definition), log-and-skip on validation failure rather than crash boot. A dedicated `GuardWiringTest` integration test proves the guard actually fires on a real kernel-dispatched save (real dispatcher, real SQLite-backed `EntityRepository`, real `WorkflowServiceProvider::boot()`) — a unit test on the guard class alone would not have caught the equivalent historical wiring bug. Revision promotion (the `default_revision` forward-draft mechanic) is WP-2; group/department transition constraints are WP-3; API transition endpoints and admin SPA wiring are WP-4.

### Fixed

- **`Waaseyaa\Config\ConfigFactoryInterface` was bound in NO production ServiceProvider, so any consumer reaching for it via `resolveOptional()` silently no-opped in a real boot** (#1920, CW-v1 WP-1 follow-up). Grepping the framework confirmed the gap: neither `packages/config` nor any other package's `ServiceProvider::register()` bound `ConfigFactoryInterface`/`ConfigFactory` anywhere — `Waaseyaa\SSR\ThemeServiceProvider` even carried a dead comment noting the factory "will be available at runtime," but it never was. This made the CW-v1 engine's forthcoming `WorkflowServiceProvider` guard/seed wiring — which depends on `resolveOptional(ConfigFactoryInterface::class)` — unreachable in production once #1929 (WP-1) merges. New `Waaseyaa\Config\ConfigServiceProvider` (`packages/config/src/ConfigServiceProvider.php`, registered via `packages/config/composer.json`'s `extra.waaseyaa.providers`) binds `ConfigFactoryInterface` as a container singleton backed by `FileStorage` at `<projectRoot>/config/active` — the same active store `OptimizeServiceProvider` already compiles for `optimize:config`, so this does not construct a second store instance. New integration test `tests/Integration/Config/ConfigFactoryProductionBindingTest.php` boots a real kernel (mirrors `DefinitionValidatorBootTest`'s pattern) and proves a third-party consumer provider's `resolveOptional(ConfigFactoryInterface::class)` resolves to a working factory, and that `get()`/`getEditable()->set()->save()`/`get()` round-trips through the active store.

## [0.1.0-alpha.255] - 2026-07-06

### Security

- **The GraphQL `<entity>List` query accepted a filter/sort on ANY field-name string, so a caller could filter or sort on an undeclared `_data` JSON key or a declared `internal`-flagged secret and read `total` / item-presence as a per-value oracle over data the field-access layer never lets them read (audit A11, R15; the structural follow-up the R14 entry flagged as left-open on `EntityResolver::resolveList()`).** `EntityResolver::resolveList()` had the R14 per-entity field-access gate (`queryFieldForbidden()` / `rejectForbiddenSort()`), but that gate only fires for a field a dynamic `FieldAccessPolicy` returns Forbidden for; it cannot express the STRUCTURAL classes REST's `JsonApiController::validateQueryFields()` allowlist has rejected since audit R2 WP1. Two of those classes were live oracles on GraphQL: (1) an UNDECLARED field name (no policy → `checkFieldAccess` Neutral → `isFieldViewForbidden` false) resolved in storage to `json_extract(_data, '$.<field>')` (SQL injection itself already contained by `JsonFieldName::assertQueryable`'s `^[A-Za-z0-9_]+$` sink guard, R2 WP1) and became a filter-presence / sort-rank oracle over arbitrary blob keys; (2) a DECLARED field flagged `settings['internal'] => true` — `internal` is a settings flag, not a `FieldAccessPolicy`, so R14 never fired — plus the credential floor (`pass`/`password`/`password_hash`). Those `internal` fields are real secrets never returned in output yet fully oracle-able: `User.two_factor_secret` (Base32 TOTP secret), `User.two_factor_recovery_code_hashes`, `OidcClient.client_secret_hash`. Because the `/graphql` route is `allowAll()` (public, `GraphQlRouteProvider`), the oracle was reachable at anonymous-and-up privilege over any entity row the caller can entity-view (a filter/sort on `two_factor_secret` on the `user` list, or `sort: "-password"`, recovers the secret's value char-by-char via presence/pagination-rank while the field is never disclosed). Fixed by porting REST's structural allowlist to GraphQL: `EntityResolver::assertQueryableFields()` runs at the top of `resolveList()` BEFORE any storage query and throws a `UserError` for any filter/sort field that is not a declared field (`resolveFieldDefinitions()`) or an entity key, is in `ALWAYS_INTERNAL_FIELDS` (`pass`/`password`/`password_hash`), or is a declared field with `getSetting('internal') === true`. Value- and account-independent, exactly like the REST gate, so it adds no signal of its own; per-row classification/clearance fields (declared, non-internal) still pass here and remain handled value-independently by the R14 gates. A previously-passing test that documented the vulnerable behavior — `GraphQlDataBlobTest::testFilterOnNonExistentFieldReturnsEmpty` (an undeclared field silently returning empty via `json_extract`) — is replaced by `testFilterOnNonExistentFieldIsRejected` asserting the rejection. New exploit-closed coverage, confirmed RED pre-fix and GREEN post-fix: `EntityResolverStructuralFieldAllowlistTest` (undeclared filter/sort rejected, `internal`-flagged `two_factor_secret` filter/sort rejected, credential-field rejected, plus declared-field / entity-key positive controls). Nested `entity_reference` resolution (`ReferenceLoader`) resolves by stored FK id and accepts no caller-supplied field name, so it exposes no equivalent sink; `resolveList()` is the only caller-controlled filter/sort entry point.
- **The genealogy living-person concealment covered only 1 of its 4 content types: `genealogy_family` and `genealogy_event`, both carrying a REQUIRED free-text `display_name` that in practice names living people, were anonymously viewable (and crawler-enumerable) on published-entity + published-tree alone, disclosing living-person identities the framework is meant to protect (genealogy m-a, security).** `GenealogyContentAccessPolicy`'s living-person guard was gated behind `getEntityTypeId() === 'genealogy_person'` on both the anonymous path (`anonymousPublishedViewAccess()`) and the authenticated-non-owner path (`viewAccess()`), so `genealogy_family` and `genealogy_event` fell through to `allowed()` for anonymous viewers. `genealogy_family` is anonymously SSR-viewable via the `family()` route (`GenealogySsrController`), and both are reachable through the generic entity read/Markdown surface and were crawler-enumerable because `SeoPublicController::NON_PUBLIC_TYPES` omitted them. Unlike `genealogy_person` (whose `is_living` flag lets the enumeration and the guard drop only living rows), the family/event `display_name` free-text channel has no living/deceased axis to test per row, so the guard now fails CLOSED for both types: a new `GenealogyContentAccessPolicy::concealsForLivingPrivacy()` returns `true` for every `genealogy_family`/`genealogy_event` (and keeps the existing `effectiveIsLiving()` rule for `genealogy_person`), and both view guards call it, so a non-owner / anonymous viewer is refused even when the row and its tree are published. Tree owners (ownership bypass) and the built-in dev `DevAdminAccount` (short-circuits to Allowed in `viewAccess()`) keep access, so the local demo still renders. As an independent second gate for the crawler surface, `genealogy_family` and `genealogy_event` are added to `SeoPublicController::NON_PUBLIC_TYPES` (the access-aware sitemap/`llms.txt` enumeration already drops them now that anonymous view is Forbidden; the list entry skips the type wholesale without a per-row access probe, defense in depth). **Folded-in residual (m2):** the redacted-neighbor placeholder in `GenealogyPedigreeService::neighborSlots()` was `'Private living relative'` vs `'Private ancestor'`, leaking the concealed relative's living/deceased status — the very axis the redaction protects — as a one-bit side channel on an already-redacted slot; both now render a single uniform `'Private relative'` (`REDACTED_RELATIVE_LABEL`). The other recorded residual (m2a, the preserved COUNT of redacted relative slots) is left as-is: it is intrinsic to the redacted-placeholder pedigree design (the chart deliberately shows that a relative exists while concealing identity), and cannot be closed without collapsing that surface. New exploit-closed coverage, RED on main and GREEN after: `GenealogyFamilyEventConcealmentTest` (anonymous family and event refused; authenticated-non-owner family refused; tree-owner, dev-admin, and deceased-person positive controls) and `GenealogySeoEnumerationTest::sitemap_and_llms_never_enumerate_genealogy_family_or_event`; `GenealogySsrConcealmentTest` updated to the uniform placeholder. The 4th content type (`genealogy_tree`) was assessed and is unaffected: its `display_name` is a workspace label, not a person name, and its visibility is decided by `treeView()`/`anonymousPublishedViewAccess()` before the person-privacy guard is reached.
- **A caller-supplied collection filter or sort on a field gated only by a dynamic `FieldAccessPolicy` (e.g. a classification / clearance field with no static `internal` flag) was a presence / `meta.total` oracle on both the JSON:API and GraphQL list surfaces, letting a caller who may list a type and view its rows but lacks a field's clearance read that field's per-value row count (audit A11, R14; class-mate of R13 WP1).** `JsonApiController::index()`'s structural allowlist (`validateQueryFields()`, added after audit R2 WP1) only rejected unknown, `ALWAYS_INTERNAL_FIELDS`, or `internal`-flagged fields, so a structurally normal field that a per-entity field-access policy forbids passed straight through and was applied as a raw storage `condition()`. Entity-level access filtered the rows (`setAccount` / `accessFilteredTotal`), but never field-level access, so `meta.total` and the returned row set still reflected the forbidden field's value: filtering `filter[classification_field]=secret` returned the count of rows carrying that value even though the caller could not read the field, and sorting on it ordered rows by the hidden value. The GraphQL sibling `EntityResolver::resolveList()` had the identical gap on its filter-argument path (`total` was computed with only the entity-level `guard->canView()` predicate). Fixed with two complementary, value-independent, fail-closed gates. (1) FILTER (and per-row data exposure): `JsonApiController` excludes a row from BOTH the page and `accessFilteredTotal()` when any of its filter/sort fields is view-Forbidden (`queryFieldForbidden()`), and `EntityResolver::resolveList()` does the same in its count and item loops via the new `GraphQlAccessGuard::isFieldViewForbidden()` — a row is dropped because the caller may not READ the queried field, never because of the field's value, so no operator (including `NOT_EQUALS`) and no probe value moves the row set or `meta.total`. (2) SORT (pagination-rank ordering): the drop alone is insufficient for a sort, because `QueryApplier` runs `sort()` and `range(offset, limit)` in storage BEFORE the post-fetch drop, so a Forbidden row still occupies a pagination RANK whose empty-vs-populated slot across offsets leaks its ordering relative to readable rows (adversarial-review finding, confirmed by a mixed-per-row PoC that the initial all-rows-forbidden tests missed). Storage cannot evaluate per-row field-access policy, so a sort on a field that is view-Forbidden on any entity-level-viewable matched row is now REJECTED outright (`JsonApiController::rejectForbiddenSort()` returns a 400; `EntityResolver::rejectForbiddenSort()` throws a `UserError`), rather than allowed to order rows the caller cannot fully read. Both the drop and the reject are value-independent — the reject depends only on WHICH viewable rows carry a Forbidden sort field, never on the field's value or the sort direction, so it adds no oracle beyond the per-row field-read boundary `show()`/`resolveSingle()` already expose. Together this is the per-entity companion to the structural allowlist, mirroring R13 WP1's admin-surface shape, needed because classification/clearance gating varies per row and a static allowlist cannot express it. A sort on a field the caller can read on every viewable matched row is unaffected (positive-control tested). The credential floor (`ALWAYS_INTERNAL_FIELDS`: `pass`/`password`/`password_hash`, plus `internal`-flagged fields such as the 2FA settings) stays rejected outright by the unchanged structural gate, and the no-account system context is unchanged (it keeps the storage-derived total and does no field gating). New exploit-closed coverage, confirmed RED pre-fix and GREEN post-fix: `JsonApiControllerFieldFilterOracleTest` (REST filter presence oracle, value-indistinguishability, single-page sort reject, MIXED-per-row paginated-sort reject across offsets, readable-filter and readable-sort positive controls, credential-field regression) and `EntityResolverFieldFilterOracleTest` (GraphQL filter-argument oracle, value-indistinguishability, sort reject, readable-filter and readable-sort positive controls), plus full-stack `GraphQlDataBlobTest::testSortOnFieldForbiddenBlobFieldIsRejected`. **Reported class-mate, left for follow-up (out of R14 scope, matching R13's disposition):** `EntityResolver::resolveList()` still has NO structural allowlist on filter/sort field names, so an unknown field name reaches `SqlEntityQuery`'s raw `json_extract('$.<field>')` interpolation the same way the REST path did before audit R2 WP1; this is a separate injection-class gap (R2), not the field-access oracle closed here.
- **`text_long` ("richtext") entity field values were never sanitized server-side, so an authenticated content author could save markup containing `<script>`/event-handler attributes and have it execute in ANY OTHER admin's session that later viewed the same record: a cross-admin stored XSS reachable through the JSON:API write path, the GraphQL write path, and the field auto-save endpoint (audit A11, R13 WP2).** `text_long` is the field type SchemaPresenter's `WIDGET_MAP` maps to the `richtext` widget, and the admin SPA renders any `x-widget === 'richtext'` field via `v-html` (`packages/admin/app/components/schema/SchemaView.vue:81-84`). No HTML sanitizer was attached to `text_long` on the write path: `JsonApiController::store()`/`update()` persisted the attribute verbatim, GraphQL's `EntityResolver::resolveCreate()`/`resolveUpdate()` did the same, and `FieldAutoSaveController::update()` wrote `$body['value']` straight through (`SafeMarkupValidator` is opt-in and self-documents as "NOT a real sanitizer"; the only real sanitizer, migration-only `HtmlSanitizeProcessor`, was never wired into any of these paths). The read path then served the raw markup unmodified to every consumer: `ResourceSerializer::castAttributes()` only cast `boolean`/`timestamp`/`datetime` and returned everything else, including `text_long`, unchanged (JSON:API `show`/`index`/write-echo, and by extension the admin-surface host and the SSR Markdown presenter, all of which construct a `ResourceSerializer`); GraphQL's plain-field resolver in `EntityTypeBuilder::buildOutputFields()` returned `$data[$fieldName]` raw (covering both queries and the create/update mutation response, since both resolve through the same field resolver); and `FieldAutoSaveController::update()` echoed the just-saved value back in its 200 response unsanitized. Fixed with a single shared sanitizer, `Waaseyaa\Api\Sanitizer\RichTextSanitizer` (`packages/api/src/Sanitizer/RichTextSanitizer.php`), wrapping `symfony/html-sanitizer`'s `HtmlSanitizerConfig::allowSafeElements()->forceHttpsUrls()` (the SAME allowlist config `Waaseyaa\SSR\Formatter\HtmlFormatter` already uses for SSR rendering, so the server-side API/GraphQL/admin surfaces now match SSR's existing sanitized posture instead of diverging from it. The fix is applied NON-LOSSILY at every read/serialization boundary, never at write time, so the value AS STORED in the entity/database is left byte-for-byte as the author submitted it: `ResourceSerializer::castAttributes()` now sanitizes any attribute whose field type is `text_long` (the only field type in `RichTextSanitizer::HTML_FIELD_TYPES` (plain-text types like `string`/`text` are deliberately excluded, since they render as literal text, not markup, and sanitizing them would corrupt legitimate content such as a literal `<` in a title); `EntityTypeBuilder::buildOutputFields()` wraps the plain-field resolver for a `text_long` field with the same sanitizer (safe to share the one stateless instance across the R12 per-process schema cache, since `RichTextSanitizer` carries no per-request/account state); and `FieldAutoSaveController::update()` sanitizes the echoed value when the target field's type is `text_long`. All three classes take an optional `?RichTextSanitizer` constructor parameter (default: a fresh instance, resolved in the constructor body per the PHP 8.4 constructor-default-with-`new` convention), so sanitization is DI-injected once per instance rather than re-constructed per call, and every existing call site (JSON:API routers, `GenericAdminSurfaceHost`, `SsrPageHandler`'s Markdown presenter, `SchemaFactory`) is covered without any wiring change. Indigenous-language content (double vowels, the glottal-stop apostrophe, syllabics) is confirmed non-lossy through the sanitizer: exploit tests save Anishinaabemowin text containing `<script>` in the SAME field value and confirm the full text content survives (post HTML-entity-decode) while the script is removed. **Decision flag for review:** sanitization happens at the read/serialization boundary: the raw author input is what is stored, unchanged; served output is filtered through `allowSafeElements()`, which permits a fixed set of "safe" elements/attributes (headings, paragraphs, lists, links, emphasis, tables, etc.) and strips everything else, including scripts, inline event handlers, and non-allowlisted elements such as `<iframe>`. An author who intentionally stored an iframe/embed will see it stripped on every serve, which is a product trade-off, not an oversight. New exploit-closed coverage, confirmed RED pre-fix and GREEN post-fix: `JsonApiControllerRichTextSanitizationTest` (JSON:API store/update write-then-read-back, non-lossy-storage proof, orthography proof), `EntityTypeBuilderRichTextSanitizationTest` (GraphQL plain-field resolver), `FieldAutoSaveRichTextSanitizationTest` (auto-save echo), `RichTextSanitizerTest` (sanitizer unit coverage).**

- **`GenericAdminSurfaceHost::list()` applied caller-supplied filter/sort field names against the raw entity value with no field-level access check, letting a low-tier account reconstruct another user's credential material one probe at a time (audit A11, R13 WP1).** Entity-level `view` access was checked, but the filter/sort field name itself (fully caller-controlled via `filter[<field>][operator]=...` and `sort=<field>`, `packages/admin-surface/src/Query/SurfaceQueryParser.php`) was never validated: `applyFilter()` read `(string) $entity->get($field)` and the sort comparator read `$a->get($field)`/`$b->get($field)` directly. An account holding only the low-tier "access user profiles" permission could therefore filter the auto-cataloged `user` list on `pass` (or the 2FA fields: `two_factor_secret`, `two_factor_recovery_codes_hash`, `two_factor_last_used_step`, Forbidden for everyone, including admins, via `UserAccessPolicy::fieldAccess`) and use per-row presence/absence as a one-bit oracle, walking a guessed substring to reconstruct the stored bcrypt hash or TOTP secret. This is the same class of gap the REST JSON:API surface already closed via `JsonApiController::validateQueryFields()` ("audit R2 WP1"), the admin-surface list path never got the equivalent gate. Fixed with two layers, fail closed: (a) a structural allowlist (`GenericAdminSurfaceHost::validateSurfaceQueryFields()`) mirroring `validateQueryFields()`, rejecting (400) a filter/sort field that is not a declared field or entity key, is in the mirrored `ALWAYS_INTERNAL_FIELDS` list (`pass`/`password`/`password_hash`), or carries the `internal` field-setting; (b) per-entity `EntityAccessHandler::checkFieldAccess()` enforcement inside `applyFilter()` and the sort comparator, needed because some fields are Forbidden only for *some* entities of a type (e.g. classification/clearance-gated fields), which a static allowlist cannot express: a Forbidden field never matches a filter (the entity is excluded, never read) and never drives sort order (a neutral placeholder replaces the value, so ordering carries no signal). Legitimate filters and sorts on non-restricted fields are unaffected.

- **The GraphQL static per-process schema cache leaked one request's account and loaded-entity access across every later request that hit the cache, defeating every per-entity access policy under FrankenPHP worker mode (audit A10, R12).** `SchemaFactory::$schemaCache` (`packages/graphql/src/Schema/SchemaFactory.php:48`) is keyed only by entity-type ids plus mutation-override keys, not by account. `GraphQlEndpoint::handle()` builds a fresh per-request `GraphQlAccessGuard`/`EntityResolver`/`ReferenceLoader` bound to the request account on every call, but `SchemaFactory::build()` returned the CACHED `Schema` on a cache hit, and that cached `Schema`'s query/mutation resolver closures (`SchemaFactory` `resolve` closures) and entity-reference field resolver (`EntityTypeBuilder::buildEntityReferenceOutputField()`) had captured the FIRST request's `EntityResolver`/`ReferenceLoader` by closure. Under FrankenPHP worker mode (the documented production runtime, `public/index.php`) a process serves many requests without teardown, so every request after the first one to build a given schema shape executed queries AND mutations under the first request's account and saw the first request's loaded-entity cache. This defect lives only under FrankenPHP worker mode; it is invisible under php-fpm/`php -S`/the test suite, which tear down (or reset) per-process state between requests. Fixed, fail-closed, by making the cached `Schema` itself account-free and request-free: the per-request `EntityResolver` and `ReferenceLoader` now travel as a new `GraphQlExecutionContext` (`packages/graphql/src/GraphQlExecutionContext.php`) passed as the GraphQL `contextValue` (the resolver's 3rd argument) into `GraphQL::executeQuery()`, and every default resolver closure in `SchemaFactory`/`EntityTypeBuilder` reads its collaborator from that per-request context instead of from a captured constructor property. `SchemaFactory`/`EntityTypeBuilder` no longer take `EntityResolver`/`ReferenceLoader` constructor dependencies at all, so it is structurally visible that the built schema holds no per-request state; the static schema cache itself is kept (it is a deliberate worker-mode optimization, not the defect). R11's "Entity not found" collapse (`EntityResolver::resolveUpdate()`/`resolveDelete()`) is unchanged and unaffected, reached the same way via the execution context.
- **`WorkflowVisibility::isEntityPublic()` treated a MISSING `status` key as public/visible for non-`node` entity types, while a present-but-unrecognized status value correctly resolved to unpublished — a fail-OPEN gap where an entity that never set a status was MORE visible than one carrying a garbage value (audit R16, L3-workflows.md, #1915, #1916).** Fixed by flipping the missing-key branch to fail closed (`false`); `Node` is unaffected (the field scaffolder always projects `status` for nodes, and `isNodePublic()` was untouched). Adversarial review found the flip regressed `Media`: `Media::isPublished()` defaults `?? true` on a missing key, but unlike `Term`/`PathAlias`/`Node`, `Media::__construct()` never backfilled a missing `status`, so `DiscoveryApiHandler` — which calls the now-fail-closed `isEntityPublic()` **before** its access check — 404'd every un-status'd media item for every caller, including admins. `Media::__construct()` now backfills `status = true` when absent, the same `array_key_exists()` pattern `Term` already used, making the entity class itself the single canonical source of its published-default. A repo-wide sweep of every publish-status-bearing entity type (`Term`, `PathAlias`, `Node`, `Comment`, `ThreadMessage`, `Relationship`, `Group`, `User`, the four genealogy entities, `MenuLink`) confirmed `Media` was the only gap; the sweep table is recorded in the PR. New coverage: `WorkflowVisibilityTest` (missing-key → false, recognized/unrecognized status values), `MediaTest::testStatusBackfilledWhenAbsentFromConstructor`, and `DiscoveryApiHandlerTest` regression + negative cases for un-status'd and explicitly-unpublished media. (#1919, R16 batch, part of the #1915 audit)
- **CW-v1 WP-0: an account holding only edit/create permissions (no publish permission) could self-publish a `node` — either explicitly via `status`/`workflow_state` in the request body, or implicitly through the entity constructor's born-published default (`Node` defaults `status = 1`) — creating no floor between "may author content" and "may make it publicly visible" (#1920, #1927).** `NodeAccessPolicy::fieldAccess()` now edit-Forbids `status`/`workflow_state` for any account lacking the new `NodeAccessPolicy::PUBLISH_PERMISSION` (`'use editorial transition publish'`), on BOTH create and update — deliberately no `isNew()` carve-out, since the concern is "may this account publish at all," not the pre-existing `uid`/`type`/`created`/`changed` history-rewrite lock. `JsonApiController::store()` adds a companion floor: when the client omits `status` from the create payload, the constructor-defaulted entity already carries a non-null `status`, and the acting account is field-edit-Forbidden on `status`, the controller sets `status = 0` before save — so a create can no longer silently inherit a published default the account could never have set explicitly. An explicitly-supplied `status` is unaffected by this floor; it still goes through the existing per-attribute access-check loop (Forbidden → 403). `promote`/`sticky` remain ungated pending the editorial engine. This is the WP-0 slice of the CW-v1 content-workflow initiative; the full spec (`docs/specs/content-workflow.md`) lands separately on `feat/content-workflow-spec` (#1921). New coverage: `NodeFieldAccessPolicyTest` (status/workflow_state edit Forbidden without the permission, on both create and update; Allowed with it) and `tests/Integration/Phase7/JsonApiAccessIntegrationTest` (`createWithoutStatusDefaultsUnpublishedForAccountsThatMayNotPublish`, `createWithoutStatusStaysPublishedForPublishers`).

### Fixed

- **`ingest:run` key normalization collapsed non-Latin-script titles to an empty key, failing the entire ingest batch for a single Indigenous-orthography record (audit A7, F7).** `IngestRunHandler::normalizeKey()` used byte-level `strtolower()` and an ASCII-only regex with no `/u` modifier, so a title written entirely in a non-Latin script (for example Canadian Aboriginal Syllabics) had every character stripped, producing an empty key. Because `mapRecords()` pushes any empty-key record onto `diagnostics['errors']`, and `execute()` suppresses emission of the whole mapped batch whenever `diagnostics['errors']` is non-empty, one syllabics-titled record failed the entire batch, not just that record. Fixed with a Unicode-safe, transliterate-or-hash pipeline: pure-ASCII input still runs through the original lowercase-and-collapse pass unchanged, so existing keys are byte-identical; non-ASCII input is first run through ICU's `Any-Latin; Latin-ASCII` transliterator when the `intl` extension is available, so diacritics (for example "Wasēyāa Ziibi") resolve to a readable Latin key ("waseyaa_ziibi") instead of an underscore-mangled one; and any input that still normalizes to nothing (syllabics with no Latin mapping, or `intl` unavailable) falls back to a deterministic hash-derived key (`k` plus a truncated SHA-256 of the NFC-normalized title), so the same title always yields the same key and relationships between non-Latin-titled nodes still resolve. Only a genuinely empty or whitespace-only source title/key still normalizes to an empty key; the empty-key diagnostic now names that cause ("Record %d produced an empty key: source title/key is empty.") but is a defensive invariant rather than an exercised path, since a blank title is already rejected upstream as "missing title" and `normalizeKey()` is total for non-blank input. Behavior changes to note (see `docs/upgrade-notes/ingest-unicode-keys.md`): previously-mangled non-ASCII derived keys DRIFT after upgrade ("w_s_y_a_ziibi" becomes "waseyaa_ziibi", "caf" becomes "cafe"), rekeying `key`/`source_ref` for consumers keyed on them, and a dangling non-Latin relationship target flips from a silently-skipped warning (exit 0) to the same hard "Relationship target key missing" error (exit 1) that ASCII targets always had. Because transliteration can collapse distinct titles ("Cafe" with and without the accent) onto the same title-derived fallback `source_uri`, `buildSchemaEnvelope()` now salts those fallback collisions deterministically by record order (`item://cafe`, `item://cafe_2`) so the batch no longer fails with `schema.duplicate_source_uri`; explicit duplicate `source_uri` values remain a schema error. `waaseyaa/cli` now suggests `ext-intl`. New coverage in `IngestRunCommandTest`: a syllabics-titled record maps successfully end to end (exit 0, non-empty stable key) where it previously failed the whole batch; a diacritic title latinizes to a readable key (skipped without ext-intl); two titles that transliterate to the same fallback source_uri map with distinct salted source_uris (exit 0, RED pre-fix as a whole-batch `schema.duplicate_source_uri` failure); a syllabics title referenced by a relationship resolves to the same key on both ends; a plain ASCII title still produces its pre-fix key; and a whitespace-only title still errors as missing title.
- **`queue:retry` deleted the failed-job row before dispatching it, so a corrupt payload or a throwing dispatch permanently lost the job with no way to retry it again (audit A7, F5).** `QueueRetryHandler::execute()` called `FailedJobRepository::retry($id)` first, which stamps `retried_at` and deletes the row, and only afterwards unserialized the payload and called `$this->queue->dispatch($message)` with no try/catch: by the time either check could fail, the row was already gone. The `retryAll()` path had the same ordering bug per job, plus its "corrupt payload" skip message used an em dash. Fixed by reordering both paths around the row: `find()` the record first, unserialize and validate the payload before touching storage, dispatch inside a try/catch on `\Throwable`, and only call `retry($id)` (which stamps and deletes) once dispatch has actually succeeded. A corrupt payload or a failing dispatch now leaves the row in place, prints an error naming the job id, and exits 1 instead of exiting 0 while quietly discarding the job. `retryAll()` applies the same per-job reordering, tracks failures separately from successes, reports both counts, and now returns 1 if any job failed to re-dispatch (previously always 0). New coverage in `QueueRetryHandlerTest`: a throwing dispatch and a corrupt payload each leave the failed-job row queryable via `find()` afterward (RED pre-fix: the row was already deleted), and a mixed `retryAll()` run removes the succeeding job while preserving the failing one and exits 1.
- **The NorthCloud SQLite response cache was default-enabled but could never be constructed, and `NorthCloudClient` treated non-2xx HTTP responses as success (so an error body would have been cached and returned to callers had the cache ever worked), while all diagnostics went to `error_log` instead of the framework logger.** `NorthCloudServiceProvider` resolved `NorthCloudCache::class` inside a `try { … } catch (\Throwable) {}` with an empty body, but `NorthCloudCache`'s constructor type-hints a raw `\PDO` with no container binding, so `resolve()` threw `RuntimeException("No binding registered for …NorthCloudCache")` on every call and the empty catch swallowed it silently: the cache had never once been active in any environment, and the configured `cache.ttl` was never threaded through. Independently, `NorthCloudClient::doRequest()`/`doAuthenticatedRequest()` fetched via `file_get_contents(..., ['ignore_errors' => true])` and never inspected the status line, so a 4xx/5xx body was a non-false string returned to callers as if successful and (line 337 pre-fix) written to the cache for the full TTL. Fixed three ways in one change. (1) `NorthCloudCache` is now bound in `NorthCloudServiceProvider::register()` via a factory that resolves `\PDO::class` from the kernel-services bus (`ProviderRegistryKernelServices`) and threads `cache.ttl`; the client factory's previously-silent catch now logs the degradation (via `resolveOptional(LoggerInterface::class)`) instead of hiding it. (2) The client's transport is switched from the hand-rolled `file_get_contents` seam to the already-required `Waaseyaa\HttpClient\HttpClientInterface` (default `StreamHttpClient`), and both request paths now reject a non-2xx response BEFORE any cache write: `!$response->isSuccess()` logs a warning and returns `null`, so an error body is never returned-as-success and never cached; an `HttpRequestException` is caught and logged the same way. (3) All 13 `error_log()` callsites (10 in `NorthCloudClient`, 3 in `NcSyncService`) are replaced by an injected `Waaseyaa\Foundation\Log\LoggerInterface` (default `NullLogger`), wired from the provider; the bearer token is never logged. New coverage: `NorthCloudClientTest` non-2xx-returns-null (RED pre-fix, GREEN post-fix), 2xx-positive, authenticated-path non-2xx, and `NorthCloudServiceProviderTest` container `\PDO` cache-resolution, success-is-cached-and-second-request-skips-transport, and non-2xx-leaves-cache-empty; the existing client tests migrated from the removed `callable` seam to an `HttpClientInterface` fake.
- **`packages/ai-vector` shipped no ServiceProvider, so its interfaces were unbound: the documented `semantic:warm` / `semantic:refresh` CLI commands crashed with `No binding for "Waaseyaa\AI\Vector\EmbeddingStorageInterface"`, and `EntityEmbedder::searchSimilar()` returned access-UNFILTERED similarity hits.** `ai-vector`'s `composer.json` `extra` had no `waaseyaa` key and no provider class existed, so `EmbeddingStorageInterface` had no container binding; the CLI handlers (`SemanticWarmHandler`/`SemanticRefreshHandler`, each taking `SemanticIndexWarmer`) were autowired by `KernelHandlerContainer`, which hit `SemanticIndexWarmer`'s `EmbeddingStorageInterface` parameter (an interface, `class_exists()` false) and threw, aborting both commands (a documented operations-playbook step). Separately, `EntityEmbedder::searchSimilar()` returned `VectorStoreInterface::search()` results verbatim, with no `EntityAccessHandler` / account gating: a dormant leak (zero production callers today, and both live search surfaces, `SearchController` via `search.semantic` and `VectorSearchTool`, bypass it, but any future caller would expose entity ids and label/bundle metadata regardless of view access). Fixed. (1) New `AiVectorServiceProvider` (registered via `extra.waaseyaa.providers`) binds `EmbeddingStorageInterface` to `SqliteEmbeddingStorage` (resolving `\PDO::class` from the kernel-services bus, table `embeddings` to match the HTTP search path) and binds `SemanticIndexWarmer` DIRECTLY (its nullable `?EmbeddingProviderInterface` cannot be reflection-autowired), so the CLI commands resolve; with no `ai.embedding_provider` configured the warmer receives a null provider and reports `skipped_no_provider` (correct graceful degrade instead of a crash). `EmbeddingProviderInterface` is bound only when one IS configured (`EmbeddingProviderFactory::fromConfig()` returns null otherwise, and `resolve()` requires an object), which both feeds the warmer and lets a host-wired `vector.search` tool resolve a real embedding provider off the bus (the tool lives in `waaseyaa/ai-tools`, which duck-types these interfaces via `\Closure` resolvers by design, so binding the interfaces is the ai-vector-side enablement; the tool's closure-wiring itself remains the host's responsibility per that existing design). (2) `EntityEmbedder::searchSimilar()` now REQUIRES an `AccountInterface` and filters fail-closed: each candidate is loaded through its entity type's repository and dropped unless it exists and `EntityAccessHandler::check($entity, 'view', $account)->isAllowed()`, mirroring `SearchController`'s gate; an unregistered entity type or a deleted entity is dropped. New coverage: `AiVectorServiceProviderTest` (storage + warmer resolve, `skipped_no_provider` degrade, provider bound-when-configured / unbound-otherwise) and `EntityEmbedderTest` / `VectorSearchIntegrationTest` access-filter exploit tests (RED pre-gate, GREEN post-gate) plus the migrated constructor call sites.
- `ai:purge-runs` now only deletes terminal runs (completed, failed, cancelled); queued, running, awaiting_approval and cancelling runs are no longer age-purged out from under the reaper/approval flow.
- **`schedule:run` always exited 0 even when a due task threw, so cron MAILTO, k8s `backoffLimit`, and healthcheck-on-exit-code patterns never saw a scheduler failure (audit A7, F9).** `ScheduleRunHandler::execute()` unconditionally returned 0 after calling `ScheduleRunner::run()`, and `run()` itself only aggregated successfully-executed task names, so a failure caught by `runTask()`'s per-task try/catch (kept intentionally, so one failing task never blocks the others) was silently dropped: the handler had no way to learn anything had failed. Worse, when EVERY due task failed, `$result->count === 0` triggered the misleading "No scheduled tasks are due." message even though tasks had actually run and thrown. Fixed by threading failure counts through the return value instead of changing the per-task isolation: `ScheduleRunResult` gains two new optional, backward-compatible fields, `failedCount` and `failedTaskNames`, and `ScheduleRunner::run()` now populates them from each task's `STATUS_FAILED` outcome alongside the existing success aggregation. `ScheduleRunHandler::execute()` now prints each failed task name and a failure count, returns 1 whenever `failedCount > 0`, and only prints "No scheduled tasks are due." when both `count` and `failedCount` are zero. New coverage: a handler test where the only due task throws (exit 1, task name in output, no misleading "no tasks due" message), a mixed success/failure test confirming both tasks still ran, and runner-level tests asserting `failedCount`/`failedTaskNames` for all-success, all-failure, and mixed sweeps.
- **`audit:prune` recorded the WRONG `deleted_count` in its own tamper-evident self-audit record whenever `--kind` narrowed the match set, so the tamper-evident audit trail lied about its own prune (audit A7, F10).** `PruneCommand` deletes in two disjoint paths: Path A deletes ALL sealed-segment rows up to the checkpoint horizon regardless of `--kind` (required for chain integrity, by design), while Path B kind-filters only the unsealed tail. The self-audit record's `deleted_count`, and the `--confirm` refusal message's row count, were both computed from a kind-filtered `AuditQuery`, not from what Path A actually deletes: with `--kind` set, an operator confirming "N events" and the resulting self-audit record could both understate the real number of rows removed. Fixed by computing the sealed count and the kind-filtered unsealed-tail count once, before the confirm gate, and using their sum (the real total) everywhere: the `--confirm` refusal message now reports the real total plus a sealed/unsealed breakdown, the final "Pruned N audit events" message reports the real total, and the self-audit `deleted_count` attribute equals the real total. The old kind-filtered number is retained under a new, clearly-named `kind_filtered_match_count` attribute for anyone inspecting the self-audit trail, so `deleted_count` itself is never misleading again. New coverage in `PruneCommandTest`: seeding a sealed segment with mixed event kinds and pruning with a narrow `--kind` filter now asserts `deleted_count` equals the actual rows deleted (not the kind-filtered match count), the confirm-refusal message reports the real total, a no-filter case locks in that the reported number is unchanged when `--kind` matches everything, and the dry-run breakdown (already accurate) is locked in as a regression guard.
- **`TelescopeRequestMiddleware::process()` recorded every request through a raw-PDO write chain with no fault isolation, so a telemetry write failure (locked SQLite file, full disk, a JSON-encode error) turned an already-successful response into an uncaught 500 (audit L6-telescope.md, CRITICAL, #1915, R16, #1916).** `recordRequest()` is wired via `#[AsMiddleware(pipeline: 'http', priority: 100)]` and enabled by default, so this was live on every request the moment the telescope package was installed. Fixed by wrapping the `recordRequest()` call in try/catch: on failure it logs via the framework `LoggerInterface` (new optional constructor param, default `NullLogger`) and the response proceeds unchanged — the same best-effort side-effect convention already used for cache invalidation and broadcasting. New coverage: `TelescopeRequestMiddlewareTest` (`a_store_failure_does_not_crash_the_response`, plus a happy-path regression guard).
- **`QueueController::retry()` (the admin JSON:API endpoint) called `FailedJobRepositoryInterface::retry()` — which deletes the failed-job row — BEFORE unserializing and dispatching the payload, so a corrupt payload or a `dispatch()` failure permanently destroyed the job (audit R16, #1915, #1917).** Mirrors the CLI `QueueRetryHandler` fix already shipped in #1908: find the record, unserialize, dispatch, and only forget the row from the failed table after a successful dispatch. The 404 (unknown id) and happy-path 204 responses are unchanged; the 422 (corrupt payload) response now leaves the record in the failed table instead of destroying it, and a new 502 response covers a caught dispatch failure, also leaving the record in place (previously an uncaught exception here would have lost the job with no HTTP response at all). New/updated coverage in `QueueControllerTest`: `retryReturns422WhenPayloadIsCorruptAndKeepsRecord`, `retryLeavesRecordInPlaceWhenDispatchThrows`.
- **`EntitySchemaOrgMapper::toScriptTag()` 500'd the ENTIRE page render whenever an entity's label or description contained an ill-formed UTF-8 byte sequence, because `json_encode(..., JSON_THROW_ON_ERROR)` throws on invalid UTF-8 and the SSR caller's broad try/catch spans the whole render (audit L3-seo.md, MAJOR, #1915, R16, #1918).** Fixed by scrubbing ill-formed UTF-8 (`mb_scrub`) on the label, `labelOverride`, and description before they enter the JSON-LD node — valid UTF-8, including non-ASCII scripts such as Anishinaabemowin diacritics/syllabics, passes through unchanged. `toScriptTag()` also wraps its own `json_encode()` as a second line of defense for callers that build the node directly, logging via a new optional `?LoggerInterface` constructor param before degrading to an empty string (no JSON-LD block) rather than crashing the page. New coverage in `EntitySchemaOrgMapperTest`: scrubbed label/description/labelOverride cases, plus a raw-node case exercising the catch branch directly (asserts `''` returned and a warning logged).
- **`Installer::install()` (FrankenPHP binary installer) correctly failed closed on a checksum MISMATCH, but silently installed an UNVERIFIED binary whenever the GitHub release digest was simply unavailable — API unreachable/rate-limited (60 req/hr unauthenticated), malformed response, missing `assets`, or an asset with no `digest` field — gating nothing before the fact (audit L6-frankenphp.md, MAJOR, supply-chain, #1915, R16, #1750, #1922).** `Installer` now takes a constructor flag, `allowUnverified` (default `false`); when the digest is unavailable and the flag is unset, `install()` throws before any bytes are moved into place. A checksum mismatch is unaffected either way — always fail-closed, never bypassable by the flag. `frankenphp:install` gains `--allow-unverified` (default false); `dev`'s auto-install path deliberately does NOT get the opt-out — it always fails closed and points the operator at running `frankenphp:install --allow-unverified` by hand. New/updated coverage in `InstallerTest` (missing-digest fail-closed inverted from the old fail-open assertion, `allowUnverified: true` installs-unverified / still-verifies-when-available / still-rejects-a-mismatch) and a new `InstallCommandTest` pinning the flag's registration and default.
- **`AnchorCatalogController::catalog()` (the public, unauthenticated `/.well-known/waaseyaa-anchors.json` endpoint) constructed a fresh `AnchorRegistry` on every single request instead of reusing the shared singleton `WayfindingServiceProvider` already bound — re-deriving the entire anchor catalog (every registered entity type and field, through `SchemaPresenter`) on every hit (audit L4-wayfinding.md, MAJOR, #1915, R16, #1923).** `AnchorCatalogController` and `EmitBeaconController` (same gap on the authenticated emit path, folded in as a review follow-up) now take the shared `AnchorRegistry` via constructor injection, and `AnchorRegistry::catalog()` is memoized per instance — safe because the catalog derives only from boot-stable entity-type/field definitions, never from request/record/account state, so per-instance caching holds even under a long-lived FrankenPHP worker process. No change to the published catalog shape or the FR-005/FR-007 contracts. New coverage: `AnchorRegistryTest::catalog_is_memoized_per_instance`, `AnchorCatalogControllerTest`/`EmitBeaconControllerTest` reuse assertions.
- **Nothing seeded the first `thread_participant` row when a `message_thread` was created, so an ordinary non-admin account could create a thread but never read or post to it afterward — `MessagingAccessPolicy::fieldAccess()` requires the acting account to already be a participant before creating the FIRST participant/message row, and `createAccess()` has no visibility into the target thread to grant a bypass (audit L3-messaging.md, CRITICAL, #1915, R16, #1924).** New `ThreadParticipantBootstrapSubscriber` (`packages/messaging/src/EventSubscriber/`), subscribed in `MessagingServiceProvider::boot()`, listens for `EntityEvents::PRE_SAVE`/`POST_SAVE` on `MessageThread` and, on genuine creation (`isNew()` captured at `PRE_SAVE`, mirroring `EntityLifecycleAuditListener`'s two-phase pattern), inserts a `thread_participant` row for the creator with `role: 'owner'`. The seeded account is read from `AccountContextInterface::current()`, never the entity's own mutable `created_by` field, matching the actor-attribution discipline `EntityLifecycleAuditListener` established (#1645). No acting account (CLI/queue/system context) leaves the thread with no seeded participant, same as before. New coverage in `ThreadParticipantBootstrapSubscriberTest` (4 tests against a real `EntityRepository` + `EventDispatcher`, not mocks): creation seeds the creator as owner participant; the creator can immediately read/post while an outsider still cannot (the audit's acceptance scenario); no acting account leaves the thread unseeded; updating an existing thread does not reseed.
- **`ConfigManager::import()` (the legacy config-sync path) swallowed every create/update/delete failure into `ConfigImportResult::$errors` and kept applying the rest of the batch, so a single failed write partway through a multi-key import could leave the active config store silently partial; `FileStorage::write()` was also a bare non-atomic `file_put_contents()`, so a crash mid-write could leave a truncated config file visible (audit L1-config.md, findings M2/M3, #1915, R16, #1925).** `import()` now fails fast: the first write or delete failure throws `ConfigImportFailedException::applyFailed()` (the existing exception class the modern `Sync/ConfigImporter` already uses, reused rather than duplicated), naming the failed key and reporting what had already been applied. A sync-side READ failure is unchanged (still collected into `$errors`, batch continues) since nothing was partially applied for that entry. `config:import` (`ConfigImportHandler`) now catches the exception and reports via `$io->error()` with a nonzero exit. `FileStorage::write()` now writes to a writer-unique sibling temp file under `LOCK_EX` and `rename()`s it into place atomically, matching the pattern the modern `Sync/ConfigSyncRepository::put()` already used; `ensureDirectory()`'s `mkdir()` mode tightened from `0o777` to `0o775`. The modern config-sync system (`Sync/ConfigImporter` etc.) and the dual-system architecture itself are out of scope (audit finding M1). New coverage in `ConfigManagerTest` (fail-fast-and-aborts-on-first-write-failure, partial-apply message contents) and `ConfigImportHandlerTest` (catches the exception, reports the message, exits 1).
- **`Fts5SearchProvider::search()`, when an access checker is wired, derived `totalHits`/facets from a bounded access-filtered scan but fetched the actual page from an INDEPENDENT, unfiltered `ORDER BY ... LIMIT/OFFSET` query over all candidate rows — a fixed OFFSET walking the wrong sequence, so an accessible document sharing a page window with a forbidden one was silently dropped from every page while `totalPages` promised a page that could never fully materialize (audit L3-search.md, MAJOR, #1915, R16, #1926).** Replaced the split count/fetch with one bounded, ordered, access-filtered scan (`accessFilteredSearch()`) that derives `totalHits`, the requested page, and facets together, so paging always agrees with the total it is measured against. The no-access-checker fast SQL path (`COUNT` + `LIMIT/OFFSET`) is untouched.
