Documented trade-off: the unified scan selects heavier columns (`snippet()`, full row data, rank) for up to the existing `MAX_ACCESS_SCAN` (1000) candidate rows on every access-filtered query, a bounded constant-factor cost increase over the old lighter count-only scan; a two-phase fetch (locate the page window cheaply, then fetch only the `pageSize` rows needing full metadata) is noted as a possible follow-up. New coverage: `Fts5SearchProviderPaginationAccessFilteredTest` (3-document fixture with a denied document sitting in page 1's window; asserts every accessible document is reachable across pages with a consistent total, confirmed RED against the pre-fix provider and GREEN after).

### Removed

- **`AbstractMakeCommand`**, an unused byte-duplicate predecessor of `AbstractMakeHandler` carrying the old, unhardened `toPascalCase` pattern, deleted (audit A7, F16). Zero callers verified repo-wide: no class extended it, no test instantiated it, no config referenced it, the only remaining references were two `phpstan-dead-code-baseline.neon` ignore entries (also removed) and historical planning docs.

## [0.1.0-alpha.254] - 2026-07-05

### Security

- **The GraphQL entity mutation surface let an ANONYMOUS caller enumerate the existence of any entity id: `/graphql` is `allowAll()` and executes mutations for the anonymous account, and `update{Type}`/`delete{Type}` threw textually distinct errors for "entity absent" vs "entity access denied" (audit A9, R11).** `GraphQlRouteProvider::registerRoutes()` (`packages/graphql/src/GraphQlRouteProvider.php:19-23`) registers `/graphql` `allowAll()` (anonymous-reachable), and `GraphQlEndpoint::handle()` executed every mutation for whatever account was bound, with no operation-type-aware auth gate of its own. `EntityResolver::resolveUpdate()`/`resolveDelete()` (`packages/graphql/src/Resolver/EntityResolver.php:198-200,227-229` pre-fix) threw `UserError("Entity not found: {type}/{id}")` for an absent id, but `GraphQlAccessGuard::assertUpdateAccess()`/`assertDeleteAccess()` (`packages/graphql/src/Access/GraphQlAccessGuard.php:42-54`) threw the textually distinct `UserError("Access denied: cannot update/delete entity")` for a REAL entity the caller could not modify, so an anonymous caller (or any caller lacking modify rights) could enumerate the existence of any entity id, including e.g. participant-only messaging rows, by diffing the two error messages, despite every per-entity `AccessPolicyInterface` being itself correct. REST's `PATCH`/`DELETE` have the identical un-collapsed absent-vs-forbidden shape but are `requireAuthentication()`-gated, so REST was not anonymously exploitable; GraphQL was, purely because the auth gate was missing. Fixed at BOTH layers, fail-closed: **(a) endpoint anonymous-mutation gate**, the primary close: `GraphQlEndpoint::handle()` now rejects ANY mutation (reusing the existing `selectsMutation($query, $operationName)` helper already used at the GET-mutation CSRF check) when `!$this->account->isAuthenticated()`, for every HTTP method (the pre-existing GET-mutation check at :93 only covered GET), BEFORE the schema is built or any resolver runs. The security-relevant property is precise: anonymous mutation operations are rejected with a UNIFORM error (`"Authentication required for mutation operations."`) that carries no entity id or type, before any resolver runs, and the mutation NEVER executes, so there is no distinguishable path and no timing side channel for the whole generated mutation surface (every type, create/update/delete, aliases, `operationName`-selected, because `selectsMutation()` gates at the operation-type level). (The rejection sets `statusCode` 401 in the endpoint's return array, but note the HTTP-status envelope wiring is a SEPARATE pre-existing issue, out of R11 scope and NOT touched here: `GraphQlRouter::handle()` (`packages/graphql/src/Http/Router/GraphQlRouter.php:62`) hardcodes `jsonApiResponse(200, $result)`, double-wrapping the body and serving every GraphQL response as HTTP 200 regardless of `statusCode`, so a client does not currently see a real HTTP 401. That envelope bug is orthogonal to this oracle (the rejection still names no id/type and the mutation still never runs) and is tracked as a separate follow-up.) `parseRequest()` was confirmed to accept only a single `{query, variables, operationName}` document (a JSON-array (batched) body finds no `query` key on the list and falls through to the pre-existing "Missing query" 400), so there is no batched-request path that could smuggle a mutation past this check; batching is simply not supported by this endpoint. **(b) resolver not-found/forbidden collapse**, defense-in-depth for the authenticated-but-unauthorized case the endpoint gate does not cover: `EntityResolver::resolveUpdate()`/`resolveDelete()` now catch the guard's `UserError` and rethrow the SAME `"Entity not found: {type}/{id}"` the absent-entity branch already throws, so an authenticated caller without modify rights also gets an indistinguishable not-found response. This mirrors the pre-existing `resolveSingle()` read path (already returns `null` uniformly for both absent and view-denied, unchanged here) and the same not-found/forbidden collapse pattern used to close the anonymous-MCP-read-tier existence-oracle class (audit R8-c). The collapse covers BOTH the entity-level `update`/`delete` denial AND the per-FIELD `edit` denial: `resolveUpdate()`'s field-access loop (`assertFieldEditAccess()` per input field) runs INSIDE the same try/catch, because entity-level `update` can be ALLOWED while a specific field's `edit` is FORBIDDEN, e.g. `NodeAccessPolicy` grants `edit any {type} content` at the entity level but field-forbids `uid`/`created`/`changed` for non-admins, and those fields are non-`readOnly` so they appear in the generated update input, and the distinguishable `"Access denied: cannot edit field '{name}'"` fires only for a REAL entity (the absent branch already returned "not found"), which would re-open the identical existence oracle for any ordinary editor whose entity-level update is permitted (so neither the anonymous gate nor the entity-level collapse would have caught it). Only the two access-guard calls sit inside the catch; the `FieldableInterface` support check and the `set()`/`save()` field-VALIDATION stay OUTSIDE it, so a genuine validation/support error for an AUTHORIZED caller is surfaced accurately and never masked as "not found". Both access checks still RUN (a forbidden field-edit is still refused, `bin/check-dead-code` stays clean); only their denial WORDING is collapsed. `resolveCreate()` (`:167`) was audited and left unchanged: it never loads an entity by id, so its "cannot create {type}" denial carries no id-based existence signal; there is no oracle shape to close there. Both layers are intentionally MORE conservative than REST's authenticated-write convention (REST still distinguishes 403 from 404 for an authenticated caller); that is the safe direction for closing the whole GraphQL mutation surface with no residual distinguishable path. Anonymous public QUERIES are unaffected: the new gate only inspects `selectsMutation()`, never touching the query path, and the route itself is intentionally left `allowAll()` rather than converted to `requireAuthentication()` (which would have broken legitimate anonymous public reads). New exploit-closed coverage: `GraphQlMutationOracleTest` (`tests/Integration/GraphQL/`, real endpoint + real access-policy stack): anonymous update/delete on an absent vs. a real-but-forbidden id (distinguishable pre-fix, uniformly rejected post-fix), the same pair for an authenticated-but-non-admin caller (distinguishable pre-fix, indistinguishable "Entity not found" post-fix), the field-level residual `testAuthenticatedFieldEditOracleIsClosed` (an authenticated editor whose entity-level update IS allowed but whose edit of a forbidden field is denied: absent id vs. real id must both read "Entity not found"; distinguishable "cannot edit field" pre-fix, collapsed post-fix, via a `FieldOraclePolicy` implementing both `AccessPolicyInterface` and `FieldAccessPolicyInterface`) with `testEditorCanStillUpdateAnAllowedField` as the positive control (an allowed-field edit still succeeds, proving the field check still runs and the collapse does not over-block), and a control confirming anonymous reads of publicly-viewable content are unaffected throughout; `EntityResolverTest::{resolveUpdateDeniedAndAbsentAreIndistinguishable, resolveDeleteDeniedAndAbsentAreIndistinguishable}` (resolver-level unit pins); `GraphQlEndpointTest::{anonymousPostMutationIsRejectedBeforeExecution, anonymousGetMutationIsRejectedBeforeExecution, anonymousQueryIsNotAffectedByTheMutationGate, authenticatedPostMutationIsNotBlockedByTheAnonymousGate}`. All new cases confirmed RED against pre-fix code (distinguishable messages / mutation executed for anonymous) and GREEN after.**
- **`InstallHandler` created the admin account with the WRONG entity keys and an UNHASHED password, breaking admin login and persisting the operator's `--admin-password` in cleartext at rest (audit A7 F1, R10 WP1).** `InstallHandler::execute()` (`packages/cli/src/Handler/InstallHandler.php:41-50` pre-fix) built the admin's `$userValues` with `'email' => $adminEmail` and `'password' => $adminPassword` (raw plaintext) — but `Waaseyaa\User\User`'s real entity keys are `mail` and `pass` (`pass` must always hold a bcrypt hash). `email`/`password` are unrecognized by `SqlStorageDriver::splitForWrite()`, so both were routed verbatim into the `_data` JSON blob: (a) `mail`/`pass` were left empty, so `User::checkPassword()` always returned `false` — admin login silently broken even though `install` printed success — and (b) the operator's plaintext password was persisted at rest in cleartext under the key `password` inside `_data`. Fixed by using the correct keys and hashing the password before it ever reaches the values array — `'mail' => $adminEmail` and, when a password is supplied, `'pass' => password_hash($adminPassword, PASSWORD_DEFAULT)` — mirroring the already-correct sibling `UserCreateHandler`. New exploit-closed coverage in `InstallHandlerCredentialTest` (real SQLite-backed `SqlStorageDriver` + `EntityRepository` + `User` entity, no mocks on the persistence path): `adminCanAuthenticateWithTheProvidedPasswordAfterInstall` asserts the installed admin can authenticate with the supplied `--admin-password` (`User::checkPassword()`) AND that the raw plaintext string never appears anywhere in the persisted row (including the `_data` blob), while the hashed value under `pass` matches a bcrypt (`$2y$`) shape; `adminMailColumnIsPopulated` asserts `--admin-email` lands in the `mail` column. Both confirmed RED against pre-fix code (login failed; plaintext present in `_data`) and GREEN after. Adversarial sweep of every other production user-creation site (`packages/auth/src/Controller/*`, `UserCreateHandler`, `DevAdminAccount`, OIDC claim mapping, structured-import) found no sibling instance of the bug — all already use `mail`/`pass` with hashed values.**
- **`AgentDefinition::$requiresCapability` was a documented, author-facing access gate that was plumbed end-to-end (attribute → manifest → registry → definition) but never enforced (audit A7 F2, R10 WP2).** An agent declaring `requiresCapability: 'agent.translate'` (or any other capability) would run for ANY caller regardless of permissions — reachable via both `bin/waaseyaa ai:run` (CLI) and `POST /api/ai/agent/run` (API). Both entry points dispatch a `RunAgent` Messenger message that is handled by the SAME `RunAgentHandler::__invoke()` (`packages/ai-agent/src/Message/RunAgentHandler.php`), so the fix lives there once — after `$definition` and the initiator `$account` are resolved and before `AgentExecutor::executeRun()` is called: if `$definition->requiresCapability` is set and `!$account->hasPermission(...)`, the run is refused (marked terminal `Failed`, `error_code='missing_capability'`, `run_failed` SSE broadcast) and the executor/agent loop, tools, and LLM never run. Fail-closed: an anonymous or permission-less initiator is refused identically to an explicit denial, since `AccountInterface::hasPermission()` returns `false` for both — there is no code path that silently skips the check. Covered end-to-end via the shared `RunAgentHandler` chokepoint (`tests/Integration/PhaseN/AgentRuntime/RequiresCapabilityGateTest.php`): denial when the capability is missing (zero `AgentAuditLog` rows — proof the executor never started), the positive control (authorized caller still runs to `Completed`), no-op when `requiresCapability` is null, and the anonymous fail-closed case. **Adversarial sweep of `AgentDefinition`'s other declared-but-possibly-unenforced fields:** `destructiveDefault` IS genuinely enforced end-to-end via `AgentRunController::create()` → `AgentRunRequestValidator::resolveDestructiveApproval()` → `AgentExecutor`'s HITL gate for the API entry (though `AiRunCommand` does not thread a registered definition's `destructiveDefault` through — the CLI always resolves HITL mode from its own `--destructive-approval` flag, defaulting to `none`; tracked separately, not a bypass since `none` is the conservative default). `maxIterations` and `system` are genuinely consumed by `RunAgentHandler`. Two gaps found and NOT folded into this fix because they are larger than "small + same-class" (tracked for R10b): (1) `AgentDefinition::$tools` (documented as a "tool name whitelist") is never passed to `AgentExecutor::executeRun()` — `RunAgentHandler` hardcodes `tools: []` — and `AgentExecutor`'s tool-call dispatch resolves ANY tool by name from the global `ToolRegistryInterface` with no allow-list check against the agent's declared `tools[]`, so a provider response naming an out-of-allow-list tool would still execute; (2) `AgentDefinition::$model` is parsed from the bundle/definition but never passed to the provider call — providers use their own constructor-bound model instead, so a per-agent model override is silently ignored.
- **`make:*` codegen commands interpolated unvalidated, unescaped CLI input directly into GENERATED PHP that executes on `autoload`, so a quote/newline/PHP-breakout payload in `--name`/`--domain`/`--event`/`--fields`/`--create`/`--table` yielded arbitrary PHP in a generated file, and there was no entity-type-id/table-name allowlist (audit A7 F3+F4; R10 WP3).** `AbstractMakeHandler::toPascalCase()` (`packages/cli/src/Command/Make/AbstractMakeHandler.php`) fast-pathed already-PascalCase input but its fallback (`str_replace('_', '', ucwords($name, '_'))`) passed quotes/newlines/semicolons/slashes straight through into a generated class name. Every downstream `make:*` command interpolated that (or the raw `--name`/type-id) directly into a single-quoted PHP attribute literal, a bare class/`use` position, or a filesystem path, with no allowlist: `MakeContentTypeHandler` (`$typeId = strtolower($name)` with no format check, `entity_reference` `target` unvalidated, `writeFile()` path built straight from the derived class name), `MakeEntityTypeHandler`/`MakePluginHandler`/`MakeProviderHandler` (same unchecked `strtolower($name)` machine-id pattern), `MakeMigrationHandler` (migration `name` argument built the output filename with no traversal check — `../evil` escaped the `migrations/` directory — and `--create`/`--table` were interpolated raw into `$schema->create('...', ...)` in the generated stub, a quote breaking out into arbitrary top-level PHP), and `MakeListenerHandler` (`--event` landed unescaped both in a bare `use ...;` statement and in the `__invoke(EventType $event)` type-hint position — a payload there could close the parameter list and splice a new top-level statement into the generated listener). A single `'` broke out of an attribute string literal; a newline plus a statement injected top-level PHP that runs the instant the generated file is autoloaded — no request ever needs to invoke it. **Fix — validate at the boundary, escape at the sink (the pattern `ExtensionScaffoldHandler` already used correctly, now the framework-wide template):** added `AbstractMakeHandler::validateIdentifier()`/`validateMachineName()` — strict allowlists (`^[A-Za-z0-9_]+$` for values that become a class/identifier base, `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$` for machine ids/table names, plus a new FQCN allowlist for `--event`) that REJECT (never silently sanitize) with a message naming the offending input, applied at the TOP of `MakeContentType`, `MakeEntityType`, `MakeEntity`, `MakePlugin`, `MakeProvider`, `MakeJob`, `MakePolicy`, `MakeListener` (name + `--event`), and `MakeTest`, before the value is ever used to build a class name, id, namespace, path, or literal; `MakeMigrationHandler` gained the same gate on its `name` argument (closing the `../evil` filename traversal) and on the resolved `--create`/`--table`/guessed table name (closing the stub-literal breakout), plus a defensive machine-name check on `--add-translations`'s entity-type id before it reaches the generated filename/doc-comments. `toPascalCase()` was hardened defensively to strip any non-`[A-Za-z0-9_]` character even if reached with unvalidated input. `MakeContentTypeHandler::parseFields()`'s `entity_reference` `target` now gets the same machine-name check already applied to field names; its generated attribute literals (`label`, field labels, the reference target) are now `addslashes()`-escaped at the sink regardless of upstream validation. **Adversarial sweep of other codegen/scaffold handlers** (`packages/cli/src/Handler/`): `ExtensionScaffoldHandler` — confirmed the good in-repo model (allowlist regexes + `addslashes()`); `RelationshipTypeScaffoldHandler`/`BundleScaffoldHandler` — output is `json_encode()`-only, not generated PHP or a path built from input; not vulnerable. `MakeStorageMigrationHandler`/`StorageMigrationTemplate` — real: the entity-type id (from `--add-translations`/the `entity_type_id` argument, or any consumer holding an `EntityTypeInterface` directly — `Waaseyaa\Entity\Tests\Helper\TestEntityType` confirms the entity layer itself does not constrain ids to a machine-name shape) fed a docblock comment (a `*/` in the id would terminate it early, exposing the remainder as executable PHP) and a single-quoted `$conn->quoteIdentifier('...')`/`BackfillHelper::backfill('...', ...)` literal; small and in scope, folded in: `MakeStorageMigrationHandler` now rejects a non-machine-name entity-type id before building the filename, and `StorageMigrationTemplate::render()` now escapes the table name (`addslashes()`) and sanitizes the id used in the doc-comment (`*/` → `* /`). `AddTranslationsMigrationGenerator` — same root cause found while auditing the sibling generator: its `INDEX_NAME` const was interpolated raw into a single-quoted literal (now routed through the class's own pre-existing `phpStringLiteral()` escaper, used everywhere else in the file) and the entity-type id appeared raw in several doc-comments across `render()`/`renderTwoAxisFromRevisionable()` (now routed through a new `commentSafe()` helper); folded in as the same small, mechanical, same-file fix. `AddRevisionsMigrationGenerator` — the sibling revision-axis generator carried the IDENTICAL unfixed vulnerability (found in the R10b adversarial re-sweep): `INDEX_NAME`/`REV_INDEX_NAME`/`TX_REV_INDEX_NAME` built by raw concatenation from `$entityType->id()` and interpolated unescaped into single-quoted literals, plus the raw entity-type id in several `render()`/`renderTwoAxisFromTranslatable()` doc-comments (a `*/` in the id terminates the comment early and exposes the remainder as top-level PHP on autoload). Folded in with the SAME fix as its sibling: index/constraint-name consts routed through the class's own `phpStringLiteral()` escaper, doc-comment ids through a new `commentSafe()` helper. New coverage in `AddRevisionsMigrationGeneratorTest` (`singleAxis…`/`twoAxis…EscapesAMaliciousEntityTypeIdIntoInertData`): a malicious id embedding BOTH a single-quote (index-literal breakout) and a `*/` (doc-comment breakout) plus a `system('touch <sentinel>')` — the generated source must pass `php -l` AND, when `require`d (which runs all top-level code), must NOT create the sentinel; confirmed RED against the unescaped generator (the `*/` breakout produced a parse error) and GREEN after. `FixtureScaffoldHandler`'s unvalidated `--output` path (arbitrary-file-write of JSON data, not code injection) is noted but out of scope for this PR — reported for tracking, not a `make:*`-style codegen breakout. Tests (`packages/cli/tests/`): every listed `make:*` command now has coverage rejecting a quote-breakout payload (`foo', system('x'); //`), a path-traversal payload (`../evil`), and a newline-injection payload in its identifier input, with NO file written and NO breakout reaching stdout; `make:listener` additionally covers a breakout in `--event` (both bare and backslash-qualified forms); `make:content-type` additionally covers a breakout in the `entity_reference` target; `make:migration` additionally covers a breakout in `--create`; positive-control tests assert the generated file still exists and contains the expected escaped content; the `make:content-type`, `make:migration`, and `AddRevisionsMigrationGenerator` positive/orthography controls additionally run `php -l` on the generated file to prove it parses (the stdout-only `make:*` handlers, e.g. entity/job/policy/provider/listener, assert the expected class/attribute text rather than linting a written file). `MakeMigrationAddTranslationsTest`/`MakeStorageMigration/EndToEndTest` add coverage proving a malicious-but-already-registered entity-type id (via the test-only `TestEntityType::stub()` escape hatch, bypassing the CLI's own name validation) is still rejected by the new handler-level defensive checks. All new tests confirmed RED against pre-fix code (via source-edit-and-restore of the new validation/escaping, never `git`) and GREEN after. **R10b hardening of the fix itself (two must-fix holes found in the fix's own adversarial review):** (1) **Trailing-newline regex bypass** — every allowlist used `^…$` WITHOUT the `D` modifier, so PHP's `$` matches before a trailing `\n`; a `name` with a trailing newline (e.g. `"Story\n"`) passed the gate and generated `final class Story\nServiceProvider` (a `php -l` parse error that also corrupts the composer-registered provider FQCN). Added the `D` modifier to EVERY allowlist — `IDENTIFIER_PATTERN`/`MACHINE_NAME_PATTERN`/`FQCN_PATTERN` and the `toPascalCase()` fast-path regex (the fast path's `^[A-Z][a-zA-Z0-9]*$` was the actual root cause: it returned the value unstripped, newline intact) in `AbstractMakeHandler`, plus `MakeStorageMigrationHandler`'s inline check and (pre-existing, folded in) `ExtensionScaffoldHandler`'s four allowlists. New `rejects_a_trailing_newline_name` tests (make:content-type writes no file; make:provider — name-only, no second gate — exits 1 with no class emitted); confirmed RED without `D` and GREEN after. (2) **Orthography regression (charter-critical)** — the initial ASCII-only `^[A-Za-z0-9_]+$` was applied to `name`, from which the human-facing label is derived, so a legitimate Indigenous-language content-type name with diacritics or Canadian Aboriginal Syllabics (a valid PHP identifier byte sequence — PHP allows identifier bytes ≥ 0x80 — that worked safely pre-fix) was now hard-rejected, directly against the framework's Indigenous-orthography charter. Made the allowlists Unicode-aware: `IDENTIFIER_PATTERN` → `/^[\p{L}\p{N}_]+$/uD`, `MACHINE_NAME_PATTERN` → `/^[\p{L}_][\p{L}\p{N}_]*$/uD`, `FQCN_PATTERN` Unicode per segment, the `toPascalCase()` fast-path/strip Unicode-aware, and the `entity_reference` target check switched to `MACHINE_NAME_PATTERN` (a reference target may itself be an Indigenous-orthography entity-type id). This STILL fully closes the injection — a string of `\p{L}\p{N}_` contains no quote, backslash, newline, null, whitespace, semicolon, paren, angle-bracket, dot, or slash, so it cannot break out of a single-quoted literal, a class-name token, a `use …;`, or a path (`..`/`/` rejected because `.`/`/` are not in the class) — while RESTORING the non-ASCII support pre-fix allowed; the storage layer already quotes identifiers (`quoteIdentifier`), so a Unicode machine-id/table-name is safe. Orthography regression-guard tests (`accepts_an_indigenous_orthography_name…`) assert a diacritic name AND a Canadian Aboriginal Syllabics name are ACCEPTED, generate `php -l`-clean PHP, and PRESERVE the non-Latin characters verbatim in both the class name and the label literal; confirmed RED against the ASCII pattern (the diacritic name was rejected) and GREEN after. No `--label` option was added and no non-ASCII is ever transliterated/stripped (that would corrupt orthography). `ExtensionScaffoldHandler` stays ASCII-only by design (developer-facing SDK package/class/namespace names, not orthography content) — only its trailing-newline `D` hole was closed.**
- **The SSR render path ran the entity-level `view` access check ONLY for `content`-group entity types, so a non-content-group, non-`node` entity reached via a `path_alias` rendered whole-entity to anonymous with NO entity-level access check at all (audit-remediation batch 2026-07-05, R8-a — the entity-level generalization of R6 PR2's M2 node gate).** `SsrPageHandler::shouldDenyContentGroupRender()` (`packages/ssr/src/SsrPageHandler.php:940-948` pre-fix) opened with `if (!$this->isContentGroupEntity($entityTypeId)) { return false; }`, and `EditorialVisibilityResolver::canRender()` (`packages/workflows/src/EditorialVisibilityResolver.php:27-29`) allows any non-`node` type outright — so the ONLY entity-level view gate on the SSR HTML/Markdown path skipped every entity type outside the `content` group entirely. `PathAliasResolver`/`PathAlias` (`packages/path/src/PathAlias.php`) store the target system path as an unvalidated free-text string with no restriction on which entity type an alias may target, so a `path_alias` pointing at, e.g., `oidc_client` (whose `AccessPolicy` returns Neutral/deny-by-default for anonymous `'view'`, `packages/oidc/src/Access/OidcClientAccessPolicy.php`) was disclosed WHOLE-ENTITY to anonymous once an admin-created alias to it existed. **Recon before fixing (deny-by-default safety check):** generalizing the gate to run `$accessHandler->check($entity, 'view', $account)->isAllowed()` for every type is deny-by-default — a type with no allow-granting policy yields Neutral, which is NOT allowed. Audited every shipped `AccessPolicyInterface` implementation and every registered `EntityType` group: every legitimately anonymously-viewable entity type already carries a policy that ACTIVELY grants `'view'` under some condition — `PublishedContentAccessPolicy`/`ContentAdminAccessPolicy` for the whole `content` group (published + no restriction), `MediaAccessPolicy`/`TermAccessPolicy` (published + `access media`/`access content` permission), `UserAccessPolicy` (active account + `access user profiles` permission), and the parent-cascade policies `EngagementAccessPolicy`/`ParentDelegatedAccessPolicy` (grant iff the parent entity is viewable via the identical `check()` call, so the generalized gate preserves them unchanged) — the same explicit-grant pattern `NodeAccessPolicy` already uses. Types with NO granting policy (`oidc_client`, and the `ConfigEntityAccessPolicy`-covered admin/config types `node_type`/`taxonomy_vocabulary`/`media_type`/`workflow`/`pipeline`/`path_alias`/`menu`/`menu_link`) were never meant to be publicly reachable — denying them by default is correct, not a regression. **Fix:** removed the `isContentGroupEntity()` early-return so `shouldDenyContentGroupRender()` — renamed `shouldDenyEntityRender()` to reflect the new type-agnostic scope — runs the SAME `accessHandler->check($entity, 'view', $account)` for EVERY entity type reachable via SSR render; the now-unused `isContentGroupEntity()` private method was removed (dead-code gate). `EditorialVisibilityResolver::canRender()` (Gate 1, publish/preview/workflow nuance) still runs FIRST and unchanged; both gates must pass. Fails closed exactly as before: no access handler wired -> deny. **Nodes and content-group types unaffected:** the gate's body for `content`-group entities (including `node`) is byte-identical to pre-fix — only the early-return that SKIPPED non-content-group types was removed — confirmed by the full pre-existing `SsrNodeViewAccessGateTest`/`SsrContentPublishedGateTest` suites passing unchanged (module renamed, assertions unchanged for the `content`-group cases). **Cache corollary:** `RenderCache::SCHEMA_VERSION` bumped `'v4'` -> `'v5'`, making every pre-fix cached render of a non-content-group entity unreachable so it is re-rendered through the fixed gate rather than continuing to serve a leaked whole-entity payload from cache. New exploit-closed coverage: `SsrEntityViewGateAllTypesTest::an_alias_to_a_deny_by_default_non_content_group_entity_is_denied_to_anonymous` (a real SQLite-backed non-content-group entity type + a real `path_alias` resolved end-to-end through `handleRenderPage()`, with a deny-by-default policy mirroring `OidcClientAccessPolicy` — confirmed RED against pre-fix code, the widget rendered 200 with its content; GREEN after) plus the required regression guard `::a_published_non_node_content_group_entity_still_renders_for_anonymous` (a published non-`node` `content`-group entity still renders 200 via the real boot-wired `PublishedContentAccessPolicy` shape, unaffected by the fix) and `SsrNodeViewAccessGateTest::a_standard_published_node_still_renders_for_anonymous_with_access_content` (unchanged, re-confirmed green) as the node-side positive control. `SsrContentPublishedGateTest`'s `never_gates_non_content_group_types` test — which pinned the NOW-WRONG invariant that non-content-group types are never gated — is replaced by `gates_non_content_group_types_with_no_granting_policy` (asserts the corrected invariant) and a new `never_gates_non_content_group_types_with_a_granting_policy` companion (a non-content-group type with its own view-granting policy still renders, proving the fix does not over-deny). **Scope:** `packages/ssr` only; the discovery/existence-oracle gate and `GenealogyPedigreeService` label-gating residuals noted alongside this finding in the R7 WP1 changelog entry ("Tracked-for-R8 residuals") remain separately tracked (R8-b/R8-c) and are out of scope for this PR.**
- **⚠ INTENTIONAL BEHAVIOR CHANGE (shipped, Option A — enforce the access model uniformly): SSR now applies the entity-level `view` access check to ALL entity types (previously only the `content` group).** As a deliberate, tested consequence of the R8-a leak-closure above, **anonymous SSR of non-content-group types that lack a permission-free published-view grant — `taxonomy_term`, `media`, `user` — now returns 403, matching what `/api` already enforces.** The framework-default `PublishedContentAccessPolicy` exposes ONLY the `content` group (its docblock is explicit that `user`/`taxonomy` are "never auto-exposed"); these non-content-group types' policies grant `view` only to an account holding a permission a bare anonymous account does not carry — `TermAccessPolicy` needs `access content`, `MediaAccessPolicy` needs `access media`, `UserAccessPolicy` needs `access user profiles`. Before this change SSR skipped the check for those types and rendered them whole-entity to anonymous; now SSR and `/api` are consistent. **Migration note for site builders:** a site that INTENDS public anonymous tag/media/author pages must add a granting `AccessPolicy` for those types (the same requirement `/api` already imposes) — the framework does not silently expose them. This is pinned by explicit tests so it is a documented decision, not an accident: `SsrEntityViewGateAllTypesTest::a_published_taxonomy_term_is_denied_to_a_bare_anonymous_account` (real `path_alias` → `handleRenderPage()` against the REAL `TermAccessPolicy` + the boot-default `PublishedContentAccessPolicy` + a genuinely BARE anonymous account holding zero permissions → 403, term label absent; confirmed RED against pre-fix code where it rendered 200, GREEN after) with the paired positive control `::a_published_content_group_entity_still_renders_for_a_bare_anonymous_account_via_the_real_policy` (the SAME production-shaped handler + SAME bare anonymous account still renders a published `content`-group entity 200 via the permission-free `PublishedContentAccessPolicy` grant — proving the change denies ONLY the permission-gated non-content types and does not regress the content group).
- **R9 (product decision, DEFERRED — not a bug): decide whether the framework should ship permission-free published/public `view` grants for `taxonomy_term` (and possibly `media`/`user`)** so those SSR pages are anonymously viewable BY DEFAULT, extending the `PublishedContentAccessPolicy` group-scoped pattern to those groups. This is a deliberate deferral of a product/UX decision (should tag/media/author pages be public out of the box?), not an oversight in R8-a. Until that decision is made and shipped, anonymous visibility of those types requires a site-provided granting `AccessPolicy` — the same requirement `/api` already imposes. Tracked as R9; see `SsrPageHandler::shouldDenyEntityRender()`'s docblock for the in-code note.
- **The source-existence-oracle class was open on TWO surfaces — the HTTP discovery hub/cluster/timeline actions AND the MCP `relationship.traverse` tool — neither gated the SOURCE entity's own view access (audit R8-c; audit-remediation batch 2026-07-05, R8 WP2). This entry closes the class on both.** **(1) HTTP — `DiscoveryRouter`.** `DiscoveryRouter::handleEndpoint()` (`packages/api/src/Http/Router/DiscoveryRouter.php:212-218` pre-fix, now `:~218-224`) correctly gates its own source entity — `$resolvedEntity = $this->discoveryHandler->loadDiscoveryEntity($entityType, $resolvedId); if ($resolvedEntity === null || !$this->discoveryHandler->isDiscoveryEntityPublic($resolvedEntity, $ctx->account)) { return 404 }` — BEFORE building the discovery service. `handleTopicHub()` (`:89` pre-fix), `handleCluster()` (`:125` pre-fix), and `handleTimeline()` (`:161` pre-fix) did NOT: they validated params then went straight to the cache read + `createDiscoveryService()` + `topicHub()`/`clusterPage()`/`timeline()`, never loading or access-checking the source entity. So a caller who could not view the source entity type+id still got a 200 (with hub/cluster/timeline data, or an empty-but-200 payload) — disclosing that the entity exists and, distinctly, that it is access-restricted (an oracle a truly-absent id would not produce). Fixed by adding the IDENTICAL gate `handleEndpoint()` already uses to all three handlers, placed BEFORE the cache read (`getDiscoveryCachedResponse()`) so a denied/absent source is never even consulted against the cache, matching `handleEndpoint()`'s ordering. **Fail-closed / indistinguishable by design:** the combined `$resolvedEntity === null || !isDiscoveryEntityPublic(...)` check returns the SAME 404 status and the SAME generic detail shape (`"Discovery <hub|cluster|timeline> not publicly visible: <entity_type>:<id>"`) whether the source is truly ABSENT or merely ACCESS-DENIED — mirroring `handleEndpoint()`'s pre-existing "not publicly visible" phrasing, which already conflates the two cases; an attacker cannot distinguish "does not exist" from "you may not view it" from either the status code or the response shape. Neither `handleEndpoint()` nor `isDiscoveryEntityPublic()`/`loadDiscoveryEntity()` were changed. **Cache corollary:** the hub/cluster/timeline responses are cached for anonymous callers (`getDiscoveryCachedResponse()`/`prepareDiscoveryResponse()`, `DiscoveryCachePrimitives`). Pre-fix, a restricted source could get a 200 cached under the hub/cluster/timeline key — an existence/access-oracle response that would keep being served for up to the 120s TTL. `DiscoveryCachePrimitives::CACHE_KEY_GENERATION` (`packages/foundation/src/Cache/DiscoveryCachePrimitives.php`) bumped `'2'` -> `'3'` (was bumped `'1'` -> `'2'` for R7 WP2) so every pre-fix cached hub/cluster/timeline 200 for a now-gated source is orphaned; the new gate runs before the cache read going forward, so denied/absent sources are never cached from this point on. New exploit-closed coverage: `DiscoveryRouterTest` — `hub_returns_not_found_when_source_entity_is_access_restricted`/`cluster_…`/`timeline_…` (a real `DiscoveryRouter` + a real `EntityAccessHandler` + a forbid-view `AccessPolicyInterface` on the source, anonymous account — 200 pre-fix, 404 post-fix), each paired with a `hub_returns_200_with_data_when_source_entity_is_viewable`/`cluster_…`/`timeline_…` positive control (a viewable source still serves hub/cluster/timeline data — the gate must not over-block), and a shared `assertSourceGateReturnsNotFoundAndIndistinguishableFromAbsent()` helper asserting a restricted-but-existing source and a truly-absent source (same entity type + same id string, nothing persisted) produce byte-identical 404 response bodies. `DiscoveryCachePrimitivesTest::keyDiffersFromGenerationTwoShape` pins the `2 -> 3` cache-key-generation bump against a generation-2-era key shape. All confirmed RED against pre-fix code (200 disclosed for a restricted-but-existing source) and GREEN after. **(2) MCP — `RelationshipTraverseTool`.** The adversarial sweep found the SAME oracle class still open on `relationship.traverse` (`packages/ai-tools/src/Relationship/RelationshipTraverseTool.php`), a tool on `PublicAnonymousAuth::DEFAULT_READ_CAPABILITIES` (`packages/mcp/src/Auth/PublicAnonymousAuth.php`) — hence anonymous-reachable. `execute()` took caller-supplied `source_entity_type`/`source_id`, queried relationship rows by `from_entity_type`/`from_entity_id`, and gated ONLY the edge row (`canViewEntity($row)`) and the FAR endpoint (`canViewEndpoint()` on `to_entity_type`/`to_entity_id`) — it NEVER checked the SOURCE entity's own view access, with a code comment rationalizing "the source is the caller's own query input, so its visibility is implied". That is the confused-deputy pattern: supplying an id does NOT imply the caller may view that entity. Exploit: anonymous supplies `source_id` = a restricted node; if that node has any published edge to a viewable entity, the tool returned a non-empty `edges` array echoing the restricted source id (in `from_entity_id`) + the relationship — confirming the restricted entity exists and has that relationship (the exact disclosure the HTTP `DiscoveryRouter` gate closes). Fixed with a new fail-closed `RelationshipTraverseTool::canViewSource()` (mirrors the existing `canViewEndpoint()` exactly: unknown source type / unloadable source / policy-denied `view` → not viewable; capability-only mode with no handler allows, preserving prior behavior; enforced-with-no-handler fails closed via `isAccessEnforced()`), run BEFORE the relationship query. When the source is not viewable the tool returns an EMPTY result (`edges: [], count: 0`) via a new `emptyTraversalResult()`, INDISTINGUISHABLE in its `data` from a source with genuinely zero relationships / an absent source — no existence oracle. The stale endpoint-gate comment that rationalized skipping the source check was rewritten to state the source is now gated up front (R8-c). New exploit-closed coverage in `RelationshipTraverseAccessFilterTest`: `traverse_returns_empty_when_the_source_entity_is_view_forbidden` (a view-forbidden source with a published edge to a viewable target — non-empty edges echoing the restricted source id pre-fix, empty post-fix), `forbidden_source_is_indistinguishable_from_an_absent_source` (byte-identical `data` to a genuinely-empty source built from fresh empty fixtures), and `traverse_returns_the_edge_when_the_source_entity_is_viewable` (positive control — the source gate must not over-block); the three pre-existing edge/endpoint tests now seed a viewable source `node/10` so they still exercise the edge/endpoint filters they are about (previously they relied on an unseeded — now, under the source gate, unloadable — source). Forbidden and indistinguishability cases confirmed RED against pre-fix code (the edge echoing the restricted source disclosed) and GREEN after. **(3) MCP — `EntityReadTool` / `EntityListRevisionsTool`.** The sweep found the SAME oracle class, in its absent-vs-forbidden form, on `entity.read` (`packages/ai-tools/src/Entity/EntityReadTool.php`) and `entity.list_revisions` (`packages/ai-tools/src/Entity/EntityListRevisionsTool.php`) — both carry capability `tool.entity.read`, which is on `PublicAnonymousAuth::DEFAULT_READ_CAPABILITIES` and is non-destructive, so both are anonymous-reachable. They returned TEXTUALLY DISTINGUISHABLE errors: an ABSENT id → `AgentToolResult::error('entity.read: node/5 not found')`, but an existing-but-view-FORBIDDEN id → `requireEntityAccess()` → `error('Account X is not permitted to view node/5', summary: 'forbidden')`. Both are wire-visible (`AgentToolRegistryBridge` forwards content verbatim), so an anonymous caller enumerating ids learned "exists but forbidden" apart from "absent" — the same existence oracle. Fixed LOCALLY in these two anonymous-read-tier tools (the shared `AbstractAgentTool::requireEntityAccess()` helper is UNCHANGED — the destructive write tools EntityUpdate/Delete/Rollback/Create are NOT on the anonymous tier and their distinguishable 'forbidden' is fine there): each now collapses BOTH the absent and the view-forbidden outcomes into the IDENTICAL `... not found` error via `$entity === null || !$this->canViewEntity($entity, $account)`, so absent and forbidden are byte-indistinguishable on the wire (same `content`, same `summary`, same `isError`). `canViewEntity()` fails closed under enforcement with no handler (a wiring gap drops to not-found, never discloses) and preserves capability-only behavior (allow). A VIEWABLE existing entity still returns its data / revisions unchanged. New exploit-closed coverage: `EntityReadOracleClosureTest` — `read_forbidden_is_indistinguishable_from_absent` and `list_revisions_forbidden_is_indistinguishable_from_absent` (a view-forbidden existing entity vs a genuinely-absent id built from a fresh empty repo, SAME id string, asserting byte-identical `content` + `summary`), plus `read_returns_data_for_a_viewable_entity` / `list_revisions_returns_history_for_a_viewable_entity` positive controls — the two oracle cases confirmed RED against pre-fix code ("not permitted to view" text disclosed) and GREEN after. Two pre-existing tests that pinned the OLD distinguishable `'forbidden'` summary for the read tool (`AiToolsServiceProviderWiringTest::the_provider_wires_the_kernel_access_handler_onto_hydrated_tools`, `EntityToolFailClosedTest::read_is_denied_when_enforcement_is_required_but_no_handler_resolved`) were updated to assert the new indistinguishable not-found outcome — the read still fails closed (entity data withheld, `isError` true), the wiring/fail-closed guarantee they pin is intact. **Conclusion: the entity-existence-oracle class is now closed across the HTTP discovery surface (hub/cluster/timeline) AND the entire anonymous MCP read tier.** That tier is `entity.read`, `entity.search`, `relationship.traverse`, and `bimaaji.read` (`PublicAnonymousAuth::DEFAULT_READ_CAPABILITIES`): `relationship.traverse` (source) and `entity.read`/`entity.list_revisions` (absent-vs-forbidden) are fixed here; `entity.search` already filters forbidden hits silently (returns them as absent, never an error); `bimaaji.read` is not id-scoped (no per-entity existence probe). No anonymous-MCP-read-tier entity-existence oracle is known to remain. **Out of scope, noted not fixed (R8 residual):** the both-endpoints-hidden edge-existence metadata case is a separate, disclosed finding tracked for a later pass, not addressed by this PR.**
- **`GenealogyPedigreeService` emitted person labels on the anonymous-reachable public pedigree pages gated only at the entity level, the genealogy-side residual of the R7 WP1 label channel (audit-remediation batch 2026-07-05, R8 WP3 — defense-in-depth, NOT live exploitable today).** `neighborSlots()` (`packages/genealogy/src/Service/GenealogyPedigreeService.php:150`) and `ancestorGenerationsRedacted()` (`:192`) read `$person->label()`/`$subject->label()` directly once `$gate->allows('view', $person, $account)` passed — the same field-access-bypassing pattern R7 WP1 closed on the SSR `<title>`, schema.org `name`, and Markdown H1 sites, left disclosed-not-fixed there as tracked residual R8-b. **Not live today:** the wired `GenealogyContentAccessPolicy::fieldAccess()` always returns Neutral, so there is no entity-viewable-but-label-field-Forbidden split to exploit on this surface currently — this is a landmine closed proactively, not an active leak. **Fix:** `GenealogyPedigreeService` gained an optional trailing `?EntityAccessHandler $accessHandler = null` constructor param (nullable/defensive, mirroring `SsrPageHandler`'s shape); `GenealogyServiceProvider`'s `GenealogyPedigreeService` singleton factory now wires the kernel access handler via `$this->resolve(EntityAccessHandler::class)` (the same kernel-services-bus resolution `SearchServiceProvider`/`OidcServiceProvider` already use for the same class). Both label reads now call `EntityAccessHandler::viewableLabel($person, $account, $entityTypeManager)`; when it returns `null` (label field Forbidden) OR no handler is wired, the slot fails closed to the SAME redacted-placeholder shape a fully-concealed neighbor/subject already renders (`'Private living relative'` / `'Private ancestor'` for neighbor slots, `'Private profile'` for the ancestor-chart subject row) — never the raw label. Entity-level gating (`$gate->allows('view', ...)`) is unchanged and still runs first; this is additive field-level defense-in-depth, not a replacement. The ancestor-topology-gathering system-context traversal (`ancestorGenerations($personId, $maxGenerations, null)`) is untouched — only the label EMISSION at the two sites is gated. New coverage in `GenealogyPedigreeServiceTest`: `neighbor_slots_conceal_the_label_when_the_label_field_is_forbidden` and `ancestor_generations_redacted_conceals_the_subject_label_when_the_label_field_is_forbidden` (a test-only `AccessPolicyInterface`&`FieldAccessPolicyInterface` policy grants entity-level `view` but Forbids the `display_name` label-key field — confirmed RED against pre-fix code, the raw label ("Secret Parent"/"Secret Subject") was emitted with `redacted: false`; GREEN after) plus paired positive controls (`..._show_the_real_label_when_field_access_is_neutral`) proving the fix does not over-redact when no field policy opines. `GenealogySsrConcealmentTest` updated to wire its already-present `$this->accessHandler` into the `GenealogyPedigreeService` it constructs (previously omitted), exercising the fix through the full controller path; unaffected pre-existing assertions confirm no regression since the real `GenealogyContentAccessPolicy::fieldAccess()` stays Neutral. **Scope:** `packages/genealogy` only.
- **The entity label/title bypassed field-level access at three sites, leaking a field-access-restricted label for an otherwise-viewable entity (R6's residual; audit-remediation batch 2026-07-04, R7 WP1).** `EntityInterface::label()` was read directly, with no field-access check, at `packages/ssr/templates/entity.html.twig:3` (`{% block title %}{{ entity.label }}{% endblock %}`), `packages/seo/src/SchemaOrg/EntitySchemaOrgMapper.php:69` (`'name' => $entity->label()`), and `packages/api/src/Markdown/EntityMarkdownPresenter.php:102` (`'# ' . $this->escapeInline($entity->label() ...)`). **Characterization:** two distinct cases exist. (1) A FULLY entity-level-restricted entity (`AccessPolicyInterface::access()` returns Forbidden) is already gated for all three sites — for `content`-group entities (nodes included) by R6 PR2's `SsrPageHandler::shouldDenyContentGroupRender()`, which runs BEFORE any of the three sites are reached (`handleRenderPage()` 403s and neither the `<title>`, the schema.org script, nor the Markdown H1 is ever built), so this case has no reachable exploit surface for content-group types. A narrower, disclosed-not-fixed gap remains for a non-content-group, non-node entity reachable via a path alias with a Forbidden `AccessPolicy`: `EditorialVisibilityResolver` allows non-`node` types outright and `shouldDenyContentGroupRender()` only gates the `content` group, so such an entity (if one exists with a whole-entity Forbidden policy and a path alias) would render without the entity-level gate this batch relies on — out of scope here (broader than the label channel) and noted as a separate finding, not fixed in this PR. (2) **The primary residual this PR fixes:** a VIEWABLE entity (entity-level access Allowed or Neutral) whose label-key field is field-access-**Forbidden** — the entity renders 200 with the fields bag correctly filtered (R6 PR1), but all three sites still read the raw label directly, leaking it. Fixed with one shared, fail-closed helper: `Waaseyaa\Access\EntityAccessHandler::viewableLabel(EntityInterface $entity, AccountInterface $account, EntityTypeManagerInterface $entityTypeManager): ?string` resolves the entity type's `label` entity-key field name (entity types with no label key return the label unchanged — nothing to gate) and runs it through the SAME `checkFieldAccess()` the fields bag uses — open-by-default (Neutral/Allowed shows the label), `null` on Forbidden. Applied at all three sites: **SSR `<title>`** — `SsrPageHandler::handleRenderPage()` now resolves the checked label once (`$this->accessHandler?->viewableLabel(...)`) and threads it into the Twig context as `title`; `entity.html.twig:3` renders `{{ title }}` instead of `{{ entity.label }}`. **schema.org** — `EntitySchemaOrgMapper::map()` gained an optional trailing `?string $labelOverride = null` param (backward-compatible; omitting it preserves the old `$entity->label()` behavior for callers with no access context); `SsrPageHandler::buildSchemaOrgScript()` passes the same checked label through it, so the JSON-LD `name` is never the raw label. **Markdown H1** — `EntityMarkdownPresenter::present()` (already required non-nullable `$accessHandler`/`$account` since R2 WP4/#1876) now calls `$accessHandler->viewableLabel(...)` for the H1 instead of `$entity->label()` directly. All three FAIL CLOSED to the entity type id (never the raw label, never blank) when the label field is Forbidden OR no access handler is wired — matching the pre-existing empty-label fallback shape (`label !== '' ? label : entityTypeId`) — while a genuinely public/unrestricted label still renders for real (regression-guarded). **Cache corollary:** the SSR HTML render (title + schema.org) and the SSR Markdown render are both cached in `RenderCache`; `RenderCache::SCHEMA_VERSION` is bumped `'v3'` -> `'v4'` to bust every pre-fix cached entry that may carry a leaked title, forcing a re-render through the fixed path (the api Markdown surface has no separate server cache — it is only served via SSR, so this one bump covers it too). **Adversarial sweep (`grep -rn "->label()" packages/*/src/`):** the SSR `fields` bag (R6 PR1) and JSON:API's `ResourceSerializer` (already filters the label) are access-aware; no OpenGraph meta or breadcrumb surface reads the label independently in this codebase today. **MCP enumeration tools — ALSO FIXED HERE (correcting an earlier mis-classification):** the sweep surfaced `ai-tools/src/Entity/EntityListRevisionsTool.php:91` (`'label' => $revision->label()`) and `EntityListTool.php:92` (`'label' => $e->label()`) emitting a bare, field-access-unfiltered label per row. The R4 PR1 changelog entry below lumped both as a single "low-severity deferred follow-up" — that was inaccurate and is corrected here: the two differ materially. `EntityListRevisionsTool`'s `#[AsAgentTool]` capability is `tool.entity.read`, which IS on the public MCP anonymous read tier (`PublicAnonymousAuth::DEFAULT_READ_CAPABILITIES`, `packages/mcp/src/Auth/PublicAnonymousAuth.php:32`) and non-destructive, so `entity.list_revisions` was **anonymous-reachable** — an anonymous MCP client could receive labels a `FieldAccessPolicyInterface` forbids: a LIVE leak of exactly the class this entry closes, not a deferred nicety. `EntityListTool`'s `tool.entity.list` capability is NOT on the anonymous allowlist, so it was not anonymous-reachable. Both are fixed in this PR via a new shared `AbstractAgentTool::viewableLabel(EntityInterface, AccountInterface, EntityTypeManagerInterface): ?string` (delegates to the attached `EntityAccessHandler::viewableLabel()`; capability-only mode returns the raw label unchanged to preserve prior handler-less behavior, enforced-mode-with-no-handler fails closed to `null` matching `canViewEntity()`); each tool now omits the `label` key when the helper returns `null` (Forbidden) rather than emitting the raw label. Pinned by `EntityListLabelFieldAccessTest` (`list_revisions_omits_the_label_when_the_label_field_is_forbidden` confirmed RED — raw label emitted — pre-fold and GREEN after; plus positive-control, capability-only-mode, and an `EntityListTool` defense-in-depth companion). **Tracked-for-R8 residuals (out of scope here — genuine scope forks, disclosed not fixed):** (R8-a) **non-content-group entity-level SSR bypass** — a `path_alias` pointing at a non-content-group, non-`node` entity type renders via SSR with NO entity-level `view` check (`EditorialVisibilityResolver` allows non-`node` outright; `SsrPageHandler::shouldDenyContentGroupRender()` only gates the `content` group), so a type whose `AccessPolicy` returns Neutral / deny-by-default (e.g. `oidc_client`) is disclosed WHOLE-ENTITY to anonymous once an admin-created alias to it exists. This is entity-level (broader than the label channel) with low practical reachability (needs an admin-created alias to such a type) → R8, the entity-level generalization of the R6 PR2 M2 gate. (R8-b) **`GenealogyPedigreeService`** (`packages/genealogy/src/Service/GenealogyPedigreeService.php:138,180`) emits person `label()` gated only at the entity level on the anonymous-reachable public pedigree pages — NOT live today (`GenealogyContentAccessPolicy::fieldAccess()` always returns Neutral, so there is no entity-viewable-but-label-restricted split), a defense-in-depth landmine → R8, optional swap to `viewableLabel`. **Other unfiltered `->label()` emissions, out of scope, not missed:** `relationship/src/RelationshipTraversalService.php:738-772` builds discovery/browse navigation labels through the publish-status-only `VisibilityFilterInterface` (the tracked R7 WP2 / R5-residual discovery scope); `ai-vector/src/{EntityEmbeddingListener,EntityEmbedder}.php` feed the label into search-embedding generation (indexing-time, not per-request-account); `packages/cli/src/Handler/EntityListHandler.php` (operator console) and `foundation/src/Http/Router/BroadcastRouter.php` (`$account->label()`, an account label, not entity content) are operator/account contexts outside this PR's anonymous-HTTP threat model. New/updated exploit-closed coverage: `EntityAccessHandlerLabelAccessTest` (unit coverage for `viewableLabel()` — Forbidden -> null, open-by-default -> real label, no label key -> unchanged, unregistered entity type -> unchanged), `SsrEntityLabelAccessTest` (full `handleRenderPage()` round trip against a real SQLite-backed `node` through the REAL shipped `entity.html.twig` — the `<title>` tag and the JSON-LD `name` both fall back to the entity type id placeholder when the label field is Forbidden, and both show the real title as a positive-control regression guard), and a new `EntityMarkdownPresenterTest::label_is_replaced_with_a_placeholder_when_the_label_field_is_forbidden` (+ positive control) — all confirmed RED against pre-fix code (the real label present at each site) and GREEN after.**
- **The discovery/browse API's relationship endpoint-visibility gate was publish-status only and never ran the entity access policy — the last publish-status-only relationship read surface (audit R5 residual #1; audit-remediation batch 2026-07-05, R7 WP2).** `RelationshipTraversalService::filterByEndpointVisibility()`/`mapTraversalRelationships()` (`packages/relationship/src/RelationshipTraversalService.php:~87,~584`) gate every non-source relationship endpoint's disclosure ONLY through the injected `?VisibilityFilterInterface`, and `DiscoveryApiHandler::createDiscoveryService()` (`packages/api/src/Http/DiscoveryApiHandler.php:195-205` pre-fix) constructed it with `new WorkflowVisibilityFilter()` — publish/unpublish status only, never an `AccessPolicyInterface` opinion. `DiscoveryApiHandler::isDiscoveryEntityPublic()` (`:191` pre-fix, `(string $entityType, array $values): bool`) — the discovery "endpoint" route's own primary-entity/identity gate, and the transitive gate `isDiscoveryEndpointPairPublic()` uses for a relationship entity's own from/to pair — likewise delegated straight to `WorkflowVisibility::isEntityPublic()`. So a PUBLISHED-but-access-restricted entity (a private node, a tenant-scoped/held entity) still had its type/id/path/label disclosed through `topicHub`/`clusterPage`/`timeline`/`endpointPage`/`relationshipEntityPage` (all route through `RelationshipTraversalService::browse()`) to any caller — including anonymous — who could not otherwise view it, and could itself anchor a discovery "endpoint" page. Neither R5 (#1882: REST `/api/relationship` + `entity.read` + GraphQL, field-access redaction) nor R6 PR2 (#1884: SSR relationship-nav, a same-page post-filter) covered this surface — both explicitly documented it as a remaining residual. Fixed by making the gate ACCESS-AWARE, additive to the existing publish-status gate rather than replacing it: `RelationshipTraversalService` gained OPTIONAL `?EntityAccessHandler $accessHandler` / `?AccountInterface $account` constructor params; when both are wired, every non-source endpoint must ALSO pass `EntityAccessHandler::check($endpoint, 'view', $account)->isAllowed()` (fail-closed: an empty id/type, unregistered type, or failed load is NOT viewable) — computed once per endpoint alongside the existing `is_public`/label summary (no extra query) and checked independently of `$statusMode`, so a published-but-restricted endpoint is dropped in `published` mode and an admin's `status=all`/`status=unpublished` discovery keeps working (`NodeAccessPolicy`'s `administer nodes` bypass grants `view` regardless of publish state). `DiscoveryApiHandler::createDiscoveryService()` is now `createDiscoveryService(AccountInterface $account)`, threading the kernel's real `EntityAccessHandler` (new 4th constructor param, wired from `HttpKernel::finalizeBoot()`'s `$this->accessHandler`, already populated by `discoverAccessPolicies()` earlier in `boot()`) and the request account into `RelationshipTraversalService`; `DiscoveryRouter` passes `$ctx->account` at all four call sites. `isDiscoveryEntityPublic()` signature changed to `(EntityInterface $entity, ?AccountInterface $account = null): bool` (both call sites already held the loaded entity) and now ANDs the same access check onto the publish-status check; `isDiscoveryEndpointPairPublic()` gained a matching `?AccountInterface $account = null` parameter it forwards. When `$accessHandler`/`$account` are absent (any caller other than the discovery API, and any legacy/unwired test construction), the gate is OFF and behavior is byte-identical to pre-fix — SSR's independent R6 PR2 post-filter (`SsrPageHandler::canViewRelatedEndpoint()`) is untouched. **Cache corollary:** the discovery API caches ANONYMOUS responses only (`DiscoveryApiHandler::getDiscoveryCachedResponse()`/`prepareDiscoveryResponse()` both gate on `!$account->isAuthenticated()`) under a cache key that is NOT account-scoped — safe going forward (every anonymous caller gets the same access decision) but a response cached BEFORE this fix could still hold a leaked restricted-endpoint identity for up to its 120s TTL. `DiscoveryCachePrimitives::CACHE_KEY_GENERATION` (new private constant, embedded in `buildKey()`'s hashed input, distinct from and never surfaced alongside the public `CONTRACT_VERSION`) is bumped 1->2, making every pre-fix cache entry an orphaned miss so the fix is effective immediately on deploy rather than waiting out the TTL. New exploit-closed coverage: `RelationshipTraversalServiceTest` (4 new cases — published-but-restricted endpoint dropped from `browse()`/`traverse()`; positive control that a viewable published endpoint still surfaces; fail-closed unloadable-endpoint case), `DiscoveryRouterTest` (3 new integration cases against real SQLite + a real `EntityAccessHandler` + a forbid-view `AccessPolicyInterface`, through the real `DiscoveryApiHandler`/`DiscoveryRouter`), `DiscoveryCachePrimitivesTest` (generation-bump key-diff case) — all confirmed RED against pre-fix code (the restricted endpoint disclosed) and GREEN after. **Adversarial sweep — is this the LAST publish-status-only relationship endpoint-disclosure surface?** Every production construction site of `RelationshipTraversalService`/`RelationshipDiscoveryService` was enumerated: `DiscoveryApiHandler` (this PR, now access-aware) and `SsrPageHandler` (R6 PR2's independent post-filter, already access-aware) are the ONLY two — no other production caller exists. `RelationshipTraverseTool` (`packages/ai-tools/src/Relationship/RelationshipTraverseTool.php`, the `relationship.traverse` MCP/agent tool) queries the repository directly and never constructs `RelationshipTraversalService`; it already carries its own independent, already-access-aware `canViewEndpoint()` fail-closed gate (R4 PR2, a separate remediation batch, unaffected either way). `packages/graphql` and `packages/bimaaji` do not depend on `waaseyaa/relationship` and have no relationship-endpoint resolver of their own — any relationship entity they surface goes through the SAME shared `EntityAccessHandler` field-access plumbing R5's `RelationshipEndpointVisibilityPolicy` already covers. `packages/wayfinding` has no relationship-endpoint disclosure surface. The `genealogy` distribution extension (out of framework-substrate scope, DIR-004) queries `from_entity_type`/`to_entity_type` directly in `GenealogyPedigreeService`/`GenealogyFamilyService` but is independently gated at the ENTITY level by its own `GenealogyRelationshipAccessPolicy` (`packages/genealogy/src/Access/GenealogyRelationshipAccessPolicy.php`), which already checks both endpoints via `EntityAccessHandler::check($endpoint, 'view', $account)->isAllowed()` and forbids the WHOLE edge if either is hidden — not modified or newly verified line-by-line in this PR, flagged here for completeness rather than left silently unaudited. **Conclusion: this PR closes the LAST publish-status-only relationship endpoint-disclosure surface in the framework substrate.** No further surface of this class is known to remain.**
- **The SSR primary-entity render gate for `node` was publish-status only and never ran the entity access policy — a published-but-access-restricted node still rendered 200 to anonymous (audit M2, the R5 residual realized; audit-remediation batch 2026-07-03, R6 PR2).** `SsrPageHandler::shouldDenyContentGroupRender()` (`packages/ssr/src/SsrPageHandler.php:803-811` pre-fix) opened with `if ($entityTypeId === 'node' || !$this->isContentGroupEntity($entityTypeId)) { return false; }` — an EXPLICIT exclusion of `node` from the one gate on the SSR HTML/Markdown path that calls `$this->accessHandler->check($entity, 'view', $account)`. The only node gate left was `EditorialVisibilityResolver::canRender()` (`packages/workflows/src/EditorialVisibilityResolver.php:25-34`), which returns Allowed for any node with `status === STATE_PUBLISHED` alone — it has no concept of a per-entity `AccessPolicyInterface`. So a published node whose access policy returns `Forbidden` for `('view', $account)` (e.g. a legal-hold or insufficient-clearance `ClassificationFieldAccessPolicy` per-entity opinion, `#[PolicyAttribute(entityType: '*')]`) rendered its full title and fields to anonymous with `accessHandler->check()` NEVER invoked. Fixed by removing the `$entityTypeId === 'node'` exclusion: every `content`-group entity — node included — now runs the entity-level view-access check on top of the editorial publish/preview gate; BOTH must pass (`EditorialVisibilityResolver::canRender()` still runs first at `SsrPageHandler.php:182`, owning publish/preview/workflow nuance; `shouldDenyContentGroupRender()` runs second at `:192`, owning per-account entity-level access). The method still fails closed when no access handler is wired (deny). **Regression guard:** a genuinely public published node — standard `NodeAccessPolicy`, no restrictive policy, anonymous holding `access content` — is unaffected: `NodeAccessPolicy::access()` (`packages/node/src/NodeAccessPolicy.php:69`) already grants `view` for a published node with that permission, so ordinary content keeps rendering exactly as before; confirmed by a new full-pipeline test driving `handleRenderPage()` end to end against a real SQL-backed node (`SsrNodeViewAccessGateTest::a_standard_published_node_still_renders_for_anonymous_with_access_content`). New exploit-closed coverage: `SsrNodeViewAccessGateTest` (full `handleRenderPage()` round trip against a real, SQLite-backed `node` entity — a published-but-view-forbidden node is denied whole-page 403 with its title absent from the response; the standard published-node regression case above) and `SsrContentPublishedGateTest::gates_a_view_forbidden_node` (private-seam coverage; the test previously named `never_gates_nodes_here` PINNED the wrong invariant — that nodes are never gated by this method regardless of status — and has been renamed/rewritten to assert the corrected invariant, alongside a new `allows_a_published_node_with_no_restrictive_policy` companion pinning the regression guard at the same seam). Both confirmed RED (node rendered 200 with its content) against pre-fix code and GREEN after. **Cache corollary:** a denied (403) render is never cached, but a node that leaked 200 BEFORE this fix — including any post-R6-PR1 held node whose title/fields survived the PR1 field-filter fix because the whole-page gate never ran — could still be served from `RenderCache` under the R6 PR1 `'v2'` key schema. `RenderCache::SCHEMA_VERSION` is bumped to `'v3'`, making every pre-PR2 cache entry unreachable and forcing a re-render through the fixed node gate. **This also closes the practically-reachable case of the R6 PR1 label/title leak** flagged there as a follow-up (R7): PR1 documented that the entity label/title bypasses its `fields`-bag filter (read directly via `$entity->label()` in the `<title>` block and schema.org JSON-LD), but a classification-held published node — the only realistic way to have a viewable-but-label-restricted node in practice — is now denied WHOLE-PAGE by this fix before any title/JSON-LD is emitted, so that residual has no practically-reachable exploit surface (the cross-package label-vs-field-policy gap itself remains open as a narrower, no-longer-urgent follow-up). **Adversarial sweep — related-entity identity leak in the relationship-navigation side context, ALSO CLOSED in this PR (audit m1):** `SsrPageHandler::buildRelationshipRenderContext()` surfaces the type/id/path/label of every entity RELATED to the rendered page, gated only by `WorkflowVisibilityFilter` (publish-status-only, no `$account`) — so a PUBLIC node A related to a PUBLISHED-BUT-VIEW-FORBIDDEN node B still disclosed B's identity in node A's `relationship_navigation` context even though B's own `AccessPolicy` denies anonymous 'view' (the same disclosure class this PR closes for the primary entity, on the same rendered page). This was closable as a same-page, SsrPageHandler-LOCAL post-filter — `SsrPageHandler` already holds `$this->accessHandler` and `handleRenderPage()` already has `$account`, so no change to `RelationshipTraversalService`/`RelationshipDiscoveryService` internals was needed. Fixed by threading `$account` into `buildRelationshipRenderContext()` and, for every disclosed related endpoint (the `related_entity_type`/`related_entity_id` of each node-browse edge, and the `from_endpoint`/`to_endpoint` type+id of the relationship-entity page), re-checking it against the SAME per-entity view `AccessPolicy` via a new fail-closed `canViewRelatedEndpoint()` helper (`$this->entityTypeManager->getRepository($type)->find($id)` then `accessHandler->check($endpoint, 'view', $account)->isAllowed()`) and DROPPING the whole edge — never partially disclosing it — when the handler is absent, the type is unregistered, the entity fails to load, or view is denied (mirrors `RelationshipTraverseTool::canViewEndpoint()`'s "prove nothing → drop" contract at the SSR boundary; the queried source entity is the already-gated page and is not re-checked). The existing additive try/catch (`[]` on error) is preserved. **N+1 tradeoff, accepted:** each disclosed endpoint is re-loaded to run its access policy — the leak is closed now; a cleaner future optimization could push the per-account filter down into `RelationshipTraversalService`'s own visibility resolution. New exploit-closed coverage: `SsrRelationshipNavAccessTest` (a public node related to a published-but-view-forbidden node — B's id absent from the emitted `relationship_navigation`; positive control that a viewable related node still surfaces; a fail-closed no-handler case that drops all endpoints) — confirmed RED (B's id present) against pre-fold code and GREEN after. **Remaining deferred (R7):** the discovery/browse API and other non-SSR consumers of `RelationshipTraversalService::browse()`/`traverse()` still use the publish-status-only `WorkflowVisibilityFilter` and are NOT covered by this SSR-boundary filter; and the narrower cross-package label-vs-field-policy gap noted above.
- **The public SEO surface (`/sitemap.xml`, `/llms.txt`) enumerated published-but-access-restricted entity paths to any anonymous crawler or agent — the R5 residual on the SEO surface (audit M3, audit-remediation batch 2026-07-03, R6 PR3).** `SeoPublicController::sitemapXml()`/`llmsTxt()` (`packages/ssr/src/Http/SeoPublicController.php:35` `NON_PUBLIC_TYPES`, routes registered `->allowAll()` in `SsrServiceProvider`) delegate to `Waaseyaa\Seo\SitemapGenerator::collectFromEntityTypes()` (`packages/seo/src/SitemapGenerator.php:101,109-110` pre-fix) and `Waaseyaa\Seo\Llms\LlmsTxtGenerator::collectTopics()` (`~:118-119` pre-fix), both of which built their entity-ID enumeration query with `getQuery()->accessCheck(false)` and gated ONLY on entity-type membership in `NON_PUBLIC_TYPES` plus a bare `condition('status', 1)` — neither ever consulted an `AccessPolicyInterface`. A PUBLISHED node carrying a `ClassificationFieldAccessPolicy` hold/clearance restriction (entity-level Forbidden for anonymous), or a restricted `genealogy_person` (a type absent from `NON_PUBLIC_TYPES`), still had its canonical path enumerated in the public sitemap/llms output. Fixed by making the enumeration ACCESS-AWARE: both generators' `collectFromEntityTypes()`/`collectTopics()` gained an optional trailing `?AccountInterface $account` parameter and now call `getQuery()->setAccount($account)` instead of `accessCheck(false)` when an account is supplied, so `SqlEntityQuery::execute()` runs its per-candidate `EntityAccessHandler::check($entity, 'view', $account)->isAllowed()` pipeline — the SAME policy surface every other read path in the framework uses (deny-by-default per audit C-6: `Forbidden` AND `Neutral` both exclude a row) — verified against `SqlEntityQueryAccessCheckTest::anonymousAccountSeesEmptyWhenPolicyForbidsAll`'s existing query-layer guarantee. When no account is supplied, the query is left at its fail-closed default (no `setAccount()`, no `accessCheck(false)` fallback) rather than silently reopening the bypass — a caller building a public surface MUST pass an account; this is a deliberate API-signature change, not a bolt-on opt-out. `SeoPublicController` now passes an explicit `Waaseyaa\User\AnonymousUser` on every `sitemapXml()`/`llmsTxt()` call, so the public documents always reflect the anonymous-visible surface regardless of the requesting session's own account. **`genealogy_person` handling:** NOT added to `NON_PUBLIC_TYPES` — the access-aware `setAccount()` enumeration already excludes a restricted `genealogy_person` via the real, unmodified `GenealogyContentAccessPolicy` (unpublished-tree gate, living-person redaction for anonymous), proven end-to-end by a new `packages/genealogy/tests/Integration/GenealogySeoEnumerationTest.php` (real SQLite + real policy, through the real `SeoPublicController`) rather than special-cased. **Layering:** `waaseyaa/seo` (L3) added `waaseyaa/access` (L1) as a `require` for the `AccountInterface` type hint — a downward, legal edge; `waaseyaa/ssr` (L6) added `waaseyaa/user` (L1) for `AnonymousUser` — also downward and legal; `bin/check-package-layers` confirmed clean (no new WARN/FAIL). **Cache corollary:** the sitemap/llms/robots routes return a bare `Response` with only a `Content-Type` header — no `Cache-Control`/`max-age` is set, and there is no `RenderCache` path for `SeoPublicController` (confirmed) — so the fix takes effect immediately on the very next request; no CDN purge or TTL wait is required. New/updated regression coverage: `SeoPublicControllerAccessTest` (real SQLite + real `EntityAccessHandler` + a forbid-view policy, through the real controller — a published-but-restricted entity excluded from BOTH `/sitemap.xml` and `/llms.txt`, a public published entity present as positive control), `GenealogySeoEnumerationTest` (living-person and unpublished-tree `genealogy_person` exclusion via the real `GenealogyContentAccessPolicy`), plus mechanism-level unit tests in `SitemapGeneratorTest`/`SitemapGeneratorBindingTest`/`LlmsTxtGeneratorTest` pinning `setAccount()` binding and the no-account fail-closed default (superseding the old `#1527`/C-004 tests that asserted a blanket `accessCheck(false)`) — all exploit tests confirmed RED against pre-fix code (the restricted path enumerated in both documents) and GREEN after. **Adversarial sweep** of other public enumeration/listing surfaces gated only on `status`: `robots.txt` (`RobotsTxtGenerator`) carries no entity enumeration at all (out of scope); the discovery/browse API (`RelationshipTraversalService::browse()`/`traverse()`, `DiscoveryApiHandler`) and SSR's relationship render context already carry their own documented, separately-tracked `VisibilityFilterInterface` gate (a known, disclosed residual from the R5 changelog entry, same class, not this PR's scope — tracked for R7); no first-party RSS/Atom feed or OpenGraph enumeration surface exists in the framework today.**
- **The public SSR HTML render path applied NO field-level access control — a `FieldAccessPolicyInterface`-forbidden field of a viewable, published entity leaked to anonymous callers and was cached (audit M1, the api-M4 class re-opened on the HTML entry; audit-remediation batch 2026-07-03, R6 PR1).** `EntityRenderer::render()` (`packages/ssr/src/EntityRenderer.php:40`) built the Twig `fields` bag excluding only `ALWAYS_INTERNAL_FIELDS` (three credential names, `:20`) and the field-definition `internal` setting (`:63-80`), and took no account and no `EntityAccessHandler` at all — `SsrPageHandler` constructed it (`:204`) with only `entityTypeManager`/`formatterRegistry`/`viewModeConfig`. `RenderController::renderEntity()` (`packages/ssr/src/RenderController.php:103-128`) passed the unfiltered bag straight to Twig, and the shipped fallback `packages/ssr/templates/entity.html.twig:12-14` looped every field and printed `{{ field.formatted|raw }}` unconditionally — unlike the Markdown representation, which already threaded `$account` + `EntityAccessHandler` through `EntityMarkdownPresenter`/`ResourceSerializer::filterFields()` and failed closed with a 500 when unwired (`SsrPageHandler::renderEntityMarkdown()`). Fixed by mirroring that discipline on the HTML path: `EntityRenderer` now takes an optional `?EntityAccessHandler` constructor param and an optional `?AccountInterface $account` on `render()`; when an account is supplied, the **entity `fields` bag** is filtered through `EntityAccessHandler::filterFields($entity, ..., 'view', $account)` (open-by-default — only `Forbidden` drops a field) on top of the existing exclusions, so the field-content loop no longer prints a field the same account's JSON:API/Markdown representations withhold. `RenderController::renderEntity()` gained a matching `?AccountInterface $account` param it forwards to the renderer. `SsrPageHandler` now constructs `EntityRenderer` with its own `$accessHandler` and calls a new private `renderEntityHtml()` seam (mirroring `renderEntityMarkdown()` exactly) that fails CLOSED with a 500 — refusing to render at all — when no access handler is wired, rather than falling back to unfiltered output; `EntityRenderer::render()` itself also fails closed defensively (drops every field) if ever called with an account but no handler. **Scope carve-out (not a parity claim):** this change filters the `fields` bag only. The entity **label/title** is emitted separately — via the template's `<title>` block (`entity.html.twig:3`) and the schema.org JSON-LD (`EntitySchemaOrgMapper::map()`, `packages/seo/src/SchemaOrg/EntitySchemaOrgMapper.php:69`), both reading `$entity->label()` directly from raw storage, bypassing the bag — so a policy that forbids the label-key field on `'view'` would still expose the title. That is the **same pre-existing gap the Markdown H1 has** (JSON:API's `ResourceSerializer` does filter the label, so full HTML/Markdown/JSON:API parity is NOT claimed); its only practically-reachable case (a classification-held published node) is already denied whole-page by R6 PR2's node entity-view gate. Closing the residual cross-package label channel is tracked as a follow-up (**R7**). **Cache corollary:** the leaking HTML was written to the anonymous `RenderCache` under a key with no schema-version segment, so already-cached field-leaky pages would have kept serving after the code fix alone — `RenderCache::SCHEMA_VERSION` (`'v2'`) is now folded into `RenderCache::buildKey()`, making every pre-fix cache entry unreachable and forcing a re-render through the fixed, filtering path. **`|raw` investigation:** `entity.html.twig:14`'s `{{ field.formatted|raw }}` was audited for stored-XSS risk — every built-in formatter in `packages/ssr/src/Formatter/` either HTML-sanitizes (`HtmlFormatter` via `symfony/html-sanitizer`) or `htmlspecialchars()`-escapes its output (`PlainTextFormatter`, `EntityReferenceFormatter`, `ImageFormatter`), and `FieldFormatterRegistry`'s unknown-type fallback is `PlainTextFormatter` (escaped) — so `|raw` is safe for all shipped formatters; a third-party formatter registered via the manifest that skips escaping remains a theoretical extension-point risk, out of scope for this PR.**
