- **Two related agent-tool field-access-control holes in `packages/ai-tools`, same root cause: the tool never called a fail-closed field guard `AbstractAgentTool` already provides (audit-remediation batch 2026-07-03, R4 PR1, ai-tools M1/M2/M3).** Both reopen the same class of leak/escalation the sibling read/write tools (`EntityReadTool`, `EntityUpdateTool`) already close for their own paths. **(1) `entity.search` content oracle (M1).** `EntitySearchTool::execute()` (`packages/ai-tools/src/Entity/EntitySearchTool.php:93`) correctly gates entity-level view via `canViewEntity()`, but `matches()`/`haystackMatches()` (formerly lines 112-144) then substring-matched the needle against the entity's FULL values from `getValues()`/`toArray()` with no field-level filtering — an entity that is entity-level viewable but carries a `FieldAccessPolicyInterface`-forbidden, `internal`-marked, or hardcoded-credential field (SSN, 2FA secret, password hash) became a content oracle: search reported a match/no-match against a field the caller could not see. Fixed by threading `$account` into `matches()` and applying the same three-step filter `EntityReadTool::filterReadableValues()` already applies — now extracted into a shared `Waaseyaa\AI\Tools\Entity\EntityFieldRedaction::stripInternal()` helper (drop `ALWAYS_INTERNAL_FIELDS` credential keys, drop `FieldDefinition`-internal fields) followed by `AbstractAgentTool::applyFieldAccessFilter()` — to the haystack BEFORE matching, so a forbidden/internal field is never part of the search haystack; `EntityReadTool` now calls the same shared helper (behavior unchanged, its own regression suite still green). **(2) `entity.rollback` / `entity.set_current_revision` privileged-field self-escalation (M2/M3).** Both tools call `requireEntityAccess($entity, 'update', $account)` (`packages/ai-tools/src/Entity/EntityRollbackTool.php:80`, `EntitySetCurrentRevisionTool.php:80`) but never called `requireFieldEditAccess()` before the whole-row restore write (`EntityRollbackTool.php:84` `rollback()`, `EntitySetCurrentRevisionTool.php:84` `setCurrentRevision()`). `EntityRepository::rollback()`/`setCurrentRevision()` write the WHOLE target-revision row back — every field — so restoring an old revision could silently re-apply a `PRIVILEGED_EDIT_FIELDS` field (e.g. user `roles`/`status`, `UserAccessPolicy.php:96-103`, forbidden on `edit` without `administer users`) the account could never set through `entity.update` directly, reopening the `roles:['administrator']` self-escalation `entity.update` was already patched to close. Fixed by loading the target revision via `EntityRepository::loadRevision()`, computing the fields the restore would actually CHANGE relative to the current entity via a new shared `EntityRevisionRestoreGuard::changedValues()` helper (present-in-target-with-a-different-or-absent-current-value; revision-table bookkeeping columns AND `EntityFieldRedaction::ALWAYS_INTERNAL_FIELDS` credential keys excluded — credentials are unconditionally edit-forbidden for everyone, are not an escalation vector, and ride every revision snapshot, so gating on them would only break restore across any password rotation without adding protection; privilege-bearing fields like `roles`/`permissions`/`status` are deliberately kept in the set), and calling `requireFieldEditAccess($entity, $changedValues, $account)` before the write in both tools — refusing (no write) when a changed field is edit-forbidden, while a restore that leaves privileged fields untouched (or a caller with the required permission) still succeeds; if the target revision cannot be loaded, today's not-found/non-revisionable error handling is unchanged. New regression coverage: `EntitySearchToolFieldFilterTest` (field-access-forbidden, internal, and always-internal-credential fields never enter the search haystack for a restricted account, while search still works for an allowed account and for unrestricted content), `EntityRollbackToolFieldAccessTest` and `EntitySetCurrentRevisionToolFieldAccessTest` (a non-admin account with entity-level `update` but not `administer users` cannot restore/point-to a revision whose `roles` differ from current — no write happens — while an admin, or a restore that changes only non-privileged fields, still succeeds) — all confirmed RED against pre-fix code (the leak/escalation reproduced) and GREEN after. Adversarial sweep of every tool in `packages/ai-tools/src/Entity/` found no other tool missing `canViewEntity()`/`applyFieldAccessFilter()`/`requireFieldEditAccess()` where the risk pattern applies (`EntityListTool`/`EntityListRevisionsTool` filter every candidate via `canViewEntity()` but expose a bare `label()` unfiltered by `applyFieldAccessFilter()` — flagged as a low-severity follow-up, not fixed here — **CORRECTED 2026-07-04, R7 WP1: this "low-severity" classification was wrong for `EntityListRevisionsTool`, whose `tool.entity.read` capability IS on the public MCP anonymous read tier (`PublicAnonymousAuth::DEFAULT_READ_CAPABILITIES`), making its bare-label emission a LIVE anonymous field-access label leak; both tools are now fixed in the R7 WP1 entry above via the shared `AbstractAgentTool::viewableLabel()` helper**); `packages/ai-agent/src/Tool/Bimaaji/*` does not touch entity field access at all (not applicable); `packages/ai-agent/src/Tool/Wayfinding/*` (trail read/write tools) rely solely on a coarse capability check with none of the four `AbstractAgentTool` guards wired — already tracked as a separate remediation-batch PR (ai-agent M1), left untouched here as scoped; `relationship.traverse` is likewise a separate remediation-batch PR, left untouched here as scoped.
- **Endpoint-identity leak in `relationship.traverse` (ai-tools M4, audit-remediation batch 2026-07-03 R4 PR2).** Two coupled defects in `RelationshipTraverseTool.php`: (1) `execute()` (`:88-94` pre-fix) queried the relationship repository by `source_type`/`source_id`/`type` — criteria that do not exist on the real relationship schema (`RelationshipSchemaManager`: `from_entity_type`, `from_entity_id`, `to_entity_type`, `to_entity_id`, bundle key `relationship_type`), so the query always fell through to a `_data` JSON lookup that never matched and the tool silently returned 0 edges, masking the leak below entirely; (2) once queried correctly, the tool gated only the relationship EDGE record's own `AccessPolicy` (`canViewEntity($row)` at `:107` pre-fix) and ran `applyFieldAccessFilter()` on the edge's own values (`:112` pre-fix), but never checked whether the account could view the ENDPOINT entities whose identities the edge discloses (`to_entity_type`/`to_entity_id`) — the same disclosure class as the api M2 fix in #1874 (`RelationshipTraversalService::filterByEndpointVisibility()`/`nonSourceEndpoints()`). Confirmed live: with only the field-name fix applied, an edge whose own record is fully viewable but whose `to_entity_id` points at a view-forbidden entity was still returned in full. Fixed by (a) querying the real `from_entity_type`/`from_entity_id`/`relationship_type` columns for the out-direction traversal the tool already intends, and (b) adding a fail-closed per-endpoint visibility gate (`canViewEndpoint()`): for each edge that passes the edge-record gate, the tool resolves the non-source endpoint via `EntityTypeManagerInterface::getRepository($endpointType)->find($endpointId)` and drops the entire edge — never partially disclosing it — when the endpoint cannot be loaded (unknown type, missing record) or fails `canViewEntity()`, mirroring `filterByEndpointVisibility()`'s "prove NOTHING -> drop" fail-closed contract at the tool layer. A latent third defect (`method_exists($row, 'getValues') ? ... : []`, which always evaluated to `[]` since no relationship entity defines `getValues()`) was also fixed to fall back to the `EntityInterface`-guaranteed `toArray()` (mirroring `EntityReadTool`/`EntitySearchTool`), since the endpoint gate needs `to_entity_type`/`to_entity_id` to actually be present in the extracted values to check them.
- **Cross-account trail-overwrite hole in the Wayfinding MCP write tools (ai-agent M1, audit-remediation batch 2026-07-03, R4 PR3).** `EditTrailTool` (`packages/ai-agent/src/Tool/Wayfinding/EditTrailTool.php:90`, `TrailStore::editAsHuman()`) and `ReRecordTrailTool` (`ReRecordTrailTool.php:~87`, `TrailStore::reRecord()`) gated only the coarse `present guided content` capability before writing to whatever `trail_id` was supplied, and `TrailStore` writes via the raw `EntityRepository` with no account binding. `Waaseyaa\Wayfinding\Access\TrailAccessPolicy::access()` routes `update` owner-only (forbidden unless the account is the trail owner) — but that per-entity policy was never consulted by the two write tools, so any authenticated holder of `present guided content` (the same capability every trail author holds) could edit or re-record ANOTHER account's trail by supplying its `trail_id`, silently overwriting the owner's content. Confirmed exploit payload: `{"trail_id":"<victim-uuid>","title":"pwned","langcode":"en","beacons":[{"anchor_id":"any","content":"attacker text"}]}`. Fixed by a shared `AbstractTrailTool::requireTrailUpdateAccess()` helper that loads the target trail and calls `requireEntityAccess($trail, 'update', $account)` (fail-closed) BEFORE the store write, wired into both tools' `execute()` after the existing capability + argument checks. `RecordTrailTool` (self-attributes ownership to the caller on create) and `GetTrailTool` (view policy is owner-OR-capability-holder, matching its existing capability-only gate) were swept and confirmed already correct. Acceptance: `WayfindingTrailToolsTest::edit_is_forbidden_for_a_non_owner_even_with_the_capability`, `::rerecord_is_forbidden_for_a_non_owner_even_with_the_capability`, `::edit_and_rerecord_still_work_for_the_owner_with_a_real_handler_attached`.
- **Relationship endpoint-identity leak across every read path that applies field access (audit-remediation batch 2026-07-03, R5).** `Waaseyaa\Relationship\RelationshipAccessPolicy::access()` (`packages/relationship/src/RelationshipAccessPolicy.php`) gates entity-level `view` on only the edge's own `status`/`access content` permission (plus an `administer nodes` bypass) — it never checked whether the viewing account could see the edge's ENDPOINT entities named by `from_entity_type`/`from_entity_id`/`to_entity_type`/`to_entity_id`. Because a relationship edge is a thin pointer row, a viewable-but-otherwise-unrelated edge disclosed the identity of a hidden/unpublished/access-restricted endpoint to any baseline caller — including anonymous on a public install — through `GET /api/relationship` (collection AND single, via `JsonApiController`/`ResourceSerializer`) and the MCP `entity.read` tool (`EntityReadTool::applyFieldAccessFilter()`). Fixed with a new field-only access policy, `Waaseyaa\Relationship\RelationshipEndpointVisibilityPolicy`, implementing both `AccessPolicyInterface` (neutral on every operation — it never denies the whole edge, which stays `RelationshipAccessPolicy`'s job) and `FieldAccessPolicyInterface`: `fieldAccess()` loads the named endpoint via `EntityTypeManagerInterface::getRepository($type)->find($id)` and delegates to `EntityAccessHandler::check($endpoint, 'view', $account)`, redacting the (type, id) PAIR for whichever endpoint(s) the account cannot view — fail-closed (forbidden) when the endpoint id/type is empty, the type is unregistered, or the entity fails to load. Because `ResourceSerializer::filterFields()`, `JsonApiController::checkFieldAccess`, and `AbstractAgentTool::applyFieldAccessFilter()` all delegate to the same shared `EntityAccessHandler::filterFields()`/`checkFieldAccess()` plumbing, one field policy closes all three read paths at once. **Registered via attribute discovery** — `#[PolicyAttribute(entityType: 'relationship')]` with a `(EntityTypeManagerInterface, EntityAccessHandler)` constructor — so the SHARED-boot `AccessPolicyRegistry` (`AbstractKernel::discoverAccessPolicies()`) wires it on BOTH the `HttpKernel` AND the `ConsoleKernel`. The `ConsoleKernel` path is load-bearing: `entity.read` has real ConsoleKernel production callers (`bin/waaseyaa ai:run --inline`, `queue:work` driving `RunAgentHandler`), and an earlier cut that registered the policy only via `RelationshipServiceProvider::configureHttpKernel()` (a hook `ConsoleKernel` never invokes) left `entity.read` STILL leaking in CLI/queue contexts. The apparent discovery-time cycle (the policy needs the `EntityAccessHandler` to delegate to endpoint entities) is resolved by the registry's existing two-phase algorithm: a policy whose constructor takes `EntityAccessHandler` is DEFERRED to phase 2 and instantiated with the phase-1 preliminary handler injected (`KernelPolicyDependencyResolver`), and `EntityTypeManagerInterface` is resolved off the kernel-services bus (`ProviderRegistryKernelServices::get()`) — the same wiring `Waaseyaa\Engagement\EngagementAccessPolicy` and `Waaseyaa\Messaging\MessagingAccessPolicy` already use. New regression coverage: `RelationshipEndpointVisibilityPolicyTest` (isolated field-access unit coverage, mocked dependencies), `RelationshipEndpointVisibilityRestTest` (real `JsonApiController`+`ResourceSerializer`+`EntityAccessHandler` stack — collection and single, hidden-endpoint redaction for a baseline account, full record for `administer nodes`, a fully-public edge as positive control, and the both-endpoints-hidden residual pinned), `RelationshipEndpointVisibilityEntityReadTest` (same fixtures through the real `EntityReadTool`), and `RelationshipEndpointVisibilityDiscoveryTest` (BOOT-wiring guard: asserts the `#[PolicyAttribute]` is present and that the real two-phase `AccessPolicyRegistry->discover()` produces a handler that redacts a hidden endpoint WITHOUT hand-adding the policy — this fails if anyone reverts to the `configureHttpKernel` approach; the real `PackageManifestCompiler` was also confirmed to list the class in `manifest->policies`). All three exploit tests confirmed RED against pre-fix wiring (the hidden endpoint's `to_entity_id`/`to_entity_type` present in the response) and GREEN after. **Adversarial sweep** of other paths that could disclose `to_entity_id`/`from_entity_id`: GraphQL's `EntityResolver`/`GraphQlAccessGuard::filterFields()` delegates to the same shared `EntityAccessHandler`, so it is covered by the same fix; `relationship.traverse` (ai-tools M4, R4 PR2) already carries its own independent bespoke `canViewEndpoint()` fail-closed gate and was unaffected either way. **Two documented residuals, disclosed not fixed (both out of scope for this field-level PR):** (1) the discovery/browse API (`RelationshipTraversalService::browse()`/`traverse()`, `DiscoveryApiHandler`) and SSR (`SsrPageHandler::buildRelationshipRenderContext()`) BYPASS the field-access plumbing entirely — they use a separate `VisibilityFilterInterface` fail-closed gate (wired with `WorkflowVisibilityFilter` in production) that only understands workflow publish/unpublish status, not the full `AccessPolicyInterface` surface (a custom access-restricted endpoint that isn't simply "unpublished" could still leak there); (2) the both-endpoints-hidden case — when the account can view NEITHER endpoint, this field-only policy redacts all four endpoint identity fields but the edge itself is still returned (id, `relationship_type`, `status`), leaking edge-EXISTENCE metadata ("an edge of type X exists between two entities you cannot see", e.g. a sensitive `relationship_type`) with no identity crossing. Closing (2) is an ENTITY-level decision that belongs to `RelationshipAccessPolicy` (which this batch must not modify), so it is documented and pinned by `RelationshipEndpointVisibilityRestTest::both_endpoints_hidden_still_returns_the_edge_with_all_four_endpoint_fields_redacted` rather than fixed here.

## [0.1.0-alpha.253] - 2026-07-02

### Security

- **Nested-wrapper stored-XSS bypass in `HtmlSanitizeProcessor`'s DOMDocument allowlist filter — reopens B-4 at nesting depth (migration M1, audit-remediation batch 2026-07-02, R3 WP1).** `filterNode()` (`packages/migration/src/Plugin/Process/HtmlSanitizeProcessor.php:235-256` pre-fix) snapshotted an element's children ONCE into a local array, then iterated the snapshot; for each disallowed child it called `replaceWithTextContent()` and moved on. `replaceWithTextContent()` (`:306-338` pre-fix), for any wrapper other than `script`/`style`, hoisted ALL of the disallowed element's descendants up to become direct children of the parent verbatim (`while ($element->firstChild !== null) { $parent->insertBefore($child, $element); }`) before dropping the wrapper — but the hoisted nodes were never part of the pre-snapshotted `$children` array, so their tag was never re-checked against the allowlist and their attributes never passed back through `filterAttributes()` (where the `onerror`-style event-handler strip and the `javascript:`/`data:` URL-scheme check live). Confirmed by execution pre-fix: `<p><span><img src=x onerror=alert(1)></span></p>` sanitized to `<p><img src="x" onerror="alert(1)"></p>` (the `onerror` attribute survived intact), `<div><span><script>alert(1)</script></span></div>` retained the live `<script>` tag and its raw payload, and `<div><a href="javascript:alert(1)">x</a></div>` retained the `javascript:` href — all reachable via any migrated rich-text field (e.g. WordPress `post_content`) with markup nested one or more levels inside a disallowed tag. Fixed by extracting the per-child allowlist check into `applyChildPolicy()` and re-applying it to every node `replaceWithTextContent()` promotes, immediately after each `insertBefore()`, in its new position — recursively, at any nesting depth, to a fixpoint (each hoist strictly removes one wrapper level, so termination is guaranteed; a promoted node is either stripped again if still disallowed or attribute-filtered and recursed into if allowed). The regex/`strip_tags` fallback path (used only when `DOMDocument::loadHTML()` fails entirely) removes every tag unconditionally and was not affected — swept and confirmed clean; `filterAttributes()` was also swept and confirmed it cannot re-open a stripped attribute (it snapshots attribute names before mutating, same discipline as `filterNode()`). No other hand-rolled HTML-tree sanitizer exists elsewhere in the repo — `packages/validation/src/Constraint/SafeMarkupValidator.php` is a documented defense-in-depth regex blocklist (not a DOM allowlist filter, no tree mutation) that explicitly defers to `HtmlSanitizeProcessor` for untrusted rich text, and `packages/ssr`/`packages/genealogy` both delegate to the third-party `symfony/html-sanitizer` component rather than a first-party DOM walker. New regression coverage in `packages/migration/tests/Unit/Plugin/Process/HtmlSanitizeProcessorTest.php`: nested `onerror` under a stripped `<span>`, a nested `<script>` under a stripped `<span>`/`<div>`, a nested `javascript:` href and a nested `data:` URI under a stripped wrapper, three-levels-deep nesting around an `onerror` payload (proves the fixpoint, not a single extra re-check pass), and a benign-nesting case (`<div><p>hello</p></div>`) proving the fix does not over-strip allowed content hoisted out of a disallowed wrapper — all five exploit cases were confirmed RED (dangerous markup/attributes present in the output) against pre-fix code and GREEN after. **Sibling bypass closed in the same change — the CDATA-content-model tag group (`xmp` / `iframe` / `noembed` / `noframes` / `plaintext`), same B-4 class, no nesting required.** libxml2's HTML parser resolves the *content model* of these five tags (alongside `script`/`style`) by parsing their inner payload as a raw `\DOMCdataSection` (nodeType 4), NOT a `\DOMText` node, and `\DOMDocument::saveHTML()` serializes a CDATA section VERBATIM (no HTML-entity escaping). The first fix's `applyChildPolicy()` re-filter only re-ran on `instanceof \DOMElement`, so a hoisted `\DOMCdataSection` was skipped and emitted verbatim — a LIVE payload survived with no wrapper nesting: `<xmp><script>alert(1)</script></xmp>` sanitized to a live `<script>alert(1)</script>`, `<xmp><img src=x onerror=alert(9)></xmp>` to a live `<img … onerror=…>`, and the same for `iframe`/`noembed`/`noframes`/`plaintext` (RCDATA tags `title`/`textarea` were already safe — libxml parses them as `\DOMText`, which saveHTML escapes). Fixed two ways: (1) `replaceWithTextContent()`'s hoist loop now detects a `\DOMCdataSection` child and, via the shared `convertCdataToText()` helper, replaces it in place with an escaped text node (`createTextNode($cdata->data)`) so once hoisted into a normal-content parent saveHTML entity-escapes it (`<script>` → `&lt;script&gt;`), neutralizing the markup while preserving the author's visible text; `filterNode()` applies the same conversion to any direct `\DOMCdataSection` child. (2) Because saveHTML decides escaping from a node's PARENT content model — a text node kept INSIDE a raw-text element still serializes verbatim — the five CDATA-content-model tags (plus `script`/`style`) are now force-stripped even when a custom allowlist names them (`RAW_TEXT_CONTENT_MODEL_TAGS`, checked in `applyChildPolicy()`), routing their payload through the hoist path where escaping is guaranteed; they can never be kept as raw-content wrappers. New regression coverage adds a data-provider case per CDATA-model tag (`xmp`/`iframe`/`noembed`/`noframes`/`plaintext` wrapping a `<script>` → no live `<script>`), an `<xmp><img onerror>` case (no live `<img>`), a nested `<div><span><xmp><script>` case (hoist path), a custom-allowlist `allowedTags: ['xmp']` case (the force-strip path), and a benign `<xmp>` case carrying Indigenous orthography (`ā`, `ʼ`, `ᐊᓂᔑᓈᐯ`) proving the CDATA-to-text conversion preserves multibyte visible text — all dangerous cases confirmed RED (raw `<script>`/`<img>` in output) against the post-nested-fix code and GREEN after.

### Fixed

- **A backward wall-clock step mid-run turned a fully-committed, successful migration into an uncaught CLI crash — the CI "timing flake" (migration M2, audit-remediation batch 2026-07-02 R3 WP2).** `RunReport::__construct()` (`packages/migration/src/Runner/RunReport.php:98-102`) throws `\InvalidArgumentException('RunReport::$startedAt must be <= $finishedAt.')` when `$startedAt > $finishedAt`. `MigrationRunner::runInternal()` (`packages/migration/src/Runner/MigrationRunner.php`) captured `$startedAt` once from the injected clock, which defaults to non-monotonic wall-clock `new \DateTimeImmutable('now', UTC)`, and then captured `$finishedAt = ($this->clock)()` independently before each of the three `RunReport` construction sites — the halt-on-error abort (formerly line ~266), the run-level FR-048 abort (formerly line ~296), and the happy-path return (formerly line ~324) — with no monotonic clamp. A backward wall-clock step during a run (NTP step, VM/WSL suspend-resume, leap-second smear) tripped the invariant; the happy-path construction sits outside the outer `try`/`catch`, so the throw was uncaught and crashed the CLI (`ImportRunCommand` only catches `MigrationAbortedException`) on a migration whose data was already fully committed (every entity save / id-map upsert commits per-record before the run finishes). Fixed by adding `MigrationRunner::nowClampedTo(\DateTimeImmutable $startedAt): \DateTimeImmutable`, which captures `($this->clock)()` and advances a regressed finish stamp forward by 1 microsecond over `$startedAt` — mirroring `RollbackWalker::rollback()`'s existing identical clamp for `RollbackReport`. All three `$finishedAt = ($this->clock)();` captures now call `nowClampedTo($startedAt)`. `RunReport`'s invariant itself is unchanged (kept as a last-resort guard against a genuine caller bug). Sweep of other wall-clock/duration invariants in the package: `RollbackReport`'s own `finishedAt >= startedAt` invariant has exactly one construction site (`RollbackWalker::rollback()`), already clamped before this fix — no change needed; `MigrationRunState::upsert()` only asserts `$position >= 0`, no timestamp-ordering invariant; no other throwing timestamp/duration invariant found in `packages/migration`. Acceptance (TDD red→green): new `MigrationRunnerTest` cases inject a descending-clock test seam (`$startedAt` then `$startedAt - 1s`) and prove, against the pre-fix source, that `run()` throws the uncaught `InvalidArgumentException` on the happy path, the halt-on-error abort path, and the run-level FR-048 abort path — all three confirmed red before the clamp; post-fix, `run()` returns/raises normally with `report->finishedAt >= report->startedAt` clamped in every case. This is the same `RunReport::$startedAt must be <= $finishedAt` assertion that surfaces intermittently under full-suite Integration ordering on non-monotonic-clock hosts (WSL2, CI runners); the clamp removes the crash path so a completed run always reports rather than throwing.

## [0.1.0-alpha.252] - 2026-07-02

### Security

- **Anonymous SQL injection via an unvalidated JSON:API filter/sort field name — the audit headline finding (audit-remediation batch 2026-07-02 R2, WP1).** `QueryParser::parseFilters()`/`parseSorts()` (`packages/api/src/Query/QueryParser.php:48,75-87`) took the filter/sort field straight from the raw query-string array key with zero validation. `JsonApiController::rejectInternalQueryFields()` (`packages/api/src/JsonApiController.php`) was a DENY-LIST only (`ALWAYS_INTERNAL_FIELDS` + `settings['internal']`), so any other field name passed through unchanged. `QueryApplier::apply()` (`packages/api/src/Query/QueryApplier.php:29-35`) forwarded the field verbatim into `EntityQueryInterface::condition()`/`sort()`. `SqlEntityQuery::resolveField()` (`packages/entity-storage/src/SqlEntityQuery.php`, three call sites) then interpolated the field name RAW into a `json_extract(<alias>._data, '$.<FIELD>')` SQL fragment emitted verbatim via `whereRaw()`/`orderByRaw()` — only the filter *value* was bound as a parameter; the field *identifier* was not. A field name containing a single quote broke out of the `'$.<FIELD>'` string literal, reachable by any anonymous `GET /api/{entityType}?filter[...]=...` or `?sort=...` request. Fixed at both layers (defense-in-depth): **(a) API-layer allowlist** — `rejectInternalQueryFields()` is replaced by `JsonApiController::validateQueryFields()`, which rejects any filter/sort field that is not in `array_keys(EntityTypeManagerInterface::resolveFieldDefinitions($entityTypeId))` union the entity type's structural keys (`EntityTypeInterface::getKeys()` — id/uuid/label/bundle/langcode/...); the always-internal/credential-field and `settings['internal']` checks are preserved on top of the allowlist, so a *declared* secret field is still rejected. Note the allowlist resolves `resolveFieldDefinitions($entityTypeId)` **without a bundle**, so a bundle-specific field (one declared only on a bundle, not as a base/core field) is not filterable/sortable on the cross-bundle collection endpoint — a deliberate tightening (no current caller relies on it; the allowlist is intentionally NOT expanded to bundle fields). **(b) Storage-layer sink guards — BOTH json_extract sinks.** There are two independent SQL query engines that resolve a non-column field to a raw `json_extract(_data, '$.<field>')` expression: `SqlEntityQuery::resolveField()` (`packages/entity-storage/src/SqlEntityQuery.php`, three call sites; the `getQuery()->execute()` path) and its **twin** `SqlStorageDriver::resolveField()` (`packages/entity-storage/src/Driver/SqlStorageDriver.php`; the `EntityRepository::findBy()`/`count()` path). The twin was reachable **today** and bypassed both layer-(a) allowlist and the SqlEntityQuery guard: `EntityRepository::findBy(criteria, orderBy)` / `count(criteria)` forward attacker-controlled array keys verbatim, and `ai-tools` `EntityListTool` (`packages/ai-tools/src/Entity/EntityListTool.php`, an `#[AsAgentTool]` whose filter/sort JSON schema is open) drives exactly that path. Both sinks now call one shared identifier allowlist, `Waaseyaa\EntityStorage\Query\JsonFieldName::assertQueryable()` (validates `/^[A-Za-z0-9_]+$/`, throws `\InvalidArgumentException` otherwise), immediately before interpolating a field name into a json_extract expression — SqlEntityQuery at each of its three sites, SqlStorageDriver at its one site. With both sinks guarded, the raw fragment is un-injectable regardless of caller (API request, agent tool, or any future direct `EntityRepository` consumer). Fixture follow-on: `packages/api/tests/Fixtures/TestEntity.php`-based test suites (`JsonApiControllerFilterTest`, `JsonApiControllerSortingTest`, `JsonApiControllerInternalFieldQueryTest`) previously filtered/sorted on undeclared `_data` fields (`status`, `body`, `category`, `weight`) that real entities (node/user) declare as `#[Field]`s but the bare test fixture did not — those fixtures now declare the fields via `EntityType`'s `_fieldDefinitions` slot so legitimate declared-field filtering stays green under the new allowlist; `JsonApiControllerSortingTest::indexSortByNonexistentFieldIsRejected` (formerly `...ReturnsDefaultOrder`) now asserts the correct new behavior — an undeclared field is a 400, not a silent no-op. The top-level integration test `tests/Integration/Phase7/JsonApiFilterSortPageIntegrationTest.php` got the same treatment: it registers its own bare `node` `EntityType` and filters/sorts on `status` (undeclared `_data`), `type` (bundle key — already allowlisted), and `title` (label key — already allowlisted), so only `status` needed declaring via `_fieldDefinitions`; done in this same change so no integration test is left red and no gate is masked. New tests: `packages/api/tests/Unit/Query/JsonApiControllerFieldAllowlistTest.php` (malicious filter/sort field names and plain undeclared fields are rejected 400; declared fields and entity-key fields still work) and `packages/entity-storage/tests/Unit/SqlEntityQueryJsonFieldNameGuardTest.php` (covers BOTH sinks: a malicious condition/sort field name on the `SqlEntityQuery` path throws `\InvalidArgumentException` before reaching SQL, a malicious `EntityRepository::findBy()`/`count()` criteria/orderBy key on the `SqlStorageDriver` twin path throws likewise, and a valid `_data` field name still resolves and executes on both paths).
- **The unauthenticated relationship-discovery endpoints let an anonymous caller pass `?status=all` (or `?status=unpublished`) straight through to `RelationshipTraversalService::browse()`, bypassing the per-account visibility filter and leaking unpublished/private related-entity identities (`to_entity_type`/`to_entity_id`) and edge metadata (audit-remediation batch 2026-07-02, R2 WP2, api M2).** `Waaseyaa\Api\Http\Router\DiscoveryRouter` built its `status` browse option the same unguarded way at all four discovery actions — `handleTopicHub()`, `handleCluster()`, `handleTimeline()`, `handleEndpoint()` (`packages/api/src/Http/Router/DiscoveryRouter.php:73/109/146/193` pre-fix) — reading `$ctx->query['status']` verbatim and forwarding it to `RelationshipDiscoveryService`/`RelationshipTraversalService::browse()`. `status: 'all'` is browse()'s explicit "system context, unfiltered" spelling (no endpoint-visibility filtering at all — see `docs/specs/relationship-modeling.md` "Endpoint visibility"), and `status: 'unpublished'` surfaces draft edges; neither should ever reach an unauthorized caller. Fixed by adding `DiscoveryRouter::resolveDiscoveryStatus()`, a private helper called from all four sites, that clamps any non-`'published'` request to `'published'` unless the caller is `$ctx->account->isAuthenticated() && $ctx->account->hasPermission('administer nodes')` — the exact same admin-bypass permission `RelationshipAccessPolicy` already uses for entity-level relationship access, so the discovery-status gate stays consistent with the entity-level policy. Regression coverage in `packages/api/tests/Unit/Http/Router/DiscoveryRouterTest.php` seeds a real published source node, an unpublished "secret" related node, and a relationship edge between them (mirroring `tests/Integration/Phase14/DiscoveryFixtureConsumersIntegrationTest.php`'s fixture shape), then proves: an anonymous `status=all`/`status=unpublished` request is clamped to `published` and the secret node's identity never appears in the response (both failed against pre-fix code), while an `administer nodes` account's `status=all` request is honored unchanged.
- **`SchemaController::show()` failed OPEN when the field-access prototype entity could not be constructed, letting the schema endpoint emit unfiltered field metadata to an authenticated caller (audit-remediation batch 2026-07-02 R2, api M3, WP3).** In the production, `requireAuthentication()`-gated path (`accessHandler` and `account` both non-null), `show()` builds a prototype entity via `new $class($protoValues)` so `SchemaPresenter::present()` can run per-field access checks against it. `SchemaController.php:77-86`'s catch block logged a warning and left `$entity = null`, but the method still called `present($entityType, ..., $entity, $this->accessHandler, $this->account)` with that null entity. `SchemaPresenter::present()`'s field-access-filtering block (`packages/api/src/Schema/SchemaPresenter.php:180`) is gated on `$entity !== null && $accessHandler !== null && $account !== null`; with `$entity === null` the ENTIRE per-account filtering step — view-denied field removal and edit-denied `readOnly`/`x-access-restricted` marking — was skipped, so the response carried a 200 with a fully unfiltered schema, including fields a real per-account check would have hidden. Any entity class whose constructor throws on the minimal `$protoValues` shape SchemaController built (only the requested bundle key) triggered this. Fix, two parts: **(a) restore availability by seeding the prototype** — before constructing the prototype, `show()` now seeds a non-null, type-appropriate placeholder (`0` for integer fields, `false` for boolean, `''` otherwise — presence, not value validity, is what constructor `isset()` gates test) for every declared field (from `resolveFieldDefinitions($entityTypeId, $bundle)`) and every entity key (from `getKeys()`), in addition to the bundle key. This makes `new $class($protoValues)` succeed for constructor-strict-but-seedable production types that require certain fields to be present — e.g. `UserBlock` (requires `blocker_id`, ships via `waaseyaa/user` → `waaseyaa/core`, so it reaches every core consumer), engagement `Comment`/`Reaction`/`Follow`, messaging `MessageThread`/`ThreadMessage`/`ThreadParticipant` — which pre-fix returned a leaky-but-functional 200 and, under the first cut of this WP, would have unconditionally 500'd. With a real prototype, `present()` runs its per-account field filtering as intended (no leak, 200 schema). **(b) fail CLOSED as a last-resort backstop** — the `try`/`catch` is kept: if a constructor STILL throws after all declared fields + keys are seeded (a genuinely pathological type), the catch block logs at ERROR (naming the entity type, class, and exception message — server-side only) and returns a `JsonApiError::internalError()` 500 document with a generic client-facing detail (`Could not generate the access-filtered schema for '{entityTypeId}'.`) instead of falling through to `present()` with a null entity — so no unfiltered schema is ever emitted, and the exception detail never reaches the client. The non-access-context path (`accessHandler`/`account` both null — no account to filter for, presenting the base schema is not a leak) is unchanged. Acceptance (TDD): `SchemaControllerTest::showSeedsRequiredFieldsSoConstructorStrictTypeReturnsFilteredSchema` registers a fixture (`RequiredFieldTestEntity`) whose constructor throws unless `owner_id` is present (mirroring the affected production types) plus a policy forbidding view of a `secret` field, and asserts a 200 whose schema is present, has `secret` filtered out, and retains the non-restricted `owner_id` — proving both restored availability and that filtering ran against the constructed prototype. `::showFailsClosedWhenPrototypeConstructionThrows` keeps the backstop pinned with `ThrowingPrototypeTestEntity`, a fixture whose constructor throws UNCONDITIONALLY even with every field seeded: proven to return 200 with `secret` present in the unfiltered schema against the pre-fix (fall-through) source, and post-fix a 500 with no `meta`/`schema` and no occurrence of `secret` anywhere in the response body, and no exception class name/message in the error `detail`. `::showStillReturnsFilteredSchemaWhenPrototypeConstructionSucceeds` pins the plain happy path unaffected; all pre-existing `SchemaControllerTest` cases remain green.
- **Two sibling `ResourceSerializer` per-account-filter-skip leaks — an entity-view-restricted field could appear in SSR Markdown AND in every translation CRUD response — because both call sites serialized with a null account, so `ResourceSerializer::serialize()` applied only the static internal-field strip and skipped the dynamic per-account field filter (audit-remediation batch 2026-07-02, R2 WP4, api M4).** `ResourceSerializer::serialize()` only applies the per-account `FieldAccessPolicyInterface` filter `if ($accessHandler !== null && $account !== null)`; with both null it falls back to the static internal/credential-field-only strip, so a field a policy forbids `view` for the requesting account still serializes. Two independent surfaces hit this: **(1) SSR Markdown.** `EntityMarkdownPresenter::present()` (`packages/api/src/Markdown/EntityMarkdownPresenter.php`) documented that field-access filtering was delegated to `ResourceSerializer::serialize()`, but its `$accessHandler`/`$account` parameters were nullable and defaulted to `null`, and its ONLY caller — `SsrPageHandler::renderEntityMarkdown()` (`packages/ssr/src/SsrPageHandler.php:~854` pre-fix) — hardcoded both to `null` (`$presenter->present($entity, $viewMode->name, null, null, $canonicalUrl)`), so the `?raw` / `Accept: text/markdown` representation leaked restricted fields. **(2) Translation CRUD.** `TranslationController` (`packages/api/src/Controller/TranslationController.php`) called `$this->serializer->serialize($translation)` with no handler/account at all four response sites — `index()` (line 63), `show()` (line 104), `store()` (line 184), `update()` (line 252) — leaking restricted fields in every translation response to any account with entity view/create/update access (an `AnonymousUser` satisfies the controller's non-null-account gate). Fixes: **(1)** `EntityMarkdownPresenter::present()`'s `$viewMode`, `$accessHandler`, and `$account` parameters are now REQUIRED and non-nullable (`EntityAccessHandler $accessHandler, AccountInterface $account`, no defaults) — making the guarantee structural, so omitting the filter is a `TypeError`, not a silent bypass; `SsrPageHandler::renderEntityMarkdown()` gained an `AccountInterface $account` parameter (threaded from `handleRenderPage()`'s existing account) and passes `$this->accessHandler`/`$account` through, failing CLOSED with a 500 when `$this->accessHandler` is null (unreachable in production — `SsrServiceProvider` always wires it from the kernel's non-nullable `getAccessHandler()` — but possible via direct/test construction). **(2)** `TranslationController` now resolves the account the same way `checkAccess()` does (`$request->attributes->get('_account')`, guarded non-null) and threads `$this->accessHandler` + that account into all four `serialize()` calls; `store()`/`update()` hoist the already-resolved account so the existing field-edit gate and the response filter share one account. New/updated tests: `packages/api/tests/Unit/Markdown/EntityMarkdownPresenterTest.php` — `access_filtering_omits_forbidden_fields_identically_to_serializer` now contrasts the presenter's filtered output against `ResourceSerializer::serialize($entity)`'s unfiltered static-only path (the leaking path a null/null presenter call used to reach); new `present_throws_when_called_with_a_null_account_or_handler` proves the vulnerable null/null call shape now raises a `TypeError`. New `packages/ssr/tests/Unit/SsrEntityMarkdownAccessTest.php` drives `renderEntityMarkdown()` end to end (forbidden `secret_note` field absent from Markdown; fail-closed 500 with no leaked content when no handler is wired). New `tests/Integration/Phase29/TranslationFieldAccessTest.php` boots `TranslationController` with a policy that allows entity-level view/update but forbids `view` on a `secret` field, and asserts the field is absent from the `index()`/`show()`/`update()` responses while a non-restricted field is present, plus a baseline test showing `ResourceSerializer::serialize($translation)` (the pre-fix no-account path) still leaks the field.

## [0.1.0-alpha.251] - 2026-07-02

### Security

- **`EngagementAccessPolicy` let an anonymous visitor own — and DELETE, or view unpublished — any reaction/comment/follow row with `user_id` 0, and the field was client-writable with no field policy at all, so any authenticated account could mint such a row (audit-remediation batch 2026-07-02, WP1).** Two compounding halves, both fixed: (1) `EngagementAccessPolicy::isOwner()` (`packages/engagement/src/EngagementAccessPolicy.php`) compared `(int) $entity->get('user_id') === (int) $account->id()` with no `isAuthenticated()` guard. `AnonymousUser::id()` returns `0`, so any row with `user_id === 0` made every anonymous visitor its "owner", granting anonymous `DELETE` (`ownerCheck()`) and anonymous view of otherwise-hidden unpublished comments (`viewAccess()`'s moderation branch) — the exact same class of hole `NodeAccessPolicy::access()` already guards against for `uid`/`getAuthorId()` (`$isOwner = $account->isAuthenticated() && $account->id() === $entity->getAuthorId()`), just never ported to engagement. `isOwner()` now carries the identical `isAuthenticated() &&` guard. (2) `user_id` had zero `FieldAccessPolicyInterface` coverage — `EngagementAccessPolicy` implemented only `AccessPolicyInterface` — so `JsonApiController::store()`'s open-by-default `checkFieldAccess('edit')` pass let any authenticated attacker `POST` a comment/reaction/follow with an arbitrary `user_id`, including `0` (minting the anonymous-owned row the first half exploits) or another account's id (falsely attributing a row to a stranger). `EngagementAccessPolicy` now also implements `FieldAccessPolicyInterface`: a new `fieldAccess()` forbids editing `user_id` outright on an existing entity (ownership is immutable after creation — no "own" permission ever reassigns it, only `administer content` bypasses), and on create forbids any submitted `user_id` that does not equal the caller's own account id (the entity constructors already require `user_id`, so a legitimate client always submits its own — this is what actually closes the anonymous-ownership hole, since the exploit payload never reaches storage). Mirrors `NodeAccessPolicy`'s existing `ADMIN_ONLY_EDIT_FIELDS` field-lock pattern, stricter: node's `uid` stays admin-editable and author-settable-on-create, engagement's `user_id` is immutable outright once the row exists. Acceptance (all TDD red→green against the pre-fix source): `EngagementAccessPolicyTest::anonymous_is_never_owner_of_a_user_id_zero_row_on_delete` and `::anonymous_cannot_view_unpublished_comment_with_user_id_zero` (both proven to return `Allowed` pre-fix); new `EngagementFieldAccessPolicyTest` — `non_admin_cannot_reassign_user_id_on_existing_entity`, `non_admin_cannot_create_row_owned_by_anonymous_zero`, `non_admin_cannot_create_row_owned_by_someone_else`, `non_admin_can_create_self_owned_row`, `admin_may_edit_user_id`, `field_gate_ignores_view_and_other_fields`, `implements_field_access_policy_interface` (all six behavioral cases proven to fail against the pre-fix policy — the field-access ones via `Call to undefined method fieldAccess()`). Existing `owner_can_delete` / `view_of_unpublished_comment_is_allowed_to_owner` tests updated to mock `isAuthenticated(): true` for the legitimate owner case, now that ownership requires authentication.
- **Media upload MIME validation was fail-open (attacker-declared fallback) with a loose allowlist that included `image/svg+xml` and `application/octet-stream` — now sniff-only and fail-closed (audit-remediation batch 2026-07-01, WP4).** `MediaRouter::handleMediaUpload()` validated `getMimeType() ?? getClientMimeType()`: whenever Symfony's guesser was unavailable or returned null, the ATTACKER-DECLARED client MIME was trusted. Worse, on this monorepo (and any consumer without `symfony/mime` — it is not installed here, only suggested transitively) `getMimeType()` THROWS `LogicException`, so every real upload 500'd; the unit tests masked this by stubbing `getMimeType()`, and the strict image-only `UploadHandler::DEFAULT_ALLOWED_TYPES` validator was never consulted by the router (its own `validate()` also silently fell back to the client `type` when no sniffable file was present). Fixes, unified on `UploadHandler` as the single validator: (1) MIME is now detected ONLY from file contents — new `UploadHandler::detectMimeType()` (ext-fileinfo, injectable detector seam for tests); when detection is unavailable or inconclusive the upload is REJECTED (415 `File type could not be verified.` / validate() error) — the client-declared type is never consulted, not even as a fallback, and Symfony's `getMimeType()` is no longer called at all (also un-breaking real uploads on symfony/mime-less installs). (2) `application/octet-stream` removed from the router's default allowlist AND the shipped `config/waaseyaa.php`/`skeleton` allowlists — it is finfo's answer for ANY unrecognized binary, so allowlisting it allowlisted arbitrary content. (3) `image/svg+xml` likewise removed from defaults (option chosen over serve-with-`Content-Disposition: attachment`+`nosniff`, which is not implementable this WP: media bytes are served by the host's static `/files/` mapping, no PHP serving path exists — `packages/media/README.md`); config can opt back in explicitly via `upload_allowed_mime_types` (no SVG sanitization attempted, per WP scope). (4) Allowlist matching unified in `UploadHandler::mimeTypeMatches()` (exact + `type/*` wildcards, now also honored by `UploadHandler::validate()`); the stored `File.mimeType`/upload response now record the sniffed type. Known residuals noted in the PR: the sanitized filename keeps the client extension without MIME cross-check; the container `UploadHandler` singleton still reads the separate unused `media.*` config keys; shipped `files_dir` config key is consumed by nothing (router reads `files_root`). Acceptance (all TDD red→green): `MediaRouterTest` — spoofed-client-MIME rejected with sniffed type in the error, octet-stream rejected by default, SVG rejected by default, SVG accepted via explicit config opt-in, null-detection fails closed (415), defaults exclude svg/octet-stream, happy path + move-failure paths now run WITHOUT `getMimeType()` stubs (real PNG/text bytes); `UploadHandlerTest` — client-declared type without sniffable file rejected, injected-null detector rejected, sniffed-type validation, wildcard allowlists, `detectMimeType()`/`mimeTypeMatches()` contracts.
- **Framework-wide dead-subscriber sweep: three more `ServiceProvider::boot()` sites silently registered nothing in a real kernel boot because they resolved the event dispatcher under an FQCN the kernel-services bus never serves — the same class of bug #1852 fixed for the relationship delete guard (audit-remediation batch 2026-07-01/02, WP4).** `ProviderRegistryKernelServices::get()` serves the dispatcher ONLY under `\Symfony\Contracts\EventDispatcher\EventDispatcherInterface`; resolving `Waaseyaa\Foundation\Event\EventDispatcherInterface` or `Symfony\Component\EventDispatcher\EventDispatcherInterface` instead returns null (`resolveOptional()`) or throws (`resolve()`), and any `addListener()`/`addSubscriber()` guarded by that resolution never registers — no exception, no log line, just a silently absent guard or telemetry stream. A repo-wide sweep of every `addListener(`/`addSubscriber(` call site found: (1) **`packages/field/src/FieldServiceProvider.php`** — the classification-label lifecycle subscriber (`EntityLifecycleSubscriber`, which `docs/specs/classification-and-retention.md` documents as running "on every save") never registered; entities have been saving without their effective classification label being resolved or audited. (2) **`packages/ai-observability/src/AgentTelemetryServiceProvider.php`** — the AgentRun telemetry listener (cost/token accounting, best-effort try-catch internally) never registered. (3) **`packages/ai-observability/src/ObservabilityServiceProvider.php`** — resolved the wrong FQCN AND instanceof-checked the wrong concrete class (`Symfony\Component\EventDispatcher\EventDispatcher`, which the served `SymfonyEventDispatcherAdapter` is not); the LLM-call/tool-call cost telemetry listeners never registered (separately noted: their producer-side events, `Waaseyaa\AI\Agent\Event\{LlmCallCompleted,ToolCallStarted,ToolCallCompleted}`, are not yet dispatched anywhere in the codebase — this fix corrects the dead registration mechanism but the feature remains otherwise unwired; follow-up tracked outside this WP). All three re-keyed to the #1852 pattern (resolve the served Symfony-contracts FQCN, then instanceof-check the foundation contract) with production-mirroring wiring tests (`FieldServiceProviderClassificationWiringTest`, `AgentTelemetryServiceProviderTest`, `ObservabilityServiceProviderTest::boot_wires_llm_and_tool_call_listeners`) that stub the kernel-services bus under the SAME key production uses — each proven red against the pre-fix source before the re-key. Verified-alive (no change needed): `packages/relationship` and `packages/attachment` (already fixed by #1852 / the WP2 attachment cluster), `packages/audit/src/AuditServiceProvider.php` and `packages/search/src/SearchServiceProvider.php` (already resolve the correct contracts key), `packages/listing/src/ServiceProvider.php` (defensively tries three keys including the served one), `packages/ssr/src/SsrServiceProvider.php` (dispatcher passed directly by `HttpKernel`, no container lookup), and `packages/foundation`'s `AbstractKernel`/`EventListenerRegistrar` (same direct-dispatcher-property pattern). **Deliberately left dead**: `packages/media/src/MediaServiceProvider.php`'s versioning subscribers (`MediaVersionStorageDriver`, `MediaCascadeDeleteSubscriber`) — re-keying would part-activate the versioned-blob-media cascade-delete subsystem, including data-lossy cascade deletes, ahead of the finish-or-park decision tracked in #1742; a prominent docblock at the resolution site documents this explicitly. New CI gate `bin/check-dispatcher-keys` (wired into `composer verify`) scans every `register()`/`boot()` method for this exact pattern — an unserved dispatcher FQCN resolution followed by `addListener()`/`addSubscriber()` in the same method — and fails on any NEW occurrence; the one pre-existing occurrence (media) is baselined in `tools/dispatcher-key-baseline.txt` with a mandatory rationale comment, and the gate's own self-test (`tests/Integration/Phase31/DispatcherKeysGateTest.php`) plants synthetic offending/exempt/served-key fixtures to prove the scanner is not a silent no-op. Activating the field subscriber also fixed a latent test corruption: `ClassificationRetentionIntegrationTest` had hand-wired its own duplicate `EntityLifecycleSubscriber` onto `PRE_SAVE` as a documented workaround for the very wiring bug this WP fixes — with the production wiring now live, the SECOND subscriber pass read the label the FIRST pass had just written, treated it as an explicit override, and wiped `classification_inherited_from` back to null (silently corrupting inheritance provenance on every save); the hand-wired workaround is removed with an explanatory comment, and the test's provenance assertions pass through the production wiring alone. **Same-day adversarial-review hardening (4 confirmed findings, TDD where behavioral):** (a) **the new gate never actually ran in CI** — `composer verify`'s script array is not invoked by CI; `check-dispatcher-keys` is now in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s `ci/verify-gates` `run_gate` list like its siblings. (b) **the now-live classification subscriber polluted every entity's value bag** — it unconditionally wrote three NULL classification keys into EVERY entity on EVERY save (leaking into the `_data` blob of all sql-blob entities, including types with zero classification exposure — the common case); `EntityLifecycleSubscriber` now skips the write-back when the decision is all-null AND nothing needs clearing (no previous label, no stale in-memory classification value), while an all-null decision that CLEARS a previous label or stale provenance is still written and audited (pinned red→green: `classification_uninvolved_entity_gets_no_stray_keys_written` + the production-wired-dispatcher pair in `FieldServiceProviderClassificationWiringTest`, plus clearing counter-cases). (c) **gate blind spot** — the scanner missed the direct `$this->kernelServices?->get(...)` bus-access shape (the style `packages/listing` actually uses); now caught (red→green via a planted self-test fixture), with a listing-style multi-key-fallback exemption (a method that also resolves the served contracts FQCN anywhere in its body is skipped, since its registration is reachable in a real boot), and the remaining known-not-caught shapes (split-method registration, variable/alias indirection, resolves-both-registers-dead) documented explicitly in the script header as accepted regex-scanner limits. (d) `docs/specs/ai-integration.md` gains an "Observability Wiring Status" section documenting what is live post-WP4 (AgentRun telemetry — producers active) versus registered-but-producer-inert (`LlmCallListener`/`ToolCallListener` — their `LlmCallCompleted`/`ToolCallStarted`/`ToolCallCompleted` events are defined but dispatched nowhere; a follow-up issue tracks producer wiring).
- **`TermAccessPolicy::checkViewAccess()` treated a taxonomy term with a missing/absent `status` as published, granting public view — the taxonomy analogue of the class of policy-side `?? true` fail-open the audit flagged and WP1 already fixed in `EngagementAccessPolicy` (audit-remediation batch 2026-07-02, WP4).** `packages/taxonomy/src/TermAccessPolicy.php`'s `checkViewAccess()` computed `$published = (bool) ($entity->get('status') ?? true)`, so any `EntityInterface` the policy is handed that cannot yield a `status` value — hydrated without the column, a stub/mock, a future non-`Term` implementor of the interface — was treated as published and viewable by any account with `access content`, bypassing the unpublished branch's stricter vocabulary-editor/admin gate entirely. The fallback is now fail-closed: `$entity->get('status') ?? false`, so an undetermined status is treated as unpublished and routes through the same editor-or-admin-only branch a genuinely unpublished term already uses. `Term::isPublished()` and `Term`'s own constructor default (which seeds `status = true`, i.e. published-by-default) are deliberately untouched — this is a policy-side access-check hardening only, not a change to the entity's product-level default. Acceptance (TDD red→green): `TermAccessPolicyTest::testViewAccessDeniedWhenStatusAbsentFailsClosed` proven to return `Allowed` against the pre-fix `?? true` fallback, green after the flip; companion `testViewAccessAllowedForAdminWhenStatusAbsent` and `testViewAccessAllowedForVocabularyEditorWhenStatusAbsent` confirm the admin bypass and the unpublished-branch editor grant both still apply to an absent-status term. Two pre-existing tests that had pinned the fail-open default (`testViewAccessAllowedWithAccessContentPermission`, `testViewAccessNeutralWithoutPermission` — both previously relied on the mock's default `published: null` reaching the published branch) now construct an explicitly `published: true` term; tests that already set status explicitly (published or unpublished) were unaffected. `TermAccessPolicyContractTest` (resurrected in WP2) re-verified green — it asserts outcome mutual-exclusivity, not specific published/unpublished results, so it does not pin the old default either way.
- **`RelationshipTraversalService::traverse()` leaked restricted endpoint identities; the relationship delete guard was hardcoded to `node` AND never registered (audit-remediation batch 2026-07-01, WP3).** Two fixes in `packages/relationship`: (1) **traverse() endpoint-visibility leak** — the public `@api` `traverse()` returned raw `Relationship` entities filtered only by the relationship row's own `status`, never checking the far endpoint's visibility, so callers could obtain `to_entity_type`/`to_entity_id` (and all edge metadata) for entities the viewer cannot see — while the near-identically-named `browse()` was already fail-closed (alpha245, audit #36). `traverse()` now applies the SAME fail-closed gate: in `published`/`unpublished` mode every non-source endpoint must pass the wired `VisibilityFilterInterface`, direction-independent and strictly at-least-as-closed as browse since a `Relationship` entity exposes both endpoint identities; with **no filter wired, BOTH modes return no edges** ("not provably public" is not "provably draft" — unpublished mode fails fully closed, deliberately stricter than browse's pre-existing unwired unpublished behavior, which is unchanged since both live browse consumers wire filters). `status: 'all'` stays unfiltered as the explicit system-context spelling (browse parity) — no separate `@internal` unfiltered method was added because `traverse()` had ZERO production/test callers (verified repo-wide), so no consumer needed one. Decision note: the surface remains publication-visibility-gated, not per-account access-checked — it is account-less by design like browse and the anonymous discovery API; the account-bound `getQuery()->setAccount()` alternative is documented in the spec as a different contract. (2) **Delete guard scope + wiring** — `RelationshipDeleteGuardListener` hardcoded `guardedEntityType='node'` twice (constructor default AND the query's endpoint-type conditions), so deleting any other relatable entity type silently orphaned edge rows; worse, the listener was **never registered to any event in production** (only its unit test invoked it — same gap as `RelationshipPreSaveListener`, still open). It now guards EVERY entity type by matching the deleted entity's own type plus **id OR uuid** (endpoints may legitimately reference entities by UUID — `RelationshipValidator` accepts them) against both endpoint slots, and `RelationshipServiceProvider::boot()` registers it on `EntityEvents::PRE_DELETE` (throw aborts the delete before row removal; single-`delete()` path only — `deleteMany()` buffers events post-commit via `UnitOfWork`). Same-day adversarial-review hardening: boot() resolves the dispatcher under the **Symfony-contracts FQCN** the kernel-services bus actually serves — the first cut resolved the foundation FQCN, which `ProviderRegistryKernelServices::get()` never answers, so the guard silently registered NOTHING in a real boot (the wiring test had masked it by stubbing the bus with the wrong key; the test now mirrors the production key set); the uuid endpoint form and a fully fail-closed unpublished mode for unwired traverse() (below) came from the same review pass. Cascade semantics were considered and not chosen; blocking is the fail-safe default (a blocked delete surfaces as a 500 today; 409 mapping is a follow-up). The guard keeps `accessCheck(false)` system-context queries (inline-justified), and via the entity-query path is storage-shape tolerant (`json_extract` fallback on `_data`-blob tables). `docs/specs/relationship-modeling.md` gains "Endpoint visibility" and "Referential-Integrity Delete Guard" sections. Acceptance: `RelationshipTraversalServiceTest::testTraversePublishedFailsClosedWithoutVisibilityFilter` (mirrors the browse pin) + published-mode exclusion + `all`-mode parity tests; `RelationshipDeleteGuardListenerTest` (non-node guarded, relationship-as-endpoint guarded, node behavior preserved); `RelationshipServiceProviderTest::boot_wires_delete_guard_to_pre_delete_event`.

### Fixed

- **`waaseyaa/groups`'s auto-mounted JSON:API CRUD surface for `group`/`group_type` was fail-closed DEAD for every account, including admins, with zero consumers — parked explicitly instead of patched with an access policy (audit-remediation batch 2026-07-02, WP5).** `JsonApiRouteProvider::registerRoutes()` mounts CRUD for every registered entity type with no per-type opt-out, and `group`/`group_type` were registered via `GroupsServiceProvider` for storage/bundle purposes only — the package ships zero `AccessPolicyInterface` implementations for either type, so `EntityAccessHandler` has no policy to consult, `check()`/`checkCreateAccess()` always return Neutral, and `JsonApiController` treats Neutral as denied (deny-by-default, not fail-open): `/api/group*` CRUD rejected every request, for every account, with no consumer anywhere depending on it. Decision: PARK, do not build an access policy — full route suppression requires the planned entity-type `#[ContentEntityType(..., api: false)]` exposure flag (not built yet; see the roadmap's FEATURE-entity-attribute-discovery.md), tracked in issue #1871. This WP does the cleanly-possible part instead: both `EntityType` registrations in `GroupsServiceProvider::register()` now pass `discoverable: false`, de-advertising `group`/`group_type` from the `GET /api` discovery index (`ApiDiscoveryController` skips non-discoverable types) — a real de-advertisement, not just documentation. `packages/groups/README.md` gains an "API exposure (parked)" section stating plainly that group-over-API is not exposed and that a consumer wanting it must ship its own access policy. The two existing integration tests' `accessCheck(false)` callsites (`TwoBundleCoexistenceTest`) — which legitimately need it, since they exercise bundle-scoped subtable JOIN routing and query-time ambiguity detection, not access control — now carry inline comments explaining why the bypass is there and pointing at the parked-state tests, rather than silently masking the deadness. New `GroupApiSurfaceParkedTest` pins the parked/deny-by-default state as intentional: `group`/`group_type` are asserted `isDiscoverable() === false`, and a real `EntityAccessHandler` constructed with zero policies (mirroring production, where `GroupsServiceProvider` registers none) is asserted to deny `view`/`update`/`delete`/create for both a plain authenticated account and a `DevAdminAccount` — so a future change that adds a policy or flips exposure back on must consciously update this test instead of silently reactivating a surface nobody vetted. `docs/specs/access-control.md`'s existing Neutral-admits-a-row discussion (which already named `group`/`group_type` as Neutral-reliant for the query-layer/list path) gets a review-ack note distinguishing that from this WP's entity-level CRUD finding.
- **`phpunit.xml.dist` hand-enumerated exactly 7 packages' `tests/Contract` directories, so the other 12 — including `note` and `taxonomy`'s access-policy contract tests — silently never ran in CI (audit-remediation batch 2026-07-02, WP2).** The `Unit` testsuite listed `packages/{ai-agent,api,config,entity,foundation,migration,queue}/tests/Contract` one line per package; any package whose `tests/Contract` directory wasn't on that list (access, audit, entity-storage, field, listing, media, note, relationship, ssr, structured-import, taxonomy, user — 12 in all) had its contract tests silently skipped by every `phpunit`/`composer test`/`composer verify` run, with no error or warning anywhere. `NoteAccessPolicyContractTest` and `TermAccessPolicyContractTest` — the two the audit flagged — are examples of tests that had literally never executed. The naive fix (`<directory>packages/*/tests/Contract</directory>`) is unsafe on its own: `phpunit.xml.dist` sets `failOnWarning="true"`, and PHPUnit's default directory collection matches any `*Test.php` file and reflects every class it declares, including abstract ones — scanning an abstract `FooContractTest` emits a fatal "Class Foo is abstract" warning. Four abstract contract base classes violated the naming convention the repo had already established elsewhere (`packages/queue/tests/Contract/AbstractTransportContract.php`, `packages/entity-storage/tests/Contract/TwoAxisStorageContract.php` — no `Test` suffix) and were renamed to match, with every concrete subclass's `extends`/`use` updated: `Waaseyaa\Access\Tests\Contract\AccessPolicyContractTest` → `AbstractAccessPolicyContract` (6 subclasses across media/note/relationship/taxonomy/user), `Waaseyaa\EntityStorage\Tests\Contract\EntityStorageDriverContractTest` → `AbstractEntityStorageDriverContract` (2 subclasses), `Waaseyaa\Field\Tests\Contract\FieldFormatterContractTest` → `AbstractFieldFormatterContract` (6 subclasses in `ssr`), and `Waaseyaa\StructuredImport\Tests\Contract\StructuredImporterContractTest` → `AbstractStructuredImporterContract`. The last one carried a further latent bug: its sole concrete subclass, `GfmTableImporterContractTest`, was declared in the *same file* as the abstract base — once the base file is renamed off the `Test.php` suffix, PHPUnit's directory scan would stop finding that file at all, silently re-orphaning the concrete test it contained. Split into its own `GfmTableImporterContractTest.php` so it is picked up by the default suffix scan like every other concrete contract test. With the rename in place, `phpunit.xml.dist`'s hand-enumerated list is replaced by a single `<directory>packages/*/tests/Contract</directory>` glob, so any future package with a `tests/Contract` directory is covered by construction. Resurrected suites all pass (221 tests across the 12 newly-covered directories, 332 across all 19 `tests/Contract` dirs; full `Unit` suite 9092 tests, unchanged pass count elsewhere) — no latent bugs surfaced in the code under test, only the missing wiring. New gate `bin/check-contract-suite-coverage` (wired into `composer verify` right after `check-phpstan-paths`) asserts both halves stay true: the `Unit` testsuite still globs `packages/*/tests/Contract` (not a hand-enumerated subset), and no abstract class under any `packages/*/tests/Contract/*.php` is named with the `*Test` suffix.
- **`packages/path` had zero Unicode normalization and no alias uniqueness at any layer — an NFC/NFD twin of an existing alias, or a flat exact duplicate, was freely creatable through the live JSON:API write path (audit-remediation batch 2026-07-02, WP3).** Two compounding gaps, both fixed: (1) **no normalization** — `PathAlias` stored whatever byte form of an alias it was given, so the NFC-precomposed and NFD-decomposed forms of the same visible alias (e.g. `"página"` vs. `"pa" . "\u{0301}" . "gina"`) were two distinct byte strings, both storable as separate rows resolving first-match-wins via `PathAliasResolver`. `PathAlias` now exposes `public static normalizeAlias()` (`\Normalizer::normalize($value, \Normalizer::FORM_C)`, guarded per the pattern in `SlugGenerator::generate()` — canonical composition ONLY, no lowercasing, no slugification, no character stripping, since Waaseyaa aliases legitimately carry case, slashes, long-vowel diacritics, the glottal `ʼ` U+02BC, and Canadian syllabics) and applies it in both `setAlias()` and the constructor (the latter closes the JSON:API `EntityRepository::create()`/POST path, which assigns `$values` straight into entity state via `EntityBase::__construct()` with no domain-setter call). Because the JSON:API PATCH path applies attributes via the generic `EntityBase::set()` (bypassing `setAlias()` and its entity-side normalization), the entity-side guards alone left a hole where a PATCH could persist raw NFD bytes; the uniqueness listener (below) closes it universally by rewriting the alias in place — `BeforeSaveEvent` fires in `EntityRepository::doSave()` BEFORE it reads `$entity->toArray()` for the write, so the listener's `set('alias', $normalizedAlias)` is the single NFC-storage guarantee across EVERY write path (create AND PATCH), with the entity-side normalization kept as defense-in-depth. (2) **no uniqueness enforcement** — the pre-existing `PathAliasManagerInterface`/`InMemoryPathAliasManager`/`PathProcessor` trio is vestigial and not on the live write path (left untouched, out of scope), so nothing had ever stopped a duplicate `(alias, langcode)` pair from being saved. New `PathAliasUniquenessListener` hooks `Waaseyaa\EntityStorage\Event\BeforeSaveEvent` — the provably-live, entity-agnostic pre-write hook dispatched by `EntityRepository::doSave()` for every entity save — and throws `AbortOperationException` (no backend write, no `AfterSaveEvent`) when a system-context, `accessCheck(false)`-justified query (mirroring `PathAliasResolver`'s existing justification) finds a conflicting `path_alias` row for the same normalized alias AND langcode, excluding the saving entity's own id (so re-saving an unchanged entity never self-conflicts). Scoping is deliberately `(alias, langcode)`, not `alias` alone: the same alias under a different langcode is legitimate, since `PathAliasResolver::resolve()` matches on alias+langcode together. Wired in `PathServiceProvider::boot()` following the #1852/WP4 dead-subscriber-safe pattern — resolves the dispatcher under the Symfony-contracts FQCN the kernel-services bus actually serves, then instanceof-checks the foundation contract, so this listener does not join that bug class. `packages/path/composer.json` gains a `waaseyaa/entity-storage` require (L1, legal downward edge from `path`'s L2). Acceptance (TDD red→green): `PathAliasTest` — NFD→NFC normalization via both the constructor and `setAlias()`, plus round-trip-unchanged cases for an already-NFC syllabics alias and a glottal-stop alias (proving normalization never corrupts already-canonical Indigenous-orthography text). `tests/Integration/Phase32/PathAliasUniquenessTest.php` — a real SQLite-backed `EntityRepository` wired to a real Symfony `EventDispatcher` with `PathAliasUniquenessListener` registered exactly as `PathServiceProvider::boot()` wires it: NFD-twin-of-existing-alias rejected (proven red with the listener unregistered — the duplicate saved and left two rows), exact-duplicate-same-langcode rejected, same-alias-different-langcode allowed, self-resave-unchanged does not self-conflict, and a PATCH-shaped generic-`set()` write of raw NFD bytes persists as NFC in the stored column (asserted against the raw DB row, not a re-hydrated entity — since `PathAlias::fromStorage()` re-normalizes on load and would mask stored NFD bytes; proven red with the listener's in-place rewrite disabled — the column held NFD). Known limitations, deliberately disclosed rather than implied away: (a) enforcement is application-level (query-then-write inside the pre-save listener), NOT a DB unique index, so it is not atomic — two concurrent creates of the same `(alias, langcode)` can each pass the pre-write check and both commit (unlike attachment's WP2 partial-unique-index backstop, `path_alias` gets no DB-level constraint here); (b) because self-exclusion removes only the saving row, a pair of duplicate rows that predates this listener (legacy dirty data) will make any future save of EITHER row abort until an operator resolves the collision by hand — no dedup/migration tool ships with this WP.
- **`bin/check-package-layers` PL008 was blind to inline fully-qualified references in Layer-0 sources, and its raw-text-regex first cut was itself evadable — rewritten tokenizer-based after same-WP adversarial review; `ControllerDispatcher`'s SSR probe converted to the string-constant convention it should already have used (audit-remediation batch 2026-07-02, WP7).** PL005 (file-level `use` imports) and the original PL008 regex both require a syntactic anchor — a `use` statement or a leading quote character — that an inline `\Waaseyaa\<Ns>\...::class` token with no `use` import has neither of, so a Layer-0 file could reference a higher-layer FQCN this way with zero gate coverage; WP7's first cut added an inline-`::class` regex to close that. Same-day adversarial review then found the regex approach structurally leaky (all with executed repros): (1) static access `\Waaseyaa\Node\Foo::make()` — REAL runtime coupling that autoloads, worse than `::class` — passed, because only the literal `::class` suffix was matched; (2) `'\Waaseyaa\Node\Foo'` (leading backslash inside the quotes) and single-backslash single-quoted `'Waaseyaa\Node\Foo'` (valid PHP — `'\N'` is a literal backslash + N) both evaded the quote-anchored, double-backslash-separator quoted-literal pattern; (3) a trailing same-line `//` or `/* */` comment mentioning an FQCN FAILED the gate on legitimate code, because the hand-rolled stripper only skipped whole-comment lines. Instead of four regex patches, `$scanStringLiteralFqcns` is now **tokenizer-based** (`token_get_all`, purely lexical): `T_COMMENT`/`T_DOC_COMMENT` dropped as whole tokens (exact comment stripping — kills the false positive and keeps docblock `@see` references inert); sub-pattern (a) scans string-scalar tokens (`T_CONSTANT_ENCAPSED_STRING`/`T_ENCAPSED_AND_WHITESPACE` — quoted strings, interpolated parts, heredoc/nowdoc) with a separator-tolerant regex (one or two raw backslashes, up to two leading); sub-pattern (b) flags any `T_NAME_FULLY_QUALIFIED` token starting `\Waaseyaa\` — `::class`, `::method()`, `::CONST`, `::$prop`, `new`, `instanceof`, type hints, even leading-backslash `use \Waaseyaa\…;` imports (also invisible to PL005), with names split across lines before `::class` caught for free (`T_NAME_QUALIFIED` deliberately not flagged — relative names are PL005's territory). Rule ID, per-file baseline mechanism, env overrides, and emit path unchanged; the FAIL message now reads "references a Waaseyaa\<Ns>\… FQCN … as a fully-qualified name in code or inside a string scalar". Running the broadened scan against the live tree surfaced two pre-existing offenders: `foundation/src/ServiceProvider/ServiceProvider.php` + `ServiceProviderInterface.php`, whose `routes()` extension hook uses inline fully-qualified type hints (`\Waaseyaa\Routing\WaaseyaaRouter` L4, `\Waaseyaa\Entity\EntityTypeManager` L1) — baselined with rationale rather than fixed (type hints resolve lazily, cannot be string constants, and changing the signature would break every provider override via parameter contravariance; follow-up noted in the baseline). The baseline header now warns that baselining is per-FILE — re-audit the entire file for unrelated upward references before adding or keeping an entry, since one legit entry also hides later smuggled refs in the same file. `packages/foundation/src/Http/ControllerDispatcher.php` — the file the first cut immediately caught — used `!\class_exists(\Waaseyaa\SSR\SsrPageHandler::class)` as an optional-dependency probe (return a 501 when `waaseyaa/ssr` is not installed); its already-declared-but-unused `SSR_PAGE_HANDLER` string constant is now actually used at the callsite, and the file is baselined with a rationale. Acceptance (review-shape tests written TDD red against the regex implementation → green on the tokenizer): `tests/Architecture/CheckPackageLayersGateTest.php` — 11 PL008 fixture cases (quoted literal; inline `::class`; static-method access; leading-backslash quoted; single-backslash quoted; `new \Waaseyaa\…\Foo()`; baseline suppression; same-layer inline reference not flagged; trailing `//` and `/* */` comments not flagged; docblock `@see` pinned not-flagged), with the fixture writer extended to plant arbitrary raw class-body text and `runGate()` passing `WAASEYAA_LAYER_STRING_LITERAL_BASELINE=/dev/null` for isolation from the real repo baseline; `bin/check-package-layers-pl008-self-test` (wired into `composer verify`) extended to exercise BOTH sub-patterns (fires on the quoted-literal violator; fires on an inline fully-qualified `::handle()` violator while only the first is allowlisted; green after allowlisting both). Independent re-verification of the tokenizer rewrite confirmed all round-1 findings fixed and surfaced one further gap, also fixed: PL005's `use`-import regex did not tolerate a leading backslash (`use \Waaseyaa\Api\...;` — valid PHP, identical semantics), so that shape evaded the file-level scan in EVERY layer (PL008(b) only compensated in Layer 0); the regex now accepts the optional `\`, pinned by a fixture test in a non-Layer-0 package, with zero real-tree offenders on the tightened scan. PL008's per-dep error line now lists ALL violating files instead of only the alphabetically first, and two accepted lexical-scan limits are documented in place: lowercase FQCNs evade (harmless — Composer PSR-4 prefix matching is case-sensitive, so a lowercase string cannot trigger upward autoloading) and human-readable diagnostic messages mentioning an FQCN are a known false-positive class (prefer rephrasing; noted in the baseline header).
- **`PackageManifestCompiler::compile()` ran its full reflective class scan twice per compile, and a corrupt manifest cache self-healed silently with no log line (audit-remediation batch 2026-07-02, WP7).** (1) `scanClasses()` is invoked both directly (the attribute-scan loop) and indirectly via `scanScheduleEntryClasses()` (the schedule-entries pass, since `filterDiscoveryClasses()` already admits `ScheduleEntriesInterface` implementors into the same discovery-class set the attribute loop iterates) — every `compile()` therefore ran the classmap/PSR-4 scan and per-class `ReflectionClass` construction twice. `scanClasses()` is now memoized per compiler instance (`$scannedClasses`); compiler instances are created fresh per boot, so there is no cross-request staleness window. The schedule-entries pass stays a separate logical pass (documented decision) — with memoization it is now a cheap in-memory filter over an already-computed array, not a second scan. (2) `load()`'s corrupt-cache recovery (`catch (\Throwable) { /* recompile */ }`) previously said nothing; it now logs a warning naming the cache path, the exception class, and its message before falling through to recompile, so an operator can see WHY a recompile happened. The wrong-return-type shape (a cache file that returns a non-array without throwing) is unaffected — it still self-heals without a warning, since no exception is thrown on that path. (3) `compile()`'s two inline `\Waaseyaa\Entity\DefinesEntityType::class` references (an upward L0→L1 type reference within `::class`, harmless only because `::class` on an unimported FQCN is a compile-time literal that never autoloads) are now a private string constant, `ENTITY_TYPE_DEFINITION_INTERFACE`, matching the file's other cross-layer constants (`POLICY_ATTRIBUTE` et al.) — behaviourally identical, consistent convention, and the baseline rationale in `tools/package-layers-string-literal-baseline.txt` is updated to name it.
