Acceptance (TDD red→green): `PackageManifestCompilerTest::compile_performs_exactly_one_reflective_class_scan_per_compile` (a fixture with no classmap and no app PSR-4 prefixes logs the "Falling back to full PSR-4 directory scanning" warning exactly once post-fix, was twice pre-fix); `::load_logs_a_warning_naming_the_cache_path_and_exception_before_recompiling_on_corrupt_cache` (spy logger asserts a WARNING containing "recompiling" and "RuntimeException", plus the cache path; `load()` still returns a `PackageManifest`); `::load_recompiles_silently_when_cache_returns_a_non_array_wrong_type` (no warning expected on the no-exception self-heal path, still no crash).

- **`packages/foundation/tests/Unit/LayerDependencyTest.php`'s file-scan gate only covered `src/Http/`, so `src/Diagnostic/` was importing Layer-1 `Waaseyaa\Entity`/`Waaseyaa\Field` (plus a dead `Waaseyaa\EntityStorage` import) undetected by the PHPUnit-level check; five small `Diagnostic` correctness fixes ride along (audit-remediation batch 2026-07-02, WP6).** (1) **Layer-gate scope**: extended `LayerDependencyTest` with a new test that scans ALL of `packages/foundation/src/` (not just `Http/`) for upward `use Waaseyaa\<L1+>\...` imports, skipping `Kernel/` (bulk-exempt entry-point orchestrators) and `Http/Router/`/`Http/Inbound/` (governed only by `bin/check-package-layers`' allowlist scan — the HTTP-substrate PHPUnit test skips those two directories as well), with its own `SRC_SCAN_EXEMPT_FILES` allowlist mirroring `bin/check-package-layers`' `$kernelExemptFiles` one-for-one, each entry carrying a one-line rationale. `bin/check-package-layers` itself already caught these same imports via its own allowlist (so the extended scan finds zero *new* framework-level violations — it closes a blind spot in the faster PHPUnit safety net, not a live production bug); a stale `$kernelExemptFiles` entry (`HasCommandsInterface.php`, renamed to `ProvidesConsoleCommandsInterface.php` during the earlier symfony/console removal and no longer importing any Waaseyaa namespace at all) was removed while auditing the list. Same-day adversarial-review hardening: (i) the review's planted-violation probe exposed a pre-existing CASING bug in `bin/check-package-layers`' `$namespacePrefixToShort` map — `Cli`/`Github`/`Northcloud`/`Graphql`/`Ssr` vs the real PSR-4 prefixes `CLI`/`GitHub`/`NorthCloud`/`GraphQL`/`SSR` — which made PL005/PL008 silently ignore any file-level import from those five packages repo-wide (a planted L0→L6 `use Waaseyaa\CLI\...` passed the gate); the five keys are corrected, and re-running the gate surfaced zero previously-hidden violations; (ii) a new PHPUnit cross-check parses the script's `$kernelExemptFiles` array literal as text (the script calls `exit()`, so it cannot be `require`d) and asserts every `SRC_SCAN_EXEMPT_FILES` entry is mirrored there, closing the two-hand-maintained-lists drift risk; (iii) the same personal-handle sweep also caught `packages/admin-surface/src/AdminSpaFallback.php`'s `SPEC_URL` pointing at the dead personal `github.com/jonesrussell/waaseyaa` repo — now the canonical `waaseyaa/framework` URL. (2) Deleted the dead `use Waaseyaa\EntityStorage\SqlSchemaHandler;` import in `HealthChecker.php` — it was referenced only inside a docblock comment, not actual code; the docblock now names the FQCN as plain text. (3) **No relocation**: investigated moving `HealthChecker`/`BootDiagnosticReport` to a Layer-1 package (the `CacheConfigResolver` #1807/#1836 precedent); rejected because the class spans BOTH `entity` and `field` package concerns with no single non-arbitrary destination, and because this exact codification-not-relocation call was already made in mission #1257 K6(c) (`docs/specs/infrastructure.md` "Kernel exemption surface") — this WP re-affirms rather than re-litigates that decision; see `docs/specs/operator-diagnostics.md` "Kernel-adjacent codification" for the full reasoning. (4) `HealthChecker::checkIngestion()` now reads from an injected `IngestionLogger` (new optional constructor param, defaulting to `new IngestionLogger($projectRoot)` in the constructor body per the PHP 8.4 parameter-default gotcha) instead of `new`-ing one inline every call. (5) `checkSchemaDrift()` no longer reports a blanket "pass" when EVERY registered entity table is lazily uninitialized — that claimed a comparison happened when none did. A new `SCHEMA_DRIFT_CHECK_SKIPPED` warn code (warn, not fail — an uninitialized pre-`waaseyaa install` table is a legitimate transitional state) names the skip count instead; a partial skip (some tables checked, some lazy) is unaffected and still reports a plain pass. (6) The three `PRAGMA table_info(...)` interpolations in `HealthChecker` now quote the table/subtable identifier via `DatabaseInterface::quoteIdentifier()` instead of hand-built `"{$tableName}"` string interpolation, which does not double embedded `"` characters — the same identifier-quoting hardening class as database-legacy #1816. (7) `DiagnosticEmitter::emit()` now encodes with `JSON_UNESCAPED_UNICODE` (an Indigenous-language diagnostic message no longer round-trips through `\uXXXX` escapes) and catches its own `json_encode()` failure (`JsonException`, e.g. a `NAN`/`INF` context value) — falls back to a minimal hardcoded-safe line built only from the code's ASCII backing value so the best-effort diagnostic path can never throw into its caller. (8) `DiagnosticCode::UNAUTHORIZED_V1_TAG`'s remediation text no longer hardcodes `@jonesrussell` — now points at filing an issue on the `waaseyaa/framework` repository, since remediation copy ships in every consumer's own health-check output. Acceptance (all TDD red→green): `LayerDependencyTest` (new scan test green with the 5-entry exemption list; a sanity check temporarily removing the `HealthChecker.php` exemption entry confirmed the gate correctly re-detects all 4 remaining forbidden imports); `HealthCheckerTest` (skipped-state honesty for all-lazy vs. partial-lazy; injected-logger DI proven via a projectRoot/logger-root mismatch that only passes if the injected instance is actually consulted; a quote-embedded table identifier proven to throw pre-fix at the exact `PRAGMA` call site and pass post-fix); `DiagnosticEmitterTest` (Unicode round-trip; NAN-context never-throws + fallback line; all `DiagnosticCode` remediation texts scanned for the personal handle).

- **`ViteAssetManager::loadManifest()` failed open with zero signal, and `TenantAssetResolver`'s ssr/admin entries shared one URL namespace across two filesystem roots (audit-remediation batch 2026-07-02, WP5).** (1) **Silent fail-open manifest load**: a missing, unreadable, corrupt-JSON, or non-array `manifest.json` all resolved to `[]` (and memoized it), so `url()`/`preloadLinks()`/`assetTags()` silently fell back to un-hashed paths — a truncated or absent manifest after a bad deploy meant sitewide asset 404s with no log line anywhere. `ViteAssetManager` now accepts an optional `?Waaseyaa\Foundation\Log\LoggerInterface $logger = null` (defaulting to `NullLogger`, per repo convention — NOT psr/log) and logs the failure KIND (`missing`/`unreadable`/`corrupt-json`/`non-array`) plus the probed path(s) at ERROR, exactly once per bundle (the existing `$manifests` memoization already guarantees `loadManifest()` computes — and now logs — only once). One quiet exception: `missing` is downgraded to DEBUG when `$devServerUrl` is set, since a missing production manifest is the expected dev-mode state (`assetTags()` falls through to Vite dev-server tags); `unreadable`/`corrupt-json`/`non-array` are always ERROR regardless of dev-server config, since those mean a manifest exists but is broken. Wired at the one production composition root that constructs `ViteAssetManager` today — `Waaseyaa\Inertia\InertiaServiceProvider` — via `resolveOptional(LoggerInterface::class)` against the kernel-services bus, mirroring every other optional-framework-service pull in the codebase. (2) **`TenantAssetResolver` URL/filesystem mismatch**: `buildResolverChain()`'s ssr and admin entries both used the bare `$baseUrl` (no distinguishing suffix) while pointing at two different `basePath` roots (`<base>/ssr` and `<base>/admin`) — `url()`'s existence check (`entryUrlToFilePath()`) could pass against one entry's file while the URL it returned was, under a real one-root-per-URL-prefix static server (the only serving mechanism found anywhere in this repo: the FrankenPHP `Caddyfile`'s `root ./public` + `php_server`, and the `cli-server` passthrough in `public/index.php`), indistinguishable from the other entry's namespace. Investigation confirmed `TenantAssetResolver` is not currently wired to `AssetManagerInterface` at any composition root (the only production binding, `InertiaServiceProvider`, constructs a bare `ViteAssetManager` directly), so there is no live serving contract to validate against; the fix (`<baseUrl>/ssr` ↔ `<basePath>/ssr`, `<baseUrl>/admin` ↔ `<basePath>/admin`, mirroring the tenant-theme entry's pre-existing `<baseUrl>/themes/<theme>` pairing) is the only internally-consistent choice given that constraint — a **BREAKING** URL-shape change for any external consumer constructing this `@api`, split-mirrored class directly (none exist in first-party code, pre-1.0) — documented in the class docblock with the constraint spelled out for whoever wires this class up. Acceptance (TDD red→green): `ViteAssetManagerTest` (missing/unreadable/corrupt-json/non-array each logs ERROR with the right kind and probed paths; missing is DEBUG when a dev server is configured; a valid manifest logs nothing; repeated `url()`/`preloadLinks()` calls for the same bundle log exactly once; the `NullLogger` default does not crash when unwired); `TenantAssetResolverTest` (ssr/admin entries resolve into disjoint URL prefixes; a manifest that maps to a file that exists under one entry's root but not the other's is never served under the wrong namespace; and, as regression pins of pre-existing behavior (passed before this WP too): tenant-theme → ssr → admin resolution order with all three tiers populated, `getResolvers()`/`preloadLinks()` unaffected); `InertiaServiceProviderTest::register_threads_the_kernel_logger_into_the_asset_manager` pins the end-to-end kernel-bus logger wiring (stubbing the bus under the served foundation `LoggerInterface` FQCN — the WP4 wrong-key bug class — and asserting the missing-manifest ERROR reaches it), added after adversarial review flagged the wiring as manually-verified-only.

- **`waaseyaa/attachment`'s composer.json promises an "at-most-one-active invariant" but only `AttachmentRepository::setActive()` enforced it — a direct `save()` of a second `is_active=1` row, or any write through the generic entity API, could silently leave two active attachments for the same parent, and `getActive()`'s unordered `LIMIT 1` masked the corruption instead of surfacing it (audit-remediation batch 2026-07-02, WP2).** Four surfaces closed, three mechanisms, one honestly-documented residual: (1) **`AttachmentRepository::save()` guard** — when the entity being saved has `is_active` truthy, a new shared helper `AttachmentActiveInvariant::demoteSiblings()` clears every other active row for the same parent and the entity save is wrapped in the SAME `$this->database->transaction()`, mirroring `setActive()`'s demote-then-persist semantics (Doctrine DBAL nests `beginTransaction()`/`commit()` by ref-count on a single connection — verified via `DBALTransaction`/`Connection::beginTransaction()` — so `EntityRepository::save()`'s own internal transaction nests safely inside this one without a savepoint). Inactive saves are byte-identical to the pre-fix path (no transaction, no demote). (2) **Generic entity API guard** — the exact hole `AttachmentRepository` cannot see: `getRepository('attachment')->save()` bypasses the repository class entirely. New `AttachmentActiveGuardListener`, registered by `AttachmentServiceProvider::boot()` on `EntityEvents::PRE_SAVE` for every attachment save, applies the same demote-siblings-first semantics via the shared helper. Wiring uses the SAME Symfony-contracts-FQCN pattern `RelationshipServiceProvider::boot()` established for the delete guard (#1852) — the kernel-services bus serves the dispatcher only under `\Symfony\Contracts\EventDispatcher\EventDispatcherInterface`, resolving the foundation FQCN silently no-ops — and the wiring test (`AttachmentServiceProviderTest`) stubs the kernel bus under the SAME key set production uses, so a wiring regression fails the test the way it would fail production. **Honest residual**: `EntityRepository::save()` dispatches `PRE_SAVE` OUTSIDE its write transaction (before `$this->database?->transaction()` opens), so the listener's demote UPDATE and the subsequent INSERT are two separate commits, not one atomic unit — two processes racing this exact interleaving can both "win" and leave two active rows on platforms without mechanism (3) below. This is documented in the listener's docblock, not glossed over. (3) **Optional SQLite/PostgreSQL partial unique index** — `AttachmentSchema::ensureActivePartialUniqueIndex()` (mirroring the existing `SqlSchemaHandler::ensureSqlBlobTranslatablePartialUuidIndex()` pattern for platform detection and quoting) materializes `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX ... ON attachment(parent_entity_type, parent_entity_id) WHERE is_active = 1` on platforms with partial-index support (SQLite ≥3.8, PostgreSQL ≥9.0) — this turns the residual race in (2) from silent corruption into a loud `UniqueConstraintViolationException` for the losing writer on those platforms. No-ops with a logged warning on MySQL/MariaDB (no partial-index support at all — the framework's `SchemaInterface::addIndex()` has no `WHERE`-clause support either, so a portable partial index was never on the table) and is wrapped in try/catch (unlike its precedent) since it is a hardening backstop, not load-bearing schema — a pre-existing install whose data already violates the invariant, or a platform this code fails to recognize, must not block `ensureTable()`/install. (4) **`getActive()` detection** — was `findBy([..., 'is_active' => 1], null, 1)`: no `ORDER BY`, `LIMIT 1`, so a multi-active state was invisible and the returned row was backend-nondeterministic. Now orders `['id' => 'DESC']` (newest wins — the least-surprising tie-break for "which one is displayed") and fetches `limit: 2`; when 2 rows come back it logs an ERROR via the repo-convention `Waaseyaa\Foundation\Log\LoggerInterface` (constructor-injected, `?LoggerInterface $logger = null` defaulting to `NullLogger`, wired to the kernel logger by `AttachmentServiceProvider` when available) naming the parent and both ids, and still returns the deterministic winner rather than throwing (callers keep getting a usable result). Acceptance (TDD red→green — stashed `src/` changes first, confirmed each new test failed for the intended reason, then restored): `AttachmentRepositoryTest` (direct `save()` of a second active demotes the first; inactive `save()` leaves siblings untouched; multi-active manufactured via a raw `DatabaseInterface` UPDATE after dropping the partial index — the index itself blocks the same bypass once present, so the test explicitly drops it to reach the platform/legacy-data state the detection code exists for — logs exactly once and returns the higher-id winner; single-active happy path never logs); `GenericEntityApiActiveGuardTest` (generic-API save demotes via the listener wired production-style; without the listener and without the index, both rows stay active — the documented hole; without the listener but WITH the index, the second save fails loudly instead of corrupting — pinning the real behavioral difference between SQLite/PostgreSQL and MySQL/MariaDB); `AttachmentServiceProviderTest` (mirrors `RelationshipServiceProviderTest`'s production-key wiring test, plus no-dispatcher and no-database no-op cases); `SaveActiveConcurrencyTest` (sibling to the existing `SetActiveConcurrencyTest` pcntl-fork harness — 30 concurrent processes each `save()` a brand-new active attachment for the same parent; exactly one row is active afterward, the same SQLite-WAL-plus-busy-timeout writer-serialization guarantee `setActive()`'s concurrency test already relies on, since the new `save()` guard is structurally the same demote+single-transaction shape). Scope-fork assessment: the composer.json claim survives for the primary, portable mechanism (save-path guards, both surfaces) and is measurably STRENGTHENED to full atomicity on SQLite/PostgreSQL by the partial index; only the generic-entity-API cross-process race on index-less platforms (MySQL/MariaDB, or a legacy install with pre-existing violations) remains genuinely un-atomic, and that gap is explicitly documented rather than asserted away. **Same-day adversarial-review hardening (three findings, all TDD red→green):** (5) **BLOCKER — `saveMany()` was an unguarded fifth surface AND the new listener corrupted it**, fixed at root cause in `EntityRepository` itself: `saveMany()` runs the batch through one `UnitOfWork`, which buffered ALL lifecycle events until after commit — so a batch of two active attachments either rolled back entirely on the partial index (raw `UniqueConstraintViolationException`, both attachments lost) or, without the index (MySQL/legacy shape), committed both rows active and then the two buffered guard listeners fired post-commit and CROSS-DEMOTED each other (each demotes all-except-own-id), leaving ZERO active rows while both in-memory entities still said `is_active=1` — silent corruption the WP2 listener itself introduced, invisible to `getActive()` detection (count 0, nothing to log). Root-cause contract fix: `EntityRepository::doSave()`'s two PRE-write dispatch sites (`EntityEvents::PRE_SAVE`, `BeforeSaveEvent`) no longer buffer under `UnitOfWork` — they dispatch IMMEDIATELY inside the batch transaction. Rationale (encoded in code comments + `docs/specs/entity-system.md` § "Event dispatch semantics under UnitOfWork"): a PRE event announces intent — listeners that mutate the entity (classification label resolution's `$entity->set()` writes were silently never persisted in batches under the old buffering) or issue guarding DB writes only work before the row is written; buffering exists so listeners never observe rolled-back work, and an immediate PRE listener's writes JOIN the batch transaction and roll back with it, satisfying that goal; and `BeforeSaveEvent`'s documented abort contract ("no write occurs") was unfulfillable buffered — the "abort" fired after every row had committed (and its throw then hit the committed transaction's rollback path, `RuntimeException: Transaction is no longer active`). POST/AFTER events stay buffered; the delete path (`PRE_DELETE` under `deleteMany()`) is deliberately untouched — #1852 documents batch deletes as unguarded, and aligning it is flagged as a follow-up in the `dispatchEvent()` docblock. With immediate dispatch, `saveMany()` of N active attachments converges to sequential-save semantics: exactly one active row, last in batch wins, with or without the index. Blast radius verified: all four production PRE_SAVE/BeforeSaveEvent listener families re-checked (classification `EntityLifecycleSubscriber` — its batch entity mutations now actually persist, a latent bug fixed; audit `EntityLifecycleAuditListener`/`EntityWriteAuditListener` + the kernel wiring — memory-only `pendingIsNew` capture, best-effort try/catch, now captures `isNew()` while it is still accurate; migration `EntityDestination` — dispatch-site consumer only), full Unit (8818) + Integration (1327) suites green. One existing test renamed with a comment: `EntityRepositoryTest::saveManyDispatchesEventsAfterCommit` → `::saveManyDispatchesPreDuringAndPostAfterCommit` (its order assertion always held, but the NAME pinned the retired all-events-buffered semantics). New pins: `EntityRepositoryTest::saveManyDispatchesPreWriteEventsBeforeRowsAreWritten` (row-count-observing listener: entity 1's PRE events see 0 rows, entity 2's see 1), `::saveManyAbortFromBeforeSaveOnSecondEntityRollsBackWholeBatch` (mid-batch abort → whole batch rolled back, zero rows), `GenericEntityApiActiveGuardTest::saveManyOfTwoActiveAttachmentsConvergesToOneActiveRow` + `::saveManyConvergesWithoutThePartialIndexToo` (both reviewer repro shapes pinned to the converged outcome; the batch loser's in-memory/DB `is_active` desync — same as sequential saves — documented in-test). (6) **MAJOR — `JsonApiController::update()` surfaced the index trip as a raw 500**: `create()` had caught `UniqueConstraintViolationException` → 409 but neither of `update()`'s save paths did, so two racing PATCHes activating different attachments for one parent returned a raw DBAL exception (possible SQL leak under APP_DEBUG). Both PATCH save paths (plain and expectation-stated `saveWithExpectation()`) now map it to a codeless 409 `Conflict` mirroring `create()`'s shape — detail names the REAL entity id (locator honesty, contract §15), `code: REVISION_CONFLICT` remains the discriminator for the optimistic-locking 409 (three-shape 409 catalogue documented in `docs/specs/api-layer.md`). Pinned with REAL constraint trips (a `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX` on the fixture table + a colliding PATCH, no hand-built driver exceptions): `JsonApiControllerConflictTest::patchWithoutExpectationMapsUniqueConstraintViolationTo409` / `::patchWithExpectationMapsUniqueConstraintViolationTo409`. (7) **NIT — `AttachmentActiveInvariant::isActive()` hardened from `(bool)` cast to a strict allow-list** (`true`/`1`/`'1'` only): `(bool) 'false' === true`, so PHP-truthy garbage in `is_active` would have triggered a sibling demotion; garbage is now inert (`AttachmentActiveInvariantTest`, 11 cases including the `'false'` string).
- **`AttachmentDownloadRouter` degraded Indigenous filenames to underscores in the download `Content-Disposition` header — now emits an RFC 5987/6266 `filename*` extended parameter alongside the ASCII fallback (audit-remediation batch 2026-07-02, WP1).** `safeFilename()` (`preg_replace('/[^A-Za-z0-9._-]/', '_', ...)`, byte-wise over the raw UTF-8 filename) was the ONLY filename the response emitted, so an Anishinaabemowin filename like `ᐊᓂᔑᓈᐯᒧᐎᓐ.pdf` downloaded as `________________________.pdf` — same class of loss as the pre-fix `SlugGenerator` (batch 2026-07-01, WP1) and `MediaRouter` filename handling (batch 2026-07-01, WP5), but here the destructive transform reaches the wire on every download since there is no non-ASCII-preserving counterpart. Fix: `AttachmentDownloadRouter::contentDisposition()` now builds `attachment; filename="<ascii-safe>"; filename*=UTF-8''<percent-encoded>` — the existing byte-wise `safeFilename()` sanitizer is UNCHANGED and still supplies the quoted `filename=` fallback (pinned byte-identical for pure-ASCII names), and a new `rfc5987Encode()` supplies the extended parameter: `basename()` the raw filename, reject with `null` (omit `filename*` entirely) when the basename is empty or fails `mb_check_encoding(..., 'UTF-8')` (malformed stored data must not produce a malformed header, and must not throw), otherwise `rawurlencode()` the UTF-8 bytes. `rawurlencode()` escapes everything outside `A-Za-z0-9-_.~`, a strict subset of RFC 5987 `attr-char` — over-encoding a few extra `attr-char` characters (`!#$&+^\`|`) is RFC-compliant and safe, unlike under-encoding, so no bespoke attr-char encoder was written. Header-injection safety is two-layered and both layers are pinned: the pre-existing `safeFilename()` sanitizer already strips `"`/CR/LF from the quoted fallback (unchanged), and `rawurlencode()` percent-encodes the same characters (`%22`/`%0D`/`%0A`) in `filename*`, so neither parameter can smuggle a raw quote or a header-splitting newline onto the wire regardless of stored filename content. Decision: `filename*` is always emitted (even for pure-ASCII names) — one code path, no ASCII-only special case — pinned by an exact-string assertion. Same-day adversarial-review hardening: (1) directional-formatting characters (U+202E RTLO, LRM/RLM/ALM, isolate controls U+2066–U+2069) are stripped from the name before percent-encoding — the pre-change ASCII-only header could never carry them, and an unfiltered `filename*` would have opened the classic RTLO extension-spoofing vector (`photo\u{202E}gnp.exe` rendering as `photoexe.png` in the save dialog); ZWJ/ZWNJ are deliberately kept (orthographically meaningful in Persian/Indic scripts); (2) both header parameters are capped at 255 characters (the filename lives in the unbounded `_data` blob and percent-encoding triples multibyte names — a 1000-char syllabics name produced a 12KB header value, past typical ~8KB proxy line limits; names ≤255 chars are untouched, preserving the pure-ASCII byte-identity pin). Acceptance (TDD red→green, `AttachmentDownloadRouterTest`): syllabics filename (`ᐊᓂᔑᓈᐯᒧᐎᓐ.pdf`) and a diacritic+glottal-stop filename (U+02BC, e.g. `Ozhibiiʼigan Ākí.pdf`) both pinned to their exact expected `filename="..."; filename*=UTF-8''...` header string; a filename containing `"`/CR/LF pinned to the sanitized-and-percent-encoded exact header (only the two structural wrapping quotes survive, no raw CR/LF anywhere in the header); pure-ASCII filename pinned byte-identical to the pre-change `filename="report.txt"` fallback plus the new `filename*`; invalid-UTF-8 filename pinned to ASCII-fallback-only (no `filename*`, no exception, 200 response); `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` presence now asserted (was set but untested).
- **`waaseyaa/attachment` schema/wiring minors: `AttachmentSchema::ensureTable()` — the sole source of the package's `#[Field]`-declared columns and composite/partial indexes — was invoked from nowhere in production; `setActive()`/`demoteSiblings()` skipped `updated_at`; a `require` dependency had zero `src/` imports (audit-remediation batch 2026-07-02, WP3).** (1) **Schema wiring gap**: investigation (a schema-parity test builds the `attachment` table both ways in separate SQLite DBs and diffs `PRAGMA table_info`/`index_list`) found the generic sql-blob schema-sync path (`SqlSchemaHandler`, the ONLY path `EntityTypeManagerFactory`/`EntitySchemaSync` ever invoke for this entity type) materializes only the framework-standard base columns (`id`/`uuid`/`bundle`/`filename`/`langcode`/`_data`) — it has no mechanism to materialize entity-level `#[Field]` columns for the `sql-blob` backend, so `parent_entity_type`/`parent_entity_id`/`is_active`/`created_at`/`updated_at` and every attachment-specific index (including the WP2 partial unique active-row index) were NEVER created on a real kernel boot; only test `setUp()` methods called `AttachmentSchema::ensureTable()` directly, so no test exercised the shape production actually boots with. `AttachmentServiceProvider::boot()` now calls it (gated on database availability, independent of the event dispatcher so a dispatcher-less CLI/migration boot still gets the schema), and `AttachmentSchema::ensureTable()` is now self-healing: if the generic path already created the base-only table first (out-of-order boot, or a pre-existing install), it additively adds the missing columns/indexes via new `healMissingColumns()`/`ensureIndexes()` instead of no-op'ing because the table already exists (same additive idea as `RelationshipSchemaManager` — which itself has zero production callers, so this heal path is the first LIVE use of the pattern, not a follow-on to an established one). (2) **`updated_at` stamping**: `setActive()`'s two raw `UPDATE`s and `AttachmentActiveInvariant::demoteSiblings()`'s raw `UPDATE` set `is_active` but never `updated_at`, freezing a demoted/activated row's audit trail; both now stamp `updated_at = time()` alongside `is_active` (no injectable clock convention exists elsewhere in the framework to mirror; `time()` matches how other raw-SQL writes stamp timestamps, e.g. `media`'s version rows). (3) **Composer drift**: `waaseyaa/entity-storage` was in `require` with zero `src/` imports (only 5 test files use it) — moved to `require-dev`; `symfony/event-dispatcher` (used only by tests, previously undeclared) added to `require-dev` at the same `^7.0` constraint the rest of the monorepo uses. `symfony/http-foundation` stays in `require` (legitimate `src/` dependency — the download router). (4) **Access-check contract**: `listFor()`/`getActive()` use `EntityRepositoryInterface::findBy()`, which applies no per-account access check (unlike `getQuery()`); a repo-wide grep found no production caller of either method today, so both gained docblock contract notes documenting that any caller exposing results to an end user must apply its own per-result access check, the way `AttachmentDownloadRouter` gates its own `find()`. NOTE: this is a documented convention only, NOT a mechanical gate — `findBy()` callsites are invisible to the `check-getquery-bindings` CI gate, so nothing enforces the contract beyond the docblocks and spec note. `docs/specs/work-surface.md` § F4 updated for all four. **Same-day adversarial-review hardening (two BLOCKERs, both independently reproduced, TDD red→green):** (5) **Self-heal silently destroyed pre-existing rows' data** — rows saved under the degraded base-only schema carry parent linkage/active flag/timestamps in the `_data` JSON blob; adding the real columns with static defaults (`''`/`0`) made those defaults WIN over the blob at hydration (`SqlStorageDriver::mergeFromRead()` gives real columns precedence on key collision), so a healed install's pre-existing attachments lost their parent linkage — `listFor()` stopped finding them and the download router's parent-delegated access check 404'd them permanently, with no log. New `backfillNewColumnsFromDataBlob()` copies each NEWLY-ADDED column's value out of the blob before anything can read the healed table: rows are read via the query builder and decoded in PHP (no `json_extract` SQL — platform syntax diverges), one UPDATE per row needing backfill (acceptable: the heal is one-time; subsequent boots find the columns present and never reach it), only for the columns just added and only when the blob carries a usable value — `parent_entity_type`/`parent_entity_id` scalar→string, `is_active` via the strict `AttachmentActiveInvariant` allow-list (blob garbage `'false'` backfills as 0, never active), `created_at`/`updated_at` numeric→int; healed row count logged at INFO for operator visibility; blob keys deliberately stay in `_data` (harmless — columns win on read, and the next entity save rebuilds the blob without column-routed keys). (6) **Heal crashed non-SQLite boots** — the first-cut `indexExists()` probe queried `sqlite_master` unconditionally (no platform check, no try/catch — unlike `ensureActivePartialUniqueIndex()` right next to it), and since `boot()` runs `ensureTable()` on EVERY boot with the table existing after the first, it threw on PostgreSQL/MySQL (`sqlite_master` doesn't exist) straight through `ProviderRegistry::boot()` — kernel boot crash on 2 of 3 supported platforms. The ENTIRE heal path (columns + value backfill + indexes) is wrapped in the same defensive try/catch + `warning()` posture as the partial-index method — best-effort schema healing must degrade loudly in the log, never crash boot; the index-creation mechanics were then reworked again in the final round, see (7). **Final review round (two more reproduced findings on the mid-heal-FAILURE recovery path):** (7) **Destructive, non-convergent retry after a mid-heal failure** — the round-2 cut committed each heal DDL statement individually and created the partial backstop index OUTSIDE the heal try/catch, so a failed backfill left columns-added-but-unfilled plus the partial index ahead of the composites; the NEXT boot's `ensureIndexes()` then went through `DBALSchema::addIndex()`, whose introspect-diff-RECREATE-TABLE path on SQLite strips the partial index's WHERE clause (DBAL introspection loses it), replays it as a FULL unique index that fails on duplicate inactive rows mid-rebuild, and silently DROPS the uuid unique constraint (duplicate-uuid INSERTs then succeeded) — repeating identically on every subsequent boot. Fix: heal-path index creation NEVER routes through DBAL's recreate machinery — composite indexes are raw `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS` on SQLite/PostgreSQL (`IF NOT EXISTS` needs PG ≥9.5; the partial-index docblock's version floor corrected 9.0→9.5 accordingly) and, on the MySQL family (no `IF NOT EXISTS` on stock MySQL 8), an `information_schema.statistics` probe scoped by `DATABASE()` (also eliminating round 2's `pg_indexes` multi-schema false positive — that probe is gone entirely) followed by plain `CREATE INDEX`; and `ensureActivePartialUniqueIndex()` now runs LAST, inside the heal try/catch, so a failed heal can never leave the partial index ahead of the composites. (8) **One-shot, non-resumable backfill + false remediation advice** — after a mid-heal failure the added columns survived (individually committed), so the next boot's "columns just added" trigger was empty and the remaining rows stayed blob-shadowed forever, while the warning's "re-run db:init" advice could not re-trigger anything (`db:init` only drives the generic `EntitySchemaSyncRunner`). Fix: column adds + value backfill run in ONE `$database->transaction()` — on SQLite/PostgreSQL DDL is transactional, so a mid-backfill failure rolls the column adds back and the whole heal retries convergently next boot; on MySQL/MariaDB DDL implicitly commits and the rollback cannot undo column adds, so the failure warning now states the honest per-platform recovery (SQLite/PG: automatic retry next boot; MySQL: values remain in `_data` but hydrate blank — the message gives the manual blob→column UPDATE shape including the `is_active` allow-list; no `db:init` promise). The stale-blob-never-overwrites property is preserved: the backfill trigger stays "columns just added", which the transaction makes sufficient on SQLite/PG. Final-round red→green: `AttachmentSchemaSelfHealTest::midHealFailureRollsBackAndTheNextBootConvergesFully` — REAL SQLite, no stub: a decorator kills the backfill's second UPDATE on boot 1 → boot 1 survives with the transactional-recovery warning AND the column adds are rolled back; boot 2 (healthy) heals fully — both rows backfilled at the raw-column level, ALL five indexes present per `sqlite_master` (uuid unique, bundle, both composites, partial with its WHERE clause intact), duplicate-uuid INSERT rejected; boot 3 is a pure no-op (no warnings, no heal logs). Acceptance — red→green: `AttachmentSchemaParityTest::ensureTableSelfHealsWhenGenericPathCreatesTheBaseTableFirst`; `AttachmentSchemaSelfHealTest` (degraded-row round-trip survives the heal, verified at both the hydrated and raw-column level, healed-count logged, post-heal `listFor()`/`setActive()` work; blob `is_active` allow-list; catalog-query failure on a non-SQLite stub completes `ensureTable()` with a warning instead of throwing; mid-heal DDL failure degrades to a warning; heal idempotency — a second run re-touches nothing and never lets stale blob values overwrite real columns); `AttachmentServiceProviderTest::bootMaterializesAttachmentSpecificSchema` / `::bootMaterializesSchemaEvenWithoutDispatcher`; `AttachmentActiveInvariantTest::demoteSiblingsStampsUpdatedAtOnEveryDemotedRow`; `AttachmentRepositoryTest::setActiveStampsUpdatedAtOnBothTheDemotedSiblingAndTheActivatedTarget`. Regression pins (pre-existing behavior, passed before this WP — not red→green): `AttachmentSchemaParityTest::baseColumnsMatchWhatSqlSchemaHandlerAutoGenerates` / `::genericPathAloneDoesNotProduceAttachmentSpecificColumns` / `::attachmentSchemaBuildIncludesAllDocumentedIndexes`.
- **Media version-row integrity: `nextVid()` failed OPEN (any error → vid 1, silently colliding with version history) and vid allocation was a check-then-act race; plus the original client filename was destroyed on upload (audit-remediation batch 2026-07-01, WP5).** (a) `MediaVersionRepository::nextVid()`'s `catch (\Throwable)` converted ANY query failure into `vid = 1` — on real kernel-booted installs, where the `media_version` table has the generic `SqlSchemaHandler` shape (no `vid`/`media_uuid` columns; the packaged create-table migration was undiscoverable because `packages/media/composer.json` declared no `extra.waaseyaa.migrations`), EVERY version row got vid 1 with no unique constraint to object. `nextVid()` now throws on failure (the driver's existing best-effort boundary logs a warning instead of writing colliding rows), and allocation is race-safe following the #1706 revision-id pattern: a new additive idempotent migration adds `UNIQUE (media_uuid, vid)` (`2026_07_01_000001_add_media_version_vid_unique_index.php` — shape-guarded so the generic kernel-boot table is skipped without error; pre-existing duplicates fail index creation LOUDLY for operator resolution), `extra.waaseyaa.migrations` is now declared (making both media migrations discoverable), and `MediaVersionStorageDriver::createVersion()` retries on `UniqueConstraintViolationException` (≤5 attempts, fresh MAX re-read, entity rebuilt per attempt; audit event carries the final vid). The wider CAS/versioning finish-or-park decision (subsystem disconnected + in-memory CAS) is deliberately untouched (#1742). (b) **Original-filename retention:** `MediaRouter` stored only the sanitized disk name (`generateSafeFilename()` strips all non-ASCII byte-wise), so Anishinaabemowin upload names were destroyed with no record. `File` gains a trailing `?string $originalName = null` (display metadata; never used for filesystem paths — invalid-UTF-8 names are dropped rather than poisoning the JSON sidecar), persisted through the `LocalFileRepository` sidecar and returned as `original_filename` in the upload response, while the disk name stays sanitized. Acceptance (TDD red→green): `MediaVersionRepositoryTest` (throws on unusable table — was "returns 1"; MAX+1 scoped per media), `MediaVersionStorageDriverTest::vid_allocation_retries_past_a_stolen_vid_and_never_duplicates` (stealing-writer harness mirroring `RevisionIdAllocationRaceTest`) + incrementing-vids pin, `MediaVersionVidUniqueIndexMigrationTest` (unique index + idempotent re-run; blob-shaped table skipped; missing table skipped), `FileTest`/`LocalFileRepositoryTest`/`MediaRouterTest` non-ASCII original-name round-trips (`Ozhibiiʼigan ᐊᓂᔑᓈᐯᒧᐎᓐ.png` survives in metadata; disk name stays `Ozhibii__igan_<hex>.png`).
- **`SlugGenerator` destroyed Indigenous orthography — Unicode titles now produce Unicode-preserving slugs (audit-remediation batch 2026-07-01, WP1).** `generate()` was byte-wise `strtolower()` + `preg_replace('/[^a-z0-9]+/', '-')`, which stripped ALL non-ASCII: an Anishinaabemowin title with long-vowel diacritics ("Anishinaabemowin Ākí") collapsed to `anishinaabemowin-k`, the glottal `ʼ` (U+02BC — a *letter* in the orthography) became a hyphen, and a fully-syllabics title (`ᐊᓂᔑᓈᐯᒧᐎᓐ`) collapsed to the EMPTY slug — colliding or meaningless URLs for an Indigenous-language CMS. Policy: preserve Unicode, do NOT transliterate to ASCII (transliteration destroys meaning). New pipeline: NFC-normalize (`\Normalizer::FORM_C`, so decomposed input slugs identically to its precomposed form) → `mb_strtolower` → NFC-normalize again (lowercasing can emit combining marks, e.g. `İ` → `i` + U+0307) → replace runs of `[^\p{L}\p{N}\p{M}]+` with `-` (`/u` flag; `\p{M}` kept so combining marks with NO precomposed form — the S̱aanich macron-below, the Tłı̨chǫ ogonek on dotless `ı` — survive attached to their base letter; hardened after adversarial review) → trim hyphens; invalid UTF-8 degrades to the historical byte-wise ASCII slugging instead of erroring; empty input still yields `''`. Pure-ASCII inputs produce byte-identical slugs to before — all 5 pre-existing fixtures untouched as regression pins. URL handling verified end-to-end: Symfony's `UrlMatcher` `rawurldecode()`s the request path so percent-encoded Unicode slugs match `{slug}` placeholders, and `WaaseyaaRouter::generate()` percent-encodes on the way out — round-trip pinned by two new `WaaseyaaRouterTest` tests. No in-monorepo consumer has an ASCII-only slug contract (node's `slug` field validates as a plain string; path-alias lookup is byte-exact match — NFC normalization also removes the previous NFC/NFD alias-miss hazard; the only ASCII gates found, `RenderController::pathSegmentToTemplate()` and `JsonApiController::toMachineName()`, are not slug consumers). `symfony/polyfill-mbstring` + `symfony/polyfill-intl-normalizer` added as explicit `waaseyaa/foundation` requires so standalone installs get `mb_strtolower`/`\Normalizer` without the extensions. Acceptance: `SlugGeneratorTest` (7 new Unicode fixtures: diacritics, glottal U+02BC, Canadian syllabics, mixed ASCII+Unicode, NFD input, idempotency + percent-encoding round-trip, invalid-UTF-8 fallback), `WaaseyaaRouterTest` (2 new match/generate round-trip tests).

### Changed

- **C-22 WP2: migrate every production `getStorage()->getQuery()` callsite to `getRepository()->getQuery()` (~35 callsites across 20 files / 15 packages).** No behavior change — `EntityRepository::getQuery()` is parity-tested against `SqlEntityStorage::getQuery()` (C-22 WP0/#1846). Query consumers now obtain the account-filtered query builder from the canonical repository; `load()`/`loadMultiple()`/`save()`/`delete()` calls on the same objects are left on `getStorage()` for now (C-22 WP3 migrates those). Access-sensitive consumers (`JsonApiController`, genealogy's `GenealogyFamilyService`/`GenealogyPedigreeService`) bind the account exactly as before. System-context (`accessCheck(false)`) consumers — CLI handlers, classification retention jobs, SEO sitemap/llms.txt generators, path alias resolution, GraphQL resolver, semantic search, OIDC client registry, messaging/relationship integrity checks — are a mechanical swap. Two consumers needed a widened constructor signature to carry the new repository handle alongside their existing storage handle: `AuthController::findUserByName()` and `PathAliasResolver` (both gained a second `EntityRepositoryInterface` parameter; `OidcClientLookup`/`OidcClientSeeder` likewise). Added `Waaseyaa\Entity\Testing\QueryOnlyStubRepository` (`packages/entity/testing/`) — a shared, `@api` test double for `EntityRepositoryInterface` that only implements `getQuery()` (accepting either a fixed query instance or a `\Closure(): EntityQueryInterface` for callers whose fake storage builds a fresh query per call), so existing test fixtures wiring a storage's `getQuery()` double can wire the same double for `getRepository()->getQuery()` without duplicating query configuration. `packages/api/tests/Fixtures/InMemoryEntityRepository.php` and `packages/relationship/tests/Fixtures/StubEntityRepository.php` similarly wrap their package's existing in-memory storage fixture. No consumer's read/write path was touched; `SqlEntityStorage` remains fully wired. Context: `docs/notes/c22-consumer-inventory.md`.

- **C-22 WP3: migrate every production `getStorage()`-based `load()`/`loadMultiple()`/`create()`/`save()`/`delete()`/`loadByKey()` callsite onto `getRepository()` (~50 files across ~25 packages), and retire `EntityStorageFactory` as a consumer surface.** `SqlEntityStorage`'s core CRUD methods (`create`/`load`/`loadByKey`/`loadMultiple`/`save`/`delete`) are now unreferenced by first-party production code — confirmed by the dead-code gate, which now baselines them as known-dead pending deletion in C-22 WP4 (`phpstan-dead-code-baseline.neon`). Mechanical swaps: `load($id)` → `find((string) $id)`, `loadMultiple($ids)` (id-keyed) → `findMany($ids)` (plain list, re-keyed by `$entity->id()` at the callsite when the caller relied on id-keying), `loadMultiple()` (no-arg "load all") → `findBy([])`, `delete([$entity])` → `delete($entity)` (single-entity, not array). Two API-shape gaps required design decisions before they could migrate cleanly:
  - **`EntityRepositoryInterface` gains `create(array $values = []): EntityInterface`** (additive), backed by the SAME `Waaseyaa\EntityStorage\Hydration\EntityInstantiator` field-default-application logic `SqlEntityStorage::create()` already used — extracted onto `EntityInstantiator::applyFieldDefinitionDefaults()` so both engines share one implementation, not two independently-maintained copies. Parity proven by `tests/Integration/PhaseN/EntityStorageEngineParity/CreateFieldDefaultsParityTest.php` (folded into the WP1 harness): given the same `$values`, both engines fill the same defaults for omitted fields, leave explicit values untouched, and mark the entity `isNew()`. All 8 production `getStorage()->create()` callsites (`JsonApiController::store()`, `EntityResolver::resolveCreate()`, `UserCreateHandler`, `EntityCreateHandler`, `InstallHandler`, `NcSyncService`, `NoteIngester`, `OidcClientSeeder`) now call `getRepository()->create()`; `NoteIngester`'s and `OidcClientSeeder`'s/`OidcClientLookup`'s constructors narrowed from `EntityStorageInterface`/dual-handle to a single `EntityRepositoryInterface` param (breaking change for any external caller constructing these directly — none exist in first-party code).
  - **`loadByKey()` has no repository equivalent** — the 5 callsites (`ForgotPasswordController`, `RegisterController`, `ClassificationLabelRegistry`, `GenericAdminSurfaceHost` ×3 call sites via a new shared `findByIdOrUuid()` helper) now do a bounded `getQuery()->accessCheck(false)->condition($key, $value)->range(0, 1)->execute()` + `find()` instead — same system-context `accessCheck(false)` semantics `SqlEntityStorage::loadByKey()` used internally, same single-row result.
  - **`SessionMiddleware`/`BearerAuthMiddleware` constructors narrowed from `EntityStorageInterface` to `EntityRepositoryInterface`** (the kernel's `HttpKernel::buildMiddlewareStack()` now passes `getRepository('user')` instead of `getStorage('user')`) — external code constructing these middlewares directly must update the call.
  - **JSON:API no-expectation PATCH now cuts a revision (behavior change, superseded contract).** `JsonApiController::update()`'s two save branches (with and without a stated `expected_revision_id`) previously diverged: the expectation-stated path went through `getRepository()->save(..., context: ...)` (revision-aware) while the no-expectation path stayed on the legacy `getStorage()->save()` (revision-less), per a documented contract (`kitty-specs/archive/optimistic-locking-01KTXCHY/contracts/conflict-surfaces.md` clause 14, FR-003/SC-003). C-22 WP3 unifies both branches onto `getRepository()->save($entity)` — a no-expectation PATCH on a revisionable type now cuts a new revision, simply without the `SaveContext::withExpectedRevisionId()` conflict-detection guard. The archived contract doc and `docs/specs/api-layer.md` are updated with dated supersession notes; `JsonApiControllerConflictTest::patchWithoutExpectationKeepsTheLegacyStoragePath` renamed to `...StillUsesRevisionAwareRepository` with its assertion flipped (`assertSame($revisionsBefore, ...)` → `assertGreaterThan($revisionsBefore, ...)`). Confirmed no other consumer depended on the revision-free behavior — the AI-tools `entity.update` surface (`EntityUpdateTool`) already saved unconditionally through `getRepository()->save()` for both paths, so this brings the two write surfaces (REST, MCP tool) into alignment rather than introducing a new asymmetry.
  Shared test-helper additions: `Waaseyaa\Entity\Testing\StorageBackedStubRepository` (`packages/entity/testing/`, `@api`) wraps an existing `EntityStorageInterface` double and delegates `create()`/`find()`/`findMany()`/`findBy()`/`save()`/`delete()`/`getQuery()`/`exists()`/`count()` to it, so a test that already configured a storage double's expectations can wire the SAME double as the repository double instead of duplicating them across two mocks. `QueryOnlyStubRepository` (WP2) gained a matching `create()` stub (throws — it is intentionally query-only). Context: `docs/notes/c22-consumer-inventory.md`.

- **C-22 WP4: delete the legacy save engine — `SqlEntityStorage.php` and `EntityStorageFactory.php` are removed outright.** `EntityRepository` (`getRepository()`) is now the framework's only persistence engine, closing out issue C-22. WP3 confirmed via the dead-code gate that `SqlEntityStorage`'s core methods and `EntityStorageFactory::getStorage` had zero remaining production callers; this WP deletes the classes.
  - **Kept, deliberately:** `EntityStorageInterface` (`RevisionableStorageInterface extends` it as dormant public API; several test doubles implement it directly — deleting it would be a needless breaking change to a still-valid interface), and the shared `SqlEntityQuery`/`BundleSubtableGateway`/`SqlStorageDriver`/`EntityInstantiator` classes `EntityRepository` builds on independently (none of these were `SqlEntityStorage`-only). `EntityTypeManager::getStorage()`/`EntityTypeManagerInterface::getStorage()` are also kept, but demoted to a **dormant "bring your own `EntityStorageInterface`" extension seam** — the kernel (`EntityTypeManagerFactory`) no longer supplies a `storageFactory` closure, and `EntityType::fromClass()`'s `$storageClass` default changed from `'Waaseyaa\EntityStorage\SqlEntityStorage'` to `''`. Calling `getStorage($entityTypeId)` on any first-party entity type now throws `RuntimeException` ("No storage class configured…") unless the caller supplies their own storage class/factory — this is intentional, not a regression: nothing first-party used `getStorage()` after WP3.
  - **`SqlStorageDriver` gains an optional `?FieldDefinitionRegistryInterface $fieldRegistry = null` constructor param**, mirroring the deleted `SqlEntityStorage::$fieldRegistry`. Without it, `EntityRepository` would have silently regressed the K2 write/read symmetry fix for `FieldStorage::Data` fields (issue #1308): `splitForWrite()` previously routed by schema-column-existence alone, so a field whose storage hint migrated to `FieldStorage::Data` but still has a lingering legacy column would write to the STALE column instead of `_data` once `SqlEntityStorage` (which consulted the registry hint) was gone. The kernel now threads the field registry into `SqlStorageDriver` so `EntityRepository` inherits the same symmetry guarantee.
  - **Test suite:** the WP1 behavior-identity harness (`tests/Integration/PhaseN/EntityStorageEngineParity/`, including WP3's `CreateFieldDefaultsParityTest.php`) is deleted — its sole purpose was pinning cross-engine parity during the WP1–WP4 migration window; nothing remains to compare with one engine. `tests/Integration/PhaseN/EntityQueryAccessCheck/RepositoryStorageQueryParityTest.php` (the WP0 `getQuery()` parity test) is likewise deleted — its assertions were already duplicated in `SqlEntityQueryAccessCheckTest`/`AnonymousAccountFilterTest`. ~45 test files across `packages/entity-storage/tests/` and top-level `tests/` were converted from `SqlEntityStorage`-based fixture seeding to `EntityRepository` (mechanical: `create()`/`save()`/`load()`→`find()`/`loadMultiple()`→`findMany()`/`getQuery()` unchanged), several renamed for clarity (e.g. `SqlEntityStorageBundleFieldsTest.php` → `EntityRepositoryBundleFieldsTest.php`, `SqlEntityStorageTest.php` → `EntityRepositorySqlCrudTest.php`, `SqlEntityStorageBundleQueryRoutingTest.php` → `EntityRepositoryBundleQueryRoutingTest.php`).
  - **One accepted, documented coverage/behavior loss:** `SqlEntityStorage`'s load-side `[MISSING_BUNDLE_SUBTABLE]` notice (its private `mergeBundleSubtableRow()`/`mergeBundleSubtableRowsBatch()`) had no `EntityRepository` equivalent — `EntityRepository`'s load path silently skips a missing bundle subtable with no operator-facing notice. The **save-side** notice (`BundleSubtableGateway::logMissingSubtableOnSave()`) — the more load-bearing one, since it signals an actual data-placement decision — is preserved and still tested. The load-side notice was purely diagnostic (the entity still loads correctly either way).
  `docs/specs/entity-system.md` "The legacy save engine is gone (C-22)" § rewritten for the single-engine reality. Context: `docs/notes/c22-consumer-inventory.md` §7.

### Fixed

- **`EntityRepository::doSave()` could silently leave a newly-created `User` entity's in-memory id stuck at the anonymous sentinel `0` instead of the real assigned id (found during C-22 WP4's test-conversion sweep).** The new-entity id-backfill branch keyed off `(string) ($entity->id() ?? '')` to detect "id not yet assigned." `User::id()` overrides the base accessor to satisfy `AccountInterface`'s contract that "anonymous is `0`, never `null`" — so for a brand-new `User`, `id() ?? ''` returned `'0'`, never the empty string the backfill branch checked for, and the branch (along with `SqlStorageDriver::write()`'s own empty-id insert path) silently never fired. The saved row got a real autoincrement id from the database, but the in-memory `$entity` kept `uid = 0` — colliding with the `AnonymousUser` sentinel id documented in CLAUDE.md ("Account sentinel IDs… never use low integers for non-real accounts"). Any entity subclass overriding `id()` to return a non-null default for unassigned entities was equally affected, not just `User`. Fixed by reading the id key from the entity's raw `toArray()` value bag instead of the polymorphic `id()` accessor, which has no such override. Caught by the existing test suite once entity creation across the codebase was routed through `EntityRepository` in this WP (previously `SqlEntityStorage::create()`/`save()`, which had no such bug, masked this on many code paths).

### Added

- **C-22 WP1: behavior-identity test harness comparing `SqlEntityStorage` and `EntityRepository` (no production change).** New suite `tests/Integration/PhaseN/EntityStorageEngineParity/` saves the same entity values through both persistence engines against one shared SQLite database and diffs the raw stored rows, so the eventual removal of the legacy engine (see `docs/notes/c22-consumer-inventory.md`) can be validated mechanically rather than by inspection. Covers: base columns vs `_data` blob split (byte-identical `_data` for values with no dedicated column), bundle subtable rows (both engines route through the same `BundleSubtableGateway`, byte-identical), and the sql-blob translatable layout (cross-engine parity — each engine can read the row the other wrote). It also **pins four real behavioral divergences** rather than silently normalizing over them: (1) a `json`-typed core field that collides with a real base-table column is correctly JSON-encoded by `SqlEntityStorage::splitForStorage()` but silently mangled into the literal string `"Array"` by `SqlStorageDriver::splitForWrite()` (currently inert in production — no first-party entity type has this collision, since only entity-key fields get real base-table columns); (2) `EntityRepository` has **no clock parameter and does not auto-populate `created`/`changed`-shaped fields at all** (`SqlEntityStorage` does, via its injected `EntityClockInterface`) — flagged as a risk for C-22 WP3: a write consumer migrated from `getStorage()->save()` to `getRepository()->save()` that relies on storage-layer timestamp population will silently stop getting fresh timestamps; (3) the sql-column translatable layout has no `EntityRepository` equivalent at all (`EntityRepository`'s translation surface is sql-blob only, gated by `assertTwoAxis()`) — not currently a migration blocker since no first-party entity type uses sql-column for a translatable type; (4) the already-documented event-model divergence (`docs/specs/entity-system.md` "Two save engines") — `SqlEntityStorage` fires only `EntityEvents::PRE_SAVE`/`POST_SAVE`; `EntityRepository` additionally fires `BeforeSaveEvent`/`AfterSaveEvent`, and a `BeforeSaveEvent` subscriber may throw `AbortOperationException` to veto the save (no write occurs, `AfterSaveEvent` never fires) — flagged as a risk for C-22 WP2/WP3.

- **`EntityRepository` gains an access-checked query surface — `EntityRepositoryInterface::getQuery(): EntityQueryInterface` (C-22 prerequisite; additive).** The canonical `@api` repository engine can now build the SAME access-checked `SqlEntityQuery` the legacy `SqlEntityStorage::getQuery()` builds, so consumers depending on the account-filtered query surface (chiefly `packages/api` `JsonApiController`) can eventually migrate off `EntityTypeManager::getStorage()` and the legacy engine can be removed. `EntityRepository::getQuery()` mirrors the storage path exactly: same `SqlEntityQuery` construction, same lazily-resolved `Waaseyaa\Access\EntityAccessHandler` threading (fail-closed — an unbound account throws `MissingQueryAccountException` on `execute()`; callers MUST `setAccount()` or opt out with `accessCheck(false)`), and an id-keyed entity loader matching `SqlEntityStorage::loadMultiple()`'s shape so the per-row `EntityAccessHandler::check($entity, 'view', $account)` filter runs identically. Two new optional trailing `EntityRepository` constructor params (`?EntityAccessHandler $accessHandler = null`, `?\Closure $accessHandlerResolver = null`) carry the handler; the kernel (`EntityTypeManagerFactory`) threads the SAME lazy resolver it already threads into the storage engine, so kernel-built repositories are fail-closed by default. **Strict scope: additive only** — `SqlEntityStorage`/`EntityStorageFactory` are unchanged and still wired; no consumers were migrated; existing `EntityRepository` save/find behavior is unchanged; both engines keep working. A parity integration test (`tests/Integration/PhaseN/EntityQueryAccessCheck/RepositoryStorageQueryParityTest.php`) proves the two `getQuery()` surfaces are behaviourally identical — same fail-closed throw without an account, same access-filtered id set for an account-bound query — against one shared SQLite database and access handler. **Interface note:** `getQuery()` is a new method on the `@api` `EntityRepositoryInterface`; any external implementer must add it (all first-party test doubles were updated). Context: `docs/notes/c22-consumer-inventory.md`.

### Changed

- Foundation: invert `BuiltinRouteRegistrar` L0→L4 route-table coupling (WP5). The 14 `Waaseyaa\Api\*` FQCN route blocks previously hard-coded in `BuiltinRouteRegistrar` are now registered by `ApiServiceProvider::routes()` (called via the existing provider loop). Foundation registers only the framework-substrate routes it owns: OpenAPI, entity-type lifecycle, broadcast SSE, media upload, attachment download, semantic search, discovery, and the SSR catch-alls. All route names, paths, and access options are preserved — behavior-neutral refactor.
- Foundation: add string-literal layer gate (PL008) to `bin/check-package-layers`. Scans Layer-0 `src/` files for quoted `Waaseyaa\<Ns>\…` FQCN string literals that reference packages at a higher layer, fails on new violations. Allowlist in `tools/package-layers-string-literal-baseline.txt` (one entry: `PackageManifestCompiler.php` — legitimate cross-layer attribute FQCNs per CLAUDE.md layer-discipline rule). Self-test in `bin/check-package-layers-pl008-self-test` (wired into `composer verify`).
- **`HttpKernel::serveHttpRequest()` and `AbstractKernel` god-methods extracted to focused collaborators (foundation wave-2 WP4 — behavior-neutral).** Three private methods extracted from `serveHttpRequest()`: `matchRoute(string, string): HttpRequest|HttpResponse` (route context + matching + request attribute population), `buildMiddlewareStack(): HttpPipeline` (middleware collection, sorting, and pipeline assembly), and `buildRouterChain(): ControllerDispatcher` (domain-router list + BroadcastRouter merge). `AbstractKernel::bootEntityTypeManager()` is now a thin delegator to the new named class `EntityTypeManagerFactory` (`packages/foundation/src/Kernel/EntityTypeManagerFactory.php`), which receives the database, dispatcher, field registry, logger, and three callable seams (access-handler resolver, community-scope resolver, account-context attacher) as explicit constructor/method params. `AbstractKernel::buildHandlerContainer()` returns a `KernelHandlerContainer` instance (`packages/foundation/src/Kernel/KernelHandlerContainer.php`) — a `final class implements ContainerInterface` — replacing the inline anonymous class with identical resolution semantics (kernel bindings → provider walk → reflection auto-wiring). Zero assertion changes to `HttpKernelTest`, `AbstractKernelTest`, and all sibling kernel tests; `composer verify` green with only the 2 known pre-existing `InertiaMultipartCsrfIntegrationTest` failures.
- **`ConfigLoader::load()` now accepts an optional `?LoggerInterface $logger = null` parameter; the static mutable `$logger` field and `setLogger()` / `logger()` helpers are removed (foundation wave-2 WP3, behavior-neutral).** Callers in `AbstractKernel` and `AppEntityTypeLoader` (which both hold a real logger) now pass it explicitly; the two CLI callers (`AboutHandler`, `HealthReportHandler`) use the default `null` path unchanged. `ConfigLoaderTest` updated to pass the logger via the new `load()` param instead of the removed `setLogger()`.
- **`BuiltinRouteRegistrar` `api.telescope.codified_context.*` route block documented as intentional backward-compat aliases for `api.telescope.agent_context.*` (foundation wave-2 WP3, documentation-only).** Both blocks map to `CodifiedContextController`; the `codified-context` URL prefix is a legacy alias kept for backward-compatibility (spec: `telescope-agent-context-telemetry.md`). A one-line comment is added to the alias block; no routes changed.
- **`EventListenerRegistrar::registerDiscoveryCacheListeners()` and `registerMcpReadCacheListeners()` now delegate to a shared private `registerEntityCacheInvalidationListeners()` helper (foundation wave-2 WP3, behavior-neutral).** The two methods were near-identical: the helper is parameterised by tag prefix (`discovery` / `mcp_read`), error label, and an optional `?\Closure(string): list<string> $extraTags` for discovery's `discovery:surface:discovery_api` surface tag on relationship/node entity types. Public signatures and registered listener behavior are unchanged.
### Fixed

- **`ExceptionHandler` was leaking raw exception messages and file paths to clients in production — generic exceptions now return a static safe detail (foundation wave-2 WP2).** `renderGenericException()` was returning `$e->getMessage()` verbatim as the JSON:API `detail` field (status 500): SQL fragments, file paths, and interpolated user input were visible to any API consumer. `renderForCli()` exposed `File: <path>:<line>` for all generic throwables. The fix adds two optional trailing constructor parameters (`bool $debug = false`, `?LoggerInterface $logger = null`) that are safe by default — existing callers `new ExceptionHandler()` / `new ExceptionHandler($context)` are unaffected. In production (`$debug = false`) the HTTP `detail` is now the static string `'Internal Server Error'` and the CLI line drops the raw message and file:line; in debug mode both are present (unchanged developer UX). The real exception message, class, file, line, and trace are always logged internally via the injected logger (defaults to `NullLogger`) so operators retain diagnostics without client exposure. A class-level docblock notes that three error-rendering paths coexist (this handler, the `error-handler` package renderer, and `HttpKernel`'s inline boot-error path) — reconciliation is deferred as a follow-up. TDD: new tests were written and confirmed RED before implementation; two existing tests that asserted the old leaky contract (`renders_generic_exception_as_internal_error`, `renders_generic_cli_error`) were updated to the new safe-by-default contract.
### Security

- **The foundation logging pipeline now redacts secret-bearing context values before any handler (or S3 sovereignty sink) can receive them (foundation wave-2 WP1).** `LogManager::log()` and `ChannelLogger` pass records through processors, but `LogManager::fromConfig()` had no redaction in its default processor set — `$log->info('login', ['password' => $pw])` or a logged `Authorization` header / API key / DSN was written to disk in cleartext and shipped off-box to S3 sovereignty profiles. A new `RedactorProcessor` (`packages/foundation/src/Log/Processor/RedactorProcessor.php`) is prepended unconditionally to the global processor list in `fromConfig()` — it runs before any config-named processors so no enrichment step can re-introduce cleartext credentials. **Key denylist (primary):** a context key whose lowercased form contains any of `password`, `token`, `secret`, `authorization`, `api_key`, or `cookie` as a substring → value becomes `[REDACTED]`. **Value backstop (secondary, conservative):** a string value under a benign-looking key is also redacted if it contains any denylist keyword (case-insensitive substring) — catches verbatim `Authorization: Bearer …` header strings, URL-encoded passwords in raw bodies, etc. **Recursive:** nested arrays are walked to arbitrary depth; non-sensitive siblings preserved byte-identical. Replacement sentinel: `[REDACTED]`. Extra keywords accepted via constructor (`array $extraKeys = []`). `buildProcessor('redact')` wires it via the config `processors` list too. `RedactorProcessorTest` (19 assertions) verifies all six denylist keys, benign passthrough, value backstop, recursive walk, immutability, and extra-key injection — each proven RED before implementation. `LogManagerTest::from_config_redactor_is_on_by_default_without_explicit_config` verifies the always-on wiring: `password` and `Authorization` keys are `[REDACTED]` in the JSON file output of a `fromConfig()`-built manager with no explicit processor config; `user_id` survives intact. `docs/specs/infrastructure.md` processor table updated with `RedactorProcessor`.
### Fixed

- **CSRF integration-test runner now routes PHP display output to stderr so a PHP 8.5 deprecation notice can no longer pollute subprocess stdout, mark `headers_sent()`, and break `session_start()` — local `composer verify` now matches CI (test-infra fix, no production code changed).** `csrf_kernel_runner.php` is spawned via `shell_exec(PHP_BINARY ...)` and inherits the ambient `php.ini`. In PHP 8.5 the `ini_set('session.use_only_cookies', '0')` call emits a `Deprecated` notice; under the WSL2/local default of `display_errors=1` that notice printed to stdout *before* the kernel booted, causing `headers_sent()` to return true. `SessionMiddleware::process()` then called `session_start()` which failed ("cannot be sent after headers already sent"), leaving the session inactive; `CsrfMiddleware::attachCookieIfHtml()` bailed at its `session_status() !== PHP_SESSION_ACTIVE` guard and emitted no `XSRF-TOKEN` Set-Cookie header, making the two `assertNotNull($cookieHeader)` assertions fail. In CI `display_errors=0` the notice went to the log, stdout stayed clean, and both tests passed — a pure environment false alarm. Fix: `ini_set('display_errors', 'stderr')` is added as the very first executable statement (before `require` and any session ini_set) so stdout is always a clean JSON/headers channel regardless of ambient php.ini; the now-redundant deprecated `ini_set('session.use_only_cookies', '0')` line is removed (the explicit `session_id($sessionId)` call plus `ini_set('session.use_cookies', '0')` already provide the intended behaviour). Red→green proof: `-d display_errors=1 phpunit InertiaMultipartCsrfIntegrationTest` showed 2 failures before the fix and 4/4 pass after.
- **database-legacy (WP6, #1816):** closed the last SQL-injection vector in the
  entity read engine. `SelectInterface::condition()`, `orderBy()`, `isNull()`,
  and `isNotNull()` now auto-quote their `$field` via the platform's
  `quoteIdentifier` (a reserved-word / metacharacter-bearing column name is
  rendered inert), matching the long-standing behaviour of `Update` / `Delete`.

### Changed
- **database-legacy / entity-storage (WP6, #1816):** added two raw-expression
  seams to `SelectInterface` — `whereRaw(string $expression, array $parameters = [])`
  and `orderByRaw(string $expression, string $direction)` — emitted verbatim with
  positional `?` parameters bound in order (developer-supplied-only contract,
  same as the `join()` ON-condition). `SqlEntityQuery` and `SqlStorageDriver` now
  resolve each referenced field to a `ResolvedField` value object and route
  identifiers through the auto-quoting `condition()`/`orderBy()` path and SQL
  expressions (`json_extract(...)`) through the verbatim raw seams; the K3
  native-type `CAST(... AS TEXT)` casting for `_data` JSON fields is preserved
  inside the raw path. `Queue\DbalTransport`'s `COALESCE(...)` predicate migrated
  to `whereRaw()`.

- **`#[UniqueJob]` and `#[RateLimited]` were silent no-ops in `DbalQueue` — the persistent driver never consulted `AttributeGuard`, which performs in-process / per-PHP-process tracking only (queue M2, WP5).** Both attributes are correctly enforced by `SyncQueue` (same-process execution) but had zero effect when jobs were dispatched through the `DbalQueue` transport-backed driver — silently misleading consumers. Cross-process enforcement requires a distributed dedup/rate-limit store and is out of scope. The fix makes the surface HONEST and the no-op NON-SILENT: (1) `UniqueJob.php`, `RateLimited.php`, and `AttributeGuard.php` gain prominent docblock warnings that these attributes are in-process / SyncQueue-only and are NOT enforced by `DbalQueue`; (2) `DbalQueue` gains an optional trailing `?LoggerInterface $logger = null` constructor parameter (existing callers `new DbalQueue($transport)` / `new DbalQueue($transport, 'queue')` remain valid), resolving to `NullLogger` by default; (3) when a message carrying `#[UniqueJob]` or `#[RateLimited]` is dispatched, a `warning` is logged naming the unenforced attribute(s) and the job class — deduplicated once per job class per process instance to prevent log spam. The message is still pushed to the transport (never suppressed).

- **`DbalTransport::pop()` returned a false-empty under contention — a lost claim race was miscounted as an empty queue (queue M3, WP5).** The atomic claim loop used `for ($i = 0; $i < 3; $i++)`, treating both "SELECT returned no candidate" and "UPDATE affected 0 rows" (a lost race) as loop-exhaustion conditions. Under heavy contention a worker could lose three claim races in a row and return `null` even though claimable jobs existed. The fix restructures the loop so the ONLY definitive "queue empty" signal is a SELECT that returns no claimable candidate. A lost claim race (0 rows affected) increments a contention counter and retries; the safety bound is raised to `MAX_CLAIM_RETRIES = 50` (from 3) with a documented rationale — generous because each lost race means another worker won (system making progress) and a false-empty is worse than a briefly delayed pop. `UPDATE ... RETURNING` was considered and rejected: MySQL (a supported driver alongside SQLite/PostgreSQL) does not support it, so portability forbids it. All existing semantics are preserved: fresh-claim guard (`reserved_at IS NULL`), expired-lease reclaim with `attempts` bump + exact-prior-`reserved_at` guard, visibility timeout.
### Changed

- Relocated `CacheConfigResolver` from `waaseyaa/cache` (L0) to `waaseyaa/routing` (L4) to eliminate the only non-event-decoupled upward type edge in the cache package; `Waaseyaa\Cache\CacheConfigResolver` is now `Waaseyaa\Routing\CacheConfigResolver`. Introduces zero new composer `require` edges — both production consumers (`ssr`, `workspace`) already require `waaseyaa/routing`. Removed `cache/src/CacheConfigResolver.php` from `check-package-layers` `kernelExemptFiles`. Behavior-neutral relocation; fixes #1807. W4-3 follow-up: drive `waaseyaa/cache` to full foundation-purity.
- **`waaseyaa/ingestion` README now accurately describes the shipped surface (WP2 Part A — doc reconciliation).** Three false claims corrected: (1) the required-field list no longer mentions a "dedupe key" or "signature" that do not exist — it now lists the actual eight required fields (`payload_id`, `version`, `source`, `snapshot_type`, `timestamp`, `entity_type`, `source_url`, `data`); (2) `ValidationResult` is correctly documented as carrying a `list<string>` of free-form error message strings, not structured `ENVELOPE_*` error codes (those belong to the foundation-layer ingestion package, a separate stack); (3) `EnvelopeValidator` is correctly described as abstract and `@internal` (extended by applications, not a ready-to-use public class). Doc-only — no production code changed.

- **`packages/foundation/src/Ingestion/EnvelopeValidator` renamed to `MessageEnvelopeValidator` to eliminate naming collision with `waaseyaa/ingestion`'s unrelated abstract `EnvelopeValidator` (WP2 Part B — rename).** The foundation class validates a *message* envelope (`source`/`type`/`payload`/`timestamp` + optional `trace_id`/`tenant_id`/`metadata`) and returns an `Envelope` DTO or throws `InvalidEnvelopeException`; the ingestion-package class validates an *entity ingest* payload and returns a `ValidationResult`. The rename covers: `packages/foundation/src/Ingestion/EnvelopeValidator.php` → `MessageEnvelopeValidator.php`; the docblock reference in `Envelope.php`; the test class + file (`EnvelopeValidatorTest` → `MessageEnvelopeValidatorTest`); and the class name in `docs/specs/ingestion-defaults.md` and `docs/specs/infrastructure.md`.

- **`MessageEnvelopeValidator` now accepts an injectable `TraceIdGeneratorInterface` instead of minting trace IDs inline with `Uuid::v4()` (WP2 Part B — DI seam).** The validator previously hard-coupled `symfony/uid` directly; the new `TraceIdGeneratorInterface` (`@api`) and its default implementation `UuidV4TraceIdGenerator` extract the minting behind an injectable seam. The constructor uses the nullable-default pattern (`?TraceIdGeneratorInterface $traceIdGenerator = null` → `$this->traceIdGenerator = $traceIdGenerator ?? new UuidV4TraceIdGenerator()`) so all existing `new MessageEnvelopeValidator()` callers continue to work unchanged. `symfony/uid` is retained in `packages/foundation/composer.json` — `DomainEvent` still depends on it. TDD: `MessageEnvelopeValidatorTest::injectedTraceIdGeneratorIsUsedWhenTraceIdAbsent` was written and confirmed RED (class not found) before the implementation landed.

### Fixed

- **F1 — `validation`: null `existsChecker`/`existsChecker` now throws `\InvalidArgumentException` instead of silently passing all values.** `EntityExistsValidator` and `UniqueFieldValidator` previously returned early when their checker callable was `null`, making the constraint a no-op rather than signalling misconfiguration. Both validators now throw immediately so callers discover the wiring error at registration time, not at first validation.

- **F2 — `mail`: `LocalTransport` throws on log-write failure; `SendGridTransport` throws on empty recipient list.** `LocalTransport::send()` ignored a `false` return from `file_put_contents`, silently swallowing log entries. It now throws `\RuntimeException` on write failure. `SendGridTransport::send()` previously attempted to send with an empty recipients array; it now throws `\InvalidArgumentException` early (before any HTTP call) when every recipient address is blank.

- **F3 — `scheduler`: clock injection for lock TTL and run-record timestamps.** `LockInterface::acquire()`, `DatabaseLock::acquire()`, `InMemoryLock::acquire()`, and `ScheduleStateRepository::recordRun()` each accept an optional `?\DateTimeInterface $now = null` parameter (defaults to `new \DateTimeImmutable('now', UTC)`). `ScheduleRunner` threads the injected `$now` through to every callsite, replacing direct `time()` / `date()` calls. Lock-expiry and run-record timestamps are now deterministically testable.

- **F4 — `i18n`: `Translator::has()` walks the fallback chain; `LanguageManager::resetToDefault()` added.** `has()` previously checked only the active locale, missing translations available via the fallback chain (`trans()` already walked the chain — this was a behavioral inconsistency). It now walks the full chain and treats empty-string values as absent, matching `trans()` semantics. `LanguageManagerInterface` gains `resetToDefault(): void` (implemented by `LanguageManager`) to restore the system default after a per-request locale switch — a missing lifecycle hook for worker-mode use.

- **F5 — `analytics`: `UmamiClient` language is now injectable instead of hardcoded `'en'`.** A new optional trailing constructor parameter `string $language = 'en'` (backward-compatible — existing three/four/five-arg callers are unaffected) is sent in the Umami payload, replacing the hardcoded `'en'`. Set it to the application's active locale so analytics reflects each event's actual language.

- **F6 — `queue`: `DbalTransport::release()` increments `attempts` atomically.** The previous implementation called a private `getAttempts()` read followed by a write — a non-atomic SELECT + UPDATE that could race under concurrent workers. `release()` now uses a single `UPDATE … SET attempts = attempts + 1` DML statement, closing the TOCTOU race. The now-unused `getAttempts()` private method is removed.

- **F7 — `error-handler`: `EditorLinkGenerator` is now a pure value object; env resolution moved to `DevExceptionRenderer`.** `EditorLinkGenerator::__construct()` previously read `getenv('EDITOR')` directly, making it impure and untestable in isolation. The env-resolution logic (`getenv('EDITOR')` → normalise → default to `'vscode'`) is moved to `DevExceptionRenderer` (the composition root). `EditorLinkGenerator` now accepts only `string $editorId = 'vscode'` — a deterministic, side-effect-free constructor. `DevExceptionRenderer` gains an optional `?string $editorId = null` constructor parameter; when `null`, it falls back to the env-resolved value.

- **F8 — `geo`: `GeoDistance::haversine()` validates coordinate ranges.** Latitude arguments outside `-90..90` and longitude arguments outside `-180..180` now throw `\InvalidArgumentException` naming the offending parameter. Valid boundary values (`±90`, `±180`) are accepted without error. Previously the method silently accepted out-of-range values and produced mathematically undefined results.

## [0.1.0-alpha.250] - 2026-06-27

### Added

- **Audit tamper-evidence — data-model foundation (`L1-audit.md` M2, WP1 of 4).** The OCAP audit log gains a hash-chain + checkpoint substrate so post-hoc edits/deletions/gaps become *detectable* (the append-only decorator only prevents mutation through sanctioned paths). This PR lands the foundation: `audit_event` gains `row_hash`/`prev_hash` columns (`DEFAULT ''` = unsealed, additive idempotent migration — the writer's append path is unchanged); a new append-only `audit_checkpoint` table seals contiguous segments; `AuditEventCanonicalizer` (`HASH_VERSION v1`, length-prefixed, NULL-explicit, fixed column order) defines the reproducible row hash; `AuditCheckpointHasher` defines the shared checkpoint hash; and a **genesis anchor** is written once over the current `MAX(id)` so pre-migration rows are attested as "predates chaining" without false back-computed assurance. `audit_checkpoint` joins `AppendOnlyAuditDatabase::APPEND_ONLY_TABLES`. The checkpoint builder, `audit:verify`, and checkpoint-aware `audit:prune` follow in subsequent WPs. Design: `craftsmanship/AUDIT-TAMPER-EVIDENCE-DESIGN.md`.
- **Audit tamper-evidence — checkpoint builder + external sink (`L1-audit.md` M2, WP2 of 4).** `AuditCheckpointBuilder` seals unsealed `audit_event` rows into a continuous hash chain and a new `audit_checkpoint` row, via the raw `DatabaseInterface` (idempotently re-derived after a crash).
