It runs on a schedule (`AuditCheckpointScheduleEntries`, default every 15 min) and on demand via `bin/waaseyaa audit:checkpoint`. Each checkpoint is exported through a pluggable **`CheckpointSink`** — the load-bearing tamper-evidence anchor; the default `FileCheckpointSink` appends NDJSON locally (+ optional stdout) but its docblock warns **loudly** that production requires an off-box / WORM / external append-only sink (a host that can edit `audit_event` can also edit a local file). Optional HMAC over the checkpoint hash is supported (`audit.checkpoint_hmac_key`); asymmetric/KMS signing deferred. `audit:verify` and checkpoint-aware `audit:prune` follow.
- **Audit tamper-evidence — `audit:verify` chain verification (`L1-audit.md` M2, WP3 of 4).** `AuditChainVerifier` walks every sealed segment and detects tampering: it checks the checkpoint chain, the per-segment row count, each row's chain link + content recompute (`AuditEventCanonicalizer`), that the last `row_hash` equals the checkpoint's `segment_hash`, and that each `checkpoint_hash` recomputes — stopping at the first break with a machine-readable `failureKind` (`genesis`/`checkpoint_chain`/`row_count`/`chain_link`/`row_content`/`segment_hash`/`checkpoint_hash`) and the first broken id. `bin/waaseyaa audit:verify [--json]` exits non-zero on tamper and emits an `audit.verify` self-audit event (`allowed`/`denied`). New `AuditEventKind::AuditVerified`. Detection tests prove content-edit, row-deletion, checkpoint-forgery, and stored-`row_hash` tamper are all caught. Checkpoint-boundary-aware `audit:prune` follows in WP4.
- **Audit tamper-evidence — checkpoint-aware `audit:prune` (`L1-audit.md` M2, WP4 of 4, completes v1).** Retention pruning no longer looks like tampering. `audit_checkpoint` gains a `pruned` flag. `audit:prune` now prunes **sealed** rows only at whole checkpoint-segment boundaries (deletes `id <= horizon` where the horizon segment is entirely older than the cutoff, and marks those checkpoints `pruned=1`; `--kind` does not apply to sealed rows — whole-segment deletion preserves the chain), while **unsealed-tail** rows keep the legacy `created_at`(+`--kind`) deletion. The self-audit records `sealed_pruned_through_id` / `pruned_checkpoint_hash` / `unsealed_deleted_count`. `audit:verify` treats a `pruned=1` checkpoint as a valid anchor (still verifies its chain link + recomputes its `checkpoint_hash` — forgery is caught — but skips row checks and advances the chain from the retained `segment_hash`). A sealed row deleted **without** the `pruned` flag still fails verification, distinguishing a sanctioned prune from a malicious gap. Remaining hardening (external-sink cross-check; fail-open write-path marker+metric) is tracked separately.

### Changed

- **The lefthook `pre-push` `spec-drift` check is now advisory (does not block the push), so it can no longer train contributors into `--no-verify`-ing the real gates.** `tools/drift-detector.sh` is a docs↔spec coupling heuristic — it is *not* part of `composer verify` or CI, and it legitimately flags every bugfix that uses a justified `spec-reviewed:` ack. As a *blocking* pre-push command it failed the push for those acks, and because git's `--no-verify` skips the **entire** pre-push hook, contributors who bypassed the spurious drift failure also bypassed the genuine gates running alongside it (`composer-policy`, `phpstan`, `phpunit`) — defeating those gates. The `spec-drift` command now runs the detector for visibility, prints an advisory banner when it reports affected specs, and always `exit 0`; the real gates remain hard pre-push blockers. Drift remains available on demand via `bash tools/drift-detector.sh` and can be promoted to a dedicated non-blocking CI annotation later if hard enforcement is wanted. Config-only change to `lefthook.yml`; no application code touched.

- **`waaseyaa/cache` docs now describe the package that actually ships, and the two unused PSR cache deps are dropped (Remediation Wave 4 W4-5 / `L0-cache.md` m4, m5 — behavior-neutral).** The cache `README.md` previously advertised a `CacheInterface`, a `FilesystemBackend`, and "atomic file writes (write-to-temp-then-rename)" — none of which exist; the real surface is `CacheBackendInterface` with `MemoryBackend` / `DatabaseBackend` / `NullBackend`. The README is rewritten to match the shipped package (backends, `CacheFactory`/`CacheConfiguration` per-bin resolution, `CacheTagsInvalidator` + the lifecycle listeners, `CacheConfigResolver`, contexts), explicitly noting there is no filesystem backend. The framework `CLAUDE.md` "corrupt file … recovery paths" testing note — which the missing `FilesystemBackend` had made misleading — now scopes the PHP-file recovery pattern to the `PackageManifestCompiler` manifest cache + config file caches, and points at `DatabaseBackendTest` for the `DatabaseBackend`'s BLOB corrupt-row recovery (added in W4-4). Finally `psr/cache` and `psr/simple-cache` are removed from `packages/cache/composer.json` `require` — both were declared but never `use`d (no adapter was ever implemented); they remain available transitively to other packages, so nothing is removed from the installed set (`composer install` is a no-op) and the change is behavior-neutral.

### Added

- **Audit write-path fail-open hardening: observable marker + metric seam (WP4 of the tamper-evidence batch, #1792, design §10.4).** Prior to this change, any `audit_event` INSERT failure was silently discarded after a single `warning` log line — an undetectable gap in the tamper-evidence hash chain. This PR closes that gap without hard fail-closed behaviour. `AuditEventWriter` now (a) calls a new L1 observer seam `AuditWriteFailureObserver` (`Waaseyaa\Audit\Contract\AuditWriteFailureObserver`, `@api`) so operators can bind a real Prometheus/Telescope counter at L6 — the default `NullAuditWriteFailureObserver` preserves zero-config behaviour; (b) attempts one best-effort marker INSERT of kind `AuditEventKind::AuditWriteDegraded` (`audit.write_degraded`) carrying `dropped_kind`, `error_class`, and `error_message` in attributes — turning a silent gap into an attested degraded window visible to `audit:verify` and operator dashboards; (c) guards the marker write in its own try-catch (if the marker ALSO fails, just log — no recursion, no re-entry, maximum 2 INSERT attempts). The raw INSERT is factored into a private `insertRow()` helper shared by the normal and marker paths. The originating request is never 500'd (FR-005/NFR-001 best-effort contract preserved). `AuditEventKind::AuditWriteDegraded` is a new additive enum case (21st case). The spec (`docs/specs/ocap-audit-log.md`) and public surface map are updated; `AuditWriteFailureObserver` is listed as a public extension point. TDD: tests were written and run RED before the implementation; green after. W2-3 follow-up: wire a real Prometheus observer in the Telescope package.

### Fixed

- **`UmamiClient::send()` was untestable and a silent fail-open — `file_get_contents` was hard-wired with a suppressed `@` and falsy returns were discarded without a trace (analytics M1+M2).** A new `Transport` interface (`Waaseyaa\Analytics\Transport`, `@api`) replaces the inline stream call: `StreamTransport` moves the exact `stream_context_create` + `file_get_contents` behaviour behind the seam (the `@` is dropped — PHP errors surface as a `false` return). `UmamiClient` gains two optional trailing constructor parameters: `?Transport $transport = null` (defaults to `new StreamTransport()`; existing three-arg callers are unaffected) and `?\Closure $logger = null` (a zero-dependency local logger sink; invoked as `($logger)(string $message, array $context = [])` on both the misconfig early-return path and the failed-send / transport-exception path; null default preserves the previous silent behaviour). `send()` remains `void` and never throws — transport exceptions are caught, logged via the sink, and swallowed. No new `require` entries in `packages/analytics/composer.json` — the package stays php-only with zero waaseyaa dependencies. This WP also closes the framework's last zero-test package: `UmamiClientTest` covers payload shape + Umami contract fields, hostname extraction (URL with path + bare-host fallback), the misconfig no-op paths (empty `trackerUrl`/`siteId` → transport not called + logger called), and the failure paths (transport returns false / transport throws → logger called + `send()` does not throw). TDD: tests were written and run RED before the implementation.
- **The legacy `SchemaBuilder::drop()` and `dropIfExists()` methods now quote the table identifier, fixing a syntax error on reserved-word table names and matching the v2 compiler standard (foundation migration m1).** Both methods interpolated the (possibly prefixed) table name directly into `DROP TABLE <name>` DDL with no identifier quoting — while `create()` already routes identifiers through DBAL's `Schema::toSql($platform)` auto-quoting. A table named with a SQL reserved word (e.g. `order`) produced invalid DDL that was a syntax error on every driver. Both methods now call `$this->connection->getDatabasePlatform()->quoteIdentifier($this->tablePrefix . $table)` to quote the full prefixed name before interpolation, matching the v2 standard. Acceptance: `SchemaBuilderTest::drop_reserved_word_table_without_syntax_error` and `drop_if_exists_reserved_word_table_without_syntax_error` — each creates a table named `order`, drops it, and asserts no exception (both proven to FAIL on the unquoted DDL and pass after the fix).
- **`SqlState::set()` no longer throws on concurrent new-key writes — the non-atomic UPDATE-then-INSERT race is closed (state m2).** `set()` used an UPDATE-first / INSERT-if-zero-rows pattern with no transaction or atomic upsert: two concurrent writers on a NEW key both received 0 rows affected from the UPDATE and both attempted INSERT; the second INSERT hit the PRIMARY KEY constraint on `name` and threw `UniqueConstraintViolationException`, corrupting the logical "set" semantics. The fix retains the UPDATE-first fast path for existing keys and wraps the INSERT in a `catch (UniqueConstraintViolationException)` block that re-applies the value via a second UPDATE, making `set()` last-writer-wins and safe under concurrency on all supported drivers (SQLite, MySQL, PostgreSQL) without dialect branching. The pattern follows the precedent established by `RevisionableStorageDriver`. `SqlStateTest::testSetDoesNotThrowOnConcurrentNewKeyRace` pins the regression: a spy `DatabaseInterface` decorator intercepts the first `update('state')` call, inserts the competitor row out-of-band, and returns 0 — reproducing the exact interleaving that caused the collision — proven to throw on the pre-fix code and to complete with correct last-writer-wins semantics on the fix.

- **The scheduler overlap lock is now ownership-scoped, closing a split-brain double-run window (scheduler m15 — also closes production-audit m15).** `DatabaseLock::release()` deleted the lock row by `task_name` alone, with no ownership check. Under task overrun the lease TTL could expire mid-run, a second node's `acquire()` cleanup removed the stale row and started the same task, and then the first node's `release()` deleted the **second** node's *live* lock — letting a third node acquire and run the task concurrently. Acquire was already atomic (PK on `task_name` + INSERT-catch-duplicate); the single hole was the unscoped release. Now `acquire()` mints a random owner token (`bin2hex(random_bytes(16))`), persists it in a new `waaseyaa_schedule_locks.locked_by` column (additive, idempotent migration guarded by `SchemaBuilder::hasColumn`), and returns it; `release(name, token)` is scoped to `task_name = ? AND locked_by = ?`, so a stale node whose lease was already reclaimed deletes nothing instead of tearing down the new owner's lock. `InMemoryLock` mirrors the ownership model for parity. Paired with **m2**: the lock TTL is now a per-`ScheduledTask` property (`$task->lockTtl`, default 300s) threaded through `ScheduleRunner` instead of a hardcoded `300`, so long-running tasks can set a TTL above their runtime and avoid the mid-run expiry that opens the window in the first place. Out of scope (separate follow-ups): m3/m4 (clock injection), n5/n6. Acceptance: the previously-absent `DatabaseLockTest` reproduces the steal (node A acquires, lease expires, node B reclaims, then A's `release()` does **not** delete B's live lock — proven to fail on the un-scoped release) and asserts a normal owner release deletes its own row; `InMemoryLockTest` covers the parity path.

- **A crashed worker's queue job is now reclaimed via a lease/visibility timeout instead of being lost forever (queue M1, data-loss fix).** `DbalTransport::pop()` claimed a job by setting `reserved_at = now` but nothing ever reclaimed a job whose worker died (SIGKILL / OOM / reboot) between the claim and ack/release — `pop()` only selected `reserved_at IS NULL`, so that row stayed `reserved` permanently: never retried, never recorded as failed (the docblock advertised atomic claim but was silent on the lost-job side). A claim is now a **lease** that expires after a configurable `visibilityTimeout` (constructor arg, default `90s`, configurable via `queue.visibility_timeout`). `pop()` reclaims rows that are either unreserved **or** past their lease deadline — folded into one race-safe condition `COALESCE(reserved_at, 0) <= now - visibilityTimeout` — keeping the claim atomic (fresh claims guarded by `reserved_at IS NULL`; reclaims guarded by the exact prior `reserved_at` so a concurrent reclaimer loses the race) and **bumping `attempts` on reclaim**. `Worker::processJob()` gains a pre-handler safety net: when `attempts >= maxTries` (`Job::$tries` or `WorkerOptions::$maxTries`; `0` = unlimited) the job is recorded failed (+ `Job::failed()`) and rejected instead of run again, so an always-crashing job lands in the failed-job repository like any other exhausted job rather than reclaiming forever. The fix re-uses the existing `reserved_at` column — **no schema change / migration**. (`InMemoryTransport` is single-process and dies with its queue, so it is unchanged.) Out of scope and untouched: queue M2 (`AttributeGuard` no-op) and M3 (claim retry budget). Acceptance: `DbalTransportTest` reclaim cases (expired lease re-popped + attempts incremented, live lease not reclaimed, monotonic increment per cycle — each proven to fail on the pre-fix `pop`) and `CrashRecoveryTest` (a reclaimed job with remaining budget is processed not lost; a repeatedly-abandoned job is recorded failed after its budget is exhausted and is genuinely gone, not reclaimed forever).

- **Declared 18 previously-undeclared same/lower-layer dependencies across 6 L6 interface packages — PL007 baseline batch 4 of 4; the baseline is now empty (issue #1806 closed).** Declared with `^<current-tag>`: `cli`→`ai-vector`,`database-legacy`,`field`,`foundation`,`oidc`,`plugin`,`routing`,`search`,`workflows`; `debug`→`entity`,`routing`; `inertia`→`entity`; `mcp`→`foundation`; `ssr`→`database-legacy`,`i18n`,`path`,`relationship`; `telescope`→`foundation`. With batches 1–3 this resolves **all 48** undeclared-dependency edges the W4-3 sweep found across 25 packages — every first-party package now declares the `waaseyaa/*` siblings it imports, so installing any one package off the monorepo no longer hits a fatal missing transitive dependency. `tools/package-layers-undeclared-baseline.txt` is now empty; **PL007 is a strict zero-tolerance gate** (any new undeclared same/lower-layer use-edge fails CI). Across all four batches: every edge was same-or-lower layer (no layer inversion) and **no new same-layer cycles** were introduced. Dependency-metadata only — no runtime behavior change.

- **Declared 8 previously-undeclared same/lower-layer dependencies across 6 L3/L4/L5 packages — PL007 baseline batch 3 of 4 (issue #1806).** Declared with `^<current-tag>`: `workflows`→`foundation`; `routing`→`foundation`,`user`; `wayfinding`→`access`; `ai-agent`→`config`,`routing`; `ai-pipeline`→`foundation`; `ai-vector`→`foundation`. All same-or-lower layer; no new same-layer cycles. Baseline lines removed; PL007 stays armed. Dependency-metadata only — no runtime behavior change.

- **Declared 12 previously-undeclared same/lower-layer dependencies across 7 L2 content-type packages — PL007 baseline batch 2 of 4 (issue #1806).** Same latent missing-transitive-dependency class as batch 1, now declared with `^<current-tag>`: `media`→`access`,`audit`,`database-legacy`; `menu`→`access`,`foundation`; `node`→`foundation`; `note`→`foundation`; `path`→`access`,`foundation`; `relationship`→`foundation`; `taxonomy`→`field`,`foundation`. All same-or-lower layer; no new same-layer cycles. Baseline lines removed; PL007 stays armed. Dependency-metadata only — no runtime behavior change.

- **Declared 10 previously-undeclared same/lower-layer dependencies across 6 L0/L1 packages — PL007 baseline batch 1 of 4 (issue #1806).** Each package imported a `waaseyaa/*` sibling from `src/` that it did not list in `composer.json` `require` (it resolved only transitively), so installing the package alone off the monorepo would hit a fatal missing dependency. Now declared with the canonical `^<current-tag>` constraint: `mercure`→`foundation`; `audit`→`scheduler`; `auth`→`access`,`database-legacy`,`entity`; `config`→`foundation`; `oidc`→`database-legacy`,`entity`,`entity-storage`; `testing`→`field`. All are same-or-lower layer (no layer inversion) and introduce **no** new same-layer cycles. The corresponding lines are removed from `tools/package-layers-undeclared-baseline.txt`; PL007 stays armed (fail-on-new). Pure dependency-metadata change — no runtime behavior change.

- **The package-layer gate now catches undeclared same-or-lower-layer `use` edges, closing the blind spot that hid `cache`'s upward imports (Remediation Wave 4 W4-3 / `L0-cache.md` M3).** `bin/check-package-layers` already rejected upward file-level `use` edges (PL005) but never checked that a *legal* same-or-lower-layer `use Waaseyaa\X\…` import is actually declared in the importing package's `composer.json` `require` — so a consumer installing a single package off the monorepo hit a fatal missing dependency for every undeclared, transitively-resolved import. A new **PL007** check flags these, fail-on-new with a committed baseline (`tools/package-layers-undeclared-baseline.txt`, regenerate with `--generate-baseline`), exactly like the dead-code / getquery-bindings gates. The one-time sweep surfaced **48 existing undeclared edges across 25 packages** (all pre-existing) — tracked to zero in #1806; none are fixed in this WP per scope. `cache` itself no longer appears as a violation: its four upward-importing files (three event-decoupled cache-invalidation listeners + `CacheConfigResolver`) stay on the constitution-blessed `kernelExemptFiles` allowlist, now with per-entry rationale comments (CLAUDE.md claimed these existed; they now do); relocating `CacheConfigResolver`'s direct `AccountInterface` coupling out of L0 is filed as #1807. The gate's scan root and baseline path are env-overridable (`WAASEYAA_LAYER_ROOT` / `WAASEYAA_LAYER_UNDECLARED_BASELINE`) so the new behavior is provable: `CheckPackageLayersGateTest` builds throwaway fixture packages and asserts PL007 fires on an undeclared edge, PL005 on an upward edge, the baseline suppresses a known edge, and declaring the dep clears it.

- **Cache tag invalidation no longer over-matches on LIKE metacharacters (Remediation Wave 4 W4-2 / `L0-cache.md` M2).** `DatabaseBackend::invalidateByTags()` stores tags comma-joined in one TEXT column and matched them with `LIKE` patterns that escaped neither `%` nor `_` and carried no `ESCAPE` clause. Because `_` and `%` are LIKE wildcards, invalidating a real tag like `node_list` over-matched any key whose blob contained `nodeXlist,…` (the `_` matched the `X`), silently evicting unrelated cache entries — a silent cache-coherence bug in the exact mechanism every entity/config/translation save funnels through, with zero collision-test coverage. The three positional `LIKE` arms now escape the backslash first, then `%`/`_`, and each carries an explicit `ESCAPE '\'` clause (required because SQLite's default `LIKE` has no escape char); the equality arm keeps binding the raw tag. Matching is now exact. Acceptance: `DatabaseBackendTest` gains underscore- and percent-collision regression tests (each proven to FAIL on the un-escaped code and pass on the fix) plus a start/middle/end multi-tag-blob positivity test; the existing shared-tag tests still pass. (The comma-blob+LIKE design is kept — a junction table is the noted-better follow-up, out of scope for this stopgap-correct fix.)

- **Retention scan scale, part 2 — the classification retention jobs now scan in bounded keyset batches with SQL label/age pushdown (`L1-field.md` M1, WP2 of 2).** `PurgeJob`/`RedactJob`/`HoldScanJob` ran on a 6-hour cron and `loadMultiple()`-ed **every** classification-labelled row of **every** entity type into memory, then matched labels in PHP — an unbounded full-table hydration that would OOM a large site and, because it runs unattended, silently take retention down. All three now iterate through a new `RetentionScanner` that pages by **ascending id with `id > lastId`** (keyset — robust under `PurgeJob`'s in-loop deletes, which offset paging would skip) and never hydrates more than `batchSize` (default 500) rows per round-trip. It pushes the age cutoff and, when a policy reduces to a single `applies_to` pattern, the label predicate into SQL (literal → `=`, `prefix*` → `STARTS_WITH`); the pushdown is a *sound narrowing* only — each job's `matchesLabel()`, the `PurgeJob` legal-hold `hold-*` guard, exemptions, PII discovery, and the prune-is-itself-audited behaviour are **unchanged**, so the same rows are acted on as before, only with O(batch) peak memory instead of O(all-rows). Acceptance: `RetentionScanParityTest` (identical purge/redact/conflict set across a multi-batch fixture incl. the hold guard, exemptions, and a multi-pattern policy) and `RetentionScannerTest` (exactly `ceil(n/batch)` `loadMultiple` calls of ≤ batchSize each — bounded hydration). Together with WP1's index this clears the one production-scale defect in the field-classification craftsmanship audit.

- **Retention scan scale, part 1 — the `classification_label` column is now indexed (`L1-field.md` M1, WP1 of 2).** The classification retention cron jobs (`PurgeJob`/`RedactJob`/`HoldScanJob`) filter entities by `classification_label`, but that column was unindexed (its field type declared an `index` hint that nothing in the schema builder consumes), so every label-filtered query was a full table scan. An additive, idempotent migration (`2026_05_25_000005_index_classification_label`) creates `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS` on `classification_label` for every table that has the column. It covers tables present when it runs (not entity tables created later by runtime schema sync — a systemic fix in the schema builder is a noted follow-up). The bounded-iteration scan that uses this index follows in WP2. Acceptance: `ClassificationLabelIndexMigrationTest` — the migration indexes the column where present, skips tables without it, and is idempotent.

- **Craftsmanship cleanup batch — dedup, DI, and stale-doc fixes across field/config/access/user/audit (Wave 3 W3-7, behavior-neutral).** A batch of low-risk tidy-ups, each verified to leave behavior unchanged: `FieldDefinition::legacyJsonSchema()` now delegates to `AbstractFieldType::jsonSchemaFor($this)` instead of duplicating the ~25-line `match` (no more drift between two copies); the three `FieldDefinition` withers (`storedIn`/`indexed`/`translatable`) collapse through a private `with()` helper (a new constructor param threads through one place, not three); `EntityLifecycleSubscriber` and `AuditEventWriter` default their logger at construction (`$logger ?? new NullLogger()`) instead of newing a `NullLogger` in the catch path; `EntityAccessHandler` uses `GateInterface::{VIEW,UPDATE,DELETE}` + a local `OPERATION_TRANSLATE` constant instead of bare operation literals; `AuthMailer` URL-encodes the reset/verify tokens in the email URLs (defensive); and several stale docs are corrected (field README `FieldTypeRegistry`→`FieldTypeManager`, `FieldTemplate::$pii` now documents its live `RedactJob::discoverPiiFields()` consumer, the `ConfigSyncRepository` `fsync`/`fflush` comment no longer over-claims durability, and the `ConfigImporter` dry-run comment drops the stale "WP05 owns the stub" framing). **Intentionally deferred** (would change behavior — out of scope for a behavior-neutral batch): converging the `RevisionPolicyComposition` `translate→edit` vs `EntityAccessHandler` `translate→update` fallback (ADR-017/FR-022-pinned and test-guarded), and wiring `ConfigDiffer` into the importer's dry-run path for per-entry diff status (a constructor-signature change).

- **The `audit_event` schema now has a single source of truth — the standalone migration delegates to the runtime schema handler (Wave 3 W3-6 / `L1-audit.md` m1).** Two definitions of `audit_event` had diverged: `AuditEventSchemaHandler::ensureSchema()` (called by `AuditServiceProvider::boot()`) creates it with `actor_uid`, `row_hash`, `prev_hash` (plus the retention + checkpoint tables and genesis anchor), while the migration `2026_05_25_000001_create_audit_event_table.php` hand-wrote a stale `CREATE TABLE` that **lacked `actor_uid`** (and the tamper-evidence chain columns). Since `AuditEventWriter` always inserts `actor_uid`, a standalone `migrate` (not the boot path) produced a schema the writer's INSERT failed against — and the fail-open writer then silently swallowed it. The migration now **delegates to `AuditEventSchemaHandler::ensureSchema()`**, so `migrate` and `boot()` produce byte-identical schema and can never drift again. Acceptance: `AuditEventMigrationSchemaTest` runs the migration against a fresh DB and asserts `audit_event` has `actor_uid`/`row_hash`/`prev_hash` and that `audit_checkpoint` was created (full delegation).

- **`User` identity predicate hardened, and the `email_verified` setter no longer drifts from its cast (Wave 3 W3-5 / `L1-user.md` m1, m2).** Two corrections: (1) `User::isAuthenticated()` was `id() !== 0`, but `id()` coerces a null uid to `0`, so a freshly-constructed *unsaved* user reported the same signal as the anonymous user. It is now `!$this->isNew() && $this->id() > 0` — authenticated only for a persisted record with a real uid. (2) `setEmailVerified()` stored `int` `0`/`1` into a field that carries a `'bool'` cast and a `bool` property; it now stores a native `bool`, so cast + property + setter agree (reads are unchanged — the cast already normalized them). **`status` is intentionally left as-is:** the framework's `FieldTypeInferrer` enforces that its `bool` property matches its `#[Field(type: 'boolean')]` (so the type layer is already consistent), and `status` has no cast — `get('status')` returns the stored integer `0`/`1` by deliberate, documented, validator-compatible convention (accessed via `isActive()`), so coercing it to a bool would change a public data representation for no correctness gain. Acceptance: `UserTest` — unsaved/uid-0 users are not authenticated, a uid-bearing user is; `email_verified` round-trips a native bool; `status` stays int `0`/`1`. User + auth + entity-storage round-trip + OIDC userinfo suites green.

- **`DatabaseRateLimiter` is now cross-driver portable and increments atomically (Wave 3 W3-4 / `L1-auth.md` m3).** The live brute-force throttle hand-wrote `CREATE TABLE rate_limits (key TEXT PRIMARY KEY, count INTEGER ...)` — `key` (MySQL reserved word) and `count` (function name) are emitted unquoted by the DBAL DDL/query paths and break on non-SQLite drivers, where the throttle then throws. It also used a read-modify-write increment (fetch row, write `count+1`) that loses increments under concurrency (undercount). Fixed: the columns are renamed to non-reserved `bucket_key` / `hits` (+ `reset_at`); the table is provisioned through the portable schema builder (TOCTOU-safe, mirroring `AuthTokenRepository`); and `hit()` now does an idempotent ensure-row + an **atomic** `UPDATE … SET hits = hits + 1` (raw quoted-identifier statement), so concurrent hits never undercount. Public API unchanged. Acceptance: `DatabaseRateLimiterTest` — exact count after N hits, and a schema-introspection guard asserting the columns are `bucket_key`/`hits`/`reset_at` (no `key`/`count`). (A live second-driver run needs a MySQL/Postgres service not present in unit CI; portability is guaranteed structurally by reserved-word removal + schema-builder DDL + quoted identifiers.)

- **Admin SPA dist freshness is now enforced in blocking CI, and the stale committed bundle was rebuilt (charter §3.2-4).** Seven dependabot bumps to `packages/admin/package.json` / `package-lock.json` (#1617–#1673) advanced the admin SPA's build inputs without rebuilding the committed `packages/admin-surface/dist` bundle or its content signature, so `check-admin-dist-fresh` (D6) was RED on `main` and `composer verify` failed — but the gate ran only in `composer verify` and an out-of-band `admin-dist.yml` fix-up workflow, never in the blocking CI job set, so the drift was invisible on PRs. The dist + `packages/admin-surface/dist.signature` are regenerated via the canonical `bin/build-admin-dist`, and `check-admin-dist-fresh` is now wired into the blocking `ci/verify-gates` job alongside the other `composer verify` gates, so a stale admin dist fails the PR instead of silently drifting. This restores `composer verify` to green on `main` and makes the §3.2-4 beta criterion ("CI enforcement live & green; composer verify passes on main") CI-enforced.

- **Revision timestamps are now written through the injected clock seam (UTC, deterministic) instead of server-local `date()` (audit `L1-entity-storage.md` m4).** `RevisionableStorageDriver::writeDefaultRevision()` / `writePerLangcodeRevision()` stamped `revision_created` with `date('Y-m-d H:i:s')`, which used the server's local timezone and was not controllable in tests — the same revision could record a different wall-clock value on differently-configured hosts, and the value was not clock-testable. The driver now takes an optional `Waaseyaa\Entity\DateTime\EntityClockInterface` (defaulting to `UtcEntityClock`, matching the base-table `SqlEntityStorage` path), so `revision_created` is always UTC and a fixed clock yields a deterministic value. The stored string format (`Y-m-d H:i:s`) is unchanged; the only behavioural change is UTC instead of server-local. The kernel construction site (`AbstractKernel`) is unaffected — the new parameter defaults to the same UTC clock. Acceptance: `RevisionableStorageDriverClockTest::write_revision_uses_injected_clock_for_revision_created`.

### Security

- **`SafeMarkup` over-promised XSS protection — its docblock claimed real XSS defence, `allowedTags` was never enforced, and a non-base64 `data:text/html` URI bypassed the data: blocklist (validation M1+m1).** `SafeMarkupValidator` is a regex blocklist, not a real HTML sanitizer, but its class-level docblock on both `SafeMarkup` and `SafeMarkupValidator` stated "validates that text does not contain dangerous HTML or scripts" and "Detects … common XSS vectors" — security promises that invited over-reliance and obscured the real risk level. Two compounding issues: (1) the `data:` URI pattern only matched `;base64`, so `data:text/html,<b>attacker-controlled-content</b>` (no `;base64`, no literal `<script>` tag) passed the validator — a browser navigates to a `data:text/html` URL and renders full attacker-controlled HTML, enabling phishing / stored XSS without triggering any other blocklist pattern; (2) `SafeMarkup::$allowedTags` is public API consumed by `ConstraintFactory::safeMarkup()` and documented as the list of permitted tags, but `SafeMarkupValidator` never reads it — a caller passing `allowedTags: ['p', 'strong']` gets no enforcement whatsoever. **Remedy (Option A — honest redocument + tightened data: rule):** docblocks on both classes are rewritten to describe `SafeMarkupValidator` accurately as a **coarse, bypassable regex pre-filter / defence-in-depth blocklist** that is NOT a substitute for a real sanitizer; callers needing real sanitization of untrusted HTML are pointed at `Waaseyaa\Migration\Plugin\Process\HtmlSanitizeProcessor` (the framework's DOM-based allowlist sanitizer, optionally backed by `ezyang/htmlpurifier`). The `allowedTags` option is explicitly documented as **not currently enforced** (retained as public API, reserved for future DOM-based allowlist enforcement — removing it would be a BC break). The `data:` blocklist pattern is broadened to block `data:text/html`, `data:application/javascript`, and `data:image/svg+xml` regardless of `;base64`, closing the non-base64 bypass. `allowedTags` is NOT removed — the `ConstraintFactory::safeMarkup()` API and its tests are unchanged. Acceptance: `SafeMarkupValidatorTest::testNonBase64DataTextHtmlUriTriggersViolation` (the `data:text/html` bypass payload — no `;base64`, no literal `<script>` — verified to pass the pre-fix validator / FAIL before the rule was broadened); `SafeMarkupValidatorTest::testAllowedTagsIsNotEnforcedByValidator` (documents and asserts that a tag absent from `allowedTags` is NOT stripped — making code + doc + test agree on the unenforced reality). All existing `SafeMarkupValidatorTest` cases remain green.
- **`GoogleOAuthProvider::getUserProfile` now fails loudly on a non-OK userinfo response instead of building a degenerate empty-id identity (oauth-provider Mn).** The method called `$response->json()` unconditionally and passed the result straight to `new OAuthUserProfile(...)`, so a 401/403/5xx error body — which carries no `id` or `email` — was coerced into a profile with an empty `providerId`, a fail-open that could produce or match an account with a blank identifier. Mirroring the already-hardened `GitHubOAuthProvider`: after the userinfo GET, `getUserProfile` now checks `$response->isSuccess()` and throws `\RuntimeException` (with the provider's `error_description`/`error` message, or a generic fallback) before touching the body; it then asserts that `$data['id']` is non-empty before constructing the profile, throwing if the subject id is absent. The fix follows the same shape and idioms as the GitHub provider. Acceptance: `GoogleOAuthProviderTest::testGetUserProfileThrowsOnErrorResponseInsteadOfDegenerateIdentity` (401 error body → throws, proven to fail on pre-fix code which builds the degenerate profile) and `testGetUserProfileThrowsWhenIdIsEmpty` (200 OK but missing id → throws); the existing `testGetUserProfile` success path is unchanged.
- **`Envelope` now rejects CR, LF, and NUL in header fields at construction, closing the email header-injection and log-injection vectors (mail M1+m1).** The mail package performed no input validation on the `from`, `subject`, `to` recipients, or custom header names/values: a raw `\r\n` in any of those fields is the classic header-injection vector (a hostile subject like `Subject\r\nBcc: evil@example.com` injects a Bcc header into the outgoing message) and, because `LocalTransport` logs the envelope verbatim, is also a log-injection vector. Defence was absent at the data boundary and relied entirely on the SendGrid SDK's own encoding — a transport-specific lucky accident. A private `assertNoCrlf(string $field, string $value)` helper now runs in `Envelope::__construct()` for every non-body field (`from`, `subject`, each entry in `to`, and each header name and value), throwing `\InvalidArgumentException` naming the offending field without echoing the raw payload (to avoid re-injecting into logs). `textBody`/`htmlBody` are intentionally exempt (body content legitimately contains newlines). Acceptance: `EnvelopeTest` proves the injection payload `"Subject\r\nBcc: evil@example.com"` and bare `\r`, `\n`, and `\0` in subject/from/to/header-name/header-value all throw, while a multi-line body and a fully valid Envelope construct without error (each CRLF case verified to FAIL before the guard was added).
- **`BodySizeLimitMiddleware` now enforces the body-size cap against the actual read, closing the chunked / no-Content-Length DoS bypass (foundation middleware Mn-1).** The middleware previously rejected requests by checking the client-supplied `Content-Length` header only. A request sent with `Transfer-Encoding: chunked` (no `Content-Length`), or with an absent or understated `Content-Length`, streamed an unbounded body straight past the guard — a memory-exhaustion / DoS vector. Two layers now defend it: (1) the existing **fast-path** — reject before reading the body when the declared `Content-Length` already exceeds the cap (cheap, no read); (2) a new **backstop actual-read guard** — after the fast-path, `strlen($request->getContent())` is measured and returns 413 if the real body exceeds the cap, regardless of what `Content-Length` declares. The 413 JSON:API response shape (`errors[0].status=413`, `title=Payload Too Large`) is unchanged; the 413 construction is extracted to a `payloadTooLargeResponse()` private helper to avoid duplication. Acceptance: `BodySizeLimitMiddlewareTest` gains two regression tests each proven to FAIL on the pre-fix code — `returns_413_when_no_content_length_but_oversized_body` (the chunked bypass: no `Content-Length` header, oversized actual body → 413) and `returns_413_when_content_length_understated_but_actual_body_oversized` (lying header: `Content-Length` within cap, actual body exceeds cap → 413). The existing fast-path and pass-through tests stay green.
- **`CompressionMiddleware` no longer clobbers a pre-existing `Vary` header, closing a cross-user cache-poisoning window (foundation middleware Mn-2).** `CompressionMiddleware` unconditionally called `headers->set('Vary', 'Accept-Encoding')`, which overwrote any `Vary` value a controller or upstream middleware had already set (e.g. `Vary: Cookie` or `Vary: Authorization`). A shared or reverse-proxy cache keyed on the now-truncated `Vary` field could then serve one user's authenticated, compressed response body to a different user — cross-user data exposure. The fix replaces `set()` with an append-and-dedupe helper (`appendVaryToken()`): if no `Vary` exists, it sets `Accept-Encoding`; if the existing value is `*` (varies on everything) it leaves it unchanged; if `Accept-Encoding` is already present in any case (`accept-encoding`, `ACCEPT-ENCODING`, …) it is not duplicated; otherwise `, Accept-Encoding` is appended, preserving the original tokens verbatim in order. Acceptance: four regression tests — `preserves_pre_existing_vary_and_appends_accept_encoding` (a pre-existing `Vary: Cookie` survives and becomes `Cookie, Accept-Encoding`; each proven to FAIL on the clobbering `set()` before the fix), `sets_vary_accept_encoding_when_no_pre_existing_vary` (unchanged behaviour), `does_not_duplicate_accept_encoding_when_already_present_case_insensitively` (a lowercase `accept-encoding` is not doubled), and `leaves_vary_wildcard_unchanged` (`*` is left as-is).
- **The Mercure publisher token is now scoped to the published topic instead of granting publish on every topic, and the hub channel is forced to TLS (mercure Mn-1 + mn-2).** `MercurePublisher::generateJwt()` hardcoded `'mercure' => ['publish' => ['*']]` into every minted token, so any holder of the 1-hour bearer token could publish to **every** topic on the hub — even though `publish(string $topic, …)` already knows the exact topic. **Mn-1:** the topic is now threaded into the JWT builder and the claim is scoped to `['publish' => [$topic]]` (least privilege; no wildcard, no config flag — `publish()` is single-topic), so a leaked token can only publish to the one topic it was minted for. **mn-2:** the hub POST now sets `CURLOPT_SSL_VERIFYPEER => true` / `CURLOPT_SSL_VERIFYHOST => 2` explicitly (never trusting an ambient `php.ini` that disabled them), and `isConfigured()` now requires an `https://` hub URL — a non-https hub is treated as **unconfigured** so `publish()` returns `false` without ever minting or sending a token over cleartext (preserving the best-effort, never-throw convention rather than crashing the singleton). Acceptance: the previously-untested minter is pinned — `MercurePublisherTest` proves the claim equals `[$topic]` (not `['*']`) for two distinct topics, the minted signature verifies against the configured secret (and fails under a wrong one), and an `http://`/`ftp://`/scheme-less hub is not configured.
- **The i18n `Translator` now validates the locale before `require`-ing a translation file, closing a path-traversal → code-execution vector (i18n M1).** `Translator::loadTranslations()` built `$translationsPath . '/' . $locale . '.php'` and `require`d it with the locale **never validated**. The public `trans(..., ?string $locale = null)` passes a caller-supplied locale straight through, and the fallback chain derives further langcodes by string-splitting — so the moment a locale originates from an external negotiator (Accept-Language / URL prefix, the README's stated intent), a value like `../../etc/passwd` reaches `require` and **executes** an arbitrary PHP file (not mere disclosure). It was only caller-trusted because the advertised `AcceptHeaderNegotiator`/`UrlPrefixNegotiator` don't exist yet; this closes it before any external negotiator is wired. The fix guards the single chokepoint every langcode passes through (caller-supplied **and** fallback-derived): the locale must match `^[A-Za-z0-9-]+$` (BCP-47-safe, anchored with `/D` so a trailing newline can't smuggle past) before the path is built; a rejected locale is treated as a **missing translation** (the fallback chain continues to a legitimate language), never a fatal. As defense-in-depth, the resolved file is `realpath`-confined under `$translationsPath`, so a symlink inside the dir cannot redirect `require` outside it. Legitimate locales (`en`, `fr-CA`, `oj-x-sagamok`) still load. Out of scope: i18n m2/m3 and the README negotiator cleanup. Acceptance: `TranslatorTest` — a planted sentinel file one directory up is **never executed** via a `../` locale (proven to FAIL on the unguarded code), a `/`-bearing locale cannot address a nested file, a symlink pointing outside the dir is refused, null-byte/URL-encoded locales don't fatal, and the three legitimate BCP-47 locales still resolve.

- **The outbound `StreamHttpClient` no longer leaks credentials over redirects, and is hardened against worker-hostile responses (http-client M1 + m1/m3/m4/m5/n1).** The client set no redirect options, so PHP's HTTP stream wrapper auto-followed up to 20 redirects and **re-sent the caller's `Authorization` header to the redirect target — including a different host** (a credential-leak / SSRF-pivot; both consumers send credentials — oauth-provider bearer tokens and the SendGrid API key). **M1:** the client now sets `follow_location => 0` + `max_redirects => 0` (it returns the 3xx for the caller to handle, so credentials are **never** auto-re-sent to any redirect target — chosen approach: *disable*, not strip) and restricts URLs to `http`/`https` (rejecting `file://`, `php://`, etc.). Companions, all in the same file: **m1** pins TLS verification (`ssl.verify_peer` / `verify_peer_name => true`, `allow_self_signed => false`) instead of relying on ambient `php.ini`; **m3** bounds the connect phase (`default_socket_timeout`, set+restored around the call) so a stalled TLS handshake can't hang a worker; **m4** caps the response body (`stream_get_contents` with a 16 MiB ceiling, constructor-configurable) so a runaway endpoint can't OOM the worker; **n1** rejects CR/LF in request header names/values before serialization (also hardens mail's header path at the transport layer); **m5** captures `error_get_last()` into the thrown `HttpRequestException` (message + chained `\ErrorException` previous) so transport failures are debuggable. The value objects' public API is unchanged; m2 (0-status-on-unparseable-response) is left as a documentation choice. Acceptance: the previously absent transport tests — `StreamHttpClientTransportTest` — white-box-pin the context defaults and, via a real `php -S` harness, prove a cross-host redirect target is **never contacted** (the `Authorization` header is not re-sent; verified to FAIL when redirect-following is re-enabled), plus body-cap, CRLF-rejection, scheme-restriction, and transport-error-context cases.

- **The DBAL query builder now quotes builder-owned identifiers, closing a latent SQL-injection class on column/alias/table names (database-legacy M1+M2).** The builder bound all *values* correctly but interpolated *identifiers* (column / alias / table / join names) into SQL **unquoted** — safe only as long as every call site passes a constant identifier, and an injection the first time a caller passes a user-controlled column or sort name (also the auth reserved-word bug, generalized: a column named `key`/`count`/`order` was a syntax error). The platform's `quoteIdentifier` (cross-driver; splits a qualified `alias.column` on `.` and quotes each part, doubling embedded quotes so a metacharacter name is rendered inert) is now applied to the paths where the builder owns the identifier: `DBALSelect::fields()`/`addField()` columns + `AS` alias, `join()`/`leftJoin()` `$table` + `$alias` (the ON-`$condition` stays developer-supplied raw, documented), and the WHERE-field of `DBALUpdate`/`DBALDelete` (both the simple `Connection::update`/`delete` criteria and the `applyConditions()` QueryBuilder path). Value binding is unchanged. `DBALSelect::condition()`/`orderBy()` `$field` is **formally documented as a developer-supplied raw SQL fragment** (a column, a pre-quoted identifier, or an expression such as `SqlEntityQuery`'s `json_extract(...)`) emitted verbatim and never user input — the same contract as the join-ON clause; it is intentionally **not** auto-quoted here so the entity query engine keeps working, with the full close (auto-quote + `whereRaw()`/`orderByRaw()` seams + `SqlEntityQuery::resolveField` bare-identifier refactor) tracked in #1816. Acceptance: `IdentifierQuotingTest` proves a reserved-word column (`key`/`count`/`order`) works as an identifier and a quote/space/metacharacter identifier is rendered inert through every quoted path (each verified to FAIL on the un-quoted builder), and that `condition()`/`orderBy()` pass a pre-quoted identifier and a `json_extract(...)` fragment through unchanged (the `SqlEntityQuery` guarantee). The full `entity-storage` + `genealogy` suites stay green.

- **The database cache backend now integrity-guards its `unserialize()` path: corrupt rows are a miss, not a fatal, and an optional HMAC makes tampering detectable (Remediation Wave 4 W4-4 / `L0-cache.md` M1 / D-12).** `DatabaseBackend::mapRowToItem()` called `unserialize($row['data'])` with no corruption guard and no integrity check, so a corrupt or hostile BLOB could fatal the request or deserialize junk into a live object. Two layers now defend it without violating D-12 (cache values are `mixed` and routinely hold objects, so `allowed_classes` is intentionally **not** restricted and no `is_array` guard is added): (1) **always-on corruption guard** — the unserialize is wrapped (`try/catch \Throwable` + an `@` + a `SERIALIZED_FALSE` sentinel so a legitimately-cached `false` is not misread as failure), and any unreadable payload is returned as a **cache miss** (`false`), never fatal; (2) **optional HMAC** mirroring the audit package's `audit.checkpoint_hmac_key` — when a key is configured (`cache.hmac_key`, wired through `HttpKernel`, **off by default**), `set()` prepends a SHA-256 HMAC of the serialized payload and `get()` verifies it with `hash_equals` before unserializing; a tampered, truncated, or legacy-unsigned row fails verification and is treated as a miss (self-healing on the next `set()`). Mandatory HMAC stays deferred per D-12 — key custody is part of the same pending secrets-at-rest decision as the OIDC signing keys and audit checkpoints. Acceptance: `DatabaseBackendTest` gains the corrupt-row recovery test CLAUDE.md wrongly claimed already existed (garbage BLOB → miss, no fatal), a `false`-round-trip and object-round-trip test (proving the guard doesn't break D-12 values), and HMAC tests (signed round-trip; tampered row → miss; unsigned legacy row under a configured key → miss).

- **The plugin attribute scanner no longer crashes kernel boot on an un-loadable scanned class (Remediation Wave 4 W4-1 / `L0-plugin.md` M1 + `L1-testing.md` M1).** `AttributeDiscovery::processClass()` guarded only `new \ReflectionClass()` with `catch (\ReflectionException)`, but the crash window is `class_exists($className)` one line earlier: it triggers the autoloader, and a scanned class that `extends` a dev-only parent absent in a `composer install --no-dev` install throws a fatal `\Error` (not a `\ReflectionException`) that propagated straight out of `getDefinitions()` and crashed boot — the same failure mode as the `waaseyaa/graphql` alpha.106 outage. The whole `class_exists`→`newInstance()` region is now wrapped in `catch (\Throwable)`, so an un-loadable/bad class is skipped rather than fatal (the production manifest scanner, `PackageManifestCompiler::filterDiscoveryClasses()`, already had this guard). On the trap side, `phpunit/phpunit` moved from `require` to `require-dev` in `waaseyaa/testing` so consumers are no longer forced to install PHPUnit in production (`WaaseyaaTestCase` stays in `src/` — it is consumer-facing `@api` and relocating to `autoload-dev` would break downstream extenders; the hardened scanner survives it). Acceptance: `AttributeDiscoveryTest` proves a class whose parent fatals on autoload is skipped while a valid sibling is still discovered, and `PackageManifestCompilerTest` proves `compile()` does not fatal on such a class.

- **Logout now destroys the session instead of only unsetting the uid (Wave 3 W3-3 / `L1-auth.md` m4).** `AuthManager::logout()` previously did only `unset($_SESSION['waaseyaa_uid'])`, leaving the rest of the session data and the session id intact — asymmetric with `login()`, which calls `session_regenerate_id(true)` to defeat fixation. `logout()` now clears **all** session data (`$_SESSION = []`) and, when a session is active, rotates and destroys the underlying session (`session_regenerate_id(true)` + `session_destroy()`) so the pre-logout session id cannot be reused. Guarded on `PHP_SESSION_ACTIVE` (the bootstrap owns session start; it is inactive in CLI/tests). Acceptance: `AuthManagerTest` — `logout()` clears all session data, and a `#[RunInSeparateProcess]` test starts a real session and asserts the id is regenerated and the session destroyed.

- **The public auth surface no longer advertises a no-op throttle or a plaintext recovery-code compare (Wave 3 W3-2 / `L1-auth.md` m1, n1, n2).** Three shapes that invited misuse are corrected: (1) the in-memory `Waaseyaa\Auth\RateLimiter` — whose per-instance state resets every request under the boot-per-request runtime, so it silently does *not* throttle — was marked **`@internal`** (the live, bound limiter is `DatabaseRateLimiter`); (2) `TwoFactorManager::verifyRecoveryCode()`, a dead method (no production caller — the real flow verifies Argon2id-hashed codes via `password_verify` in `TwoFactorService`) whose plaintext `hash_equals` compare implied an insecure pattern, was **removed**; (3) `TwoFactorManager::getCurrentCode()`, a test/diagnostic helper that mints a live valid TOTP from a secret, was marked **`@internal`**. No behavioural change to the bound limiter or the 2FA flow; this narrows the public extension surface so it can't be misused. Acceptance: `packages/auth` suite green; no `verifyRecoveryCode` references remain.

- **Auth no longer falls back to the source-published `'change-me'` token secret; real deployments fail loudly when unconfigured (Wave 3 W3-1 / `L1-auth.md` m2).** `AuthServiceProvider` resolved the reset/verify-token HMAC key as `auth.token_secret ?? app_secret ?? 'change-me'` — so an app with neither configured signed and verified password-reset / email-verification tokens with a key published in the open-source tree, making those token hashes forgeable. The `'change-me'` (and empty-string) fallback is removed: in production/staging (any environment outside `local`/`dev`/`development`/`testing`) the provider now throws a clear `\RuntimeException` at boot resolution, failing loudly instead of shipping a forgeable default. In dev/test only, it synthesises an **ephemeral random** secret (never a known string) so a misconfigured non-production app still boots; that secret is per-process, so token flows that must survive a reboot still require `AUTH_TOKEN_SECRET`. Acceptance: `AuthServiceProviderTest` — missing/`change-me` secret throws in production, a configured secret resolves, and a non-production app without a secret still boots.

- **`GET /api/broadcast` no longer leaks the site-wide entity-lifecycle feed to anonymous clients (production audit F-1 / `L1-audit.md` M-1).** The SSE route carried no access option and `BroadcastRouter` honoured `?channels=admin` for any connection (stripping only `session:*`), so an anonymous client could subscribe to the `admin` channel and stream the entity type + id of every create/update/delete across the site. Two layers now gate it: (1) the `api.broadcast` route requires authentication (`_authenticated`), so anonymous callers get **401**; (2) `BroadcastRouter` enforces a per-channel ACL — privileged channels (`PRIVILEGED_CHANNELS`, currently `admin`) are stripped for any account that is not authenticated **and** an admin (holds `administer site` or the `administrator`/`admin` role), and the `admin` default is applied only for authorized accounts so a stripped request can never be re-defaulted onto it. Non-privileged public channels and per-session `session:*` isolation are unchanged. Acceptance: anon and authenticated-non-admin are denied the `admin` channel; an admin still receives it; public channels still work. Tests in `BroadcastRouterTest` / `BroadcastRouterChannelIsolationTest`.

- **The audit log's append-only guarantee can no longer be bypassed via the schema builder (`L1-audit.md` M1).** `AppendOnlyAuditDatabase::schema()` returned the inner `SchemaInterface` untouched, so `->schema()->dropTable('audit_event')` (and `dropField`/`dropIndex` on it) destroyed or altered the OCAP audit log structure — bypassing the guard that `update()`/`delete()`/`query()` already enforced (the raw `query()` arm already blocked `DROP`/`ALTER`/`TRUNCATE`, so the builder side was an inconsistency). `schema()` now returns an `AppendOnlySchema` decorator that refuses destructive DDL (`dropTable`/`dropField`/`dropIndex`) targeting append-only tables with the same `\LogicException`, while additive DDL (`addField`/`addIndex`/…) and all operations on non-append-only tables pass through so legitimate audit-table migrations and retention (`audit:prune` via the raw `DatabaseInterface`) still work. Acceptance: `AuditImmutabilityTest` — drop table/field/index on `audit_event` throw; non-audit DDL and additive audit-table DDL pass.

## [0.1.0-alpha.249] - 2026-06-25

### Removed

- **BREAKING (pre-1.0): the dead TypedData instance type-system was removed (audit C-24, train 3 of 3).** It was the ancestry of the Field-API item layer deleted in train 2 (`FieldItemBase` implemented the `ComplexData`/`TypedData` instance contracts) and had **zero production consumers** once that layer was gone. Removed published `@api` symbols: **`Waaseyaa\TypedData\TypedDataInterface`**, **`Waaseyaa\TypedData\ComplexDataInterface`**, **`Waaseyaa\TypedData\ListInterface`**, **`Waaseyaa\TypedData\PrimitiveInterface`**, **`Waaseyaa\TypedData\TypedDataManagerInterface`**, and **`Waaseyaa\TypedData\CastTokenMapper`**; plus the non-surface `Waaseyaa\TypedData\TypedDataManager`, the six `Waaseyaa\TypedData\Type\{Boolean,Float,Integer,List,Map,String}Data` value objects, and the concrete `Waaseyaa\TypedData\DataDefinition` (instantiated only by the removed `TypedDataManager`). `CastTokenMapper` had no production consumer at all — `ValueCaster` never called it (it delegates only to the kept Coercion seam); its sole reference was its own test. **Kept (live):** `Waaseyaa\TypedData\DataDefinitionInterface` (extended by `waaseyaa/field`'s `FieldDefinitionInterface`) and the entity-cast Coercion seam — `Waaseyaa\TypedData\Coercion\EntityCastCoercion` + `Waaseyaa\TypedData\Coercion\CoercionException`, consumed by entity `ValueCaster`. The `waaseyaa/typed-data` package is now just that contract + coercion seam (its composer description was updated to match). Pre-1.0-alpha: removed without a deprecation shim; full Unit + Integration suites green confirm nothing live depended on the removed half. Plan: `docs/audits/c24-field-item-layer-deprecation-scoping.md`.
- **BREAKING (pre-1.0): the dead Drupal-lineage Field-API item/value-object layer was removed (audit C-24, train 2 of 3).** Following train 1's extraction of the static field-type seam onto `AbstractFieldType`, the item-object layer it was entangled with — instantiated nowhere in production — is now gone. Removed published `@api` symbols: **`Waaseyaa\Field\FieldItemBase`**, **`Waaseyaa\Field\FieldItemInterface`**, **`Waaseyaa\Field\FieldItemListInterface`**, and the `internal` **`Waaseyaa\Field\ComputedFieldInterface`**; plus the non-surface `Waaseyaa\Field\FieldItemList`, `Waaseyaa\Field\PropertyValue`, and `Waaseyaa\Field\Item\ComputedItem` (the `computed` field type, whose only behaviour was the dead `compute()` — zero production callers — and which depended on the removed instance `getValue()`). The 16 remaining field-type plugins (`StringItem`, `IntegerItem`, … `ClassificationLabelFieldType`) are **reparented from `FieldItemBase` to `Waaseyaa\Field\AbstractFieldType`** (now `@api`, the public field-type base), and their now-dead instance-contract statics (`propertyDefinitions()`/`mainPropertyName()`) were removed. The live static descriptor seam (`schema`/`jsonSchema`/`defaultSettings`/`defaultValue`/`jsonSchemaFor`/`schemaFor`) and `FieldTypeInterface` are unchanged, so `FieldTypeManager` resolution is byte-identical. Pre-1.0-alpha: removed without a deprecation shim (no production path instantiated a field-type plugin; full Unit suite — 8617 tests — green confirms nothing live depended on the removed layer). **Custom field types must `extends AbstractFieldType` instead of `FieldItemBase`** (see `UPGRADING.md`). Train 3 retires the now-unused `typed-data` ancestry. Plan: `docs/audits/c24-field-item-layer-deprecation-scoping.md`.

### Changed

- **The live field-type "descriptor" static seam was extracted off the dead Field-API item layer (audit C-24, train 1 of 3 — behaviour-preserving).** `FieldTypeManager` resolves every field-type plugin by calling static methods on its class (`defaultSettings`/`defaultValue`/`schema`/`jsonSchema`/`jsonSchemaFor`/`schemaFor`) — it never instantiates a plugin. Those statics lived on `FieldItemBase`, which also carries the **dead** Field-API item/value-object layer (the `ComplexData`/`TypedData` instance methods, the `validate()` no-op, `PropertyValue`) that is instantiated nowhere in production. Train 1 moves the static seam into a new `@internal` abstract base `Waaseyaa\Field\AbstractFieldType`; `FieldItemBase` now `extends AbstractFieldType`, so the seam is single-sourced and **inherited unchanged** — no field-type plugin is reparented and no public symbol is removed yet. Acceptance: new `FieldTypeStaticSeamResolutionTest` (every discovered field type still resolves through the seam and reaches it via `AbstractFieldType`) + the full field suite (480 tests) stays green. **Train 2** reparents the field-type plugins directly onto `AbstractFieldType` and removes the dead instance layer (`FieldItemList`/`FieldItemBase` instance methods/`PropertyValue`) + the tests that exercise it; **train 3** retires the now-unused `typed-data` ancestry. Pre-1.0-alpha: trains 2–3 break the dead published `@api` symbols rather than shimming. Plan: `docs/audits/c24-field-item-layer-deprecation-scoping.md`.

### Added

- **Authorized download path for private attachment bytes — `GET /attachment/{id}/download` (CL-13, slice 1 of 2).** Entity-row access policies protect the attachment *record*; previously nothing in the framework served its *bytes*, and the `public://` + `/files/` convention encouraged hosts to serve `storage/files` statically (which would expose any attachment's bytes by URL regardless of the entity policy). This adds the safe serving path: a new `private://` scheme on a non-served root (`storage/private-files`, deliberately a sibling of `storage/files` so a `/files` static mount can't reach it — override via `config['attachment']['private_files_root']`), resolved through `Waaseyaa\Attachment\Storage\PrivateFileStore` with `realpath()` containment (defeats `../` traversal + symlink escapes). `AttachmentDownloadRouter` (a foundation `DomainRouterInterface`, matched via the `attachment.download` controller string registered in `BuiltinRouteRegistrar` next to `media.upload` — an L2 package can't depend on routing/L4) is the enforcement point: it runs the **deny-by-default** `view` check — which `ParentDelegatedAccessPolicy` delegates to the owning parent entity — and only then streams the bytes; an unauthorized caller, a missing/unbound account, a non-`private://` `storage_uri`, or a traversal URI all return **404** (never 403 — no existence oracle). Public assets keep `public://` and the host's normal static path, unaffected. Acceptance: `AttachmentDownloadRouterTest` (authorized caller streams the bytes; unauthorized/no-account/non-private/traversal all 404 with no bytes). **Slice 2 (media, follow-up)** adds the same authorized download for `media`, resolving the file URI through the media type's source plugin (the single source of truth — not a duplicate file-uri field).

### Fixed

- **`LanguageManager::getFallbackChain()` now strips a BCP 47 singleton subtag section as one unit, so private-use and extension langcodes resolve to their base language.** The auto-derivation did a naive `explode('-')` plus a single `array_pop`, which understood region variants (`fr-CA` to `fr`) but not the `-x-` (private use) or extension (`-u-`/`-t-`) singletons. As a result `oj-x-sagamok` derived the bogus parent `oj-x` and then jumped straight to the default (`oj-x-sagamok`, `oj-x`, `en`), skipping the real base language `oj`. The derivation now locates the first single-character (singleton) subtag and drops it together with everything after it in one step, so `oj-x-sagamok` resolves to `oj` then `en`, and `oj-x-a-b` resolves to `oj` then `en`. Region behaviour (`fr-CA` to `fr`) and custom `fallbackMap` overrides (which still win over auto-derivation) are unchanged, and the chain still always ends with the default language. Acceptance: `LanguageManagerTest::{it_strips_a_bcp47_private_use_section_in_full, it_strips_a_multi_subtag_private_use_section_in_full, custom_fallback_map_overrides_private_use_auto_derivation}`.
- **`#[GateAttribute]` was a false-promise footgun: its `@api` docblock claimed "the AccessChecker reads this attribute and calls the Gate," but nothing in production scanned it — a route relying on it was silently unprotected (§3.4 residual-security-sweep).** The real, enforced mechanism is the `_gate` route option that `AccessChecker::checkGate()` already reads at request time; it was simply never wired from a fluent builder. This PR adds `RouteBuilder::gate(string $ability, mixed $subject = null): self` (mirroring `requirePermission()`/`requireRole()`) so routes can opt into Gate enforcement via `->gate('config.export')` in the same builder chain. The `GateAttribute` class is corrected and marked `@deprecated`: it is explicitly documented as a declarative marker only (not auto-enforced), with pointers to `RouteBuilder::gate()` and `AccessChecker::applyGateToRoute()` for the real enforcement paths. The attribute is kept (not deleted) because consumers may reference it; removal is a separate BC decision. **Reachability note:** footgun, not a live framework hole — no framework route relied on `#[GateAttribute]` for protection; the published security claim is now true.

- **`llms.txt` text fields and link URLs are now sanitized — entity-derived title/summary/URL values were emitted raw, enabling link/content spoofing in the agent-facing `text/plain` output (WP07 / §2.8).** `LlmsTxtGenerator::generate()` passed `$topic->title`, `$topic->summary`, `$linkTitle`, and the site title/summary directly into the output without sanitizing newlines: a CR/LF in any of these fields forged new `## section` headings or `- [link](url)` lines, letting an attacker-controlled content-author (or ingested crawl content) inject arbitrary llms.txt structure. Link URLs were also emitted raw — `escape()` only handled `[`/`]`, so a `javascript:` or `data:` URL passed straight through. Fix: `sanitizeText()` collapses all CR/LF/tab/control characters (`\x00–\x1F\x7F`) to spaces in every human-text field before it is written to a line; `isSafeLinkUrl()` skips a link URL only when it carries an explicit non-`http(s)` scheme (`javascript:`/`data:`/`file:`/`vbscript:`, …) or contains embedded whitespace/control characters. Relative URLs (no scheme — e.g. `/articles/first.md`) are still allowed, because the generator is routing-agnostic and the caller's `buildLink` callback may legitimately emit site-relative paths. Public `LlmsTxtGenerator` signature unchanged; clean `http(s)` and relative-path data produce byte-identical output. **Reachability note:** LOW severity — agent-facing `text/plain` only (not browser XSS); an attacker must control a published entity's title, summary, or URL (content author or ingested crawl content) to forge llms.txt structure or inject unsafe link schemes.

- **`/oidc/revoke` now refuses to revoke a token that was not issued to the authenticating client — any authenticated client could silently revoke any other client's token given its value (WP06 / §2.7, RFC 7009 §2.1 violation).** `RevocationController::tryRevokeRefresh()` and `tryRevokeAccess()` previously looked up the token by its opaque value and revoked it unconditionally, with no comparison against the authenticated `client_id`. The fix threads the validated `$authClientId` (from the `__invoke` authentication block) into both inner methods: after locating the record, each method compares its `client_id` field against `$authClientId` and returns `false` (no-op) if they differ — the response stays `200` in all cases (RFC 7009 §2.2 anti-enumeration posture is preserved). **Reachability note:** LOW severity — denial-of-service only (revoke denies the token's owner future use); no data escalation or disclosure. The attacker must already possess the high-entropy opaque token value (32 random bytes, URL-safe base64), which means they are already in a position to use or replay it. This is an RFC-conformance hardening rather than a live-exploit response.

- **Media uploads no longer silently overwrite each other under a predictable public filename (WP05 / §2.6).** `MediaRouter` called a deterministic `sanitizeUploadFilename()` helper that always mapped `logo.png` → `logo.png`, so a second upload of the same client filename clobbered the first file on disk and produced an identical, guessable public URL (`/files/logo.png`, `public://logo.png`). The now-removed `sanitizeUploadFilename()` is replaced by `UploadHandler::generateSafeFilename()`, which was already present in the package but unwired — it appends an 8 hex-character random suffix (`logo_a3f1c8b2.png`) making stored filenames both non-colliding and unpredictable. SVG uploads previously in the allowed list also receive the unguessable suffix, closing the same window. **Reachability note:** low-priv authenticated uploader required; the impact is integrity (files silently replaced) and predictable public URLs, not private-byte disclosure.

- **Unpublished (inactive) path aliases no longer leak to anonymous visitors — `PathAliasAccessPolicy` returned `allowed` for `view` unconditionally, ignoring the alias's own `status` field (WP04 / §2.5).** `PathAlias` documents a `status` boolean ("to control alias visibility") and exposes `isPublished()`, but the policy's `view` branch emitted `AccessResult::allowed('Path aliases are publicly viewable.')` without consulting it — so any account could view and therefore resolve an inactive/unpublished alias mapping. For non-admins, `view` now returns `neutral` when the alias is not published (which deny-by-default correctly converts to denied) and `allowed` when it is published, preserving all existing public-alias read behaviour. Admins holding `administer url aliases` continue to pass through the early-exit unconditionally. **Reachability note:** low severity — the leak discloses the existence and `system path ↔ alias` mapping of inactive aliases to any caller that can hit a `path_alias` read surface (e.g. a JSON:API `GET /api/path_alias` collection); it does not expose the aliased content itself, which is governed by the target entity's own access policy. Acceptance: `PathAliasAccessPolicyTest::{anon_cannot_view_an_unpublished_path_alias, anon_can_view_a_published_path_alias, admin_can_view_an_unpublished_path_alias}` (the first case verified to return `allowed` against the pre-fix code).

- **FTS5 `totalHits` and facet bucket counts are now access-filtered — an anonymous visitor could infer the existence of access-restricted documents through the raw SQL count and facet aggregates (WP03 / §2.2).** `Fts5SearchProvider::search()` ran a `SELECT COUNT(*)` and three `GROUP BY` facet queries over the **unfiltered** WHERE clause before the per-document access filter (a PHP `canView()` call in the hit loop, the only SQL-inexpressible predicate), so `totalHits` and facet bucket counts reflected the full candidate set — including entity-backed restricted documents (e.g. unpublished nodes) the acting account cannot view. Only `hits` was filtered; the count and facets were an anon-reachable existence/metadata oracle. Reachability: the public `search()` Twig function (`SearchTwigExtension`) wires directly to `Fts5SearchProvider` with the real `EntitySearchAccessChecker`, so any anonymous SSR search surface leaks the counts when the index holds restricted entity-backed documents (bodies were always filtered — this closes only the count/facet metadata oracle). Fix: when an access checker is wired, `totalHits` and facets are now derived from a single PHP scan (`accessFilteredCountAndFacets`) over the full candidate set (bounded by `MAX_ACCESS_SCAN = 1000` — a conservative under-count when capped, never an over-count, so the oracle stays closed). The fast SQL `COUNT` + `GROUP BY` path is preserved unchanged for the no-checker (doc-only index) case — no performance regression on that path. `totalPages` is recomputed from the filtered `totalHits`. `includeFacets: false` still skips facet aggregation; `totalHits` is still filtered-accurate regardless. Acceptance: `Fts5SearchProviderAccessFilteredCountTest::{total_hits_and_facets_are_filtered_when_checker_denies_one_document, no_checker_path_counts_all_documents_unchanged, total_hits_is_zero_when_checker_denies_all_documents, facets_excluded_but_count_still_filtered_when_include_facets_false}` (the three checker-path assertions verified to fail with `totalHits=2`/`facet bucket count=2` against the pre-fix code).

- **SSR `EntityRenderer` now drops `internal:true` and credential-named fields from rendered output — they appeared in Twig `fields` while every sibling surface (JSON:API, MCP, GraphQL) already dropped them (§2.4 defense-in-depth).** `EntityRenderer::render()` iterated `$display` (either from explicit view-mode config or from `buildDefaultDisplay()`) with no internal-field guard, so a field declared `settings['internal' => true]` or named `pass`/`password`/`password_hash` was formatted and placed into the `fields` bag passed to `entity.html.twig`. `ResourceSerializer::filterInternalFields()`, `EntityReadTool`, `RelationshipTraverseTool`, and the GraphQL `EntityTypeBuilder` (§2.3) all applied the identical `ALWAYS_INTERNAL_FIELDS` + `getSetting('internal') === true` predicate — SSR was the only read surface without it. `EntityRenderer` now carries the same `ALWAYS_INTERNAL_FIELDS` constant and skips any matching field as the first statement inside the `foreach ($display ...)` loop, covering both the explicit view-mode and default-display paths. **Reachability note:** no live credential leak existed at time of fix — the stock SSR content route only resolves content-group entity types, so `User` and other credential-bearing entities are not reachable through SSR rendering. The risk was a content entity that declares an `internal:true` field (e.g. a clearance-gated field) having it rendered via SSR while it was hidden on every other surface. This fix brings SSR to parity with its siblings (defense-in-depth). Acceptance: `EntityRendererTest::drops_internal_and_credential_fields_from_default_display` (the `secret_token` and `password` assertions verified to fail against the pre-fix renderer, then green after the guard was added).
- **GraphQL output schema now drops `internal:true` fields and credential-named fields at the schema level — they were queryable and disclosed by introspection (§2.3 residual-security-sweep).** `EntityTypeBuilder::buildOutputFields()` iterated every `FieldDefinition` from `resolveFieldDefinitions()` and emitted it unconditionally. A field declared `settings['internal' => true]` (e.g. `two_factor_secret`) or named `pass`/`password`/`password_hash` was therefore queryable via GraphQL **and** disclosed by name in introspection (`__type`, `__schema`) — while those same fields were silently dropped by `ResourceSerializer::filterInternalFields()`, `EntityReadTool`, and `JsonApiController::rejectInternalQueryFields()` on every other read surface. `EntityTypeBuilder` now applies the identical predicate (`ALWAYS_INTERNAL_FIELDS` constant + `$def->getSetting('internal') === true`) as a schema-level drop: the field is absent from the built `ObjectType`'s field map, closing both the data-query path and the introspection path simultaneously (the field map is the introspection source). The fix is output-schema only — `buildInputFields()` (write/mass-assignment) is governed by the edit-access gate and is not changed. Reachability: GraphQL is a live query+introspection surface; pre-fix, any entity marking a field `internal:true` without also writing a `FieldAccessPolicy` forbid leaked it via GraphQL while it was hidden on every other surface, and introspection disclosed the field name to any caller that could reach `/graphql`. Schema-level drop closes both without requiring a per-entity policy. Acceptance: `EntityTypeBuilderInternalFieldTest::{fieldWithInternalSettingIsAbsentFromOutputType, credentialNamedFieldPasswordIsAbsentFromOutputType, credentialNamedFieldPasswordHashIsAbsentFromOutputType, normalFieldIsPresentInOutputType}` (the three absent-field assertions verified to fail against the pre-fix schema).
- **`vector.search` agent tool now applies the per-entity `view` gate + field-access filter to every hit — it returned raw similarity rows (entity id + metadata + the embedding vector) with no access check (security).** `VectorSearchTool::execute()` checked only the coarse `tool.vector.search` capability, then echoed `EmbeddingStorageInterface::search()`'s rows verbatim. Unlike its siblings `EntityReadTool`/`EntityListTool`/`EntitySearchTool` it applied neither the per-entity `view` gate nor field-access filtering — the same disclosure class as #1768 (`relationship.traverse`), here for an **authenticated** initiator granted `tool.vector.search` without `view` on the matched entities (`tool.vector.search` is **not** in `PublicAnonymousAuth::DEFAULT_READ_CAPABILITIES`, so it is not anon-reachable). A vector hit carries only a type+id (not a hydrated entity), so the tool now takes an injected `EntityTypeManagerInterface`, **loads** each hit's backing entity, drops it unless `canViewEntity()` allows (failing closed when enforcement is required but the entity cannot be loaded — stale embedding / unknown type — so an ungateable hit is never disclosed), runs surviving metadata through `applyFieldAccessFilter()`, and reshapes the output to `{entity_type, id, score, metadata}` (the raw embedding vector is no longer echoed). Handler-less (capability-only) consumers are unaffected — raw hits pass through unchanged. Note: the stock `AutowiringToolContainer` cannot build this tool anyway (its embedding/vector `\Closure` resolvers are unbindable), so it is live only in a host that wires those closures via a real container; the fix makes the stock tool honor the enumeration-tool contract when it *is* wired. Acceptance: `VectorSearchAccessFilterTest::{search_drops_a_view_forbidden_result, without_a_handler_the_tool_is_capability_only, enforced_without_a_handler_fails_closed, metadata_is_filtered_through_field_access}`.
- **`node`'s system/identity fields are now edit-forbidden on update for non-admins — they had no `FieldAccessPolicy`, so the JSON:API write path mass-assigned them (security).** `NodeAccessPolicy` implemented only the entity-level `AccessPolicyInterface`; node declared `uid` (authorship), `type` (the `readOnly` bundle), and the `created`/`changed` timestamps as ordinary `#[Field]`s with **no** field-level policy. The JSON:API write path runs `checkFieldAccess('edit')` **open-by-default** (only an explicit `Forbidden` denies), so once an account passed the entity `update` check — e.g. a user with `edit own {type} content` — it could `PATCH /api/node/{id}` to **reassign authorship** (`uid`), **change the bundle** (`type`, despite `readOnly` — moving the node into a bundle with different field-access/visibility rules), or **forge timestamps**. `NodeAccessPolicy` now also implements `FieldAccessPolicyInterface` and **Forbids** edit of `uid`/`type`/`created`/`changed` for accounts without `administer nodes` — but only on an **existing** node (`!isNew()`), so creation (where the bundle/authorship are part of authoring) is unaffected. The editorial booleans `status`/`promote`/`sticky` are intentionally left writable (whether an author may self-publish/promote is a separate permission-model decision, recorded for follow-up). Acceptance: `NodeFieldAccessPolicyTest` (each system field forbidden for an author on an existing node, allowed for an admin and at create) + `JsonApiAccessIntegrationTest::{authorCannotReassignAuthorshipOfOwnNodeViaUpdate, authorCannotChangeBundleOfOwnNodeViaUpdate}` (end-to-end 403; both verified to succeed with `201`/`200` against the pre-fix code). No change to node creation or admin edits.
- **`relationship.traverse` agent tool now applies the per-entity `view` access gate — it enumerated relationship rows with no access check and is in the DEFAULT anonymous MCP allowlist (security, anon-reachable).** `RelationshipTraverseTool::execute()` checked only the coarse `tool.relationship.traverse` capability, then ran `EntityRepositoryInterface::findBy()` (no access filtering) and emitted every matching row's full values.
